Dedication And Appreciation

# ↩

奉献与欣赏


This book is dedicated to Elizabeth. You are my partner, my muse, and my best friend. If not for you, I would still be a hopelessly clueless Nice Guy. You are truly a gift from God. Thank you.

谨以此书献给伊丽莎白。你是我的伴侣，我的缪斯女神，也是我最好的朋友。如果没有你，我现在可能还是个无可救药的“好人”。你真是上帝赐予的礼物。谢谢你。

For David, Jamie, Steve, and Grant. You are the greatest kids a parent could want. You are each so different and unique that you make parenting a never-ending joy. Thank you for all the times you asked,

致大卫、杰米、史蒂夫和格兰特。你们是父母梦寐以求的最棒的孩子。你们每个人都如此独特，让养育子女成为一种永不枯竭的快乐。感谢你们一直以来的关怀。

"When are you going to finish your book?" Don't ever stop being just who you are.

“你什么时候才能完成你的书？”永远不要停止做你自己。

For the countless men and women who have invited me into the most personal areas of their lives. I have written this book for you. Thank you for your input and support in writing No More Mr. Nice Guy!

献给无数邀请我走进他们生活中最私密角落的男女。这本书是为你们而写的。感谢你们在《不再是好人》一书中的贡献和支持！

Especially for all the men who have been a part of my No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's groups. You will never know how much being a part of your lives and has changed my own life. Thank you.

尤其要感谢所有加入过我的“不再是好人！”（No More Mr. Nice Guy!）男士团体的男士们。你们永远不知道，成为你们生活的一部分，以及我自己的生活，对我产生了多大的改变。谢谢你们。

For Dr. Anne Hastings. Your wisdom and insight can be found on every page of this book. You have helped me believe that it is OK for me to be who I am, just as I am. Thank you.

献给安妮·黑斯廷斯博士。您的智慧和洞见在本书的每一页都能找到。您帮助我相信，做我自己就好，做我自己就好。谢谢您。

For Debby Duvall. Your editorial skills have covered a multitude of my sins. Thank you for helping make this a better book.

致黛比·杜瓦尔。你的编辑技巧掩盖了我的诸多不足。感谢你帮助我把这本书写得更好。

Introduction

介绍


Five decades of dramatic social change and monumental shifts in the traditional family have created a breed of men who have been conditioned to seek the approval of others.

五十年来剧烈的社会变革和传统家庭的巨大转变造就了一批习惯于寻求他人认可的男性。

I call these men Nice Guys .

我把这些人称为“好人”。

Nice Guys are concerned about looking good and doing it "right." They are happiest when they are making others happy. Nice Guys avoid conflict like the plague and will go to great lengths to avoid upsetting anyone. In general, Nice Guys are peaceful and generous. Nice Guys are especially concerned about pleasing women and being different from other men. In a nutshell, Nice Guys believe that if they are good, giving, and caring, they will in return be happy, loved, and fulfilled.

好男人注重外表，注重做事“正确”。当他们让别人快乐时，他们最快乐。好男人像躲避瘟疫一样避免冲突，并会竭尽全力避免惹恼任何人。总的来说，好男人平和慷慨。他们尤其注重取悦女性，并与其他男性有所不同。简而言之，好男人相信，如果他们善良、慷慨、体贴，他们也会得到快乐、被爱和满足的回报。

Sound too good to be true?

听起来好得令人难以置信？

It is.

这是。

Over the last several years, I have encountered countless frustrated and resentful Nice Guys in my practice as a psychotherapist. These passively pleasing men struggle in vain to experience the happiness they so desperately crave and believe they deserve. This frustration is due to the fact that Nice Guys have believed a myth.

过去几年，我作为一名心理治疗师，在实践中遇到过无数沮丧、怨恨的好男人。这些被动取悦的男人徒劳地挣扎着，想要体验他们如此渴望、并自以为应得的幸福。这种沮丧源于好男人相信了一个神话。

This myth is the essence of what I call the Nice Guy Syndrome. The Nice Guy Syndrome represents a belief that if Nice Guys are "good," they will be loved, get their needs met, and live a problem-free life.

这个迷思的精髓就是我所说的“好男人综合症”。“好男人综合症”代表着这样一种信念：如果好男人“好”，他们就会被爱，需求得到满足，生活无忧无虑。

When this life strategy fails to produce the desired results — as it often does — Nice Guys usually just try harder, doing more of the same. Due to the sense of helplessness and resentment this pattern inevitably produces, Nice Guys are often anything but nice.

当这种生活策略未能产生预期效果时——这种情况经常发生——好男人通常会更加努力，重复同样的行为。由于这种模式不可避免地会产生无助感和怨恨感，好男人往往一点也不好。

The concept of the Nice Guy Syndrome grew out of my own frustration of trying to do it "right," yet never getting back what I believed I deserved. I was the typical "sensitive new age guy" — and proud of it. I believed I was one of the nicest guys you would ever meet. Yet I wasn't happy.

“好人综合症”这个概念源于我内心的沮丧，我试图“做好事”，却从未得到我认为自己应得的回报。我是典型的“敏感的新时代男人”，并且以此为荣。我自认为是你能遇到的最好的男人之一。然而，我并不快乐。

As I began exploring my own Nice Guy behaviors — caretaking, giving to get, fixing, keeping the peace, avoiding conflict, seeking approval, hiding mistakes — I started noticing numerous men with similar traits in my counseling practice. It dawned on me that the script guiding my own life was not an isolated incident, but the product of a social dynamic that affected countless adult males.

当我开始探索自己的“好男人”行为——照顾他人、为了获得而付出、修复关系、维持和平、避免冲突、寻求认可、掩盖错误——时，我开始在咨询实践中注意到许多男人都有类似的特质。我逐渐意识到，我人生的剧本并非孤立事件，而是影响了无数成年男性的社会动态的产物。

Up until now, no one has taken the problem of the Nice Guy Syndrome seriously or offered an effective solution. This is why I wrote No More Mr. Nice Guy!

到目前为止，还没有人认真对待“好人综合症”的问题，也没有人提出有效的解决方案。这就是我写《不再是好人！》的原因！

This book shows Nice Guys how to stop seeking approval and start getting what they want in love and life. The information presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! represents a proven plan to help passively pleasing men break free from the ineffective patterns of the Nice Guy Syndrome. It is based on my own experience of recovery and my work with countless Nice Guys over the last twenty years.

这本书向“好男人”们展示了如何停止寻求认可，并开始在爱情和生活中获得他们想要的一切。《不再是好男人！》一书中提供的信息代表了一个行之有效的方案，可以帮助那些被动取悦的男人摆脱“好男人综合症”的无效模式。本书基于我自己的康复经验，以及过去二十年来与无数“好男人”的合作。

No More Mr. Nice Guy! is unashamedly pro-male. Nevertheless, I have had countless women support the writing of this book. Women who read the book regularly tell me that it not only helps them better understand their Nice Guy partner, it also helps them gain new insights about themselves.

《不再是好人！》一书毫不掩饰地支持男性。尽管如此，我还是得到了无数女性的支持。经常阅读这本书的女性告诉我，这本书不仅帮助她们更好地理解自己的好人伴侣，也帮助她们对自己有了新的认识。

The information and tools presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! work. If you are a frustrated Nice Guy, the principles presented in the following pages will change your life. You will:

《不再是好人！》一书中提供的信息和工具非常有效。如果你是一个苦恼的好人，那么以下几页中提出的原则将会改变你的人生。你将：

  • Learn effective ways to get your needs met.
    学习有效的方法来满足你的需求。
  • Begin to feel more powerful and confident.
    开始感觉更强大、更自信。
  • Create the kind of intimate relationships you really want.
    建立你真正想要的亲密关系。
  • Learn to express your feelings and emotions.
    学会表达你的感受和情绪。
  • Have a fulfilling and exciting sex life.
    拥有充实而刺激的性生活。
  • Embrace your masculinity and build meaningful relationships with men.
    拥抱你的男子气概，与男性建立有意义的关系。
  • Live up to your potential and become truly creative and productive.
    发挥你的潜力，变得真正富有创造力和生产力。
  • Accept yourself just as you are.
    接受真实的自己。

If the above traits sound good to you, your journey of breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome has just begun. It is time to stop seeking approval and start getting what you want in love and life.

如果以上特质对你来说很有帮助，那么你摆脱“好男人综合症”的旅程才刚刚开始。是时候停止寻求认可，开始在爱情和生活中追求你想要的一切了。

Chapter 1

# ↩

第一章

The Nice Guy Syndrome

好人综合症


"I'm a Nice Guy. I'm one of the nicest guys you're ever going to meet."

“我是个好人。我是你见过的最好的人之一。”

Jason, a chiropractor in his mid-thirties, began his first session of individual therapy with this introduction. Jason described his life as "perfect" — except for one major problem — his sex life. It had been several months since he and his wife Heather had been sexual and it didn't look like anything was going to change soon.

三十五六岁的脊椎按摩师杰森，就是在这种情况下开始了他的第一次个人治疗。杰森形容自己的生活“完美”——除了一个主要问题——他的性生活。他和妻子希瑟已经好几个月没有过性生活了，而且看起来短期内不会有什么改变。

Jason spoke openly about his marriage, his family, and his sexuality. An affable man, he seemed to welcome the opportunity to talk about himself and his life.

杰森公开谈论了他的婚姻、家庭和性取向。他为人随和，似乎很乐意有机会谈论自己和自己的生活。

More than anything, Jason wanted to be liked. He saw himself as a very generous, giving person. He prided himself on not having many ups and downs and for never losing his temper. He revealed that he liked to make people happy and that he hated conflict. To avoid rocking the boat with his wife, he tended to hold back his feelings and tried to do everything "right."

杰森最渴望的是被人喜欢。他认为自己非常慷慨大方，乐于奉献。他以平日里少有的起伏和从不发脾气而自豪。他透露自己喜欢让人开心，讨厌冲突。为了避免和妻子闹矛盾，他倾向于压抑自己的情绪，努力把每件事都做得“正确”。

After this introduction, Jason took a piece of paper out of his pocket and began to unfold it. While doing so, he stated that he had written a few things down so he wouldn't forget them.

介绍完毕后，杰森从口袋里掏出一张纸，展开来。他一边说着，一边写下了一些事情，以免忘记。

"I can never do it right," Jason began, looking over his list. "No matter how hard I try, Heather always finds something wrong. I don't deserve to be treated this way. I try to be a good husband and father, but it's never good enough."

“我永远都做不好，”杰森看着他的清单说道。“不管我怎么努力，希瑟总能找出问题所在。我不该受到这样的对待。我努力做一个好丈夫、好父亲，但永远不够好。”

Jason paused as he looked over his list.

杰森查看清单时停了下来。

"This morning is a good example," he continued. "While Heather was getting ready for work, I got our baby Chelsie up, fed her breakfast, gave her a bath. I had her all ready to go and was about to get ready myself. Then Heather walked in and got that look on her face. I knew I was in trouble."

“今天早上就是一个很好的例子，”他继续说道。“希瑟准备上班的时候，我把我们的小宝贝切尔西叫醒，给她喂了早餐，给她洗了澡。我把她准备好了，自己也正准备出发。这时，希瑟走了进来，脸上露出了那种表情。我知道我有麻烦了。”

"'Why'd you dress her in that? That's a good outfit.'" Jason mimicked his wife's tone. "I didn't know she wanted Chelsie to wear something different. After everything I did to get her ready this morning, it was still wrong."

“‘你干嘛给她穿那套？这衣服挺好看的。’”杰森模仿妻子的语气。“我不知道她想让切尔西穿别的衣服。今天早上我费了好大劲才把她打扮好，结果还是不合适。”

"Here's another example," Jason continued, "the other day I cleaned the kitchen and did a real good job.

“这是另一个例子，”杰森继续说道，“有一天我打扫了厨房，做得非常好。

I loaded the dishwasher, did the pots and pans, and swept the floor. I thought Heather would really appreciate all that I was doing to help out. Before I was finished, she walked in and asked, 'How come you didn't wipe off the counters?' I wasn't even done, for goodness sake. But instead of noticing all that I had done and thanking me, she focused on the one thing I hadn't finished yet."

我把碗碟装进洗碗机，洗了锅碗瓢盆，还扫了地。我以为希瑟会很感激我帮忙。可我还没做完，她就进来问：“你怎么没擦台面？”我的天哪，我还没做完呢。但她非但没有注意到我做的这些，也没有感谢我，反而盯着我还没做完的那件事。

"Then there is the 'sex thing,'" Jason continued. "We only messed around a few times before we got married because we're both Christians. Sex is real important to me, but Heather just isn't interested. I thought once you got married, everything was supposed to be great. After all I do for Heather, you'd think she be willing to give me the one thing I really want."

“还有‘性’的事，”杰森继续说道。“因为我们都是基督徒，所以结婚前我们只搞过几次关系。性对我来说很重要，但希瑟就是不感兴趣。我以为结婚后一切都会很美好。毕竟我为希瑟付出了那么多，你以为她会愿意给我我真正想要的东西吗？”

"I do a lot more than most guys. It seems like I'm always giving so much more than I get." Now, looking like a little boy on the couch, Jason pleaded, "All I want is to be loved and appreciated. Is that too much to ask?"

“我比大多数男人付出的都多。我付出的似乎总是比得到的多。”现在，杰森像个小男孩一样躺在沙发上，恳求道：“我想要的只是被爱和被欣赏。这要求过分吗？”


Some Of The Nicest Guys You Will Ever Meet

你会遇到一些最友善的人

Men like Jason walk into my office on a surprisingly regular basis. These guys come in all shapes and sizes yet they all have the same basic world view. Let me introduce you to a few more.

像杰森这样的人经常来我的办公室，频率之高令人咋舌。这些人形形色色，却都秉持着相同的基本世界观。让我再给你介绍几个。

Omar

奥马尔

Omar's number one goal in life is to please his girlfriend. Nevertheless, she complains that he is never emotionally available for her. In fact, every one of his previous girlfriends has had the same complaint.

奥马尔人生的首要目标是取悦女友。然而，女友却抱怨他从来没在感情上对她付出过。事实上，他之前的每一位女友都有过同样的抱怨。

Since Omar sees himself as such a giver, he can't understand these accusations. Omar states that his greatest joy in life is making other people happy. He even carries a pager so his friends can get in touch with him if they need anything.

奥马尔自认为是个乐于奉献的人，所以他无法理解这些指责。奥马尔说，他人生最大的乐趣就是让别人快乐。他甚至随身带着传呼机，这样朋友们有需要的时候就能联系到他。

Todd

托德

Todd prides himself on treating women with honesty and respect. He believes these traits set him apart from other men and should attract women to him. Though he has many female friends, he rarely dates.

托德以诚实和尊重对待女性而自豪。他相信这些特质使他与众不同，应该会吸引女性。虽然他有很多女性朋友，但他很少约会。

The women he knows tell him what a great listener he is and often call him to share their problems. He likes feeling needed. These female friends constantly tell him what a great "catch" he will make for some lucky woman. In spite of the way he treats women, he can't understand why they all seem to be attracted to jerks, rather than Nice Guys like him.

他认识的女人们都说他是个很棒的倾听者，经常打电话跟他倾诉她们的烦恼。他喜欢被需要的感觉。这些女性朋友总是告诉他，他会成为某个幸运女人多么棒的“猎物”。尽管他对待女人的方式如此，但他不明白为什么她们似乎都喜欢混蛋，而不是像他这样的好男人。

Bill

账单

Bill is the person to whom everyone turns when they need something. The word "no" just isn't in his vocabulary. He fixes cars for women at his church. He coaches his son's little league baseball team. His buddies call on him when they need help moving. He looks after his widowed mother every evening after work. Even though it makes him feel good to give to others, he never seems to get as much as he gives.

比尔是那种每个人都会在需要的时候向他求助的人。他的字典里从来就没有“不”这个词。他在教堂里为女性修车。他执教儿子所在的少年棒球联盟。他的朋友们搬家时需要帮忙时也会找他。他每天下班后都会照顾丧偶的母亲。虽然给予他人让他感觉良好，但他得到的似乎永远不如他付出的多。

Gary

加里

Gary's wife has frequent rage attacks in which she verbally shames and demeans him. Because he is afraid of conflict and doesn't want to rock the boat, Gary will avoid bringing up subjects that he knows might make his wife angry. After a fight, he is always the first one to apologize. He cannot recall his wife ever saying she was sorry for any of her behaviors. In spite of the constant conflict, Gary says he loves his wife and would do anything to please her.

加里的妻子经常勃然大怒，用言语羞辱和贬低他。由于害怕冲突，也不想惹是生非，加里总是避免提起那些他知道可能会惹妻子生气的话题。吵架之后，他总是第一个道歉。他不记得妻子曾经为自己的任何行为道歉过。尽管冲突不断，加里说他爱妻子，愿意做任何事让她高兴。

Rick

里克

Rick, a gay man in his early forties, is in a committed relationship with an alcoholic. Rick came to counseling to help his partner Jay with his drinking problem. Rick complains that it always feels as if it is up to him to hold everything together. His hope is that if he can help Jay get sober, he will finally have the kind of relationship he has always wanted.

里克是一位四十出头的同性恋男子，他与一位酗酒者保持着一段稳定的恋爱关系。里克来寻求心理咨询，是为了帮助他的伴侣杰伊解决酗酒问题。里克抱怨说，他总觉得自己必须独自承担一切。他希望，如果能帮助杰伊戒酒，他最终就能拥有他一直渴望的那种感情。

Lyle

莱尔

Lyle, a devout Christian, tries to do everything right. He teaches Sunday school and is an elder in his church. Nevertheless, he has struggled since adolescence with an addiction to pornography. Lyle masturbates compulsively, often three to four times a day. He spends hours every day looking at sexually explicit websites on the internet. He is terrified that if anyone ever finds out the truth about his sexual compulsions, his life will be destroyed. He tries to control his problem with prayer and Bible study, although neither of these approaches has done much good.

莱尔是一位虔诚的基督徒，他努力做好每一件事。他在主日学任教，也是教会的长老。然而，自青少年时期起，他就一直沉迷于色情内容。莱尔有强迫性手淫的毛病，通常一天三四次。他每天要花几个小时浏览互联网上的色情网站。他非常害怕，如果有人发现了他性冲动的真相，他的生活就会被毁掉。他试图通过祷告和研读圣经来控制自己的问题，但这两种方法都收效甚微。

Jose

何塞

Jose, a business consultant in his late thirties, has spent the last five years in a relationship with a woman he considers needy and dependent. Jose began thinking about breaking up the day she first moved in. He is afraid that his girlfriend wouldn't be able to make it on her own if he left her. Although he has made several aborted attempts to break up, his girlfriend always becomes such an "emotional basket case" that he gets back together with her. Jose spends just about every waking moment trying to figure out how to get out of the relationship without hurting his girlfriend or looking like a jerk.

何塞是一位年近四十的商业顾问，过去五年一直与一位他认为需要依赖他人的女性交往。何塞在女友搬来那天就开始考虑分手。他担心如果自己离开，女友将无法独自生活。尽管他多次尝试分手，但都以失败告终，因为女友总是变得“情绪崩溃”，最终他还是和她复合了。何塞几乎每分每秒都在思考如何才能摆脱这段感情，既不伤害女友，也不让自己看起来像个混蛋。


Who Are These Men?

这些人是谁？

Though all of these men are unique, each shares a common life script: They all believe that if they are "good" and do everything "right," they will be loved, get their needs met, and have a problem-free life.

尽管这些男人都是独一无二的，但他们每个人都有一个共同的生活脚本：他们都相信，只要他们是“善良的”，并且把每件事都做得“正确”，他们就会被爱，他们的需求就会得到满足，并且过上无忧无虑的生活。

This attempt to be good typically involves trying to eliminate or hide certain things about themselves (their mistakes, needs, emotions) and become what they believe others want them to be (generous, helpful, peaceful, etc.).

这种向善的尝试通常包括试图消除或隐藏他们自身的某些特质（他们的错误、需求、情绪），并成为他们认为别人希望他们成为的人（慷慨、乐于助人、平和等）。

I call these men Nice Guys .

我把这些人称为“好人”。

Up to now we haven't paid much attention to the Nice Guy, but he is everywhere.

到目前为止，我们还没有太多关注这个好人，但他无处不在。

He is the relative who lets his wife run the show.

他是一位让妻子掌管一切的亲戚。

He is the buddy who will do anything for anybody, but whose own life seems to be in shambles.

他是一个愿意为任何人做任何事的朋友，但他自己的生活似乎却一团糟。

He is the guy who frustrates his wife or girlfriend because he is so afraid of conflict that nothing ever gets resolved.

他会让妻子或女友感到沮丧，因为他太害怕冲突，所以什么问题都无法解决。

He is the boss who tells one person what they want to hear, then reverses himself to please someone else.

他是一位只对一个人说他们想听的话，然后却为了取悦别人而反过来说的老板。

He is the man who lets people walk all over him because he doesn't want to rock the boat.

他是一个任人欺负的人，因为他不想惹是生非。

He is the dependable guy at church or the club who will never say "no," but would never tell anyone if they were imposing on him.

在教堂或俱乐部里，他是一个值得信赖的人，他永远不会说“不”，但如果有人强迫他，他也不会告诉任何人。

He is the man whose life seems so under control, until BOOM, one day he does something to destroy it all.

他的生活似乎尽在掌握之中，直到有一天，他做出了一件毁掉一切的事情。


Characteristics of Nice Guys

好男人的特征

Every Nice Guy is unique, but all have a cluster of similar characteristics. These traits are the result of a script, often formed in childhood, that guides their lives. While other men may have one or two of these traits, Nice Guys seem to possess a significant number.

每个好男人都是独一无二的，但都拥有一些相似的特质。这些特质源于一种剧本，这种剧本通常在童年时期形成，并指导着他们的一生。虽然其他男人可能拥有其中的一两种特质，但好男人似乎拥有相当多的特质。

Nice Guys are givers . Nice Guys frequently state that it makes them feel good to give to others. These men believe their generosity is a sign of how good they are and will make other people love and appreciate them.

好男人是给予者。他们经常说，给予他人让他们感觉良好。他们相信自己的慷慨是他们优秀品质的体现，会让别人爱戴和欣赏他们。

Nice Guys fix and caretake . If a person has a problem, has a need, is angry, depressed or sad, Nice Guys will frequently attempt to solve or fix the situation (usually without being asked).

好男人会解决问题并照顾他人。如果一个人有问题、有需要、感到愤怒、沮丧或悲伤，好男人通常会主动尝试解决或修复这种情况（通常是主动要求的）。

Nice Guys seek approval from others . A universal trait of the Nice Guy Syndrome is the seeking of validation from others. Everything a Nice Guy does or says is at some level calculated to gain someone's approval or avoid disapproval. This is especially true in their relationships with women.

好男人寻求他人的认可。“好男人综合症”的一个普遍特征是寻求他人的认可。好男人所做的每一句话，在某种程度上都经过精心设计，以赢得他人的认可或避免他人的反对。在他们与女性的关系中尤其如此。

Nice Guys avoid conflict . Nice Guys seek to keep their world smooth. To do this, they avoid doing things that might rock the boat or upset anyone.

好男人避免冲突。好男人力求保持世界平稳。为此，他们会避免做那些可能引起争议或惹恼他人的事情。

Nice Guys believe they must hide their perceived flaws and mistakes . These men are afraid that others will get mad at them, shame them, or leave them if some mistake or shortcoming is exposed.

好男人认为他们必须隐藏自己察觉到的缺点和错误。他们害怕一旦错误或缺点暴露出来，别人会生气、羞辱他们，甚至抛弃他们。

Nice Guys seek the "right" way to do things . Nice Guys believe there is a key to having a happy, problem-free life. They are convinced that if they can only figure out the right way to do everything, nothing should ever go wrong.

好男人追求“正确”的做事方式。他们相信拥有幸福无忧的生活是有秘诀的。他们坚信，只要找到做每件事的正确方法，就不会出错。

Nice Guys repress their feelings . Nice Guys tend to analyze rather than feel. They may see feelings as a waste of time and energy. They frequently try to keep their feelings on an even keel.

好男人会压抑自己的感情。他们倾向于分析，而不是感受。他们可能认为感情是浪费时间和精力。他们经常试图保持情绪平和。

Nice Guys often try to be different from their fathers . Many Nice Guys report having unavailable, absent, passive, angry, philandering, or alcoholic fathers. It is not unusual for these men to make a decision at some point in their lives to try to be 180 degrees different from Dad.

好男人常常试图与自己的父亲截然不同。许多好男人都表示，他们的父亲要么不关心你，要么缺席你的照顾，要么消极被动，要么脾气暴躁，要么玩弄女人，要么酗酒。这些男人在人生的某个阶段决定尝试与父亲完全不同，这并不罕见。

Nice Guys are often more comfortable relating to women than to men. Due to their childhood conditioning, many Nice Guys have few male friends. Nice Guys frequently seek the approval of women and convince themselves they are different from other men. They like to believe that they are not selfish, angry, or abusive — traits they link to "other" men.

好男人通常与女性相处比与男性更自在。由于童年时期的习性，许多好男人几乎没有男性朋友。好男人经常寻求女性的认可，并说服自己与其他男人不同。他们喜欢相信自己不自私、不愤怒、不虐待——而这些特质是他们与“其他”男人联系在一起的。

Nice Guys have difficulty making their needs a priority . These men often feel that it is selfish to put their needs first. They believe it is a virtue to put others' needs ahead of their own.

好男人很难把自己的需求放在首位。这些男人常常觉得把自己的需求放在第一位是自私的。他们认为，把别人的需求放在自己需求之前是一种美德。

Nice Guys often make their partner their emotional center. Many Nice Guys report that they are only happy if their partner is happy. Therefore they will often focus tremendous energy on their intimate relationships.

好男人通常把伴侣视为自己的情感中心。许多好男人表示，只有伴侣快乐，他们才会快乐。因此，他们通常会将巨大的精力投入到亲密关系中。


What's Wrong With Being A Nice Guy?

做个好人有什么错？

We might be tempted to minimize the problem of the Nice Guy Syndrome. After all, how can being nice be such a bad thing? We might even chuckle at the Marvin Milquetoast behaviors of these men as portrayed in comic strips and television sitcoms. Since men already represent an easy target in our culture, the caricature of a sensitive guy might be an object of amusement rather than concern.

我们或许会试图淡化“好人综合症”的问题。毕竟，做好人怎么会是件坏事呢？我们甚至可能会对漫画和电视情景喜剧中描绘的这些男人的“马文·米尔克托斯特”式行为嗤之以鼻。在我们的文化中，男性本来就很容易被攻击，因此，对敏感男性的漫画描绘或许只是一种消遣，而非担忧。

Nice Guys themselves frequently have a difficult time grasping the depth and seriousness of their beliefs and behaviors. When I begin working with these passively pleasing men, almost without exception, they all ask, "What is wrong with being a Nice Guy?" Having picked up this book and puzzled over the title, you may be wondering the same thing.

好男人自己往往很难理解自己信仰和行为的深度和严肃性。当我开始与这些被动取悦的男人合作时，他们几乎无一例外地会问：“做个好男人有什么不好？” 拿起这本书，对书名感到困惑，你或许也会有同样的疑问。

By giving these men the label Nice Guy, I'm not so much referring to their actual behavior, but to their core belief system about themselves and the world around them. These men have been conditioned to believe that if they are "nice," they will be loved, get their needs met, and have a smooth life.

我给这些男人贴上“好人”的标签，与其说是指他们的实际行为，不如说是指他们对自己和周围世界的核心信念体系。这些男人已经习惯于相信，只要他们“好”，就会被爱，需求得到满足，生活也会一帆风顺。

The term Nice Guy is actually a misnomer because Nice Guys are often anything but nice. Here are some Not-So-Nice Traits of Nice Guys:

“好男人”这个词其实用词不当，因为好男人通常一点也不好。以下是一些“好男人”不太好的特征：

Nice Guys are dishonest . These men hide their mistakes, avoid conflict, say what they think people want to hear, and repress their feelings. These traits make Nice Guys fundamentally dishonest.

好男人是不诚实的。这些人会隐藏自己的错误，避免冲突，说他们认为别人想听的话，并且压抑自己的感情。这些特质使得好男人从根本上来说不诚实。

Nice Guys are secretive. Because they are so driven to seek approval, Nice Guys will hide anything that they believe might upset anyone. The Nice Guy motto is, "If at first you don't succeed, hide the evidence."

好男人很神秘。因为他们渴望得到认可，所以他们会隐藏任何他们认为可能惹恼别人的事情。好男人的座右铭是：“如果一开始你没有成功，就隐藏证据。”

Nice Guys are compartmentalized. Nice Guys are adept at harmonizing contradictory pieces of information about themselves by separating them into individual compartments in their minds. Therefore, a married man can create his own definition of fidelity which allows him to deny that he had an affair with his secretary (or intern) because he never put his penis in her vagina.

好男人善于将自我意识中相互矛盾的信息分割开来，从而协调它们。因此，已婚男人可以自行定义忠诚，从而可以否认自己与秘书（或实习生）有染，因为他从未将阴茎插入她的阴道。

Nice Guys are manipulative . Nice Guys tend to have a hard time making their needs a priority and have difficulty asking for what they want in clear and direct ways. This creates a sense of powerlessness. Therefore, they frequently resort to manipulation when trying to get their needs met.

好男人善于操纵。他们往往很难将自己的需求放在首位，也很难以清晰直接的方式表达自己的需求。这让他们感到无力。因此，他们常常会通过操纵来满足自己的需求。

Nice Guys are controlling . A major priority for Nice Guys is keeping their world smooth. This creates a constant need to try to control the people and things around them.

好男人控制欲强。好男人的首要任务就是让自己的世界保持平稳。这使得他们不断想要控制周围的人和事。

Nice Guys give to get . Though Nice Guys tend to be generous givers, their giving often has unconscious and unspoken strings attached. They want to be appreciated, they want some kind of reciprocation, they want someone to stop being angry at them, etc. Nice Guys often report feeling frustrated or resentful as a result of giving so much while seemingly getting so little in return.

好男人付出是为了得到。虽然好男人往往慷慨大方，但他们的付出往往带有一些不自觉的、不言而喻的附加条件。他们渴望得到赞赏，渴望得到某种回报，渴望有人不再对他们生气等等。好男人常常表示，他们付出太多，却似乎收获甚微，因此感到沮丧或怨恨。

Nice Guys are passive-aggressive . Nice Guys tend to express their frustration and resentment in indirect, roundabout, and not so nice ways. This includes being unavailable, forgetting, being late, not following through, not being able to get an erection, climaxing too quickly, and repeating the same annoying behaviors even when they have promised to never do them again.

好男人往往是被动攻击型的。他们倾向于用间接、迂回且不太友好的方式表达自己的沮丧和怨恨。这些方式包括：不联系、忘记、迟到、不坚持、无法勃起、高潮来得太快，以及即使承诺过不再做，却依然重复同样令人讨厌的行为。

Nice Guys are full of rage . Though Nice Guys frequently deny ever getting angry, a lifetime of frustration and resentment creates a pressure cooker of repressed rage deep inside these men. This rage tends to erupt at some of the most unexpected and seemingly inappropriate times.

好男人总是怒气冲冲。尽管好男人常常否认自己生气，但一生的挫败和怨恨，让他们内心深处积攒了压抑的愤怒，如同高压锅一般。这种愤怒往往会在最意想不到、最不合适的时刻爆发。

Nice Guys are addictive . Addictive behavior serves the purpose of relieving stress, altering moods, or medicating pain. Since Nice Guys tend to keep so much bottled up inside, it has to come out somewhere. One of the most common addictive behaviors for Nice Guys is sexual compulsiveness.

好男人很容易上瘾。上瘾行为的目的是为了缓解压力、改变情绪或缓解疼痛。好男人往往会把很多情绪压抑在心里，所以这些情绪必须通过某种方式释放出来。好男人最常见的上瘾行为之一就是性强迫症。

Nice Guys have difficulty setting boundaries . Many Nice Guys have a hard time saying "no," "stop," or "I'm going to." They often feel like helpless victims and see the other person as the cause of the problems they are experiencing.

好男人很难设定界限。很多好男人很难说“不”、“停下来”或“我要停下来”。他们常常觉得自己像个无助的受害者，把别人看作自己所遇到问题的根源。

Nice Guys are frequently isolated . Though Nice Guys desire to be liked and loved, their behaviors actually make it difficult for people to get very close to them.

好男人常常被孤立。虽然好男人渴望被喜欢和爱，但他们的行为实际上让人难以接近他们。

Nice Guys are often attracted to people and situations that need fixing. This behavior is often the result of the Nice Guy's childhood conditioning, his need to look good, or his quest for approval. Unfortunately, this tendency pretty much guarantees that Nice Guys will spend most of their time putting out fires and managing crises.

好男人常常会被那些需要解决的人和事所吸引。这种行为往往源于好男人童年时期的习性、对良好形象的渴望，或对认可的追求。不幸的是，这种倾向几乎注定了好男人会把大部分时间花在解决问题和处理危机上。

Nice Guys frequently have problems in intimate relationships. Though Nice Guys often put tremendous emphasis on this part of their lives, their intimate relationships are frequently a source of struggle and frustration. For example:

好男人在亲密关系中经常遇到问题。尽管他们非常重视亲密关系，但他们的亲密关系往往是他们挣扎和沮丧的根源。例如：

  • Nice Guys are often terrible listeners because they are too busy trying to figure out how to defend themselves or fix the other person's problem.
    好男人通常不善于倾听，因为他们忙于想办法为自己辩护或解决对方的问题。
  • Because of their fear of conflict, they are frequently dishonest and are rarely available to work all the way through a problem.
    由于害怕冲突，他们常常不诚实，也很少愿意全力以赴解决问题。
  • It is not unusual for Nice Guys to form relationships with partners whom they believe to be "projects" or "diamonds in the rough." When these projects don't polish up as expected, Nice Guys tend to blame their partner for standing in the way of their happiness.
    好男人与他们认为是“项目”或“未经雕琢的钻石”的伴侣建立关系并不罕见。当这些项目没有达到预期的效果时，好男人往往会责怪他们的伴侣阻碍了他们的幸福。

Nice Guys have issues with sexuality . Though most Nice Guys deny having problems with sex, I have yet to meet one who isn't either dissatisfied with his sex life, has a sexual dysfunction (can't get or maintain an erection, climaxes too quickly), or has sexually acted out (through affairs, prostitution, pornography, compulsive masturbation, etc.).

好男人也有性方面的问题。虽然大多数好男人否认自己有性方面的问题，但我还没见过哪个男人不对自己的性生活不满意，有性功能障碍（无法勃起或维持勃起，高潮过快），或者有过性行为（例如婚外情、卖淫、色情、强迫性手淫等等）。

Nice Guys are usually only relatively successful . The majority of Nice Guys I've met have been talented, intelligent, and moderately successful. Almost without exception though, they fail to live up to their full potential.

好男人通常只是相对成功。我遇到的大多数好男人都才华横溢、聪明睿智，并且取得了一定的成功。然而，几乎毫无例外，他们都没能充分发挥自己的潜力。


"But He Seemed Like Such A Nice Guy"

“但他看起来是个好人”

It is not unusual for unsuspecting people to mistake the passive, pleasing, and generous characteristics of a Nice Guy for those of a healthy male. Many women have told me that upon initially meeting these men, they believed the same to be true. Because he seemed different from other men they had been with, the Nice Guy seemed like a real catch.

毫无戒心的人常常会把“好男人”的被动、讨人喜欢和慷慨大方误认为是健康男性的特质。很多女性告诉我，她们初次见到这些男人时，也曾误以为是健康男性。因为他看起来与她们之前交往过的其他男人不同，所以“好男人”看起来确实很有吸引力。

Unfortunately, the negative traits listed above find a way to ooze out into Nice Guys' lives and personal relationships. As a result, these men tend to swing back and forth between being nice and not-so-nice. I have listened to countless wives, partners and girlfriends describe the Dr. Jekyll and Mr. Hyde qualities of Nice Guys:

不幸的是，上面列出的负面特质会渗透到好男人的生活和人际关系中。结果，这些男人往往在好与不好之间摇摆不定。我听过无数的妻子、伴侣和女友描述好男人身上那种如同化身博士般的特质：

"He can be really wonderful and he can also hurt me deeply. He'll do all the extra little things like picking up the kids and fixing dinner when I have to put in extra hours at work. But then out of the blue, he'll throw a tantrum about me never being sexually available to him."

他有时真的很棒，有时也会深深地伤害我。当我需要加班时，他会做各种额外的琐事，比如接孩子、做晚饭。但突然之间，他会因为我无法和他发生性关系而大发脾气。

"Everyone thinks he is such a great guy and I'm really lucky to have him. But they don't know what he can really be like. He's always helping people out with their car or something else that needs fixing.

“大家都觉得他很棒，能拥有他真的很幸运。但他们并不了解他真正的为人。他总是帮别人修车或其他需要修理的东西。”

When I ask him to do something he tells me that he can never make me happy and that I'm nagging and controlling like his mother."

当我要求他做某事时，他告诉我，他永远无法让我开心，而且我像他的母亲一样唠叨和控制欲强。”

"He is constantly trying to please me. He will do anything for me except really be there for me. He'll go shopping with me even though I know he doesn't want to. The whole time he will just sulk, which makes me miserable. I wish he would just tell me 'no' sometimes."

他总是想取悦我。他什么都愿意为我做，就是不肯真正陪在我身边。他会陪我去购物，尽管我知道他不想。他总是生闷气，让我很难受。真希望他有时候能直接对我说‘不’。”

"He will never tell me when something is bothering him. He'll just keep it in and it will build like a pressure cooker. I won't have a clue that anything is bothering him. And then out of the blue, he'll explode and we'll end up in a big fight. If he would just tell me when he is upset about something, it would make it a lot easier."

“他从来不告诉我什么时候心烦。他总是把心事憋在心里，像高压锅一样越压越紧。我根本不知道他有什么心事。然后他就会突然爆发，最后我们就会大吵一架。如果他能告诉我什么时候心烦，事情就会好办多了。”

"When I try to talk to him about something that is bothering me, he tries to fix it. He thinks that if I just did everything his way, it would solve all my problems. He always tells me I dwell on the negative and that he can never make me happy. All I really want is for him to listen to me."

“每当我试着跟他谈论困扰我的事情时，他总是试图帮我解决。他觉得只要我按照他的方式去做，就能解决我所有的问题。他总是说我老是想着消极的事情，他永远无法让我快乐。我真正想要的只是他能听我说完。”

"After all the other crummy men I've been with, I thought I had finally found a nice guy that I could trust. Five years into our marriage I found out that he was addicted to pornography and peep shows. I was devastated. I never even had a clue."

“我和那么多烂男人交往过，总以为自己终于找到了一个可以信赖的好男人。结婚五年后，我发现他沉迷于色情和窥视秀。我崩溃了，我以前完全不知道。”

"I wish I could wave a magic wand, keep all of his good traits, and make all the others disappear."

“我希望我能挥动魔杖，保留他所有的优点，并让其他优点消失。”


The Integrated Male

综合男性

After enrolling in a No More Mr. Nice Guy! therapy group, Gil, a pleasant man in his early fifties revealed that his wife was supportive of his joining a group. Nevertheless, he harbored a secret fear that she would be angry at what the name of the group seemed to imply — "How to stop being a Nice Guy and become an S.O.B." Using typical Nice Guy logic, Gil questioned why any woman would be supportive of men becoming "not nice."

吉尔，一位五十多岁、和蔼可亲的男士，在加入了“不再是好人！”治疗小组后，透露他的妻子支持他加入小组。然而，他内心深处却担心妻子会对小组名称的含义感到愤怒——“如何不再做好人，变成混蛋。”吉尔用典型的好人逻辑，质疑为什么会有女人支持男人变得“不好”。

Because Nice Guys tend to be very black and white in their thinking, the only alternative they can see to being nice is becoming "bastards" or "jerks." I frequently remind Nice Guys that the opposite of crazy is still crazy , so becoming a "jerk" isn't the answer.

因为好男人的思维方式往往非常黑白分明，所以他们认为除了做好人之外，唯一的选择就是变成“混蛋”或“混蛋”。我经常提醒好男人，疯狂的反义词仍然是疯狂，所以变成“混蛋”并不是解决问题的办法。

Recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome isn't about going from one extreme to another. The process of breaking free from ineffective Nice Guy patterns doesn't involve becoming "not nice." Rather, it means becoming "integrated."

摆脱“好人综合症”并不意味着从一个极端走向另一个极端。摆脱无效的好人模式的过程并不意味着变得“不友善”，而是意味着变得“融入”。


Being integrated means being able to accept all aspects of one's self.

整合意味着能够接受自我的各个方面。

An integrated man is able to embrace everything that makes him uniquely male: his power, his assertiveness, his courage, and his passion as well as his imperfections, his mistakes, and his dark side.

一个完整的男人能够接受一切使他成为独特男性的东西：他的力量、他的自信、他的勇气、他的激情，以及他的不完美、他的错误、他的阴暗面。

An integrated male possesses many of the following attributes:

一个完整的男性拥有以下许多特质：

  • He has a strong sense of self. He likes himself just as he is.
    他有很强的自我意识。他喜欢真实的自己。
  • He takes responsibility for getting his own needs met.
    他负责满足自己的需求。
  • He is comfortable with his masculinity and his sexuality.
    他对自己的男子气概和性取向感到自在。
  • He has integrity. He does what is right, not what is expedient.
    他为人正直。他做正确的事，而不是权宜之计。
  • He is a leader. He is willing to provide for and protect those he cares about.
    他是一位领导者。他愿意为他关心的人提供和保护。
  • He is clear, direct, and expressive of his feelings.
    他清晰、直接，并且善于表达自己的感受。
  • He can be nurturing and giving without caretaking or problem-solving.
    他可以滋养和付出，但不必照顾或解决问题。
  • He knows how to set boundaries and is not afraid to work through conflict.
    他知道如何设定界限，并且不惧怕解决冲突。

An integrated male doesn't strive to be perfect or gain the approval of others. Instead he accepts himself just as he is, warts and all. An integrated male accepts that he is perfectly imperfect.

一个完整的男人不会追求完美，也不会追求他人的认可。相反，他会接受自己的本来面目，包括所有缺点。一个完整的男人会接受自己完美地不完美。

Making the transformation from a Nice Guy to an integrated male doesn't come about by just trying harder to be a good man. Breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome demands embracing a totally different way of viewing oneself and the world, a complete change in one's personal paradigm. Let me explain.

从一个“好男人”蜕变成一个完整的男人，并非仅仅靠努力做一个好男人就能实现。摆脱“好男人综合症”需要你用一种完全不同的方式看待自己和世界，彻底改变你的个人范式。让我来解释一下。


Paradigms

范式

A paradigm is the road map we use to navigate life's journey. Everyone uses these road maps and everyone assumes the map they are using is up-to-date and accurate.

范式是我们指引人生旅程的路线图。每个人都使用这些路线图，并且每个人都假设自己使用的路线图是最新且准确的。

Paradigms often operate at an unconscious level, yet they determine to a large degree our attitudes and behaviors. They serve as a filter through which we process life experiences. Data that does not fit our paradigm is screened out, never reaching our conscious mind. Information that does fit our paradigm is magnified by the process, and adds even greater support for that particular way of believing.

范式通常在潜意识层面运作，但它们在很大程度上决定了我们的态度和行为。它们就像一个过滤器，我们通过它来处理生活经验。不符合我们范式的数据会被筛选掉，永远不会进入我们的意识。符合我们范式的信息则会被放大，并为特定的信念方式提供更强有力的支持。

Paradigms, like road maps, can be great tools for speeding us along on our journey. Unfortunately, if they are outdated or inaccurate, they can send us in the wrong direction or fruitlessly driving around the same old neighborhood. When this happens we often keep trying harder to find our desired destination while feeling more and more frustrated. Even though an individual following an inaccurate or outdated paradigm may think his behavior makes perfect sense, those around him may wonder what he could possibly be thinking to make him act the way he does.

范式如同路线图，可以成为加速我们旅程的绝佳工具。然而，如果范式过时或不准确，它们可能会将我们引向错误的方向，或让我们在同一个街区里徒劳地兜风。当这种情况发生时，我们常常会更加努力地寻找理想的目的地，却越来越感到沮丧。即使遵循不准确或过时范式的人可能认为自己的行为完全合理，但他周围的人可能会怀疑他究竟在想什么，才会做出那样的行为。

Most paradigms are developed when we are young, naïve, and relatively powerless. They are often based on the inaccurate interpretations of childhood experiences. Since they are often unconscious, they are rarely evaluated or updated. Perhaps most significantly, they are assumed to be 100 percent accurate — even when they are not.

大多数范式都是在我们年幼、幼稚且相对无力的时候形成的。它们往往基于对童年经历的不准确解读。由于它们往往是无意识的，因此很少被评估或更新。或许最重要的是，它们被认为是100%准确的——即使事实并非如此。


The Ineffective Nice Guy Paradigm

无效的好人范式

The working paradigm of the Nice Guy is this:

好人的工作范式是这样的：

  • IF I can hide my flaws and become what I think others want me to be
    如果我能隐藏自己的缺点，成为别人希望我成为的人
  • THEN I will be loved, get my needs met, and have a problem-free life.
    那么我就会被爱，我的需求会得到满足，并且过上无忧无虑的生活。

Even when this paradigm is ineffective, Nice Guys only see one alternative: try harder.

即使这种模式无效，好人也只看到一种选择：更加努力。

Nice Guys are notoriously slow learners and amazingly quick forgetters when their paradigms are challenged. Their inclination is to hang on to belief systems that have proven to be consistently unworkable, yet are so embedded in their unconscious mind that to challenge them is tantamount to heresy. It is difficult for Nice Guys to consider doing something different, even when what they are doing isn't working.

好人是出了名的学习迟缓，当他们的思维模式受到挑战时，他们的遗忘速度却快得惊人。他们倾向于坚持那些已被证明始终行不通的信念体系，而这些信念体系在他们的潜意识中根深蒂固，以至于挑战它们无异于异端邪说。好人很难考虑尝试不同的做法，即使他们正在做的事情行不通。

Jason, whose sexual difficulties with his wife, Heather, were introduced at the beginning of the chapter, is a good example of the frustration that can result from an ineffective Nice Guy paradigm. Jason had a controlling, perfectionist father who put unrealistic demands on Jason and his siblings. His father believed there was one right way to do everything — his way. Jason's mother was an emotionally dependent woman who lived through her children. When his mother was needy, she would smother her kids. When the children had needs, she was often too emotionally distressed to respond.

本章开头介绍了杰森与妻子希瑟在性方面遇到的困难，这很好地说明了无效的“好男人”模式可能带来的挫败感。杰森的父亲控制欲极强，追求完美，他对杰森和他的兄弟姐妹们提出了不切实际的要求。他的父亲认为，做任何事都只有一个正确的方法——按照他自己的方式。杰森的母亲是一个情感依赖型的女人，她依靠孩子生活。当母亲需要帮助时，她会扼杀孩子。当孩子们有需要时，她常常会因为情绪过于低落而无法回应。

Jason learned to cope with his childhood experience by developing a paradigm that included:

杰森学会了通过发展以下范式来应对他的童年经历：

  • Believing that if he could figure out how to do everything right, he could garner his father's approval and avoid his criticism.
    相信如果他能想出如何正确地做每件事，他就能获得父亲的认可并避免批评。
  • Believing that if he responded to his mother's neediness by being attentive and nurturing, she would be available to him when he had needs.
    相信如果他以细心和养育来回应母亲的需要，那么当他有需要时，她就会随时待命。
  • Believing that if he was never a moment's problem, he would get love and approval.
    相信如果他从不成为一时的问题，他就会得到爱和认可。
  • Believing that if he hid his mistakes, no one would ever get mad at him.
    相信如果他隐藏自己的错误，就没有人会生他的气。

As a child, Jason was too naive and powerless to realize that no matter what he did, he would never live up his father's expectations. Similarly, no matter how giving he was, his needy mother would never be available to nurture him. He could not see that there really was no way to do everything right. And regardless of how well he believed he hid his flaws or mistakes, people might still get angry at him.

小时候，杰森太过天真无力，没有意识到无论他做什么，都永远无法达到父亲的期望。同样，无论他多么慷慨，他那需要照顾的母亲也永远无法给予他养育。他无法理解，世上不可能事事都完美无缺。而且，无论他多么自以为掩饰了自己的缺点或错误，人们仍然可能会对他生气。

Even when his childhood road map failed to take him in the desired direction, the only option he could see was to just keep trying harder doing more of the same. The only thing his paradigm ever really did was to create a distraction from his feelings of fear, worthlessness, and inadequacy.

即使童年的人生规划未能引领他走向理想的方向，他唯一能做的也只是继续努力，重复同样的事情。他的人生范式唯一能做的，就是转移他的注意力，让他忘记恐惧、无价值和不足。

In adulthood, Jason tried to apply his childhood paradigm to his relationship with his wife. Like his mother, his wife was only attentive when she was emotionally needy. Like his father, she could be critical and controlling. By applying his childhood road map to his marriage — trying to do everything right, being attentive and nurturing, never being a moment's problem, hiding his mistakes — Jason created an illusion that he could get his wife to approve of him all the time, be sexually available whenever he wanted, and never get mad at him. His defective paradigm prevented him from seeing that no matter what he did, his wife would still at times be cold, critical, and unavailable, and that maybe he needed her to be that way. Even when his paradigm was just as ineffective in adulthood as it was in childhood, Jason's only option seemed to be to just keep trying harder.

成年后，杰森试图将童年的模式应用于与妻子的关系。和母亲一样，妻子只有在情感上需要帮助时才会体贴。和父亲一样，她也可能爱批评、控制欲强。杰森将童年的“路线图”应用于婚姻——力求完美，体贴入微，悉心照料，从不惹是生非，隐瞒自己的错误——从而制造了一种幻觉：他可以让妻子始终认可他，随时满足他的性需求，并且永远不会生气。他有缺陷的模式让他无法意识到，无论他做什么，妻子有时仍然会冷漠、挑剔、不愿付出，而他或许也需要她这样。即使成年后的模式和童年一样无效，杰森唯一的选择似乎就是继续努力。


Doing Something Different

做一些不同的事情

One of my all time favorite Seinfeld episodes is the one where George decided to change his life by acting the opposite of how he would have typically behaved. Ironically, by doing everything the opposite, he gets a beautiful girlfriend and a job with the Yankees. While doing everything the opposite may not be the answer for breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome, doing some things different is.

我最喜欢的《宋飞传》剧集之一是乔治决定改变人生的那一集，他做了与平常完全相反的事。讽刺的是，他做了所有相反的事，却得到了一个漂亮的女朋友和一份洋基队的工作。虽然做所有相反的事或许无法摆脱“好人综合症”，但做一些与众不同的事情却可以。

Over the last several years, I have watched countless men "do something different" by applying the principles contained in this book. These men have transformed themselves from resentful, frustrated, helpless Nice Guys into assertive, empowered, and happy individuals.

过去几年里，我见证了无数男人通过运用本书的原则“成就非凡”。他们从怨恨、沮丧、无助的“好男人”蜕变成自信、充满力量、充满快乐的人。

Just like George on the Seinfeld show, when Nice Guys decide to make a change, interesting things begin to happen. Among other things, I've watched these men:

就像《宋飞传》里的乔治一样，当“好男人”决定做出改变时，有趣的事情就开始发生了。我观察过以下这些男人：

  • Accept themselves just as they are.
    接受真实的自己。
  • Use their mistakes as valuable learning tools.
    将他们的错误视为宝贵的学习工具。
  • Stop seeking the approval of others.
    停止寻求他人的认可。
  • Experience loving and intimate relationships.
    体验充满爱和亲密的关系。
  • Make their needs a priority.
    将他们的需求放在首位。
  • Find people who are able and willing to help them meet their needs.
    寻找能够并且愿意帮助他们满足需求的人。
  • Learn to give judiciously, with no strings attached.
    学会明智地付出，不附加任何条件。
  • Face their fears.
    直面他们的恐惧。
  • Develop integrity and honesty.
    培养正直和诚实。
  • Set boundaries.
    设定界限。
  • Build meaningful relationships with men.
    与男性建立有意义的关系。
  • Create healthier, more satisfying relationships with women.
    与女性建立更健康、更令人满意的关系。
  • Experience and express their feelings.
    体验并表达他们的感受。
  • Deal with problems directly.
    直接处理问题。
  • Develop an intimate and satisfying sexual relationship.
    发展亲密而令人满意的性关系。
  • Find peace with the changing complexities of life.
    在不断变化的复杂生活中找到平静。



Asking For Help

寻求帮助

Nice Guys believe they should be able do everything on their own. They have a difficult time asking for help and try to hide any signs of imperfection or weakness. Breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome involves reversing this pattern.

好男人认为他们应该能够独立完成所有事情。他们很难寻求帮助，并且会试图隐藏任何不完美或软弱的迹象。要摆脱“好男人综合症”，就需要扭转这种模式。

Recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome is dependent on revealing one's self and receiving support from safe people. It is essential, therefore, that men who want to break free from the Nice Guy Syndrome find safe people to assist them in this process.

摆脱“好人综合症”的关键在于展现真我，并获得可靠人士的支持。因此，想要摆脱“好人综合症”的男性，找到可靠的人来帮助他们至关重要。

I encourage recovering Nice Guys to begin this process with a therapist, therapy group, 12-step group, a religious leader, or close friend. Since Nice Guys tend to seek out the approval of women, I strongly encourage them to begin this process with men. For some Nice Guys, the concept of "safe men" may seem like an oxymoron, but I highly recommend it anyway.

我鼓励正在康复中的“好男人”从治疗师、治疗小组、12步小组、宗教领袖或亲密朋友开始这个过程。由于“好男人”倾向于寻求女性的认可，我强烈建议他们从男性开始这个过程。对一些“好男人”来说，“安全男人”的概念可能听起来有些矛盾，但我还是强烈推荐它。

I have been leading men's therapy groups for recovering Nice Guys for several years. Some of the most significant aspects of my own recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome (even before I knew what it was) occurred in the context of 12-step groups and therapy groups. Even though I am sure it is possible to break free from the Nice Guy Syndrome without the help of a group, it is the most effective tool I know for facilitating the recovery process.

多年来，我一直领导男性“好男人”康复治疗小组。我自己从“好男人综合症”中康复的过程中，一些最重要的方面（甚至在我了解它之前）都发生在12步小组和治疗小组的背景下。虽然我确信即使没有小组的帮助，也能摆脱“好男人综合症”，但据我所知，这仍然是促进康复过程最有效的工具。


Breaking Free Activities

打破自由活动

If you recognize yourself or someone you love in what you have read so far, read on. This book presents a practical and effective guide for breaking free from the negative effects of the Nice Guy Syndrome.

如果你在读到的内容中找到了自己或爱人的影子，那就继续阅读吧。本书提供了实用有效的指南，帮助你摆脱“好人综合症”的负面影响。

This program has worked for countless men and it can work for you or a loved one.

该计划已为无数男性带来了成功，并且它也可能对您或您所爱的人有所帮助。

To help facilitate this process, I present numerous Breaking Free activities throughout the book.

为了帮助促进这一过程，我在整本书中介绍了许多“挣脱束缚”活动。

These Breaking Free activities serve to facilitate the paradigm shift that is necessary for recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome. They will not only help recovering Nice Guys understand where their paradigms came from, but will help replace them with more accurate and up-to-date ones. These assignments will also point recovering Nice Guys in a direction that will help them start doing things differently.

这些“挣脱束缚”活动有助于促进“好人综合症”康复过程中所必需的思维模式转变。它们不仅能帮助正在康复中的“好人”理解他们的思维模式从何而来，还能帮助他们用更准确、更贴合时代的思维模式取而代之。这些作业还能为正在康复中的“好人”指明方向，帮助他们开始以不同的方式做事。


*****************************

Breaking Free: Activity #1

挣脱束缚：活动#1

Write down three possible safe people or groups that might be able to provide support for you in your recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome.

写下三个可能安全的人或团体，他们可能能够在您从好人综合症中恢复的过程中为您提供支持。

If no one comes to mind, get out the telephone directory and look up counselors or support groups in the phone book. Write down three names and phone numbers and call them when you finish this chapter. If you are employed by a company with an Employee Assistance Program, this is another resource. If you know someone who has been to therapy or a support group, ask them for information. If you have access to the internet you can search for 12-step groups or support groups.

如果没有人选，可以翻开电话簿，查找心理咨询师或支持小组。写下三个人的姓名和电话号码，读完本章后给他们打电话。如果你所在的公司有员工援助计划，这也是一个资源。如果你认识有人接受过心理治疗或参加过支持小组，可以向他们询问信息。如果你能上网，可以搜索12步小组或支持小组的信息。

*****************************


Caution

警告

Before you decide to apply the principles presented in this book, I must first warn you about two things.

在您决定应用本书中提出的原则之前，我必须首先警告您两件事。

The first is that the program of recovery presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! is not just a few good ideas to try on for size. It represents a challenge to everything Nice Guys believe about what they must do to be loved, get their needs met, and keep their world calm.

首先，《不再是好人！》一书中提出的康复计划并非只是一些可以尝试的好主意。它挑战了“好人”们关于如何才能被爱、如何满足需求、如何保持内心平静的固有观念。

Breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome involves a radical change in perspective and behavior. Trying to do it halfway will only result in needless suffering.

要摆脱“好人综合症”，需要彻底改变自己的观点和行为。半途而废只会带来不必要的痛苦。

Second, breaking free from the Nice Guy syndrome will significantly effect your personal relationships.

其次，摆脱“好人综合症”将极大地影响你的人际关系。

If you are currently in a relationship, I encourage you to ask your partner to read this book along with you. The program of recovery presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! will significantly affect not only you, but also those closest to you. Though your partner may be supportive of you making positive changes, they may also initially frighten him or her. Reading this book together can help facilitate this transition.

如果你目前正在恋爱中，我建议你邀请你的伴侣一起读这本书。《不再是好人！》这本书中提出的康复方案不仅会对你，也会对你最亲近的人产生重大影响。虽然你的伴侣可能会支持你做出积极的改变，但最初这些改变也可能会让他/她感到害怕。一起阅读这本书可以帮助你更好地过渡。

With these warnings aside, if what you have read so far makes sense, keep reading. The following chapters contain information that can help you break free from the Nice Guy Syndrome and start getting what you want in love and life.

抛开这些警告，如果你觉得目前为止读到的内容有意义，那就继续阅读吧。接下来的章节包含的信息可以帮助你摆脱“好人综合症”，开始在爱情和生活中获得你想要的一切。


*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #2

挣脱束缚活动 #2

Why would it seem rational for a person to try to eliminate or hide certain things about himself and try to become something different unless there was a significant compelling reason for him to do so? Why do people try to change who they really are?

除非有令人信服的重大理由，否则一个人试图消除或隐藏自身的某些特质，并试图变成另一个人，这怎么会显得理性呢？为什么人们会试图改变真实的自我？

*****************************

# ↩

Chapter 2

# ↩

第二章

The Making Of A Nice Guy

《好男人的诞生》


I concluded the previous chapter with the question, "Why would it seem rational for a person to try to eliminate or hide certain things about himself and try to become something different unless there was a significant compelling reason for him to do so? Why do people try to change who they really are?"

我在上一章的结尾提出了一个问题：“除非有令人信服的重大理由，否则一个人试图消除或隐藏自己的某些特质，并试图变成另一个人，这怎么会显得合理呢？为什么人们要试图改变真实的自己？”

After spending years examining the Nice Guy Syndrome from every possible angle, there is only one answer to this question that makes sense: Because it does not feel safe or acceptable for a boy or man to be just who he is. Becoming a Nice Guy is a way of coping with situations where it does not feel safe or acceptable for a boy or man to be just who he is. Further, the only thing that would make a child or an adult sacrifice one's self by trying to become something different is a belief that being just who he is must be a bad and/or dangerous thing.

多年来，我从各个角度研究了“好人综合症”，最终只有一个合理的答案：因为男孩或男人做自己会感到不安全或不被接受。成为“好人”是应对男孩或男人做自己不安全或不被接受的情况的一种方式。此外，唯一能让孩子或成年人牺牲自我，努力成为另一个人的原因，是他们相信做自己一定是坏事或危险的。

The premise of this book is that during their formative years, all Nice Guys received messages from their families and the world around them that it was not safe, acceptable, or desirable for them to be who they were, just as they were.

本书的前提是，所有“好男人”在成长过程中，都会从家人和周围的世界收到这样的信息：以他们现在的样子，对他们来说，是不安全的、不可接受的，也是不理想的。

So how did Nice Guys receive these messages and why did they respond to them in the way that they did? The following is a short course on how families and society turn perfect little boys into men who believe they have to be "good" in order to be loved.

那么，好男人是如何接收这些信息的？他们又为何会做出那样的回应？以下是一个简短的课程，讲述家庭和社会如何将完美的小男孩变成相信自己必须“乖”才能被爱的男人。


Coping With Abandonment

应对被遗弃

The most impressionable time in an individual's life is from birth to about five years. In these first few years a child's personality is most significantly influenced by his surroundings. It is during this time that his paradigms begin to be established. Since the strongest influences during this time are usually a child's parents and extended family, this is where we must begin our examination of the origins of the Nice Guy Syndrome.

一个人一生中最易受影响的时期是从出生到大约五岁。在这最初的几年里，孩子的性格受周围环境的影响最为显著。正是在这段时间里，他的范式开始形成。由于这段时间对孩子影响最大的通常是父母和大家庭，因此我们必须从这段时间开始探究“好人综合症”的起源。

There are two important facts we must understand about children. First, when children come into the world they are totally helpless . They are dependent on others to recognize and respond to their needs in a timely, judicious manner. As a result of this dependency, every child's greatest fear is abandonment. To children, abandonment means death.

关于孩子，我们必须了解两个重要的事实。首先，孩子刚来到这个世界时，是完全无助的。他们依赖他人及时、明智地识别并回应他们的需求。由于这种依赖性，每个孩子最大的恐惧就是被遗弃。对孩子来说，被遗弃意味着死亡。

Second, children are ego-centered . This means that they inherently believe they are the center of the universe and everything revolves around them. Therefore, they believe that they are the cause of everything that happens to them.

其次，孩子以自我为中心。这意味着他们天生就认为自己是宇宙的中心，一切都围绕着他们转。因此，他们认为发生在自己身上的一切事情都是由他们造成的。

These two factors — their fear of abandonment and their ego-centeredness — create a very powerful dynamic for all children. Whenever a child experiences any kind of abandonment he will always believe that he is the cause of what has happened to him. These abandonment experiences might include any of the following experiences:

这两个因素——对被遗弃的恐惧和自我中心主义——为所有孩子创造了一种非常强大的动力。每当孩子经历任何形式的遗弃，他都会始终认为这一切都是他造成的。这些遗弃经历可能包括以下任何一种：

  • He is hungry and no one feeds him.
    他饿了，没有人喂他。
  • He cries and no one holds him.
    他哭了，没有人抱他。
  • He is lonely and no one pays attention to him.
    他很孤独，没有人关注他。
  • A parent gets angry at him.
    父母对他生气。
  • A parent neglects him.
    父母忽视他。
  • A parent puts unrealistic expectations on him.
    父母对他抱有不切实际的期望。
  • A parent uses him to gratify his or her own needs.
    父母利用他来满足自己的需求。
  • A parent shames him.
    父母羞辱他。
  • A parent hits him.
    父母打他。
  • A parent doesn't want him.
    父母不想要他。
  • A parent leaves him and doesn't come back in a timely manner.
    父母离开他，没有及时回来。

Because every child is born into an imperfect world and into an imperfect family, every child has abandonment experiences. Even though their belief that they are the cause of these painful events is, in fact, an inaccurate interpretation of their life, children have no other way to understand the world.

因为每个孩子都出生在一个不完美的世界和一个不完美的家庭，所以每个孩子都有被遗弃的经历。即使他们认为这些痛苦事件是自己造成的，这实际上是对自身人生的不准确解读，但孩子们除了被遗弃之外，没有其他理解世界的方式。


Toxic Shame

有毒的羞耻感

These abandonment experiences and the naive, ego-centered interpretation of them, creates a belief in some young children that it is not acceptable for them to be who they are, just as they are. They conclude that there must be something wrong with them, which causes the important people in their lives to abandon them . They have no way of comprehending that their abandonment experiences are not caused by something about them, but by the people who are supposed to recognize and meet their needs.

这些被遗弃的经历，以及对此幼稚、以自我为中心的解读，让一些年幼的孩子相信，他们本来的样子是不可接受的。他们认定自己一定是出了什么问题，才导致生命中重要的人抛弃他们。他们无法理解，他们被遗弃的经历并非源于自身，而源于那些本应认可并满足他们需求的人。

This naive, ego-centered interpretation of their abandonment experiences creates a psychological state called toxic shame . Toxic shame is the belief that one is inherently bad, defective, different, or unlovable. Toxic shame is not just a belief that one does bad things, it is a deeply held core belief that one is bad .

这种对被遗弃经历幼稚、以自我为中心的解读，会形成一种名为“毒性羞耻”的心理状态。“毒性羞耻”指的是认为自己天生坏、有缺陷、与众不同或不值得被爱。“毒性羞耻”不仅仅是认为自己做了坏事，而是一种根深蒂固的核心信念，认为自己是坏人。


Survival Mechanisms

生存机制

As a result of these abandonment experiences and the faulty interpretation of these events, all children develop survival mechanisms to help them do three very important things:

由于这些被遗弃的经历以及对这些事件的错误解读，所有孩子都会发展出生存机制来帮助他们做三件非常重要的事情：

1) Try to cope with the emotional and physical distress of being abandoned.

1）努力应对被抛弃所带来的情感和身体上的痛苦。

2) Try to prevent similar events from happening again.

2）尽力防止类似事件再次发生。

3) Try to hide their internalized toxic shame (or perceived badness) from themselves and others.

3） 试图向自己和他人隐藏内心的有害羞耻感（或感知到的坏事）。

Children find a multitude of creative ways to try to accomplish these three goals. Since their insight, experience, and resources are limited, these survival mechanisms are often ineffective and sometimes, seemingly illogical. For instance, a child who is feeling lonely may misbehave in a way that is sure to attract his parent's attention in a negative way. Even though it may seem illogical for a child to do something that invites painful or negative attention, the consequences of the behavior may not feel as bad as feeling lonely or isolated.

孩子们会想出各种各样富有创意的方法来实现这三个目标。由于他们的洞察力、经验和资源有限，这些生存机制往往无效，有时甚至显得不合逻辑。例如，一个感到孤独的孩子可能会做出一些不当行为，而这些行为必然会引起父母的负面关注。即使孩子做出一些引起痛苦或负面关注的行为看起来不合逻辑，但这种行为的后果可能不如感到孤独或孤立那么糟糕。

Trying to be "good" — trying to become what he believes others want him to be — is just one of many possible scripts that a little boy might form as the result of childhood abandonment experiences and the internalization of toxic shame.

努力变得“好”——努力成为他认为别人希望他成为的人——只是一个小男孩由于童年被遗弃的经历和内化有害的羞耻感而可能形成的众多剧本之一。


The Origin Of The Nice Guy Paradigm

好人范式的起源

When I first began exploring my own Nice Guy attitudes and behaviors I had no idea how all the pieces fit together. I believed that I came from a pretty good family and had lived a pretty good life. When I began observing other men with traits similar to my own, I encountered the same general lack of insight into the origins of their own emotional and behavioral patterns.

最初我开始探索自己“好男人”的心态和行为时，我完全不知道这些零散的片段是如何串联起来的。我自认为出身于一个相当不错的家庭，过着相当优渥的生活。当我开始观察其他与我性格相似的男人时，我发现他们普遍缺乏洞察力，无法理解自身情绪和行为模式的根源。

When questioned about their childhood, Nice Guys frequently tell me they grew up in "perfect," "great,"

当被问及他们的童年时，好男人经常告诉我，他们是在“完美”、“伟大”的环境中长大的，

"Leave It To Beaver," or "All-American" families. Nevertheless, these men learned to hide their flaws and tried to become what they believed others wanted them to be. These factors indicate that at some point in their early lives, their circumstances were less than ideal.

“天才小麻烦”（Leave It To Beaver）或“全美式”家庭。然而，这些男人学会了隐藏自己的缺点，并努力成为他们认为别人希望他们成为的样子。这些因素表明，在他们早年生活的某个阶段，他们的境遇并不理想。

Alan, Jason, and Jose are all Nice Guys. Each of these men had different childhood experiences. They are all unique in the way that their Nice Guy scripts are played out in their adult lives. In spite of these differences, they all developed a core belief in childhood that they were not OK just as they were. As a result of their internalized toxic shame, each developed a life paradigm that involved seeking approval and hiding perceived flaws. All of these men believed that these life strategies were necessary if they were to have any hope of being loved, getting their needs met, and having a problem-free life.

艾伦、杰森和何塞都是“好人”。他们每个人的童年经历各不相同，成年后也各自以独特的方式演绎着“好人”的剧本。尽管存在这些差异，但他们在童年时期都形成了一种核心信念：他们并非本来就好。由于内化的“毒性羞耻感”，他们各自形成了寻求认可和隐藏自身缺陷的生活模式。所有这些人都认为，如果他们希望被爱、需求得到满足，并拥有无忧无虑的生活，这些生活策略是必不可少的。

Alan

艾伦

The oldest of three children in a single parent family, Alan prided himself on having never caused his mother a moment's pain. As a child, he performed well in sports and school. He believed that these things set him apart from his siblings and made his mother proud. Alan was the first person in his family to get a college degree, another factor he believed made him special.

艾伦是单亲家庭三个孩子中的老大，他为自己从未给母亲带来过哪怕一丝痛苦而感到自豪。小时候，他在体育和学习方面都表现出色。他相信这些特质使他与兄弟姐妹区别开来，也让母亲感到骄傲。艾伦是家里第一个获得大学学位的人，他认为这也是他与众不同的另一个原因。

Alan's father, an abusive alcoholic, abandoned the family when Alan was seven. At an early age, Alan made a decision to be 180 degrees different from his father. As a result, he prided himself on being patient, giving, and even-keeled. Alan worked hard to never be angry or demeaning like his father. He was an active leader in his youth group at church and never drank alcohol or did drugs as a teenager.

艾伦的父亲是个酗酒成性、虐待成性的人，在艾伦七岁时就抛弃了家庭。艾伦很小的时候就决定要与父亲彻底改变。因此，他以耐心、乐于助人、沉着冷静而自豪。艾伦努力避免像父亲那样易怒或贬低他人。他是教堂青年团契的活跃领袖，青少年时期从未饮酒或吸毒。

Alan's mother, a fundamentalist Christian, raised Alan in a sect that preached hell-fire and brimstone. He came to believe that he was a "sinner" for having normal thoughts, impulses, and behaviors. Though he always worked hard to be a good Christian, he lived with a constant fear that he might make a mistake and suffer everlasting punishment.

艾伦的母亲是一位原教旨主义基督徒，她将艾伦抚养在一个宣扬地狱之火和硫磺的教派中。艾伦逐渐相信，自己因为拥有正常的思想、冲动和行为而是一个“罪人”。尽管他一直努力成为一名虔诚的基督徒，但他始终生活在恐惧之中，生怕自己犯错，遭受永恒的惩罚。

Alan believed his mother was a saint. She would do anything for her children. She would listen and wasn't critical. Frequently, she and Alan would commiserate with each other about all the "bad" things his dad did.

艾伦认为母亲是圣人。她愿意为孩子做任何事。她会倾听，不会批评。她和艾伦经常互相同情他父亲做的所有“坏事”。

On more than one occasion, Alan's mother told him that she was trying to raise her sons to be different from their father. She wanted them to grow up to be giving, peaceful, and respectful of women. As an adult, Alan still stays in close touch with his mother and does whatever he can to help make her life easier.

艾伦的母亲不止一次地告诉他，她试图把儿子们培养成与父亲不同的人。她希望他们长大后成为一个乐于奉献、平和、尊重女性的人。成年后的艾伦仍然与母亲保持着密切的联系，并尽其所能地帮助她生活得更轻松。

Jason

杰森

Jason, introduced in Chapter One, believed he grew up in a "Leave It To Beaver" family. In reality, both of Jason's parents lived through their children. Though he saw his childhood as "ideal", in actuality, his parents used him and his siblings to meet their own needs.

第一章中出现的杰森，自认为在一个“天才小麻烦”式的家庭中长大。但实际上，杰森的父母都靠着孩子过活。虽然他认为自己拥有“理想”的童年，但实际上，他的父母利用他和他的兄弟姐妹来满足自己的需求。

Jason believed his parents were "perfect." He described them as being strict and overprotective. He acknowledged that he was sheltered and sexually naive and admitted that he might have been smothered by his parents.

杰森认为他的父母“完美”。他形容他们严厉且过度保护。他承认自己被父母过度呵护，性方面也比较幼稚，也承认自己可能被父母过度溺爱了。

Jason's father closely directed the family. Jason reported that his father still tried to control Jason's life.

杰森的父亲对整个家庭管控严格。杰森说，他的父亲仍然试图控制杰森的生活。

Jason shared a chiropractic practice with his father who ran the business and told Jason what house he should buy, what car to drive, and what church he should attend.

杰森和他的父亲共同经营一家脊椎按摩诊所，他的父亲告诉杰森应该买什么样的房子、开什么样的车、去哪个教堂。

Jason described his mother as a "wonderful, loving woman." He reported that she was always involved with the kids. With no friends of her own, she turned to her children for companionship and affirmation of her worth.

杰森形容他的母亲是一位“美好而慈爱的女人”。他表示，她总是和孩子们在一起。由于自己没有朋友，她只能向孩子们寻求陪伴和自我价值的肯定。

Jason couldn't remember his parents showing much affection to each other. He couldn't picture them having sex, and wondered how they made three kids. Even though they did lots of things with the children, he couldn't remember them ever going out or taking a vacation just by themselves.

杰森不记得父母之间有过多少爱意。他无法想象他们发生性关系，也不知道他们是怎么生出三个孩子的。虽然他们和孩子们一起做了很多事，但他不记得他们曾经独自出去玩过或者度假过。

As an adult, Jason tried to live up to the image of perfection portrayed by his parents. Everything he did was calculated to look good: he looked like a good husband, a good father, a good Christian, and a good professional. In spite of all his efforts, he always felt inadequate and defective compared to his parents.

成年后的杰森努力活出父母所描绘的完美形象。他所做的一切都是为了让自己看起来更好：他看起来像个好丈夫、好父亲、好基督徒、好专业人士。尽管他付出了所有努力，但与父母相比，他总是觉得自己不够好，有缺陷。

Jose

何塞

A successful business consultant, Jose was afraid of intimate relationships. Jose was highly educated and had a stressful, high-powered career. He was physically active and his idea of recreation was taking a hundred-mile bike ride or climbing a mountain. He repressed his anger and tried to never say anything that would upset anyone. He saw himself as controlling and acknowledged that his drug of choice was "recognition."

何塞是一位成功的商业顾问，他害怕亲密关系。何塞受过高等教育，工作压力很大，精力充沛。他热爱运动，他认为的休闲方式是骑行一百英里或爬山。他压抑着自己的愤怒，尽量不说任何会惹恼别人的话。他认为自己控制欲很强，并承认他最钟爱的“毒品”就是“认可”。

Jose was attracted to dependent women. He found it interesting that he seemed to be attracted to incest survivors. He stayed in his present relationship because he was concerned about the financial welfare of his girlfriend. He was afraid she wouldn't make it if he left.

何塞被依赖型女性所吸引。他发现，自己似乎也对乱伦幸存者感兴趣，这很有意思。他维持目前的感情，是因为他担心女友的经济状况。他担心自己离开后，她会撑不下去。

Jose openly acknowledged that he came from a dysfunctional family. He was the second of seven children in a lower class family. At around the age of 14, he took on the role of parenting his younger siblings. Jose reported that there was tremendous chaos in his family and he saw his job as protecting his brothers and sisters from its effects.

何塞公开承认自己来自一个不健全的家庭。他在一个下层阶级家庭中排行老二，家中七个孩子。大约14岁时，他承担起了抚养弟弟妹妹的责任。何塞说，他的家庭一片混乱，而他认为自己的工作就是保护兄弟姐妹免受影响。

Jose saw his father as angry, controlling, and abusive. He was explosive and demeaning to the boys and sexually abusive to the girls.

何塞觉得父亲脾气暴躁、控制欲强、虐待成性。他脾气暴躁，对男孩们很不尊重，对女孩们则进行性虐待。

Jose's mother was manic-depressive. She had extreme mood swings and had a difficult time staying on her medication. When she was manic, the house would be spotless, she would talk of entertaining politicians and socialites, and she would begin destructive sexual relationships. When she was depressed, she kept the windows covered, the house became a wreck, and she would threaten to kill herself. When he was 15, Jose had to break through a locked door and take a loaded gun away from his mother. She had been threatening suicide while all seven kids stood by terrified. Jose saw this as a typical scenario growing up in his home.

何塞的母亲患有躁狂抑郁症。她情绪波动剧烈，很难坚持服药。躁狂发作时，家里一尘不染，她会滔滔不绝地谈论着如何款待政客和社会名流，甚至会开始一些破坏性的性关系。抑郁发作时，她会用力遮住窗户，把房子弄得乱七八糟，还会威胁自杀。何塞15岁时，不得不破门而入，从母亲手中夺过一把上了膛的枪。母亲当时威胁要自杀，而七个孩子则惊恐地站在一旁。何塞认为，这是他成长过程中常见的场景。

Jose worked hard all of his life to be different from his family. His family had him on a pedestal and he was the one to whom everyone turned whenever they had a problem. His job as a family member was fixing chaos. His job as a business consultant was fixing chaos. His role in relationships was fixing chaos. Jose's life script required chaos, because without it, he would be out of a job.

何塞一生努力让自己与众不同。他的家人把他奉为偶像，每当家人遇到问题时，他都是大家的求助对象。作为家庭成员，他的工作是解决混乱。作为商业顾问，他的工作是解决混乱。他在人际关系中的角色也是解决混乱。何塞的人生剧本需要混乱，因为没有混乱，他就会失业。

Jose considered his natural intelligence, work ethic, and ability to solve problems his "saving grace." It was these factors, he believed, that allowed him to escape his family dysfunction and make something of himself. Without them, he was convinced, he would have ended up just like his parents and the rest of siblings.

何塞认为，他天生的聪明才智、敬业精神和解决问题的能力是他的“救命稻草”。他相信，正是这些因素让他摆脱了家庭的困境，成就了一番事业。如果没有这些因素，他相信自己最终也会像父母和其他兄弟姐妹一样。


Child Development 101

儿童发展101

Alan, Jason and Jose all had very different childhood experiences, yet all developed a similar script that guided their lives. Each, in various ways, internalized a belief that they were not OK just as they were and their survival depended on becoming something different. To help us connect the dots and see how three very different childhood experiences could create three men with very similar life paradigms, it might be helpful to do a quick review of the child development principles presented earlier in this chapter.

艾伦、杰森和何塞的童年经历截然不同，却都形成了相似的人生剧本。他们各自以不同的方式内化了一种信念：他们并非一帆风顺，他们的生存取决于成为某种不同的人。为了帮助我们将这些线索串联起来，并了解三个截然不同的童年经历如何造就了三个拥有相似人生范式的人物，快速回顾一下本章前面提出的儿童发展原则或许会有所帮助。

1) All children are born totally helpless.

1) 所有的孩子生来都是完全无助的。

2) A child's greatest fear is abandonment.

2）孩子最大的恐惧就是被抛弃。

3) All children are ego-centered.

3）所有孩子都是以自我为中心的。

4) All children have numerous abandonment experiences — their needs are not met in a timely, judicious manner.

4) 所有孩子都有过多次被遗弃的经历——他们的需求没有得到及时、明智的满足。

5) When a child has an abandonment experience, he always believes that he is the cause.

5）当孩子有被遗弃的经历时，他总是相信自己是造成这一现象的原因。

6) This naive misinterpretation creates toxic shame — a belief that he is "bad".

6）这种天真的误解会造成有害的羞耻感——一种“他是坏人”的信念。

7) Children develop survival mechanisms to try to cope with their abandonment experiences, try to prevent the experiences from happening again, and try to hide their "badness" from themselves and others.

7）孩子们会发展出生存机制来尝试应对被遗弃的经历，尝试防止这种经历再次发生，并尝试向自己和他人隐藏他们的“坏”。

8) These childhood survival mechanisms reflect the child's inherent powerlessness and naive view of himself and the world.

8）这些童年生存机制反映了孩子固有的无力感以及对自己和世界的天真看法。


From Perfect Little Boys To Nice Guys

从完美小男孩到好男人

The principles above can be applied to the childhood experiences of Alan, Jason, Jose, and every other Nice Guy described in this book. The progression from perfect little boy to Nice Guy basically occurs in three stages: Abandonment, internalization of toxic shame , and the creation of survival mechanisms .

上述原则适用于艾伦、杰森、何塞以及本书中描述的所有其他“好男人”的童年经历。从完美小男孩到“好男人”的成长过程大致分为三个阶段：被抛弃、内化有害羞耻感，以及建立生存机制。

Abandonment

放弃

Like all Nice Guys, Alan, Jason, and Jose were abandoned in various ways: Alan and Jose had an angry or critical parent who communicated that they were not OK just as they were.

像所有好男人一样，艾伦、杰森和何塞以各种方式被抛弃：艾伦和何塞的父母总是愤怒或挑剔，他们认为他们现在的样子并不好。

Alan worshipped his mother, but she would not intervene when his father lashed out at Alan. This implied that he wasn't worth protecting.

艾伦崇拜他的母亲，但当他的父亲对他大发雷霆时，母亲却无动于衷。这暗示着他不值得保护。

Alan came to believe that he had to be different from his father to be seen as a good man and be loved by his mother.

艾伦开始相信，他必须与父亲不同，才能被视为一个好人并受到母亲的爱戴。

Alan and Jason were used and objectified by their parents. They were valued for always doing it "right"

艾伦和杰森被父母利用和物化。他们因总是做“正确”的事情而受到重视。

and never being a problem. This communicated that they were only lovable when they lived up to their parent's expectations.

并且从不惹麻烦。这表明，只有当他们达到父母的期望时，他们才值得被爱。

Since Jason believed his parents were "perfect" he always felt flawed and inadequate compared to them.

由于杰森相信他的父母是“完美的”，所以与他们相比，他总是觉得自己有缺陷、不够好。

Neither of Jose's parents provided any guidance, nurturing, or support. This communicated that he was of little or no value to them.

何塞的父母没有给予他任何指导、培养或支持。这表明，何塞对他们来说毫无价值。

Alan and Jason grew up in fundamentalist churches that reinforced a need to be perfect and sinless.

艾伦和杰森在原教旨主义教会中长大，这些教会强调完美和无罪的需要。

Failure to do so meant everlasting punishment.

不这样做就意味着永远的惩罚。

Jose believed he was valuable only if he was different from his crazy family.

何塞相信，只有与疯狂的家人不同，他才有价值。

All three — Alan, Jason, and Jose — believed that someone else's needs were more important than their own — a common occurrence in Nice Guy families.

艾伦、杰森和何塞三人都认为别人的需求比自己的需求更重要——这在“好人”家庭中很常见。

All of these experiences represented a form of abandonment because they communicated to these little boys that they were not OK just as they were.

所有这些经历都代表着一种遗弃，因为它们告诉这些小男孩，他们现在的样子并不好。

Shame

耻辱

Regardless of whether they were abused, abandoned, neglected, shamed, used, smothered, controlled, or objectified, all Nice Guys internalized the same belief — it was a bad or dangerous thing for them to be just who they were.

无论他们是被虐待、抛弃、忽视、羞辱、利用、压制、控制还是物化，所有好男人都内心化了同样的信念——做真实的自己对他们来说是一件坏事或危险的事。

Some of these messages were communicated overtly by parents who had no concern for the child's welfare. Some were communicated indirectly by caring parents who themselves were too young, overwhelmed, or distracted to provide a nurturing environment for their child. At times, these messages were communicated by circumstances that were beyond anyone's control.

有些信息是父母直接传达的，他们根本不关心孩子的福祉。有些信息是充满爱心的父母间接传达的，他们自己太年轻、不堪重负或心不在焉，无法为孩子提供一个充满关爱的环境。有时，这些信息是通过任何人都无法控制的情况传达的。

In every situation, the child believed these events and circumstances were telling a story about him. He believed there was something about him that caused these things to happen. Using child-like logic he concluded, "There must be something wrong with me because ____________." Fill in the blank: When I cry, no one comes.

在任何情况下，孩子都相信这些事件和环境都在讲述他的故事。他相信自己身上有某种东西导致了这些事情的发生。他用孩子般的逻辑得出结论：“我肯定出了什么问题，因为____。” 填空：我哭的时候，没人来。

Mom gets that look on her face.

妈妈脸上露出了那种表情。

Dad left and didn't come back.

爸爸走了，再也没有回来。

Mom has to do everything for me.

妈妈必须为我做一切事情。

Dad yells at me.

爸爸对我大喊大叫。

I'm not perfect like Mom and Dad.

我并不像爸爸妈妈那样完美。

I can't make Mom happy.

我不能让妈妈开心。

These childhood experiences also caused the young boy to believe, "I'm only good enough and lovable when ____________." Fill in the blank:

这些童年经历也让这个小男孩相信：“只有当 ____ 时，我才足够优秀、值得被爱。”填空：

I'm different from Dad.

我和爸爸不一样。

Mom needs me.

妈妈需要我。

I don't make any mistakes.

我没有犯任何错误。

I make good grades.

我取得了好成绩。

I'm happy.

我很高兴。

I'm not like my brother.

我和我哥哥不一样。

I don't cause anyone any problems.

我没有给任何人带来麻烦。

I make Mom and Dad happy.

我让爸爸妈妈开心。


Survival Mechanisms

生存机制

As a result of their childhood abandonment experiences and the inaccurate interpretation of these events, all Nice Guys developed survival mechanisms to help them do three very important things: 1) Try to cope with the pain and terror caused by their abandonment experiences.

由于童年时期被遗弃的经历以及对这些事件的错误解读，所有好男人都发展出了生存机制来帮助他们做三件非常重要的事情：1）试图应对被遗弃经历给他们带来的痛苦和恐惧。

2) Try to prevent these abandonment experiences from occurring again.

2）尽量防止这些被抛弃的经历再次发生。

3) Try to hide their toxic shame from themselves and others.

3） 试图向自己和他人隐藏有害的羞耻感。

For Nice Guys, these survival mechanisms took the form of the following life paradigm:

对于好男人来说，这些生存机制体现为以下生活范式：

  • IF I can hide my flaws and become what I think others want me to be
    如果我能隐藏自己的缺点，成为别人希望我成为的人
  • THEN I will be loved, get my needs met, and have a problem-free life.
    那么我就会被爱，我的需求会得到满足，并且过上无忧无虑的生活。

It is this paradigm, formed in childhood, that guides and controls everything Nice Guys do in their adult lives. Even though it is based on faulty interpretations of childhood events, it is the only road map these men have. Nice Guys believe this map is accurate, and if they follow it correctly, they should arrive at their desired location — a smooth, happy life. Even though this life script is often highly ineffective, Nice Guys frequently just keep trying harder, doing more of the same, hoping for different results.

正是这种童年时期形成的范式，引导并控制着“好男人”成年后的一切行为。尽管它基于对童年经历的错误解读，但却是他们唯一的路线图。“好男人”相信这张地图是准确的，只要他们正确地遵循它，就应该能够到达他们渴望的目的地——顺利、幸福的生活。尽管这种人生脚本通常非常低效，但“好男人”常常会更加努力，重复同样的事情，希望得到不同的结果。


Two Kinds Of Nice Guys

两种好人

The survival mechanisms that Nice Guys develop to deal with their abandonment experiences and internalized toxic shame are usually manifested in one of two ways. In one form, a Nice Guy exaggerates his belief about his "not OK-ness" and believes he is the worst kind of person. I call this man the "I'm so bad" Nice Guy.

好男人为了应对被抛弃的经历和内化的“有毒羞耻感”，通常会发展出两种生存机制。一种是，好男人会夸大自己“不好”的信念，并认为自己是最糟糕的人。我把这种男人称为“我太坏了”的好男人。

The "I'm so bad" Nice Guy is convinced everyone can see how bad he is. He can give concrete examples of bad behavior in childhood, adolescence, and adulthood that support his core belief about himself. He can tell of breaking windows and getting whippings as a little boy. He will reveal running afoul of the law and making his mother cry when he was a teenager. He will tell tales of smoking, drinking, using drugs and carousing as an adult. He is convinced his only hope for having any kind of happiness in life lies in trying his best to mask his inherent badness. He never really believes anyone will buy into his Nice Guy persona, but doesn't think he has any other choice.

“我太坏了”的好男人坚信每个人都能看到他有多坏。他能举出童年、青春期和成年期不良行为的具体例子，来支持他对自身的核心认知。他能讲述自己小时候砸窗户、挨鞭子的经历。他能揭露自己十几岁时触犯法律、惹哭母亲的经历。他能讲述自己成年后吸烟、酗酒、吸毒和纵情声色的故事。他坚信，人生中任何幸福的唯一希望，都在于尽力掩饰自己与生俱来的邪恶。他从不相信有人会相信他的“好男人”形象，但他认为自己别无选择。

The second kind of Nice Guy is the "I'm so good" Nice Guy. This man handles his toxic shame by repressing his core belief about his worthlessness. He believes he is one of the nicest guys you will ever meet. If he is conscious of any perceived flaws, they are seen as minor and easily correctable. As a child he was never a moment's problem. As a teen he did everything right. As an adult, he follows all the rules to a "t". This Nice Guy has tucked his core belief about his "not OK-ness" into a handy, air-tight compartment deep in his unconscious mind. He masks his toxic shame with a belief that all the good things he does make him a good person.

第二种好人是“我太好了”的好人。这种人通过压抑自己认为自己一文不值的核心信念来应对有毒的羞耻感。他相信自己是你见过的最好的人之一。如果他意识到自己有任何明显的缺点，也会把它们看作是小事，很容易改正。小时候，他从来都不是问题。青少年时期，他做事总是恰到好处。成年后，他循规蹈矩，一丝不苟。这种好人把自己“不OK”的核心信念藏在潜意识深处一个方便易行、密封的盒子里。他用一种信念来掩盖自己有毒的羞耻感：他所做的一切好事都让他成为一个好人。

Even though the two kinds of Nice Guys may differ in their conscious awareness of their toxic shame, both operate from the same life paradigm. All Nice Guys believe they are not OK just as they are, and therefore must hide their flaws and become what they believe other people want them to be.

尽管两种“好男人”对自身“有毒羞耻感”的意识可能有所不同，但他们都秉持着相同的生活模式。所有“好男人”都认为自己本来就不够好，因此必须隐藏自己的缺点，成为他们认为别人希望他们成为的样子。

I make the distinction between the two kinds of Nice Guys to help both see their distortions. Neither is as bad or good as they believe themselves to be. They are both just wounded souls operating from a belief system based on the inaccurate perceptions of the events of their childhood.

我区分这两种“好人”，是为了帮助他们认清自身的扭曲。他们既不像自己想象的那样好，也不像自己想象的那样坏。他们都只是受伤的灵魂，秉持着基于对童年经历的错误认知而形成的信仰体系。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #3

挣脱束缚活动 #3

It is impossible to cover every factor that might cause a young boy to try to hide his perceived flaws and seek approval from others. I don't believe it is essential for Nice Guys to uncover every experience that ever made them feel unsafe or bad. But I have found that some understanding of where a life script originated is helpful in changing that script.

不可能涵盖所有可能导致小男孩试图隐藏自身缺陷并寻求他人认可的因素。我认为好男人没有必要揭露所有让他们感到不安全或糟糕的经历。但我发现，了解人生剧本的起源有助于改变它。

Reread the stories of Alan, Jason, and Jose. Think about how these stories are similar to your own childhood experiences. On a separate piece of paper or journal, write down or illustrate the messages you received in your family that seemed to imply that it wasn't OK for you to be who you were, just as you were. Share these experiences with a safe person. As you do, make note of your feelings. Do you feel sad, angry, lonely, numb? Share this information as well.

重读艾伦、杰森和何塞的故事。想想这些故事与你童年的经历有何相似之处。在另一张纸或日记本上，写下或画出你从家人那里收到的那些似乎暗示你不应该做自己、不应该像以前那样的信息。与一个安全的人分享这些经历。同时，记下你的感受。你是否感到悲伤、愤怒、孤独、麻木？也分享这些信息。

The purpose of this assignment is to name, rather than blame. Blaming will keep you stuck.

这项作业的目的是点名，而不是责怪。责怪只会让你陷入困境。

Naming the childhood experiences that led you to believe that it was not a safe or acceptable thing for you to be just who you were will allow you replace these messages with more accurate ones and help you change your Nice Guy script.

说出童年经历中那些让你相信做真实的自己是不安全或不可接受的事情的经历，将允许你用更准确的信息替换这些信息，并帮助你改变你的“好人”脚本。

*****************************


The Baby Boom Generation And The Sensitive Guy

婴儿潮一代和敏感的男人

Every child who has ever lived has experienced various forms of abandonment. There are many ways in which children can interpret and respond to these events. As stated above, becoming a Nice Guy is just one of many possible reactions. The childhood experiences described above are probably not sufficient in just themselves, however, to account for the multitude of Nice Guys I encounter regularly.

每个孩子都经历过各种形式的被遗弃。孩子们对这些事件的解读和反应有很多种。如上所述，成为“好人”只是众多可能的反应之一。然而，上述童年经历本身可能不足以解释我经常遇到的众多“好人”。

I have no doubt that Nice Guys have always existed. There have always been Marvin Milquetoast and Walter Mitty kinds of guys out there. I'm sure there has never been a shortage of mama's boys and henpecked husbands. I believe many little boys are born with a peaceful, generous temperament and grow up to be peaceful, generous men. But after years of working with countless men, I am convinced that a unique combination of social dynamics over the last five decades has produced a plethora of Nice Guys in historically unprecedented numbers.

我毫不怀疑好男人一直存在。像马文·米尔克托斯特和沃尔特·米蒂那样的男人一直都存在。我敢肯定，妈宝男和怕老婆的丈夫从来都不缺。我相信很多小男孩天生性情平和慷慨，长大后也会成为平和慷慨的男人。但经过多年与无数男人共事的经验，我确信，过去五十年里，独特的社会动态组合造就了数量空前的“好男人”。

To truly understand the current phenomena of the Nice Guy Syndrome, we have to take into account a series of significant social changes that began around the turn of the century and accelerated following World War II. These social dynamics included:

要真正理解当前的“好人综合症”现象，我们必须考虑到始于世纪之交、并在二战后加速发展的一系列重大社会变革。这些社会动态包括：

  • The transition from an agrarian to an industrial economy.
    从农业经济向工业经济的转变。
  • The movement of families from rural areas to urban areas.
