    家庭从农村迁移到城市。
  • The absence of fathers from the home.
    父亲离开家庭。
  • The increase in divorce, single parent homes, and homes headed by women.
    离婚、单亲家庭和女性当家家庭的增加。
  • An educational system dominated by women.
    以女性为主导的教育体系。
  • Women's liberation and feminism.
    妇女解放和女权主义。
  • The Vietnam War.
    越南战争。
  • The sexual revolution.
    性革命。

These events combined to have a major impact on American boys growing up in this era. These social changes created three profound dynamics that contributed to the wide spread phenomena of the Nice Guy Syndrome in the baby boom generation.

这些事件对成长于这个时代的美国男孩产生了重大影响。这些社会变革催生了三种深刻的动力，最终导致了“好人综合症”在婴儿潮一代的广泛蔓延。

1) Boys were separated from their fathers and other significant male role models. As a result, men became disconnected from other men in general and confused as to what it meant to be male.

1) 男孩们与父亲以及其他重要的男性榜样分离。结果，男性与其他男性普遍疏离，并对身为男性的意义感到困惑。

2) Boys were left to be raised by women. The job of turning boys into men was left to mothers and a school system dominated by women. As a result, men became comfortable being defined by women and became dependent on the approval of women.

2) 男孩被留给女性抚养。将男孩培养成男人的重任落到了母亲和以女性为主导的学校体系身上。结果，男性变得习惯于被女性定义，并依赖于女性的认可。

3) Radical feminism implied that men were bad and/or unnecessary. The messages of radical feminism furthered the belief of many men that if they wanted to be loved and get their needs met, they had to become what they believed women wanted them to be. For many men, this meant trying to hide any traits that might cause them to be labeled as "bad" men.

3) 激进女权主义暗示男人是坏的和/或不必要的。激进女权主义的理念进一步强化了许多男人的信念：如果他们想要被爱、得到满足，就必须成为他们认为女人希望他们成为的样子。对许多男人来说，这意味着要努力隐藏任何可能导致他们被贴上“坏”男人标签的特质。


20th Century History 101

20世纪历史101

The following is a brief overview of how some of the dynamic social changes of the last half of the 20th century helped create the bumper crop of Nice Guys in our culture.

下面简要概述了 20 世纪下半叶的一些动态社会变革如何帮助我们的文化中诞生大量好人。

The Loss Of Fathers

失去父亲

The shift to a manufacturing society and an urban migration in the post-war years took fathers away from their sons in droves. According to the US census, in 1910 one-third of all families lived on farms.

战后，随着社会向制造业转型，以及人口向城市的迁移，父亲们大批离开儿子。根据美国人口普查数据，1910年，三分之一的家庭生活在农场。

By 1940, this number had shrunk to one in five. By 1970, 96 percent of all families lived in urban areas.

到了1940年，这一数字缩减到五分之一。到了1970年，96%的家庭居住在城市地区。

In an agrarian society, boys connected with their fathers by working alongside them in the fields. This often meant contact with extended family that included grandfathers, uncles, and cousins. This daily contact with men provided boys with an intimate model of maleness. Sons learned about being male by watching their dads, just as their own fathers had learned by watching their fathers. As families migrated from rural areas to cities and suburbs after World War II, the contact between fathers and sons diminished significantly. Dads left home in the morning and went to work. Most sons never got to see what their fathers did, let alone have much time to spend with them.

在农耕社会，男孩们通过与父亲一起在田里劳作来与他们建立联系。这通常意味着与祖父、叔叔和表兄弟姐妹等大家庭成员的接触。这种与男性的日常接触为男孩们提供了一个亲密的男性榜样。儿子们通过观察父亲来学习如何成为男人，就像他们的父亲通过观察自己的父亲来学习一样。二战后，随着家庭从农村迁移到城市和郊区，父子之间的联系显著减少。父亲们早上出门上班。大多数儿子从未有机会看到父亲们在做什么，更不用说与他们共度时光了。

Fathers became unavailable in other ways. Men's addictions to work, TV, alcohol, and sex took them away from their sons. Increases in divorce began to separate boys from their fathers. Census statistics show that the incidence of divorce among men tripled from 1940 to 1970. In 1940, just over five million households were headed by women. By 1970, this figure had almost tripled to over 13 million households.

父亲在其他方面变得无能为力。男人沉迷于工作、电视、酒精和性，导致他们远离儿子。离婚率的上升开始使男孩与父亲分离。人口普查数据显示，从1940年到1970年，男性离婚率增长了两倍。1940年，只有略多于500万户家庭由女性当家。到1970年，这一数字几乎增长了两倍，超过1300万户。

In general, the Nice Guys I have worked with do not report having had a close, bonded relationship with their fathers in childhood. Sometimes this was a result of their fathers working long hours, being withdrawn, or being passive. More often than not, Nice Guys describe their fathers in negative terms. They often see them as controlling, rageful, angry, absent, abusive, unavailable, addictive, or philandering. It is not unusual at some point in childhood for Nice Guys to have made a conscious decision to be different from their fathers.

总的来说，我接触过的“好男人”们表示，童年时期与父亲的关系并不亲密。有时，这是因为他们的父亲工作时间长、性格孤僻或被动。“好男人”们常常用负面词汇来形容他们的父亲。他们常常认为父亲控制欲强、暴躁易怒、心不在焉、虐待成性、疏远不及、成瘾或拈花惹草。“好男人”们在童年时期有意识地选择与父亲不同，这并不罕见。

The unavailability of dads during this era often required mothers to take over the job of the fathers. Women inherited the defacto job of turning boys into men. Unfortunately even the most well-meaning mothers are not equipped to teach their sons how to be men by themselves. This hasn't kept them from trying.

在那个年代，父亲的缺席常常迫使母亲们接手父亲的职责。女性继承了将男孩培养成男人的职责。不幸的是，即使是最善意的母亲，也无法独自教会儿子如何成为真正的男人。但这并没有阻止她们去尝试。

I believe the significant number of Nice Guys produced in the '40s, '50s, and '60s is the direct result of mothers, not fathers, teaching their sons how to be male. Consequently, many Nice Guys have adopted a female perspective of masculinity and are comfortable having their manhood defined by women.

我认为，40、50、60年代“好男人”的大量涌现，直接源于母亲而非父亲在教导儿子如何成为男人。因此，许多“好男人”接受了女性视角下的男子气概，并乐于接受女性来定义他们的男子气概。

The Female Dominated Educational System

女性主导的教育体系

The modern educational system has also contributed to the dynamic of boys being raised by women.

现代教育体系也导致了男孩由女性抚养的现象。

Since World War II, boys have entered schools dominated by females. For most boys, the first several years in school become basic training in how to please women. From kindergarten through sixth grade, I had only one male teacher and six females. This is pretty consistent with national norms.

自二战以来，男孩们进入了以女性为主的学校。对大多数男孩来说，入学后的最初几年是学习如何取悦女性的基础训练。从幼儿园到六年级，我的老师只有一位男老师和六位女老师。这和全国的普遍情况基本一致。

Men account for just one in four teachers nationwide. In the primary grades, they make up only 15% of the teaching staff, and that number is steadily dropping. From daycare to preschool to elementary school, little boys in the post-war era have been surrounded by women. There have been few adult males to help them through this experience. If a little boy was already disconnected from his father and trained to please a woman, the typical school system magnified this conditioning.

全国范围内，男性教师仅占四分之一。在小学阶段，男性教师仅占教师总数的15%，而且这一数字还在稳步下降。战后时期，从托儿所到幼儿园再到小学，小男孩们的周围都是女性。很少有成年男性帮助他们度过这段经历。如果一个小男孩已经与父亲疏离，并被训练去取悦女性，那么典型的学校教育体系会进一步强化这种条件反射。

The Vietnam War

越南战争

In the '60s, the Vietnam War crystallized the feeling of alienation between many baby boom boys and their fathers. Battle lines were drawn between young men protesting a war started and perpetuated by their fathers. A generation of World War II veterans could not understand the flaunting of responsibility and the social rebellion of their sons. The young men of this generation became the antithesis of their fathers and of an establishment that solved domestic and international problems with guns and bombs.

60年代，越南战争加剧了许多婴儿潮一代的男孩与父亲之间的疏离感。年轻人之间划出了一条战线，抗议由他们的父亲发起并延续的战争。一代二战老兵无法理解他们的儿子们对责任的炫耀和社会的反叛。这代年轻人与他们的父亲以及用枪炮解决国内和国际问题的当权者形成了对立。

The anti-war movement created a new breed of males focused on love, peace, and avoiding conflict.

反战运动造就了一批注重爱、和平和避免冲突的新型男性。

Women's Liberation

妇女解放运动

During this same period of time, many women were beginning to work outside of the home, birth control provided new freedom, and women's liberation was in its infancy. Some mothers during the Baby Boom era could foresee a change in gender roles on the horizon. They worked to prepare their sons and daughters for what was to come. Many of these mothers raised their daughters to not need a man. At the same time, they trained their sons to be different from their fathers — peaceful, giving, nurturing, and attentive to a woman's needs.

在同一时期，许多女性开始外出工作，节育措施带来了新的自由，妇女解放运动也处于萌芽阶段。婴儿潮时期的一些母亲预见到了性别角色即将发生的变化。她们努力让儿女为即将到来的未来做好准备。许多母亲在养育女儿的过程中，让她们不需要男人。与此同时，她们也培养儿子与父亲的不同之处——平和、乐于奉献、关爱他人，并关注女性的需求。

Radical feminism in the '60s and '70s projected an angry generalization about men. Some feminists claimed that men were the cause all of the problems in the world. Others asserted that men were merely an unnecessary nuisance. More than likely, the majority of women during this era did not feel this way about men. Nevertheless, enough angry women were significantly vocal to contribute to a social climate that convinced many men that it was not OK to be just who they were.

六七十年代的激进女权主义运动对男性进行了愤怒的概括。一些女权主义者声称，男性是世界上所有问题的根源。另一些人则断言，男性只是不必要的麻烦。很可能，那个时代的大多数女性对男性并不抱有这种看法。然而，仍有足够多愤怒的女性发出强烈抗议，最终促成了一种社会风气，使许多男性认为，做自己本来的样子是不对的。

Epitaphs like "men are pigs" and "all men are rapists" were prominent during this time. Less angry slogans of feminism asserted that "a woman needs a man like a fish needs a bicycle." Men who were already conditioned to look to women for definition and approval were especially susceptible to these kinds of messages. This added incentive for these men to try to figure out what women wanted and try to become that in order to be loved and get their needs met.

“男人都是猪”和“所有男人都是强奸犯”之类的墓志铭在当时非常流行。一些不那么愤怒的女权主义口号则宣称：“女人需要男人就像鱼需要自行车一样。” 那些已经习惯于向女性寻求定义和认可的男性尤其容易受到这类信息的影响。这进一步激励了这些男性去探究女性的需求，并努力成为她们想要的样子，以获得爱和满足。


Soft Males And Boy-Men

柔弱的男性和男孩

Robert Bly, the author of Iron John , writes about how the social changes of the Baby Boom era created a new breed of American men. Bly calls these men "soft males."

《铁人约翰》的作者罗伯特·布莱描写了婴儿潮时代的社会变革如何造就了新一代美国男性。布莱称这些男性为“软男人”。

He writes, "they're lovely, valuable people — I like them — they're not interested in harming the earth or starting wars. There's a gentle attitude toward life in their whole being and style of living. But many of these men are not happy. You quickly notice the lack of energy in them. They are life-preserving but not exactly life-giving. Ironically, you often see these men with strong women who positively radiate energy. Here we have a finely-tuned young man, ecologically superior to his father, sympathetic to the whole harmony of the universe, yet he himself has little vitality to offer."

他写道：“他们是可爱又有价值的人——我喜欢他们——他们对破坏地球或发动战争毫无兴趣。他们的整个生命和生活方式都体现着一种温和的人生态度。但这些男人中有很多并不快乐。你很快就会注意到他们身上缺乏活力。他们只顾维持生命，却不真正赋予生命。讽刺的是，你经常看到这些男人身边有坚强的女人，她们散发着积极的能量。我们在这里看到的是一位精明的年轻人，在生态方面比他的父亲更优秀，对整个宇宙的和谐充满同情，但他自己却几乎没有活力可以奉献。”

From a different perspective, Camille Paglia comments on how the social changes of the last five decades have changed roles of men and women. "The hard-driving woman has to switch personae when she gets home. She's got to throttle back, or she'll castrate everything in the domestic niche. Many white, middle-class women have dodged this dilemma by finding themselves a nice, malleable boy-man who becomes another son in the subliminally matriarchal household." ("Politically Incorrect Desires," Salon: Issue 49)

卡米尔·帕格利亚从另一个角度评论了过去五十年社会变迁如何改变了男女角色。“曾经强势的女人回家后必须转换角色。她必须收敛点，否则就会在家庭中阉割一切。许多白人中产阶级女性通过寻找一个善良、顺从的男孩来避开这种困境，而这个男孩在潜意识里是母系社会的家庭中，成了她们的又一个儿子。”（《政治上不正确的欲望》，沙龙：第49期）

Regardless of whether we call these men "soft males," "sensitive new age guys," or "Nice Guys," the unique combination of social events in the post-World War II era reinforced and magnified the messages that many little boys had already received from their families — that they weren't OK just as they were.

不管我们称这些男人为“软男人”、“敏感的新时代男人”还是“好男人”，二战后时期独特的社会事件组合强化并放大了许多小男孩从家人那里已经收到的信息——他们现在的样子并不好。

These social events further amplified the belief that if they wanted to be loved, get their needs met, and have a smooth life, they had to hide their flaws and become what others (especially women) wanted them to be.

这些社会事件进一步强化了这样的信念：如果他们想要被爱、满足自己的需求并过上顺利的生活，他们就必须隐藏自己的缺点，成为别人（尤其是女性）希望他们成为的人。

My observation in recent years points to the reality that the conditioning described above did not end with the Baby-Boom generation. I am seeing more and more young men in their twenties, and even teens, who demonstrate all of the characteristics of the Nice Guy Syndrome. Not only have these young men been effected by all of the social dynamics listed above, even more grew up in single parent families or were raised by Nice Guy fathers. As I write this, I expect that we are just beginning on our third generation of Nice Guys.

我近年来的观察表明，上述条件反射并没有随着婴儿潮一代的消亡而终结。我看到越来越多的二十几岁，甚至十几岁的年轻男性，表现出“好男人综合症”的所有特征。这些年轻人不仅受到上述所有社会因素的影响，甚至更多人是在单亲家庭中长大，或者由“好男人”父亲抚养长大。在我撰写本文时，我预计我们才刚刚开始迎来“好男人”的第三代。


The Habits Of Highly Ineffective Men

低效男人的习惯

As a result of the family and social conditioning described above, Nice Guys struggle to get what they want in love and life. Due to their shame and ineffective survival mechanisms, the road map they follow just won't take them where they want to go. This is frustrating. But rather than trying something different, their life paradigm requires that they keep trying harder, doing more of the same.

由于上述家庭和社会环境的影响，好男人在爱情和生活中难以获得自己想要的东西。由于羞耻感和无效的生存机制，他们遵循的路线图根本无法带他们到达目的地。这令人沮丧。但他们的生活模式要求他们不断努力，重复同样的事情，而不是尝试不同的事物。

I frequently tell Nice Guys, "If you keep doing what you've always done, you'll keep getting what you've always had." To reiterate what I've illustrated before, Nice Guys prevent themselves from getting what they want in love and life by:

我经常对好男人说：“如果你继续做你一直在做的事情，你就会继续得到你一直拥有的东西。” 重申一下我之前的观点，好男人会通过以下方式阻止自己在爱情和生活中得到他们想要的东西：

  • Seeking the approval of others.
    寻求他人的认可。
  • Trying to hide their perceived flaws and mistakes.
    试图隐藏自己明显的缺点和错误。
  • Putting other people's needs and wants before their own.
    将他人的需求和愿望置于自身之前。
  • Sacrificing their personal power and playing the role of a victim.
    牺牲个人权力，扮演受害者的角色。
  • Disassociating themselves from other men and their own masculine energy.
    与其他男性以及自身的男性能量割裂开来。
  • Co-creating relationships that are less than satisfying.
    共同创造的恋爱关系并不令人满意。
  • Creating situations in which they do not have very much good sex.
    创造性生活不太愉快的环境。
  • Failing to live up their full potential.
    未能充分发挥自己的潜力。

The next seven chapters present a proven plan to show recovering Nice Guys the most effective ways to do something different. Read on. It is time for you to start getting what you want in love and life.

接下来的七章将呈现一个行之有效的方案，向正在康复中的“好男人”们展示最有效的改变人生的方法。继续阅读。现在，你就可以开始在爱情和生活中追求你想要的一切了。

# ↩

Chapter 3

# ↩

第三章

Learn To Please The Only Person Who Really Matters

学会取悦唯一真正重要的人



"I'm a chameleon," revealed Todd, a 30-year-old single Nice Guy. "I will become whatever I believe a person wants me to be in order to be liked. With my smart friends I act intelligent and use a big vocabulary. Around my mother, I look like the perfect loving son. With my dad, I talk sports. With the guys at work I cuss and swear . . . whatever it takes to look cool. Underneath it all, I'm not sure who I really am or if any of them would like me just for who I am. If I can't figure out what people want me to be, I'm afraid I will be all alone. The funny thing is, I feel alone most of the time anyway."
“我是条变色龙，”30岁的单身好男人托德透露道。“为了讨人喜欢，我会变成任何我认为别人希望我成为的样子。和聪明的朋友在一起，我装聪明，词汇量很大。和妈妈在一起，我就像个完美可爱的儿子。和爸爸在一起，我会聊体育。和同事在一起，我会骂脏话……为了显得酷，什么都行。但内心深处，我不确定自己到底是谁，也不确定他们是否会仅仅因为我现在的样子就喜欢我。如果我不知道别人希望我成为什么样的人，我担心自己会孤单一人。有趣的是，反正我大部分时间都感到孤独。”

Just about everything a Nice Guy does is consciously or unconsciously calculated to gain someone's approval or to avoid disapproval. Nice Guys seek this external validation in just about every relationship and social situation, even from strangers and people they don't like. Todd is an example of a man, who, because of internalized toxic shame, believes he has to become what he thinks other people want him to be. Nice Guys believe this chameleon-like metamorphism is essential if they hope to be loved, get their needs met, and have a problem-free life.

好男人所做的一切，几乎都是有意或无意地为了博取他人的认可或避免他人的反对。好男人在几乎所有的人际关系和社交场合中，都会寻求这种外部认可，即使是来自陌生人和他们不喜欢的人。托德就是一个例子，他因为内化的“有毒羞耻感”，认为自己必须成为别人希望他成为的样子。好男人认为，这种变色龙般的蜕变至关重要，如果他们希望被爱、需求得到满足，并拥有无忧无虑的生活。

The seeking of external validation is just one way in which Nice Guys frequently do the opposite of what works. By trying to please everyone, Nice Guys often end up pleasing no one — including themselves.

寻求外界认可只是“好男人”经常做出反效果的行为之一。他们试图取悦所有人，但最终往往得不到任何人的欢心——包括他们自己。


Seeking Approval

寻求批准

Because Nice Guys do not believe they are OK just as they are, they find a multitude of ways to convince themselves and others that they are lovable and desirable. They may focus on something about themselves (physical appearance, talent, intellect), something they do (act nice, dance well, work hard), or even something external to themselves (attractive wife, cute child, nice car) in order to get value and win other's approval.

因为“好男人”不认为自己本来的样子就很好，所以他们会想方设法说服自己和他人，让他们相信自己是值得爱戴和被人吸引的。他们可能会关注自身的某些方面（外貌、才华、智力），关注自己的某些行为（举止得体、舞技精湛、工作努力），甚至关注自身外在的某些方面（迷人的妻子、可爱的孩子、漂亮的车），以此来获得价值感，赢得他人的认可。

My word for these value-seeking mechanisms is attachments . Nice Guys attach their identity and worth to these things and use them to convince themselves and others that they are valuable. Without these attachments, Nice Guys don't know what else about themselves would make anyone like or love them.

我把这些追求价值的机制称为“依恋”。好男人把自己的身份和价值与这些东西联系起来，并利用它们来说服自己和他人，让他们相信自己是有价值的。如果没有这些依恋，好男人就不知道自己身上还有什么能让人喜欢或爱上他们。

Being a Nice Guy is the ultimate attachment for these men. They genuinely believe their commitment to being "good" and doing it "right" is what makes them valuable and compensates for their internalized belief that they are bad.

对这些男人来说，做个好人是终极追求。他们真心相信，自己对“好人”和“正确”行为的承诺，正是他们自身价值所在，也弥补了他们内心认为自己是坏人的信念。

Because of their toxic shame, it is impossible for Nice Guys to grasp that people might like them and love them just for who they are. They believe they are bad (the "I'm So Good" Nice Guy is unconscious of this core belief, but it is a core belief nonetheless), therefore they assume that if anyone really got to know them, these people would discover the same thing. Being able to attach themselves to things that make them feel valuable and garner approval from others seems essential if they hope to be loved, get their needs met, and have a problem-free life.

由于他们那有毒的羞耻感，好男人无法理解人们可能仅仅因为他们本来的样子就喜欢他们、爱他们。他们相信自己是坏人（“我很好”的好男人并没有意识到这个核心信念，但这毕竟是一个核心信念），因此他们认为，如果有人真正了解他们，他们也会发现同样的情况。如果他们希望被爱、满足需求、过上无忧无虑的生活，那么能够依附于那些让他们感到有价值、并获得他人认可的事物，似乎至关重要。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #4

挣脱束缚活动#4

I've taken surveys in several No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups asking the members about the attachments they use to try to get external approval. The following are just a few of the responses.

我在几个“不再是好人！”的群里做过调查，询问成员们为了获得外界认可会用哪些执念。以下只是一些回复。

Look over the list. Note any of the ways in which you seek approval. Add to the list any behaviors that are uniquely you. Write down examples of each. Ask others for feedback about the ways in which they see you seeking approval.

仔细查看这份清单。记录下你寻求认可的所有方式。把所有你独有的行为也添加到清单中，并写下每个行为的例子。问问别人，看看你是如何寻求认可的。

  • Having one's hair just right.
    拥有一头恰到好处的发型。
  • Being smart.
    聪明伶俐。
  • Having a pleasant, non-threatening voice.
    声音悦耳，不具威胁性。
  • Looking unselfish.
    看起来无私。
  • Being different from other men.
    与众不同。
  • Staying sober.
    保持清醒。
  • Being in good shape.
    身材健美。
  • Being a great dancer.
    舞跳得好。
  • Being a good lover.
    做一个好的情人。
  • Never getting angry.
    永不生气。
  • Making other people happy.
    让别人快乐。
  • Being a good worker.
    做一个好员工。
  • Having a clean car.
    车子干净。
  • Dressing well.
    衣着得体。
  • Being nice.
    为人友善。
  • Respecting women.
    尊重女性。
  • Never offending anyone.
    永不冒犯任何人。
  • Looking like a good father.
    看起来像个好父亲。

*****************************


How Nice Guys Use Attachments

好男人如何使用附件

Cal is a typical Nice Guy in the way he uses attachments to seek approval. Cal tries to get external validation by always being in a good mood, driving a nice car, dressing well, having a cute daughter, and having an attractive wife. Let's pick one of these attachments to illustrate how Cal tries to get approval from others.

卡尔是个典型的“好人”，他利用执着来寻求认可。卡尔试图通过保持好心情、开着豪车、衣着得体、有个可爱的女儿、娶个漂亮的妻子来获得外界的认可。让我们从这些执着中选择一个来解释卡尔是如何试图获得他人认可的。

Cal likes to dress his fourteen-month-old daughter in a cute dress and take her to the park. From the moment he begins to dress her he is unconsciously attaching his value and identity to the acknowledgment he thinks he will receive from being a "good dad." He knows that when he takes his daughter walking people will look at her and smile. Some will comment about the cute little girl and her father out for a walk. A few will stop and ask her age and others will gush about what a precious little angel she is. This attention makes Cal feel good about himself.

卡尔喜欢给他十四个月大的女儿穿上可爱的裙子，带她去公园。从他开始给她穿衣服的那一刻起，他就无意识地将自己的价值和身份认同与他认为自己会因成为“好爸爸”而获得的认可联系起来。他知道，当他带女儿散步时，人们会看着她，露出笑容。有些人会评论这个可爱的小女孩和她爸爸一起散步的样子。有些人会停下来问她的年龄，还有一些人会滔滔不绝地说她真是个可爱的小天使。这种关注让卡尔自我感觉良好。

The irony is that no one really values Cal for his attachments. Further, his dependency on external validation actually prevents people from getting to know him just as he is. None of these things have anything to do with who he is as a person. Nevertheless, they are the things he believes give him identity and value.

讽刺的是，没有人真正重视卡尔的这些执念。此外，他对外界认可的依赖实际上阻碍了人们真正地了解他。这些都与他本人无关。然而，他却相信这些赋予了他身份和价值。


Seeking The Approval Of Women

寻求女性的认可

Nice Guys seek external validation in just about every social situation, but their quest for approval is the most pronounced in their relationships with women. Nice Guys interpret a woman's approval as the ultimate validation of their worth. Signs of a woman's approval can take the form of her desire to have sex, flirtatious behavior, a smile, a touch, or attentiveness. At the other end of the spectrum, if a woman is depressed, in a bad mood, or angry, Nice Guys interpret these things to mean that she is not accepting or approving of them.

好男人在几乎所有社交场合都会寻求外界的认可，但他们对认可的追求在与女性交往中最为明显。好男人将女性的认可解读为对其价值的终极肯定。女性认可的表现形式可以是性欲、调情、微笑、触碰或专注。另一方面，如果女性情绪低落、心情不好或愤怒，好男人会将这些表现解读为她不接受或不认可他们。

There are numerous negative consequences in seeking the approval of women.

寻求女性的认可会带来许多负面后果。

Seeking women's approval requires Nice Guys to constantly monitor the possibility of a woman's availability. The possibility of availability is a term I use to describe the subjective measure of a woman's sexual availability. Since Nice Guys see sex as the ultimate form of acceptance, and they believe a woman must be in a good mood before she will have sex, these men are constantly diligent to not do anything that might upset a woman whom they desire. In addition, if a woman they desire is angry, depressed, or in a bad mood, they believe they must do something quickly — lie, offer solutions, sacrifice self, manipulate — to fix it.

为了获得女性的认可，好男人需要时刻关注女性是否愿意主动。“主动性”这个术语我用来描述女性性开放程度的主观衡量标准。由于好男人将性视为接纳的终极形式，并且他们认为女性在发生性关系之前必须心情愉快，所以他们总是竭尽全力避免做任何可能让他们心仪的女性不高兴的事情。此外，如果他们心仪的女性感到愤怒、沮丧或心情不好，他们就认为必须迅速采取行动——撒谎、提供解决方案、牺牲自我、操纵——来解决这个问题。

The possibility of availability extends beyond just sex. Since Nice Guys have been conditioned by their families and society to never do anything to upset a woman, they are hyper-vigilant in responding to the moods and desires of women they don't even plan on having sex with.

可得性的可能性不仅仅局限于性。由于家庭和社会对“好男人”的刻板印象，他们从不做任何让女人不开心的事，所以即使他们不打算和女人发生性关系，他们也会对女人的情绪和欲望保持高度警惕。

Seeking women's approval gives women the power to set the tone of the relationship. Nice Guys constantly report that their own moods are often tied to the moods of their partner. If she is happy and doing OK, so is he. If she is angry, depressed, or stressed, he will feel anxious until she is fixed. This connection runs so deep that many Nice Guys have told me that they feel guilty if they are in a good mood when their partner is not.

寻求女性的认可赋予了女性掌控关系基调的权力。好男人经常表示，他们自己的情绪往往与伴侣的情绪息息相关。如果伴侣开心、过得还好，他也会开心。如果伴侣生气、沮丧或有压力，他就会感到焦虑，直到伴侣情绪平复。这种联系如此深刻，以至于许多好男人告诉我，如果他们心情好而伴侣心情不好，他们就会感到内疚。

Seeking women's approval gives women the power to define men and determine their worth. If a woman says he is "wrong" or thinks he is a "jerk," a Nice Guy will be inclined to believe she is right.

寻求女性的认可赋予了女性定义男性并确定其价值的权力。如果一个女人说他“错了”或认为他是个“混蛋”，那么好男人就会倾向于相信她是对的。

Even if the Nice Guy argues with the woman's evaluation, at some level he knows that since she is the woman, she must be right. (One Nice Guy asked me, "If a man is talking in the forest and no woman is there to hear him, is he still wrong?")

即使好男人对女人的评价提出异议，在某种程度上，他也知道，既然她是女人，她就一定是对的。（一个好男人问我：“如果一个男人在森林里说话，却没有女人在场听，他还算错吗？”）

Seeking women's approval creates rage toward women. Though most Nice Guys claim to "love"

寻求女性的认可会激起对女性的愤怒。尽管大多数好男人声称“爱”

women, the truth is, most of these men have tremendous rage toward women. This is because we tend to eventually despise whatever we make into our god. When our god fails to respond in the ways we expect, we humans tend to respond in one of two ways. We either blindly intensify our acts of worship or lash out in righteous anger. When Nice Guys put a woman or women on a pedestal and attempt to win their approval, sooner or later, this adoration will turn to rage when these objects of worship fail to live up to the Nice Guys' expectations. This is why it is not unusual to hear a Nice Guy proclaim his undying love to a woman in one breath and then ragefully call her a "f . . . c . . ." only moments later.

女人们，事实上，这些男人大多对女人怀有巨大的愤怒。这是因为我们最终往往会鄙视我们奉为神明的一切。当我们的神明未能以我们期望的方式回应时，我们人类往往会做出两种反应：要么盲目地强化我们的崇拜行为，要么义愤填膺地发泄。当好男人把一个或多个女人奉为偶像，试图赢得她们的认可时，迟早，当这些崇拜对象未能达到好男人的期望时，这种崇拜就会转化为愤怒。这就是为什么我们常常听到好男人一口气向一个女人宣告他永恒的爱，然后片刻之后就怒不可遏地骂她“混蛋”。

I have found that many gay Nice Guys are just as susceptible as straight men to seeking women's approval. As long as the gay Nice Guy can convince himself that he is not sexually attracted to women, he can delude himself into thinking that women don't have any power over him.

我发现很多男同性恋好男人和异性恋男人一样，容易寻求女性的认可。只要男同性恋好男人能说服自己，自己对女性没有性吸引力，他就能欺骗自己，认为女性对他没有任何影响力。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #5

挣脱束缚活动 #5

If you did not care what people thought of you, how would you live your life differently?

如果您不关心别人对您的看法，您的生活会有何不同？

If you were not concerned with getting the approval of women, how would your relationships with the opposite sex be different?

如果您不关心获得女性的认可，您与异性的关系会有什么不同？

*****************************


Cover-Up Artists

掩盖真相的艺术家

When my son Steve was nine years old, he accidentally poked some holes in our kitchen table with a ballpoint pen. When he realized what he had done, he immediately showed his mother the damage.

我儿子史蒂夫九岁的时候，不小心用圆珠笔在我们家的餐桌上戳了几个洞。当他意识到自己弄坏了东西后，他立刻给妈妈看了。

Steve had appropriate, healthy shame about his mistake. He knew that his actions had caused damage to the table. He also knew that he had to take responsibility. Most importantly, he knew he wasn't bad.

史蒂夫对自己的错误感到羞愧，这种羞愧感是理所当然的。他知道自己的行为损坏了桌子。他也知道自己必须承担责任。最重要的是，他知道自己并不坏。

If I had done the same thing as a child (or even as an adult), I would have had an attack of toxic shame and tried my best to hide or deny what I had done. I would have been convinced someone was going to be angry at me and stop loving me. I would have lived with the secret as well as a constant fear of being found out.

如果我小时候（甚至成年后）也做过同样的事情，我肯定会被强烈的羞耻感所困扰，并竭尽全力掩盖或否认自己的所作所为。我会确信有人会生我的气，不再爱我。我会一直带着这个秘密生活，并且时刻担心被发现。

Numerous Nice Guys have commented that they could relate to my son's situation. Without exception, every one of them has admitted that they would have done the opposite of what Steve did — tried to cover it up.

很多“好人”都表示，他们能理解我儿子的处境。他们无一例外地都承认，如果换成史蒂夫，他们肯定会选择反其道而行之——试图掩盖真相。

As stated above, everything a Nice Guy does is calculated to try to win approval or avoid disapproval.

如上所述，好男人所做的一切都是为了赢得认可或避免反对。

Since Nice Guys do not believe they are OK just as they are, they see any mistake or perceived flaw as proof that they are bad and unlovable. They believe that if anyone sees how bad they really are, they will be hurt, shamed, or abandoned. As a result, Nice Guys are consummate cover-up artists.

因为好男人不相信自己本来的样子就很好，所以他们会把任何错误或明显的缺点都看作是自己不好、不值得爱的证据。他们相信，如果有人看到他们真正的糟糕一面，他们就会受到伤害、感到羞耻，甚至被抛弃。因此，好男人是完美的掩饰大师。

Nice Guys believe they must hide or distract attention from any perceived shortcoming . . .

好男人认为他们必须隐藏或转移人们对任何明显缺点的注意力……

  • If they forget something.
    如果他们忘记了什么东西。
  • If they are late.
    如果他们迟到了。
  • If they break something.
    如果他们弄坏了什么东西。
  • If they don't understand something.
    如果他们不明白什么东西。
  • If they do something wrong.
    如果他们做错了什么。
  • If they are depressed.
    如果他们感到沮丧。
  • If they are in pain.
    如果他们感到痛苦。
  • If they generally mess up.
    如果他们总是把事情搞砸。

The Nice Guy's need to hide is often the most pronounced in areas that are just part of being human and alive.

好人隐藏的需要往往在作为人性和生命的一部分的领域中表现得最为明显。

  • That they are sexual.
    他们有性欲。
  • That they have bodily functions.
    他们有身体机能。
  • That they are getting older.
    他们正在变老。
  • That they are losing their hair.
    他们正在脱发。
  • That they have needs.
    他们有需求。
  • That they are imperfect.
    他们不完美。

Breaking Free Activity #6

挣脱束缚活动#6

Look over the lists above. Write down examples of situations in which you have tried to hide or distract attention from any of these perceived flaws. How effective do you think you are in keeping these things hidden from the people you love?

看看上面列出的内容。写下你曾经试图隐藏或转移人们对这些明显缺陷的注意力的例子。你认为你在向你爱的人隐瞒这些缺陷方面有多有效？

*****************************


Hiding The Evidence

隐藏证据

Nice Guys find many creative ways to cover up their perceived flaws and mistakes. These include:

好男人总能想出各种花招来掩盖自己明显的缺点和错误。这些方法包括：

Lying

说谎

Most Nice Guys pride themselves on being honest and trustworthy. Ironically, Nice Guys are fundamentally dishonest. Nice Guys will tell lies, partial truths, and omit information if they believe it will prevent someone from focusing on them in a negative way.

大多数好男人都以诚实守信为荣。讽刺的是，好男人从根本上来说并不诚实。如果他们认为这样可以避免别人对他们产生负面关注，他们就会撒谎、部分说实话，甚至隐瞒信息。

Drawing On Their Account

从他们的账户中提取

Since Nice Guys strive so hard to be good, giving, and caring, they believe these acts should build up a credit that wipes clean any wrong they might do. Part of the Nice Guy's belief is that if he does most things right, no one should ever notice the few things (if any) he does wrong.

好男人努力追求善良、慷慨和关爱，他们相信这些行为应该能建立起良好的信誉，从而抹去他们可能犯下的任何错误。好男人的部分信念是，如果他大部分事情都做对了，就没有人会注意到他做的少数错事（如果有的话）。

Fixing

定影

Mature people take responsibility for their actions. When they make a mistake or act inappropriately, they apologize, make amends, or repair the damage. Conversely, Nice Guys try to fix situations by doing whatever it takes to get the other person to stop being upset.

成熟的人会为自己的行为负责。当他们犯错或行为不当时，他们会道歉、弥补或弥补损失。相反，好男人会尽一切努力让对方不再生气，从而解决问题。

DEER Response

鹿的回应

DEER is an acronym I use for: Defend Explain Excuse Rationalize . These are all fear-based behaviors used to distract others from focusing on the Nice Guy's mistakes and "badness." The Nice Guy is most likely to go into the DEER Response when he has done something or failed to do something, and someone (usually wife, partner, or boss) confronts him and expresses his or her feelings.

DEER 是我常用的缩写，代表“辩护”、“解释”、“借口”、“合理化”。这些都是基于恐惧的行为，用来转移别人的注意力，让他们不再关注好人的错误和“坏事”。当好人做了某事或没做某事，而有人（通常是妻子、伴侣或老板）质问他并表达自己的感受时，他最有可能出现 DEER 反应。

Turning The Tables

扭转局势

If someone gets angry at a Nice Guy or points out some flaw or mistake, his shame will be triggered. In an attempt to distract himself and the other person from his "badness" he may try to turn the tables and do something to trigger the other person's shame. I call this shame dumping . This unconscious strategy is based on the belief that if the Nice Guy can shift the focus to the other person's badness, he can slip out of the spotlight. Typical shame dumping techniques include blame, bringing up the past, deflection, and pointing out the other person's flaws.

如果有人对好人生气，或者指出他的缺点或错误，他的羞耻感就会被触发。为了转移自己和他人对他“坏”的注意力，他可能会试图扭转局面，做一些事情来激发对方的羞耻感。我称之为“羞耻倾倒”。这种无意识的策略基于这样一种信念：如果好人能把注意力转移到对方的缺点上，他就能从聚光灯下消失。典型的“羞耻倾倒”技巧包括责备、提起过去、转移话题以及指出对方的缺点。

Walls

墙壁

Nice Guys build walls that prevent others from getting too close. Understandably, this affects their ability to be intimate, but it also protects them from the consequences of being found out. These walls might include: Addictions (food, sex, t.v., alcohol, work, etc.), humor, sarcasm, intellectualism, perfectionism, and isolation.

好男人会筑起高墙，阻止别人过于亲近。这当然会影响他们亲密的能力，但也保护他们免受被发现的后果。这些高墙可能包括：成瘾（食物、性、电视、酒精、工作等等）、幽默、讽刺、唯理智主义、完美主义和孤立。

Teflon Men

特氟龙男士

As much as Nice Guys try to look good and get people to like them, the above defenses keep people at arm's length. Like most Nice Guy patterns, these unconscious behaviors actually accomplish the exact opposite of what the Nice Guy really craves. While desiring love and connection, his behaviors serve as an invisible force field that keeps people from being able to get close to him.

尽管好男人总是努力让自己看起来不错，博得别人的好感，但上述防御机制却让人们敬而远之。就像大多数好男人的模式一样，这些无意识的行为实际上与好男人真正渴望的截然相反。虽然他们渴望爱与联系，但他们的行为却像一道无形的力场，阻止人们接近他。

Nice Guys have a difficult time comprehending that in general, people are not drawn to perfection in others. People are drawn to shared interests, shared problems, and an individual's life energy.

好男人很难理解，一般来说，人们并不会被他人的完美所吸引。人们会被共同的兴趣、共同的问题以及一个人的生命力所吸引。

Humans connect with humans. Hiding one's humanity and trying to project an image of perfection makes a person vague, slippery, lifeless, and uninteresting. I often refer to Nice Guys as Teflon Men.

人与人之间是相互联系的。隐藏人性，试图展现完美形象，只会让一个人变得模糊、狡猾、毫无生气、毫无趣味。我常把“好男人”比作“特氟龙男人”。

They work so hard to be smooth, nothing can stick to them. Unfortunately, this Teflon coating also makes it difficult for people to get close. It is actually a person's rough edges and human imperfections that give others something to connect with.

他们竭尽全力让自己变得光滑，以至于什么也粘不住他们。不幸的是，这层特氟龙涂层也让人们难以接近。实际上，一个人的棱角和人性的缺陷，才让别人与他产生共鸣。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #7

挣脱束缚活动#7

Do you believe that people can see your human imperfections and still love you?

您是否相信人们可以看到您的不完美之处但仍然会爱您？

How would you be different if you knew the people who care about you would never leave you or stop loving you — no matter what?

如果你知道关心你的人永远不会离开你或停止爱你——无论发生什么——你会有什么不同？

*****************************


Self-Approval

自我认可

Recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome involves changing core paradigms. Instead of seeking external validation and avoiding disapproval, recovering Nice Guys must begin seeking the approval of the only person who really matters — themselves.

摆脱“好人综合症”需要改变核心思维模式。与其寻求外界的认可和逃避反对，好人必须开始寻求唯一真正重要的人的认可——他们自己。

Ironically, when Nice Guys begin focusing on pleasing themselves, they actually begin to experience the intimacy and connection with others that they have always desperately craved. To help facilitate this recovery process, Nice Guys can:

讽刺的是，当“好男人”开始专注于取悦自己时，他们实际上开始体验到他们一直渴望的与他人的亲密关系和联系。为了促进这一恢复过程，好男人可以：

  • Identify how they seek approval.
    了解他们如何寻求认可。
  • Take good care of themselves.
    照顾好自己。
  • Give themselves positive affirmations.
    给予自己积极的肯定。
  • Spend extended periods of time alone.
    长时间独处。
  • Reveal themselves to safe people.
    向安全的人展现自己。



Identifying Approval-Seeking Behavior Helps Nice Guys Learn To Approve Of Themselves

识别寻求认可的行为有助于好男人学会认可自己

As odd as it may sound, Nice Guys have to practice being themselves. One way to begin this process is to pay attention when trying to impress or get approval. Recovering Nice Guys can observe themselves spending extra time on their hair, holding the door open for someone, cleaning the kitchen, or walking with their child in the park — just to get noticed or praised.

听起来可能有点奇怪，但好男人必须练习做自己。开始练习的一个方法就是在试图给别人留下好印象或获得认可时多加注意。正在恢复的好男人会发现自己会花更多时间打理头发、为别人开门、打扫厨房，或者陪孩子在公园散步——只是为了引起注意或称赞。

As they become aware of how much time and energy they spend trying to garner approval, they can begin living an inside-out kind of life . This means, rather than focusing outward for acceptance and approval, they turn inward. In doing so, they can begin asking themselves the important questions: "What do I want," "What feels right to me," "What would make me happy?"

当他们意识到自己花了多少时间和精力去争取认可时，他们就可以开始过一种由内而外的生活。这意味着，他们不再关注外部的认可和接纳，而是转向内心。这样，他们就可以开始问自己一些重要的问题：“我想要什么？”“什么让我感觉正确？”“什么会让我快乐？”

Earlier in the chapter, I presented Cal as an illustration of how Nice Guys use "attachments" — things outside of themselves to get value. During a session of individual therapy, I asked Cal to make a list of things he used to get approval from others. The next week he brought in a two-page list. I encouraged him to pick one attachment from the list and for the next month, pay attention to how he used it to get value.

在本章前面，我以卡尔为例，说明了好男人是如何利用“依恋”——自身之外的事物来获取价值的。在一次个人治疗中，我让卡尔列出他用来获得他人认可的事物清单。第二周，他带来了一份两页纸的清单。我鼓励他从清单中挑选一种依恋，并在接下来的一个月里，关注他是如何利用它来获取价值的。

Cal decided to focus on his car. Cal kept his car perfectly clean, inside and out. He believed this was one of the things that impressed people and made them like him. He made a conscious decision to not wash or vacuum his car for the next month. While doing so, he would pay attention to how he felt and how people responded to him.

卡尔决定把注意力集中在他的车上。他把车里里外外都打理得干干净净。他相信这是让他印象深刻、赢得人们喜爱的原因之一。他下定决心，在接下来的一个月里不再洗车或吸尘。在此期间，他会留意自己的感受以及人们对他的反应。

Since Cal lives in the Seattle area, his car soon developed a gray haze from rain and road grime. On numerous occasions, he had to fight the impulse to wash it. When he drove down the road, he was sure that people were looking at his filthy car and judging him. When he drove to work or a friend's house, he waited for someone to shame him. When his daughter drew pictures in the dirt with her finger, it was almost more than he could stand.

由于卡尔住在西雅图地区，他的车很快就被雨水和路面污垢弄得灰蒙蒙的。很多时候，他都不得不强忍着洗车的冲动。开车上路时，他确信人们会盯着他脏兮兮的车，评头论足。开车去上班或朋友家时，他总是等着有人来羞辱他。当他女儿用手指在泥土上画画时，他几乎受不了了。

At the end of the month Cal washed and waxed his car and felt a sense of relief. Surprisingly enough, during the month, not one person had commented on his dirty car and no one had quit liking him or removed their love from him. Likewise, washing and waxing his car after a month didn't make anyone like him better or garner him any new friends.

月底，卡尔洗车打蜡后，感觉轻松了许多。令人惊讶的是，整个月里，没有一个人评论他的车脏，也没有人不再喜欢他，也没有人不再爱他。同样，一个月后洗车打蜡也没能让他变得更喜欢，也没能让他结交新朋友。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #8

挣脱束缚活动#8

Go back to the list of approval-seeking behaviors at the beginning of this chapter. Choose one of the ways you try to get external validation and do one of the following:

回到本章开头列出的寻求认可的行为。选择一种你试图获得外部认可的方式，并执行以下操作之一：

1) Go on a moratorium from this behavior. Set a period of time to stop doing it. Tell the people around you what you are doing. If you slip, tell a safe person about it. Use the slip as information about why, in that particular moment, you felt the need to get external approval.

1）暂停这种行为。设定一段时间停止。告诉周围的人你在做什么。如果你失败了，就告诉一个安全的人。利用这次失败来解释为什么在那个特定时刻，你感到需要获得外界的认可。

2) Consciously do more of this behavior. This may not make logical sense, but it is a powerful way to explore any dysfunctional behavior. Observe how you feel when you consciously try harder to get external validation.

2）有意识地多做这种行为。这或许在逻辑上说不通，但却是探索任何功能失调行为的有效方法。观察一下，当你有意识地努力寻求外部认可时，你的感受如何。

*****************************


Taking Good Care of the Self Helps Nice Guys Learn To Approve Of Themselves

照顾好自己有助于好男人学会认可自己

Taking good care of the self is essential for changing one's belief about the self. If a Nice Guy believes he isn't worth much, his actions toward himself will reflect this belief. When a recovering Nice Guy begins to consciously do good things for himself, these actions imply that he must be worth something.

照顾好自己对于改变一个人对自我的信念至关重要。如果一个好人认为自己一文不值，他对自己的行为就会反映出这种信念。当一个正在康复的好人开始有意识地为自己做好事时，这些行为暗示着他一定有所价值。

When I address this issue with Nice Guys, they frequently can't think of more than one or two good things to do for themselves. Together, we will often brainstorm and make a list of possible things to do.

当我和“好男人”们讨论这个问题时，他们常常想不出一两件对自己有益的事。我们通常会一起集思广益，列出一份可以做的事情的清单。

These good things can range from simple acts like drinking lots of water or flossing their teeth to more extensive things like taking a trip or buying the car they have always wanted. Below are a few possibilities:

这些美好的事物可以是简单的行为，例如多喝水或用牙线清洁牙齿，也可以是更宏大的事情，例如去旅行或购买他们一直想要的汽车。以下是一些可能性：

  • Exercise, work out, go for a walk.
    锻炼，健身，散步。
  • Eat healthy food.
    吃健康的食物。
  • Get enough sleep.
    获得充足的睡眠。
  • Relax, play, goof off.
    放松，玩耍，放松。
  • Get a massage.
    按摩。
  • Go out with buddies.
    和朋友出去玩。
  • Buy a new pair of shoes.
    买一双新鞋。
  • Get shoes polished.
    擦鞋。
  • Get dental work done.
    看牙医。
  • Get a physical.
    体检。
  • Listen to music.
    听音乐。

As the recovering Nice Guy begins to do good things for himself, he will feel uncomfortable. He may actually feel frightened, anxious, guilty, or confused. These feelings are the result of what is called cognitive dissonance.

当正在康复的“好人”开始为自己做好事时，他会感到不舒服。他可能会感到恐惧、焦虑、内疚或困惑。这些感受正是所谓的认知失调造成的。

When the Nice Guy does something good for himself he is doing something that implies he is valuable.

当好人为自己做一些好事时，他所做的事情表明他是有价值的。

This will conflict with his deeply held belief that he is worthless. As a result, he will experience dissonance — a clashing of two competing messages. In time, one of the beliefs will win. I encourage recovering Nice Guys to keep being good to themselves, no matter how frightening. In time the core messages from childhood are replaced with new, more accurate beliefs that reflect their inherent worth.

这会与他根深蒂固的“自己一文不值”的信念相冲突。结果，他会经历失调——两种相互矛盾的信息碰撞。随着时间的推移，其中一种信念会占上风。我鼓励正在康复的“好男人”们继续善待自己，无论这有多么可怕。随着时间的推移，童年时期的核心信息会被新的、更准确的、能够反映其内在价值的信念所取代。

Todd, introduced in the beginning of the chapter, spent so much time trying to get the approval of others that he rarely did anything for himself. On the encouragement of other men in his No More Mr. Nice Guy! group, Todd decided to consciously begin doing things for himself. He began small by buying himself new socks and underwear.

本章开头介绍的托德，花了太多时间试图获得他人的认可，以至于很少为自己做任何事。在“不再是好人！”小组其他成员的鼓励下，托德决定有意识地开始为自己做事。他从小事做起，给自己买新袜子和新内衣。

After a few weeks he started an exercise program and began working out regularly. Even though it made him feel guilty, he started going to a massage therapist every other week. After six months, Todd decided to spend $2,000 dollars to join a singles activity club. He shared with the group that even though he occasionally heard a little voice that said he wasn't worth it, doing so was one of the most affirming things he had ever done. A couple of months later, he reported that he had gone on two dates with two different women, both of whom seemed to like him just as he was.

几周后，他开始进行锻炼计划，并开始定期锻炼。尽管这让他感到内疚，但他还是开始每隔一周去看一次按摩师。六个月后，托德决定花2000美元加入一个单身活动俱乐部。他和小组成员分享道，尽管偶尔会听到一个小小的声音说他不值得，但这样做是他做过的最令人鼓舞的事情之一。几个月后，他说他和两个不同的女人约会过，她们似乎都和他一样喜欢他。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #9

挣脱束缚活动#9

Begin with the list above and add good things that you can do for yourself. Put the list up where you will see it and choose at least one thing per day and do it for yourself.

从上面的清单开始，添加一些你可以为自己做的好事。把清单贴到你能看到的地方，每天至少选择一件自己喜欢的事去做。

*****************************


Affirming Self Helps Nice Guys Learn To Approve Of Themselves

自我肯定帮助好人学会认可自己

Positive affirmations can help change the Nice Guy's core belief about himself. Affirmations replace old, inaccurate messages about the Nice Guy's worth with new, more realistic ones. When used alone, the affects of affirmations are usually short-lived. This is because these messages are contrary to the oldest, deepest beliefs the Nice Guy holds about himself. Affirmations are only effective when used along with other processes that help change the Nice Guy's core beliefs.

积极的肯定句可以帮助改变好男人对自己的核心信念。肯定句会用新的、更现实的信息取代关于好男人价值的旧的、不准确的信息。单独使用时，肯定句的效果通常很短暂。这是因为这些信息与好男人对自己最古老、最根深蒂固的信念相悖。肯定句只有与其他有助于改变好男人核心信念的方法一起使用时才会有效。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #10

挣脱束缚活动 #10

Make a list of positive affirmations about yourself. Write them on note cards and place them where you will see them regularly. Change the cards often so they stay fresh. When you read affirmations, close your eyes and fully embrace the meaning of the words. Observe any tendency of your mind to reject the affirmations in favor of old, deeply held beliefs.

列出一些关于自己的积极肯定句。把它们写在便签上，放在你经常能看到的地方。经常更换卡片，保持内容新鲜。阅读肯定句时，闭上眼睛，完全沉浸在文字的含义中。观察你的大脑是否倾向于拒绝这些肯定句，而倾向于那些根深蒂固的旧观念。

The following are some possible affirmations:

以下是一些可能的肯定：

"I am lovable just as I am."

“我本来的样子就值得被爱。”

"I am perfectly imperfect."

“我是一个完美而不完美的人。”

"My needs are important."

“我的需求很重要。”

"I am a strong and powerful person."

“我是一个坚强而有力的人。”

"I can handle it."

“我能应付。”

"People love and accept me just as I am."

“人们喜欢我并接受我本来的样子。”

"It is OK to be human and make mistakes."

“人都会犯错，这是正常的。”

"I am the only person I have to please."

“我是唯一需要取悦的人。”

*****************************


Spending Time Alone Helps Nice Guys Learn To Approve Of Themselves

独处能帮助好男人学会自我认可

Spending extended time alone is an important process in recovering from the Nice Guy Syndrome.

长时间独处是摆脱“好人综合症”的一个重要过程。

When alone, Nice Guys can discover who they are, what they like about themselves, and what rules they choose to govern their lives. I strongly recommend that Nice Guys take trips and retreats by themselves to places where no one knows them. In this context, the Nice Guy has fewer reasons to try to win people's approval, and there is less of a need to try to hide faults and mistakes. While alone, Nice Guys can reflect on themselves and their life direction. It is also a good time to practice taking responsibility for one's needs.

独处时，好男人能发现自己是谁，喜欢自己什么，以及他们选择什么样的生活规则。我强烈建议好男人独自旅行或隐居，去没有人认识他们的地方。这样，好男人就没那么多理由去赢得别人的认可，也不需要刻意隐藏缺点和错误。独处时，好男人可以反思自己和人生方向。这也是一个练习为自己的需求承担责任的好时机。

When alone, the recovering Nice Guy can do what he wants without having to please or compromise. He will go to bed and get up when he wants. He will decide when and what to eat. He will decide where he goes and what he does. When alone he will be less likely to caretake, seek approval, sacrifice self, or try to fix someone's problems.

独处时，正在康复的“好男人”可以做自己想做的事，无需取悦他人或妥协。他想睡就睡，想起就起。他可以决定什么时候吃什么，吃什么。他可以决定去哪里，做什么。独处时，他不太可能去照顾别人，寻求认可，牺牲自我，或试图解决别人的问题。

Spending extended periods of time alone also helps recovering Nice Guys face their number one fear —loneliness and isolation. When the Nice Guy discovers that spending time alone doesn't kill him, he may also realize that he doesn't have to stay in bad relationships, tolerate intolerable behavior, or manipulate people to try to get his needs met.

长时间独处也能帮助正在康复的“好男人”面对他们最大的恐惧——孤独和孤立。当“好男人”发现独处不会让自己难受时，他或许也会意识到，自己不必继续维持糟糕的关系，不必忍受难以忍受的行为，也不必操纵他人来满足自己的需求。

This time alone is spent most effectively when the Nice Guy can observe his tendency to distract himself with addictive patterns such as keeping busy, or using sex, food, or alcohol to medicate. Writing in a journal during these times can be especially effective. Some of the most insightful periods I have experienced in life have been by myself on weekend camping trips, week-long retreats, and times when my wife has been out of town.

当好男人能够意识到自己倾向于用一些成瘾模式来分散注意力，比如让自己忙碌，或者用性、食物或酒精来缓解压力时，独处的时间就能得到最有效的利用。在这些时候写日记尤其有效。我人生中最有洞察力的时期，往往是周末独自露营、一周的静修，以及妻子出门的时候。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #11

挣脱束缚活动#11

Plan a weekend trip to the mountains or beach. If possible, plan a vacation or retreat for a week or longer by yourself to a place where no one knows you. Visit a foreign country by yourself if at all possible. Use this time as an opportunity for self-observation and reflection. Keep a journal. Practice good self-care. Take along this book and spend time doing the Breaking Free exercises. When you return home, observe how you are different and how long it takes for you to begin returning to familiar patterns.

计划一次周末山间或海滩之旅。如果可能的话，计划一次为期一周或更长时间的度假或静修，去一个没有人认识你的地方。如果可能的话，独自去国外旅行。利用这段时间进行自我观察和反思。写日记。好好照顾自己。带上这本书，花时间做“打破束缚”的练习。回家后，观察一下自己有什么变化，以及需要多长时间才能恢复到熟悉的模式。

*****************************


Revealing Self Helps Nice Guys Learn To Approve Of Themselves
展现自我帮助好男人学会认可自己

When Nice Guys try to hide their humanity from others, they reinforce their core belief that they are bad and unlovable. Changing this core belief requires that they bring their humanity out into the open, release their toxic shame, and receive more accurate messages than the ones internalized in childhood.

当好男人试图向他人隐藏自己的人性时，他们强化了自己的核心信念：自己是坏人，不值得爱。要改变这一核心信念，他们需要展现自己的人性，释放有害的羞耻感，并接受比童年时期内化的信息更准确的信息。

By necessity, this process requires a safe person or safe people.

必然地，这个过程需要一个或多个安全的人员。

As frightening as it may initially feel, finding these safe people is essential for learning to approve of self. The recovering Nice Guy cannot do this part alone. Safe people are essential for reversing the distorted beliefs all Nice Guys have about their worth.

尽管一开始可能会让人感到恐惧，但找到这些“安全感”的人对于学习自我认可至关重要。正在康复中的“好男人”无法独自完成这一步。“安全感”对于扭转“好男人”对自己价值的扭曲认知至关重要。

This process requires building trust. I suggest that the recovering Nice Guy set a regular time to meet with his safe person or group, and a little at a time, start revealing himself. This process begins by just talking about himself. This in itself makes many Nice Guys uncomfortable. Over time, the recovering Nice Guy can begin revealing the things about himself that he is the least comfortable letting others see.

这个过程需要建立信任。我建议正在康复中的好男人定期与他的“安全人”或“安全小组”见面，一点一点地开始展现自己。这个过程从谈论自己开始。这本身就让很多好男人感到不舒服。随着时间的推移，正在康复中的好男人可以开始展现那些他最不愿意让别人看到的自己。

Once trust has been established, he can begin to reveal things about himself that create fear and shame. I have watched many Nice Guys go from being secretive and evasive to revealing their deepest, darkest secrets in the presence of safe people.

一旦建立了信任，他就会开始吐露一些令对方感到恐惧和羞耻的秘密。我见过许多“好男人”从遮遮掩掩、躲躲闪闪到在安全的人面前吐露他们最深、最黑暗的秘密。

Reid, a recovering alcoholic and a member of a No More Mr. Nice Guy! group is a good example of this process.

里德 (Reid) 是一名正在康复的酒鬼，也是“不再是好人！” (No More Mr. Nice Guy!) 组织的成员，他就是这个过程的一个很好的例子。

Reid came into a group late one evening and remained quiet and detached for the first thirty or forty minutes. Reid's pattern in the group was to either join in actively or stay withdrawn. His quiet periods were often a signal that he was emotionally distraught. When an opportune time arose, I told Reid that he seemed withdrawn and I asked how he was doing.

一天晚上，里德很晚才加入一个小组，一开始的三四十分钟里，他一直保持着沉默和疏离。里德在小组里的模式要么是积极参与，要么是保持沉默。他沉默的时期往往是他情绪低落的信号。等到合适的时机，我告诉里德，他似乎有些孤僻，并问他最近怎么样。

Once the attention of the group focused on him, his appearance shifted from detachment to terror. "I almost didn't come tonight." He whispered softly as he looked down at his hands. "In fact, I was thinking about quitting the group."

当大家的注意力集中到他身上时，他的表情从冷漠转为恐惧。“我今晚差点没来。”他低头看着自己的双手，轻声说道。“其实，我当时正在考虑退团。”

A couple of the guys reflected the concern of the whole group by asking what was going on.

有几个人询问发生了什么事，反映了整个团队的关心。

"I just feel so terrible," Reid continued, "I've done something so terrible, I just didn't know if I could face all of you."

“我只是感觉很糟糕，”里德继续说道，“我做了一件非常可怕的事情，我只是不知道我是否能够面对你们所有人。”

My thoughts began to race as to what he could have done that might cause the group rise up in judgment against him. One of the group members asked if he was having an affair.

我开始胡思乱想，他可能做了什么事，让小组成员起来批判他。其中一个小组成员问他是不是有外遇。

"No, worse." Reid responded. "I've done something so terrible, I don't even know if I can tell you about it."

“不，更糟。”里德回答道。“我做了一件太可怕的事，我甚至不知道该不该告诉你。”

As the group offered their support and encouragement, Reid broke through his fear and shame and began to open up.

在大家的支持和鼓励下，里德克服了恐惧和羞耻，开始敞开心扉。

"Last week I got reprimanded by my boss and then I got into a fight with my wife. I was so depressed that I went out and bought a fifth of Vodka and got drunk. I've been on a binge ever since and I just can't make myself stop."

“上周我被老板训斥了一顿，然后又和老婆吵了一架。我当时郁闷极了，就出去买了五分之一瓶伏特加，喝得酩酊大醉。从那以后，我就一直酗酒，根本停不下来。”

Tears were running down Reid's face as the shame of his addiction to alcohol raised its ugly head once again. He had been clean and sober since joining the group six months earlier. He was active in AA, but had experienced a number of slips and relapses in his 12 years of recovery.

里德的脸上流淌着泪水，他那令人羞愧的酒瘾再次在他脸上浮现。自从六个月前加入戒酒会以来，他一直保持着清醒和清醒。他积极参与戒酒互助会，但在12年的戒酒过程中，也经历过多次失控和复发。

A group member handed him a box of tissues and Reid wiped his eyes. He then continued telling his story and revealing his shame in between sobs.

一名小组成员递给他一盒纸巾，里德擦了擦眼睛。他一边抽泣，一边继续讲述自己的故事，吐露自己的羞耻。

"I'm back to all my old ways of lying and manipulating. I'm totally out of control."

“我又开始撒谎、操纵别人了。我完全失控了。”

I asked if he had called his sponsor or gone to a meeting since this began. He shook his head and shared that he had slipped so many times that he didn't think anyone would care or want him back.

我问他自从这件事发生以来，有没有给担保人打过电话，或者参加过什么会议。他摇了摇头，说自己已经失足太多次了，所以他认为没人会在意，也没人会想让他回来。

Various members of the group shared with Reid that they didn't think he was bad nor did they have judgment of him. They could see that he was hurting. They told him that they had great respect that he came into group and revealed what was going on, especially since he had so much shame about it.

小组里的许多成员都告诉里德，他们并不认为他不好，也没有对他评头论足。他们看得出来他很痛苦。他们告诉他，他们非常尊重他来到小组，坦白自己的遭遇，尤其是考虑到他对此感到如此羞耻。

After a few moments, Reid revealed, "That's not all. There's more." He began to sob once again. Lifting his hand to his forehead he shook his head as in disgust for himself.

过了一会儿，里德开口道：“这还不算完。还有更多。”他又开始抽泣。他举起手捂住额头，摇了摇头，仿佛在厌恶自己。

"It gets worse. I went to the peep shows near my work two times this week." He turned his eyes to the floor and wept almost uncontrollably. "I had been doing so good." he choked out between sobs, "Now I've blown it all. I feel totally worthless and don't want to go on living. I've made a mess of everything."

“情况更糟了。这周我去了公司附近的两次窥视秀。”他把眼睛转向地板，几乎无法控制地哭泣。“我之前过得那么好，”他哽咽着说，“现在我把一切都搞砸了。我觉得自己一文不值，不想活下去了。我把一切都搞砸了。”

For the rest of the hour, the group supported Reid and encouraged him to go all the way through his shame. They reassured him that he wasn't bad and that no one had judgment of him. To the contrary, everyone respected him for revealing his shame and pain. They supported him in talking to his wife, calling his sponsor and going to a meeting. They asked him to call one group member each day during the next week and let them know how he was doing.

在接下来的一个小时里，小组成员们支持里德，鼓励他克服羞耻感。他们安慰他说，他并不坏，没有人会评判他。相反，每个人都尊重他坦诚自己的羞耻和痛苦。他们支持他与妻子沟通，给他的资助人打电话，并参加一个会议。他们要求他在接下来的一周里每天给一位小组成员打电话，告诉他们他的情况。

When Reid left the group that night, he was obviously shaken and scared. He had also released a heavy burden and received the support of a group of people who genuinely cared about him and wanted him to be OK. No matter how deeply Reid feared that his badness would cause people to judge him and abandon him, neither of these things happened. Instead, he received the message that there was nothing he could do that would make the men in the group stop liking him or stop caring about him.

那天晚上，里德离开小组时，显然受到了惊吓和震撼。他卸下了沉重的负担，得到了一群真心关心他、希望他一切安好的人的支持。无论里德多么担心自己的坏行为会导致别人评判他、抛弃他，这些都没有发生。相反，他得到的是这样的讯息：无论他做什么，都无法让小组里的男人们不再喜欢他，不再关心他。


Shedding Old Skin

蜕去旧皮

As recovering Nice Guys release their toxic shame and start seeking their own approval, they begin to realize several important truths.

当好男人从痛苦中恢复过来并开始寻求自己的认可时，他们开始意识到几个重要的事实。

  • They are not bad.
    他们并不坏。
  • They don't have to do anything to win other people's approval.
    他们不需要做任何事情来赢得别人的认可。
  • They don't have to hide their perceived flaws or mistakes.
    他们不必隐藏自己明显的缺点或错误。
  • People can love them just as they are.
    人们可以爱他们本来的样子。

As recovering Nice Guys begin to apply the principles described in this chapter they can embrace the reality that they are human. Like every other human, Nice Guys make mistakes, use poor judgment, and act inappropriately. Nevertheless, their humanity doesn't make them bad or unlovable nor does it cause other people to stop loving them.

当正在康复中的好男人开始运用本章所述的原则时，他们就能接受自己也是人这一现实。和其他人一样，好男人也会犯错、判断失误、行为不当。然而，他们的人性并不会让他们变得坏或不可爱，也不会让别人不再爱他们。

Imperfect humans can only connect with other imperfect humans. Most folks tend to be attracted to individuals who have some substance and sense of self. Chameleons usually don't draw much of a crowd or get many ovations.

不完美的人只能与不完美的人建立联系。大多数人倾向于被有内涵、有自我意识的人所吸引。变色龙通常不会吸引太多观众，也不会得到太多欢呼。

By shedding their chameleon skin and learning to please themselves, recovering Nice Guys begin to experience the intimacy and connection they have always desired. By learning to approve of themselves, they begin to radiate a life energy and charisma that draws people to them. As Nice Guys stop seeking approval and stop trying to hide their perceived flaws, they open a door to start getting what they really want in love and life.

通过蜕去变色龙般的外皮，学会取悦自己，正在康复中的好男人开始体验他们一直渴望的亲密关系和人际交往。通过学会认可自己，他们开始散发出吸引他人的生命能量和魅力。当好男人不再寻求认可，不再试图隐藏自己所谓的缺点时，他们就打开了一扇通往爱情和生活中真正渴望的大门。

# ↩

Chapter 4

# ↩

第四章

Make Your Needs A Priority

把你的需求放在首位


"I want you to know that I'm really uncomfortable with that thing we talked about last week."

“我想让你知道，我对我们上周谈论的事情感到非常不舒服。”

Lars, an anxiety-filled executive, began his second session of counseling with this statement. Lars had come to see me on the encouragement of his wife. He reported being generally depressed and unhappy for as long as he could remember. In recent months he had found it difficult to sleep at night and was experiencing migraine headaches on a regular basis. Even though everything in his life seemed to be "fine" — good job, nice home, family, etc. — he never seemed to be happy.

拉斯是一位焦虑的高管，他以这句话开始了他的第二次咨询。拉斯是在妻子的鼓励下来找我的。他说，从他记事起，他就一直处于抑郁和不快乐的状态。最近几个月，他发现晚上难以入睡，而且经常偏头痛。尽管他生活中的一切似乎都“很好”——好工作、温馨的家、家人等等——但他似乎从未感到快乐。

In his first counseling session, Lars revealed that he had constant fantasies of "chucking it all" and disappearing to somewhere else in the world. These thoughts made him feel guilty, so he kept them to himself.

在第一次心理咨询中，拉斯透露自己经常幻想“抛弃一切”，消失在世界的某个角落。这些想法让他感到内疚，所以他一直埋藏在心里。

In that session I asked Lars what he did for himself. He gave me a puzzled look. "What do you mean?" he asked.

在那次治疗中，我问拉斯他自己做了什么。他疑惑地看着我。“你什么意思？”他问道。

I repeated the question.

我重复了一遍这个问题。

After a pause, he answered, "Not much, I guess."

停顿了一下后，他回答说：“我想，没什么。”

For the rest of the session, I shared with him the importance of making his needs a priority and taking responsibility for finding ways to meet them. This discussion was met with both fear and resistance from Lars. The same hesitancy was repeated as he began his second counseling session.

在接下来咨询的时间里，我和他强调了优先考虑他的需求，并承担起找到满足这些需求的方法的重要性。这次讨论让拉斯既害怕又抵触。同样的犹豫在他第二次咨询开始时再次出现。

"Which part of our discussion last week made you uncomfortable?" I asked.

“我们上周的讨论中哪一部分让你感到不舒服？”我问道。

"All of it," he responded. "That part about making my needs a priority really made me uptight."

“全部都是，”他回答。“把我的需求放在第一位那部分真的让我很紧张。”

I asked him what part about taking responsibility for his needs made him anxious.

我问他，承担起满足自己需求的责任时，哪一部分令他感到焦虑。

"Everything," he responded. "That seems like that would make me selfish and self-centered."

“一切，”他回答道。“那样的话，我似乎就显得自私和以自我为中心了。”

"What's wrong with that?" I asked.

“有什么问题吗？”我问。

Lars looked at me with amazement. "What's wrong with that," he replied, "is that being selfish would make me too much like my old man. All he ever thought about was himself and the rest of us suffered as a result. I just couldn't do that. I couldn't be a self-centered S.O.B. like him. I've got a wife, kids, a job, a mortgage, and bills to pay. There's no room for me to start behaving like my father."

拉斯惊讶地看着我。“这有什么错，”他回答说，“自私会让我太像我老爸了。他满脑子都是自己，结果我们大家都受苦。我做不到。我不能像他那样自私自利。我有妻子、孩子、工作、房贷，还有各种账单要付。我根本没有资格像我父亲那样。”


Low Maintenance Kinds Of Guys

低维护类型的男人

Lars is a fairly typical Nice Guy when it comes to his needs. Nice Guys generally focus their attention on meeting everyone else's needs while trying to be "low maintenance" kinds of guys themselves. When I talk with them about making their needs a priority, their response is similar to that of Lars.

说到需求，拉斯是个典型的“好男人”。“好男人”通常专注于满足其他人的需求，同时努力成为“低调”的人。当我和他们谈论如何优先考虑自己的需求时，他们的反应和拉斯很像。

This ubiquitous pattern among Nice Guys is the result of childhood conditioning. When a child's needs are not met in a timely, healthy manner, the child may come to believe he is "bad" for having needs. He may also think that it is his needs that cause people to hurt him or abandon him. Typically Nice Guys respond to these inaccurate interpretations of their life events by developing a number of survival mechanisms.

这种在“好人”中普遍存在的模式，是童年环境所致。当孩子的需求得不到及时、健康地满足时，他们可能会认为自己因为有这些需求而“不好”。他们甚至可能认为，正是这些需求导致人们伤害或抛弃他们。通常情况下，“好人”会通过发展一系列生存机制来应对这些对他们生活事件的错误解读。

  • Trying to appear needless and wantless.
    试图表现出无用和无欲望。
  • Making it difficult for others to give to them.
    使别人难以给予他们。
  • Using "covert contracts."
    使用“隐性合同”。
  • Caretaking — focusing attention on other people's needs.
    照顾——关注他人的需求。

While creating an illusion of security in childhood, these survival mechanisms only increased the odds of their needs going unrecognized and unmet.

这些生存机制虽然在童年时期创造了一种安全的假象，但却增加了他们的需求不被认可和满足的可能性。


Trying To Appear Needless And Wantless Prevents Nice Guys From Getting Their Needs Met

试图表现出无用和无欲会阻止好人满足他们的需求

For Nice Guys, trying to become needless and wantless was a primary way of trying to cope with their childhood abandonment experiences. Since it was when they had the most needs that they felt the most abandoned, they believed it was their needs that drove people away.

对好男人来说，努力让自己变得无用和无欲是他们应对童年被遗弃经历的主要方式。由于他们越需要帮助，就越感到被遗弃，所以他们认为正是自己的需求驱使人们远离他们。

These helpless little boys concluded that if they could eliminate or hide all of their needs, then no one would abandon them. They also convinced themselves that if they didn't have needs, it wouldn't hurt so bad when the needs weren't met. Not only did they learn early not to expect to get their needs met, but also that their very survival seemed to depend on appearing not to have needs.

这些无助的小男孩得出结论：如果他们能够消除或隐藏自己的所有需求，就没有人会抛弃他们。他们还说服自己，如果他们没有需求，当这些需求得不到满足时，就不会那么痛苦。他们不仅很早就学会了不要指望自己的需求得到满足，而且他们的生存似乎完全取决于表现出没有需求的样子。

This created an unsolvable bind: these helpless little boys could not totally repress their needs and stay alive, and they could not meet their needs on their own. The only logical solution was to try to appear to be needless and wantless while trying to get needs met in indirect and covert ways.

这造成了一个无法解决的困境：这些无助的小男孩无法完全压抑自己的需求并活下去，也无法独自满足自己的需求。唯一合理的解决办法就是试图表现出无用和无欲，同时试图以间接和隐蔽的方式满足他们的需求。

As a result of these childhood survival mechanisms, Nice Guys often believe it is a virtue to have few needs or wants. Beneath this facade of needlessness and wantlessness, all Nice Guys are actually extremely needy. Consequently, when they go about trying to get their needs met, Nice Guys are frequently indirect, unclear, manipulative, and controlling.

由于这些童年生存机制，好男人常常认为少有需求或欲望是一种美德。在这种无所求和无所欲的外表下，所有好男人实际上都极其需要帮助。因此，当他们试图满足自己的需求时，他们往往表现得委婉、含糊、操纵和控制欲强。


Making It Difficult For Others To Give To Them Prevents Nice Guys From Getting Their Needs Met

让别人难以给予，会阻止好人满足自己的需求

In addition to using ineffective strategies to get their needs met, Nice Guys are terrible receivers. Since getting their needs met contradicts their childhood paradigms, Nice Guys are extremely uncomfortable when they actually do get what they want. Though most Nice Guys have a difficult time grasping this concept, they are terrified of getting what they really want and will go to extreme measures to make sure they don't. Nice Guys carry out this unconscious agenda by connecting with needy or unavailable people, operating from an unspoken agenda, being unclear and indirect, pushing people away, and sabotaging.

除了使用无效的策略来满足需求外，好男人也是糟糕的接受者。由于满足需求与他们童年时期的认知模式相悖，好男人在真正得到自己想要的东西时会感到极度不适。虽然大多数好男人很难理解这个概念，但他们害怕得到自己真正想要的东西，并会采取极端手段来确保自己得不到。好男人会通过与需要帮助或无法得到帮助的人建立联系、以不言而喻的方式行事、含糊不清、不直接表达、疏远他人以及进行破坏来执行这种无意识的计划。

A good illustration of this dynamic is the way Nice Guys commonly try to get their sexual needs met.

这种动态的一个很好的例子是好男人通常试图满足他们的性需求的方式。

Many of the Nice Guys I've worked with have expressed a heightened interest in sex, yet they frequently feel frustrated in their attempts to get these needs met. This is usually because their actions pretty much guarantee that they won't get what they believe they want.

我合作过的许多“好男人”都表现出对性爱的强烈兴趣，但他们常常在满足这些需求的过程中感到沮丧。这通常是因为他们的行为几乎注定了他们无法得到他们自以为想要的东西。

Nice Guys have an uncanny knack of picking partners who, because of childhood sexual abuse or other negative experiences with sex, tend to have a difficult time being sexually expressive. When these partners do make themselves available to be sexual, it is not uncommon for Nice Guys to do something that further ensures that they don't get their needs met. The Nice Guy may respond by taking control rather than letting the sexual experience unfold. He may focus on his partner's sexual needs before she has a chance to pay attention to him. He might start a fight by making a comment about her weight or her past unavailability. All of these strategies pretty much ensure that the Nice Guy won't have to experience the fear, shame, or anxiety that might get triggered if he actually allowed someone to focus on his needs.

好男人在挑选伴侣方面有着不可思议的本领，这些人往往因为童年时期遭受性虐待或其他负面性经历而难以表达性需求。当这些伴侣确实愿意进行性行为时，好男人通常会做一些事情来进一步确保他们的需求得不到满足。好男人可能会采取控制权，而不是让性体验自然展开。他可能会在伴侣有机会注意到他之前就关注她的性需求。他可能会因为评论她的体重或她过去不愿主动表达而挑起争吵。所有这些策略几乎可以确保好男人不必经历恐惧、羞耻或焦虑，而这些恐惧、羞耻或焦虑可能会在他真正允许别人关注他的需求时引发。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #12

挣脱束缚活动 #12

Do you believe it is OK for you to have needs? Do you believe people want to help you meet your needs? Do you believe this world is a place of abundance?

你相信你有需求是可以接受的吗？你相信人们愿意帮助你满足你的需求吗？你相信这个世界是一个富足的地方吗？

*****************************


Using Covert Contracts Prevents Nice Guys From Getting Their Needs Met

使用隐蔽合同可以防止好人满足他们的需求

All Nice Guys are faced with a dilemma: How can they keep the fact that they have needs hidden, yet still create situations in which they have some hope of getting their needs met?
所有好男人都面临着一个困境：他们如何才能隐藏自己有需求的事实，同时又能创造出让他们的需求有希望得到满足的情况？

In order to accomplish this seemingly impossible goal, Nice Guys utilize covert contracts . These unconscious, unspoken agreements are the primary way that Nice Guys interact with the world around them. Almost everything a Nice Guy does represents some manifestation of a covert contract.

为了实现这个看似不可能的目标，好男人会运用隐秘的契约。这些无意识的、不言而喻的协议是好男人与周围世界互动的主要方式。好男人所做的几乎每一件事都体现了某种隐秘的契约。

The Nice Guy's covert contract is simply this:

好家伙的秘密合同很简单：

1) I will do this ______________ (fill in the blank) for you, so that

1）我会为你做这件事____（填空），以便

2) You will do this ______________ (fill in the blank) for me.

2) 你将为我做这件事____（填空）。

3) We will both act as if we have no awareness of this contract.

3) 我们双方均视为对本合同毫不知情。

Most of us have had the experience of leaning over and whispering in our lover's ear, "I love you." We then wait expectantly for our beloved to respond with, "I love you too." This is an example of a covert contract in which a person gives to get . Giving "I love yous" to get "I love you toos" in return is the basic way Nice Guys go about trying to get all of their needs met. There is nothing wrong with asking your partner to tell you she loves you, but telling her "I love you" first to get an "I love you too" in return is indirect, unclear, and manipulative.

我们大多数人都有过这样的经历：俯身在爱人耳边轻声说“我爱你”。然后满怀期待地等待爱人回应“我也爱你”。这是一种隐性契约的例子，一个人付出是为了得到回报。说“我爱你”，然后得到“我也爱你”的回应，是“好男人”满足所有需求的基本方式。让你的爱人告诉你她爱你并没有错，但为了得到“我也爱你”的回应，先对她说“我爱你”就显得间接、不明确，而且带有操控性。

As a result of the conditioning they received in their family and society, Nice Guys believe if they are "good," then they should be loved, get their needs met, and have a problem-free life.

由于家庭和社会对他们的影响，好男人认为，如果他们是“好人”，那么他们就应该被爱，他们的需求得到满足，并且过上无忧无虑的生活。

In reality, the primary paradigm of the Nice Guy Syndrome is nothing more than a big covert contract with life.

事实上，好人综合症的主要范式不过是与生活签订的一份巨大的隐性合同。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #13

挣脱束缚活动#13

Identify at least one covert contract between you and a significant other. What do you give? What do you expect in return? Share this information with the other person. Ask the person how it feels to respond to an unclear agenda.

找出你和另一半之间至少一项隐秘的契约。你付出了什么？你期望得到什么回报？与对方分享这些信息。问问对方回应不明确的议程时的感觉如何。

*****************************


Caretaking Prevents Nice Guys From Getting Their Needs Met

照顾他人会阻碍好男人满足自己的需求

One of the most common ways Nice Guys use covert contracts to try to meet their needs is through caretaking. Nice Guys believe their caretaking is fundamentally loving and is one of the things that makes them good people. In reality, caretaking has nothing to do with being loving or good. Caretaking is an immature and indirect attempt to try to get one's needs met.

好男人利用隐性契约来满足自身需求的最常见方式之一就是照顾。好男人认为，照顾本质上是一种爱，也是他们成为好人的原因之一。但实际上，照顾与爱或善良无关。照顾是一种不成熟且间接的满足自身需求的尝试。

Caretaking always consists of two parts:

照料始终包括两个部分：

1) Focusing on another's problems, needs, or feelings in order to

1）关注他人的问题、需求或感受，以便

2) Feel valuable, get one's own needs met, or to avoid dealing with one's own problems or feelings.

2）感到有价值，满足自己的需求，或避免处理自己的问题或感受。

Reese, a graphic designer in his late twenties, is a good example of the extremes to which Nice Guys caretake in their intimate relationships. Reese, who is gay, lamented in one of his therapy sessions,

里斯是一位二十多岁的平面设计师，他就是“好男人”在亲密关系中走向极端的典型例子。里斯是一位同性恋，他在一次心理咨询中感叹道：

"Why can't I find a partner who gives as much back to me as I give to him?" He went on to describe how all of his boyfriends seemed to be takers and that he always did all of the giving.

“为什么我找不到一个能给我和我对他的付出一样多的伴侣呢？”他继续描述道，他所有的男朋友似乎都是索取者，而他总是付出一切。

Within a period of a year, Reese entered into three intense relationships. Each began wonderfully and seemed like the partnership he had been looking for. Each failed because of the same scenario: Reese picked men who needed rescuing or fixing.

一年之内，里斯经历了三段激情四射的恋情。每一段都开局顺利，看起来就像他一直寻觅的伴侣。然而，每一段都以失败告终，原因却都一样：里斯选择了需要拯救或修复的男人。

The first boyfriend lived in Canada and had recently gotten off drugs. He came to live with Reese but never applied for a work visa and struggled to stay clean. Reese went out of his way to be supportive of his boyfriend with the hope that he would find a job and stay off drugs. Finally, Reese sent him home to get his life straightened out. Later, he found out from a mutual friend that the reason his boyfriend had never applied for a work visa was because he was HIV positive, something he failed to tell Reese.

第一个男朋友住在加拿大，最近刚戒掉毒瘾。他来和里斯同居，但从未申请过工作签证，并且一直努力保持清醒。里斯竭尽全力支持他的男朋友，希望他能找到工作并戒掉毒瘾。最后，里斯送他回家，让他重新调整生活。后来，里斯从一个共同的朋友那里得知，他男朋友从未申请工作签证的原因是他感染了艾滋病毒，而他却没有告诉里斯这件事。

The next boyfriend was of a different race from Reese and had never come to grips with his homosexuality. His parents and religion kept him in constant conflict. He was never able to commit to the relationship. Nevertheless, Reese went out of his way to be supportive and giving, all with the hopes that his boyfriend would eventually get things straightened out and become available to Reese.

瑞茜的下一个男朋友和他不同种族，而且从未接受过自己的同性恋倾向。他的父母和宗教信仰让他一直处于冲突之中。他始终无法对这段感情做出承诺。尽管如此，瑞茜还是竭尽全力地支持他，付出一切，希望他的男朋友最终能理清头绪，愿意和他在一起。

The third boyfriend was in the military. He was living on base, some 40 miles from Reese's apartment and had no car. Reese had to take the initiative in getting together and would often shuttle his partner around. Because Reese made more money, he always paid when they went out. Reese frequently bought his boyfriend gifts and loaned him money. When this boyfriend got transferred to a different state, Reese quit his job, sold his car, and moved along with him, only to return in three months because his partner started running around on him.

第三个男朋友在军队服役。他住在基地里，离里斯的公寓大约40英里，而且没有车。里斯不得不主动安排约会，经常接送他的伴侣。因为里斯收入更高，所以他们出去玩的时候总是他付钱。里斯经常给这个男朋友买礼物，还借钱给他。这个男朋友调到另一个州后，里斯辞掉了工作，卖掉了车，和他一起搬了家。但三个月后，他又回来了，因为他的伴侣开始到处乱跑。

During this 12-month period while Reese was so busy caretaking the needs and problems of his boyfriends, he gave up his job and alienated most of his friends and family. Reese's caretaking allowed him to stay oblivious to his own self-destructive behaviors while investing tremendous energy in trying to fix others. As is true for most Nice Guys, no matter how much he gave to others, Reese never felt like he got as much back in return.

在这12个月里，里斯忙于照顾男友们的需求和问题，辞去了工作，疏远了大多数朋友和家人。里斯的照顾让他在投入大量精力试图修复他人的同时，对自己的自毁行为视而不见。就像大多数“好男人”一样，无论他为别人付出多少，里斯都从未感到自己得到了同等的回报。


Caring Vs. Caretaking

关怀与照料

Though Nice Guys see everything they do for others as loving, caretaking has very little to do with caring. Here are the differences:

虽然好男人视自己为他人所做的一切都是爱，但照顾他人与关爱几乎毫无关联。以下是两者的区别：

Caretaking

照顾

1) Gives to others what the giver needs to give.
1) 给予他人给予者需要给予的东西。

2) Comes from a place of emptiness within the giver.
2) 源自给予者内心的空虚。

3) Always has unconscious strings attached.
3) 总是带有无意识的附加条件。

Caring
关怀

1) Gives to others what the receiver needs.

1）给予他人接受者所需要的东西。

2) Comes from a place of abundance within the giver.

2）来自给予者内心的富足。

3) Has no strings attached.

3) 没有附加任何条件。

Nice Guys caretake for a number of reasons, none of them having anything to do with love. For them, even the most innocuous and subtle act often has some string attached. Nice Guys give in the ways they would like others to give to them. They give gifts, affection, back rubs, sex, surprises. They will encourage their partner to take a day off, buy a new outfit, go to the doctor, take a trip, quit a job, go back to school — yet would not give themselves permission to do any of the same things.

好男人会因为各种原因照顾伴侣，但这些原因都与爱无关。对他们来说，即使是最无害、最细微的举动，也往往附带着某种条件。好男人会以他们希望别人给予的方式付出。他们会送礼物、关爱、按摩、性爱、惊喜。他们会鼓励伴侣休假一天、买新衣服、去看医生、旅行、辞职、重返校园——但自己却不会允许自己做任何这些事情。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #14
挣脱束缚活动 #14

Identify two or three examples of your caretaking behavior. In order to stimulate awareness of your caretaking, do one of the following for a period of one week:

列举两三个你照顾他人行为的例子。为了激发你对照顾他人的意识，请在一周内做以下其中一项：

1) Go on a caretaking moratorium. Because Nice Guys have a difficult time differentiating between caring and caretaking, stop giving completely (except to young, dependent children). Tell people what you are doing so they won't be confused. Observe your feelings and other people's reactions.

1）暂停照顾。因为“好男人”很难区分照顾和照顾，所以不要完全付出（除非是年幼且需要照顾的孩子）。告诉别人你在做什么，这样他们就不会感到困惑。观察你的感受和别人的反应。

2) Consciously try to caretake more than you already do. As odd as this assignment may sound, it is a very effective way to create awareness of your caretaking behavior. Pay attention to how you feel and how other people react to you.

2）有意识地尝试比你现在做的更多照顾。这项作业听起来可能有点奇怪，但它确实是一个让你意识到自己照顾他人行为的有效方法。留意你的感受以及别人对你的反应。

*****************************


The Victim Triangle

受害者三角

Rather than helping Nice Guys meet their needs, covert contracts and caretaking only lead to frustration and resentment. When this frustration and resentment builds long enough, it often spills out in some not so pretty ways. Giving to get creates a cycle of craziness called the victim triangle. The victim triangle consists of three predictable sequences:

隐性契约和照顾非但不能满足“好男人”的需求，反而只会让他们感到沮丧和怨恨。当这种沮丧和怨恨积聚足够长的时间后，往往会以一些不太光彩的方式爆发出来。为了得到而付出会形成一个疯狂的循环，被称为“受害者三角”。受害者三角由三个可预测的序列组成：

1) The Nice Guy gives to others hoping to get something in return.

1）好人给予别人，希望得到回报。

2) When it doesn't seem that he is getting as much as he gives or he isn't getting what he expected, he feels frustrated and resentful. Remember, the Nice Guy is the one keeping score and he isn't totally objective.

2) 当他付出得不偿失，或者没有达到预期时，他会感到沮丧和怨恨。记住，好人只会记分，他并非完全客观。

3) When this frustration and resentment builds up long enough, it spills out in the form of rage attacks, passive-aggressive behavior, pouting, tantrums, withdrawing, shaming, criticizing, blaming, even physical abuse. Once the cycle has been completed, it usually just begins all over again.

3) 当这种挫败感和怨恨积累足够长的时间时，就会以愤怒攻击、消极攻击行为、撅嘴、发脾气、退缩、羞辱、批评、责备甚至身体虐待的形式爆发出来。一旦这个循环完成，通常又会重新开始。

My wife refers to these episodes as victim pukes. Sometimes the puking will resemble a child's temper tantrum. Sometimes the victim puke will take a more passive-aggressive form in which the Nice Guy will have an affair or act out in some hidden way. All the while the person doing the puking will feel justified because of the many ways he has been victimized. These victim pukes are one of the primary reasons Nice Guys aren't always so nice.

我妻子把这些情况称为受害者呕吐。有时，这种呕吐就像孩子发脾气。有时，受害者呕吐会以更具消极攻击性的形式出现，比如好男人有外遇，或者以某种隐蔽的方式表现出来。与此同时，呕吐者会觉得自己的行为是正当的，因为他在很多方面都受到了伤害。这些受害者呕吐是好男人并不总是那么好的主要原因之一。

Shane's relationship with his girlfriend Racquel is a good example of the victim triangle and emotional puking. Shane had Racquel on a pedestal and deep inside, he believed she could only love him if he was

Shane 和女友 Racquel 的关系就是受害者三角关系和情感呕吐的典型例子。Shane 把 Racquel 奉为圭臬，内心深处，他相信只有当他

"good enough." In order to win her love he gave her gifts, sent her cards, left phone messages, bought her clothes, planned special surprises, and helped with her home and children.

“足够好。”为了赢得她的爱，他送她礼物、寄卡片、打电话留言、给她买衣服、策划特别的惊喜，并帮助她打理家务和孩子。

All of this created a sense of emotional indebtedness for Racquel. She felt like she could never repay Shane for everything he did for her. The truth was — she couldn't. Shane was trying to buy her love —only the contract wasn't clear. In time, the only way she could cope with his pleasing and caretaking was by pushing him away.

这一切让拉奎尔产生了一种情感上的亏欠感。她觉得自己永远无法报答肖恩为她所做的一切。事实是——她真的无法报答。肖恩试图用钱来换取她的爱——只是这份合同并不明确。渐渐地，她唯一能应对肖恩的取悦和照顾的方式就是把他推开。

When this happened, Shane was devastated. He couldn't understand why if he had fulfilled his end of the contract, Racquel wouldn't keep hers. He didn't think he was that hard to please. The more Shane gave to Racquel, the more resentful he became. He would accuse her of not loving him. They would have tremendous battles in which they would break up, calling each other all kinds of names. Afterwards Shane would feel frightened and remorseful and pursue Racquel and try to fix things (all the while resenting her for not pursuing him and trying to fix things). He would then start caretaking and pleasing again to win her love. The cycle repeated itself over and over again.

这件事发生后，肖恩崩溃了。他不明白，既然他履行了合同，为什么拉奎尔却不履行她的合同。他觉得自己没那么难取悦。肖恩给拉奎尔的越多，他就越怨恨。他会指责她不爱他。他们会大吵大闹，最终分手，互相辱骂。之后，肖恩会感到恐惧和悔恨，继续追求拉奎尔，试图修复关系（同时又怨恨她没有追求他，没有努力修复关系）。然后，他会再次开始照顾和取悦她，以赢得她的爱。这个循环一遍又一遍地重复。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #15

挣脱束缚活动 #15

It can be difficult to make a direct link between your caretaking behavior and the emotional pukes which inevitably follow. Observe the ways you hurt the people you love.

很难将你的照顾行为与随之而来的情绪波动直接联系起来。观察一下你是如何伤害你所爱之人的。

  • Do you make cutting remarks or hurtful "jokes"?
    你会说尖刻的话或伤人的“笑话”吗？
  • Do you embarrass them in public?
    你会在公共场合让他们难堪吗？
  • Are you frequently late?
    你经常迟到吗？
  • Do you "forget" things they've asked you to do?
    你会“忘记”他们让你做的事情吗？
  • Do you criticize them?
    你会批评他们吗？
  • Do you withdraw from them or threaten to leave?
    你会疏远他们或威胁要离开吗？
  • Do you let frustration build until you blow up at them?
    你会让他们越来越沮丧，直到你对他们大发雷霆吗？

Ask the significant others in your life to give you feedback about your caretaking and emotional pukes. This information may be hard to hear and may trigger a shame attack, but it is important information for breaking out of the victim triangle.

问问你生活中重要的人，让他们对你照顾他人和情感上的不足之处给出反馈。这些信息可能让你难以接受，甚至可能引发羞辱感，但这对于打破受害者三角关系至关重要。

*****************************


Becoming Truly Selfish

变得真正自私

When I began writing this book I shared the early drafts with members of my No More Mr. Nice Guy!

当我开始写这本书时，我与我的“不再是好人！”组织的成员分享了早期草稿。

men's groups. On one occasion, a group member stated, "It seems like the whole emphasis of the book is about focusing on one's self. It seems really selfish and self-centered, like the Nice Guy should just think about himself and not worry about anyone else."

男性团体。有一次，一位小组成员说：“这本书似乎强调的都是以自我为中心。它看起来非常自私和以自我为中心，就好像好男人应该只考虑自己，不必担心别人。”

Even though I did not set out to write No More Mr. Nice Guy! with this theme in mind, this group member's comments contained an important truth that I hadn't even been fully conscious of before then.
