Since Nice Guys learned to sacrifice themselves in order to survive, recovery must center on learning to put themselves first and making their needs a priority.

虽然我写《不再是好人！》时并没有以此为主题，但这位小组成员的评论却包含了一个我之前甚至都没有完全意识到的重要真理。既然好人学会了为了生存而牺牲自己，那么康复也必须以学会把自己放在第一位，并将他们的需求放在首位为中心。

Most Nice Guys are astonished when I tell them that it is healthy to have needs and that mature people make getting their needs met a priority. Sometimes I have to repeat this truth many times in order for it to sink in. For Nice Guys, having needs means being "needy," and needy represents a one-way ticket to abandonment.

当我告诉“好男人”有需求是健康的，成熟的人会优先满足自己的需求时，大多数“好男人”都感到很惊讶。有时我不得不重复这个道理好几次，才能让他们真正理解。对“好男人”来说，“有需求”意味着“有需要”，而“有需要”意味着被抛弃。

I tell Nice Guys, "No one was put on this planet to meet your needs" (except their parents — and their job is done). I also remind them they weren't put on this planet to meet anyone else's needs (except those of their children).

我告诉好男人：“没有人来到这个星球是为了满足你们的需求”（除了他们的父母——他们的工作已经完成了）。我还提醒他们，他们来到这个星球上也不是为了满足任何人的需求（除了他们孩子的需要）。

This paradigm shift is always terrifying for recovering Nice Guys. The idea of making their needs a priority feels like the quickest route to being disliked, unloved, and all alone.

这种思维模式的转变总是让正在康复中的“好男人”感到恐惧。把他们的需求放在首位，感觉就像是让他们最快地陷入厌恶、不被爱和孤独的境地。

Whenever I challenge Nice Guys to focus on making their needs a priority, their responses are pretty predictable:

每当我要求好男人优先考虑他们的需求时，他们的反应都是可以预料的：

"People will get angry at me."

“人们会生我的气。”

"People will think I'm selfish."

“人们会认为我很自私。”

"I'll be alone."

“我会一个人。”

"What if everyone lived this way?"

“如果每个人都这样生活会怎样？”

I then list the benefits for Nice Guys and the people around them when they begin to put themselves first:

然后我列出了当好人开始把自己放在第一位时，他们和他们周围的人会得到的好处：

  • They increase the likelihood of getting what they need and want.
    他们增加了获得所需和所愿的可能性。
  • They can give judiciously — giving what people really need.
    他们可以明智地给予——给予人们真正需要的东西。
  • They can give without resentment and expectation.
    他们可以不带怨恨和期望地付出。
  • They become less needy.
    他们变得不那么需要帮助。
  • They become more attractive.
    他们变得更有吸引力。

Most Nice Guys will really like the last benefit on the list. Helpless, whiny, wimpy, and needy are not attractive on a man. Confidence and self-assurance are. Most folks are attracted to men who have a sense of self. Putting the self first doesn't drive people away, it attracts them. Putting the self first is essential for getting what one wants in love and life.

大多数好男人都会喜欢清单上的最后一点。无助、爱发牢骚、懦弱和需要别人照顾的男人并不吸引人。自信和自信才是。大多数人会被有自我意识的男人吸引。以自我为中心不会让人疏远，反而会吸引他们。以自我为中心对于在爱情和生活中获得想要的东西至关重要。


Taking Responsibility For Their Own Needs Helps Nice Guys Get Their Needs Met

承担起自己的责任有助于好男人满足自己的需求

In order for Nice Guys to get their needs met, they must begin to shift their core paradigms. This shift includes coming to believe:

为了满足“好男人”的需求，他们必须开始转变自己的核心思维模式。这种转变包括：

  • Having needs is part of being human.
    有需求是人性的一部分。
  • Mature people make meeting their own needs a priority.
    成熟的人会把满足自己的需求放在首位。
  • They can ask for help in meeting their needs in clear and direct ways.
    他们可以通过清晰直接的方式寻求帮助来满足自己的需求。
  • Other people really do want to help them meet their needs.
    其他人确实想帮助他们满足自己的需求。
  • This world is a place of abundance.
    这个世界是一个富足的地方。

In order to get their needs met, recovering Nice Guys must do something radically different from what they have done previously. For Nice Guys, putting the self first is not just a suggestion to try on for size.

为了满足自身需求，正在康复中的“好男人”必须做出与以往截然不同的事情。对“好男人”来说，“自我至上”并非只是个试探性的建议。

It is essential not only for getting needs met, but also for reclaiming personal power, feeling fully alive, and experiencing love and intimacy.

它不仅对于满足需求至关重要，而且对于恢复个人力量、感受到充分的活力以及体验爱和亲密也至关重要。

Interestingly enough, when Nice Guys take responsibility for their own needs and make them a priority, those around them benefit too. Gone are the covert contracts, the guessing games, the anger outbursts, and passive-aggressive behavior. Gone are the manipulation, the controlling behavior, and the resentment. I learned this lesson first-hand a few years back.

有趣的是，当“好男人”承担起自身需求的责任，并将其放在首位时，他们周围的人也会受益。那些隐秘的契约、猜谜游戏、暴怒的爆发和消极攻击行为都消失了。那些操纵、控制和怨恨也消失了。几年前，我就亲身体验到了这一点。

A holiday weekend was approaching and our kids were going to be out of town. I tried to plan some time with my wife Elizabeth, but she seemed ambivalent and unwilling to make a commitment to what she wanted to do. I felt frustrated and put my plans on hold. Finally, upon the urging of a friend, I decided to try putting myself first over the weekend. I made plans and invited my wife to join me if she felt inclined. I did several things I wanted, including spending some time with friends. As it turned out, Elizabeth decided to join me on a number of occasions. On Monday, she shared with me that she had thoroughly enjoyed the weekend and didn't want it to end.

假期周末即将来临，孩子们要出城了。我试着安排一些时间陪妻子伊丽莎白，但她似乎很矛盾，不愿意承诺自己想做的事情。我感到很沮丧，就把计划搁置了。最后，在一位朋友的劝说下，我决定在周末尽量以自己为中心。我制定了计划，并邀请妻子如果愿意的话可以一起去。我做了几件我想做的事情，包括和朋友们待在一起。结果，伊丽莎白多次决定和我一起去。周一，她告诉我，她非常享受这个周末，不想结束。


A Challenge

挑战

In a session of one of my No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's groups, I challenged each of the group members to experiment with putting themselves first for at least a week. Though the challenge created tremendous anxiety for all of the men, each decided to accept it. The experiences of three of Lars, Reese, and Shane are presented below.

在我的“不再是好人！”男士小组的一次活动中，我挑战了小组里的每一位成员，让他们尝试至少一周的时间，把自己放在第一位。尽管这个挑战让所有男士都感到非常焦虑，但每个人都决定接受。以下是拉斯、里斯和肖恩三位男士的经历。

Lars

拉尔斯

Lars, introduced at the beginning of the chapter, went home after the group and told his wife that he was going to make his needs a priority for the following week. She was initially resistant to his proclamation

本章开头介绍的拉斯在小组活动结束后回家，告诉妻子，他打算在接下来的一周优先处理自己的需求。起初，妻子对他的这一声明表示反对。

— which added to his anxiety. To boost his courage, Lars called a couple of men in the group. Their encouragement gave him the support he needed to follow through with his commitment.

——这让他更加焦虑。为了鼓舞他的勇气，拉斯打电话给了小组里的几个男人。他们的鼓励给了他履行承诺所需的支持。

Lars decided to keep it simple. His plan for the week involved making time every day to go the gym and work out. Before his children were born, Lars had been physically active. The demands of job, home and children had put an end to that. Lars decided to alternate his workouts before and after work. When he shared his plan with his wife, she applied a little guilt. "That's not fair that you get to work out and I don't," she proclaimed. Lars was tempted to back down. He had an impulse to try to find a solution so his wife could work out too. Instead, he reflected her concern and told her he was going to work out anyway.

拉斯决定简化计划。他一周的计划就是每天抽出时间去健身房锻炼。在孩子出生之前，拉斯一直很活跃。但工作、家庭和孩子的压力让他放弃了这种锻炼方式。拉斯决定在工作前后交替锻炼。当他把计划告诉妻子时，妻子感到一丝愧疚。“你能锻炼，我却不能，这不公平，”她说道。拉斯很想放弃。他一时冲动，想找到一个解决办法，让妻子也能锻炼。但他最终还是表达了妻子的担忧，并告诉她他无论如何都会去锻炼。

During his first couple of trips to the gym, Lars was overwhelmed with guilt and anxiety. Nevertheless, he persevered. After the third day, his wife actually asked him how his workout went. As the week continued, Lars began to feel more energized and optimistic about life. He started sleeping better. He enjoyed being around other people at the gym who were also taking good care of themselves.

最初几次去健身房时，拉斯内心充满了内疚和焦虑。尽管如此，他还是坚持了下来。第三天之后，他的妻子竟然问他锻炼得怎么样。随着一周的过去，拉斯开始感到精力充沛，对生活也更加乐观。他的睡眠质量也提高了。他喜欢和健身房里其他同样注重自身保养的人在一起。

Surprisingly, after his first week, his wife told him that he had inspired her to start taking better care of herself. She told him that she was going to start dropping the kids off at the daycare center at the gym and begin an aerobics class for herself.

令人惊讶的是，第一周后，他的妻子告诉他，他激励了她开始更好地照顾自己。她告诉他，她打算把孩子们送到健身房的日托中心，并开始自己上有氧运动课。

Reese

里斯

Reese had joined the No More Mr. Nice Guy! group after his break-up with his last boyfriend. At first he had been uncomfortable being the only gay man in the group, but the other men accepted him and he had begun to work on developing non-sexual relationships with men.

里斯和上一个男朋友分手后加入了“不再是好人！”（No More Mr. Nice Guy!）小组。起初，作为小组里唯一的男同性恋者，他感到很不自在，但其他男人接受了他，他也开始尝试与男性发展非性关系。

Reese's habit on weekends was to go to out with his latest boyfriend to gay bars on Friday, Saturday, and Sunday nights. By Monday morning, he was exhausted. He would spend the rest of the week playing catch up. Reese was afraid that if he didn't go out whenever his boyfriend wanted, his boyfriend would leave him.

里斯周末的习惯是周五、周六和周日晚上和他新认识的男朋友去同志酒吧。到了周一早上，他就筋疲力尽了。他会用一周剩下的时间去追赶男友。里斯担心如果他不按男友的意愿出去，男友就会离开他。

Reese decided that for one weekend, he would put himself first and do what felt right to him. He told his boyfriend in advance. Reese decided that he would go out just one night, not drink, and be in by midnight. On Saturday, he made some plans to go to a movie with a couple of guys in the group. On Sunday he stayed at home to relax and get caught up on some house cleaning and laundry. His goal was to be in bed by 10:00 p.m. on Sunday evening.

里斯决定用一个周末，把自己放在第一位，做自己觉得合适的事情。他提前告诉了男朋友。里斯决定只出去玩一晚，不喝酒，午夜前回家。周六，他计划和几个朋友一起去看电影。周日，他待在家里放松，打扫房间，洗衣服。他的目标是周日晚上10点前上床睡觉。

When Monday came, Reese felt rested and clear headed at work. His boyfriend hadn't dropped him, and the rest of the week felt productive and enjoyable.

到了周一，里斯感觉自己休息好了，工作时头脑也清醒了。他的男朋友没有抛弃他，这周剩下的时间他感觉既充实又愉快。

Shane

肖恩

Shane, also introduced earlier in the chapter, liked to do things for his girlfriend. Shane regularly gave her gifts, planned surprises, and did whatever he could to help her out.

Shane，在本章前面也介绍过，他喜欢为女朋友做事。Shane经常送她礼物，策划惊喜，并竭尽所能地帮助她。

Shane's plan for putting himself first involved paying attention to when he had an impulse to do something for his girlfriend. Whenever he felt this impulse, he would instead do something for himself.

Shane 的“以自我为中心”计划包括留意自己何时有想为女友做点什么的冲动。每当他有这种冲动时，他就会选择为自己做点什么。

When he thought about washing her car, he washed his own instead. When he felt the urge to buy her a gift, he bought himself something instead. When he thought about calling her just to see if she was OK, he called a group member instead. All of this created tremendous anxiety for Shane.

当他想给她洗车时，却洗了自己的车。当他想给她买礼物时，却给自己买了个礼物。当他想给她打电话问问她是否安好时，却给小组成员打了电话。这一切都让肖恩感到无比焦虑。

Much to his surprise, at the end of the week Racquel reported she felt a lot less smothered by Shane and actually looked forward to spending time with him. She even called late one evening after the kids were in bed and invited him over to make love.

令他惊讶的是，周末拉奎尔说，她感觉肖恩不再那么压抑了，反而更期待和他在一起的时光。一天晚上，孩子们睡着后，她甚至打电话邀请肖恩过来做爱。

A couple of weeks later, Shane and Racquel talked about the change in a couples counseling session.

几周后，肖恩和拉奎尔在夫妻咨询会上谈到了这一变化。

They decided to continue the process. For a period of six months they agreed that Shane would not give any gifts or plan any surprises for Racquel. During the following six months he refrained from giving her birthday, Christmas, and Valentine's cards or gifts. During this time he focused on taking better care of himself and getting his needs met.

他们决定继续这个过程。他们同意在六个月内，肖恩不会给拉奎尔送任何礼物，也不会给她准备任何惊喜。在接下来的六个月里，他没有给她送生日、圣诞节和情人节的贺卡或礼物。这段时间里，他专注于更好地照顾自己，满足自己的需求。

In time Shane came to see that not only did Racquel not stop loving him, she actually became more giving to Shane. One year latter, they both reported that Shane could give a gift without using it as a way to get approval or affirmation. During this time, Shane had also learned that making his needs a priority made him less dependent, needy, and fearful. Both Shane and Racquel reported enjoying all the changes they had experienced since Shane made the decision to start putting himself first.

随着时间的推移，肖恩逐渐发现，拉奎尔不仅没有停止爱他，反而更加慷慨地给予肖恩。一年后，他们都表示，肖恩可以送礼物，而不是以此来获得认可或肯定。在这段时间里，肖恩也学会了，把自己的需求放在首位，能让他不再那么依赖、需要，也不再那么恐惧。肖恩和拉奎尔都表示，自从肖恩决定把自己放在第一位以来，他们所经历的所有变化都很享受。


Making The Decision

做出决定

Nice Guys have believed a myth that promises them that if they give up themselves and put others first, they will be loved and get their needs met. There is only one way to change this illogical, nonproductive Nice Guy paradigm — putting themselves first.

好男人一直相信一个迷思：只要他们放弃自我，把别人放在第一位，就能被爱，需求也能得到满足。要改变这种不合逻辑、毫无成效的好男人模式，只有一个办法——把自己放在第一位。

Making the decision to put the self first is the hardest part. Actually doing it is relatively easy. When the Nice Guy puts himself first there is only one voice to consider — his own. Decisions are now made by one individual, rather than by a committee. He no longer has to mind read, predict, or try to please multiple voices with conflicting agendas. When putting himself first all the information he needs to make a decision is within him: "Is this what I want? Yes. Then that's what I'll do."

做出以自我为先的决定是最难的，但实际操作起来却相对容易。当“好人”把自己放在第一位时，他只需要考虑一个声音——他自己的声音。现在，决策由一个人做出，而不是由一个委员会做出。他不再需要揣测、预测，也不再需要取悦多个彼此冲突的声音。当他把自己放在第一位时，他做决定所需的所有信息都来自内心：“这是我想要的吗？是的。那我就这么做。”



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #16

挣脱束缚活动 #16

Make a decision to put yourself first for a weekend or even a whole week. Tell the people around you what you are doing. Ask a friend to support you and encourage you in this process. Pay attention to your initial anxiety. Pay attention to your tendency to revert to old patterns. At the end of the time period, ask the people around you what it was like for them when you put yourself first.

决定用一个周末甚至一整周的时间把自己放在第一位。告诉周围的人你在做什么。请一位朋友在这个过程中支持和鼓励你。注意你最初的焦虑。注意你容易回到旧模式的倾向。在这段时间结束时，问问周围的人，当你把自己放在第一位时，他们感觉如何。

Remember, you don't have to do it perfectly. Just do it.

记住，你不必做得完美。只要去做就行了。

*****************************


Breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome involves taking responsibility for one's own needs. Others may cooperate with the Nice Guy, but they are not in charge of meeting his needs. By making their needs a priority and putting themselves first, recovering Nice Guys can come to see the world as a place of abundance. They can truly come to believe that their needs are important and there are people out there who are happy and willing to help them meet them.

摆脱“好人综合症”意味着要对自己的需求负责。其他人可能会与“好人”合作，但他们无权满足他的需求。通过优先考虑自己的需求并把自己放在首位，正在康复的“好人”会逐渐发现这个世界是一个富足的地方。他们会真正相信自己的需求很重要，并且有人乐意帮助他们满足这些需求。

# ↩

Chapter 5

# ↩

第五章

Reclaim Your Personal Power

重拾你的个人力量


One Saturday morning a few years back, my wife Elizabeth and I engaged in a heated discussion over something I had done. Like many of our arguments, Elizabeth felt helpless to get me to see my denial.

几年前的一个周六早上，我和妻子伊丽莎白就我做的一件事激烈地争吵起来。就像我们许多次争吵一样，伊丽莎白觉得没法让我接受我的否认。

At the same time, I felt unjustly persecuted. Finally, when the argument reached an emotional crescendo, Elizabeth shouted in frustration,

与此同时，我感到自己受到了不公正的迫害。最后，当争吵达到高潮时，伊丽莎白沮丧地喊道：

"You're nothing but a wimp!"

“你就是个懦夫！”

Elizabeth left the room and I retreated to the bathroom to dry my eyes.

伊丽莎白离开了房间，我回到浴室擦干眼睛。

After a few minutes of reflection, Elizabeth knocked on the bathroom door. I assumed she was coming back to take another stab at her wounded prey. Instead she apologized.

伊丽莎白沉思了几分钟后，敲了敲浴室的门。我以为她又回来刺伤她受伤的猎物了。结果她道了歉。

"I'm sorry for calling you a wimp. That wasn't fair."

“我很抱歉叫你懦夫。这不公平。”

"Actually," I responded, wiping a tear away, "It was the most accurate thing you said all morning."

“事实上，”我擦干眼泪回答道，“这是你整个上午说的最准确的话。”

Nice Guys are wimps. This may not sound like a nice thing to say, but it's true. Nice Guys tend to be wimpy victims because their life paradigm and childhood survival mechanisms require them to sacrifice their personal power.

好男人都是懦夫。这话听起来可能不太好听，但事实确实如此。好男人往往是懦弱的受害者，因为他们的生活模式和童年的生存机制要求他们牺牲个人权力。

As stated in previous chapters, a common denominator for Nice Guys is that they did not get their needs met in a timely, healthy fashion in childhood. These little boys were helpless to prevent people from abandoning them, neglecting them, abusing them, using them, or smothering them. They were victims to the people who failed to love them, pay attention to them, meet their needs, and protect them.

正如前几章所述，“好男人”的共同点在于，他们在童年时期未能得到及时、健康的满足。这些小男孩无力阻止人们抛弃他们、忽视他们、虐待他们、利用他们或扼杀他们。他们是那些不爱他们、不关注他们、不满足他们的需求、不保护他们的人的受害者。

As a result of these childhood experiences, feeling like a victim feels familiar for most Nice Guys. These men tend to see others as causing the problems they are experiencing in life. As a consequence, they often feel frustrated, helpless, resentful, and rageful. You can see it in their body language. You can hear it in their voices.

由于这些童年经历，大多数“好男人”都对受害者的感觉习以为常。这些男人往往认为生活中遇到的问题都是别人造成的。因此，他们常常感到沮丧、无助、怨恨和愤怒。你可以从他们的肢体语言中看出这一点，也可以从他们的声音中听出来。

"It's not fair."

“这不公平。”

"How come she gets to make the rules?"

“她怎么能制定规则呢？”

"I always give more than I get."

“我付出的总是比得到的多。”

"If she would just. . . ."

“如果她愿意……”


A Paradigm Of Powerlessness

无能为力的典范

In an attempt to cope with their childhood abandonment experiences, all Nice Guys developed the same paradigm: "If I am good, then I will be loved, get my needs met, and have a problem-free life."

为了应对童年被遗弃的经历，所有好男人都形成了同样的思维模式：“如果我表现好，我就会被爱，我的需求会得到满足，我的生活也不会有任何问题。”

Unfortunately, this paradigm not only produces the opposite of what is desired, it guarantees nothing but feelings of perpetual powerlessness.

不幸的是，这种模式不仅产生了与期望相反的结果，而且只会导致永久的无力感。

Even though Nice Guys are obsessed with trying to create a smooth, problem-free life, two major factors prevent them from attaining this goal. The first is that they are attempting the impossible. Life is not smooth. Human existence is by nature chaotic. Life is filled with experiences that are unpredictable and beyond anyone's control. Therefore, trying to create a predictable life in which everything always goes as planned is an exercise in futility.

尽管“好男人”们执着于创造一种平稳无忧的生活，但有两个主要因素阻碍了他们实现这一目标。首先，他们试图追求不可能的事情。生活并非一帆风顺。人类生存的本质是混乱的。生活中充满了不可预测、无人能掌控的经历。因此，试图创造一种一切按计划进行的可预测生活是徒劳的。

In spite of the fact that we live in a chaotic, unpredictable world, Nice Guys are not only convinced that life can be smooth, they believe it should be. This belief is the direct result of their childhood abandonment experiences. The unpredictability of not having their needs met in a timely, judicious fashion was not only frightening, it was potentially life-threatening.

尽管我们生活在一个混乱、不可预测的世界，好男人不仅坚信生活可以一帆风顺，而且相信生活就应该如此。这种信念直接源于他们童年被遗弃的经历。无法及时、明智地满足自身需求的不确定性不仅令人恐惧，甚至可能危及生命。

In attempt to cope with the uncertainty of their chaotic childhood, Nice Guys developed a belief system that if they could just do everything right, then everything would go right in their lives. Sometimes these men also developed belief systems that their childhood was ideal and problem-free (the opposite of reality) in order to cope with their abandonment experiences. These were all distorted beliefs, but these illusions helped these helpless little boys deal with the turmoil that was out of their control.

为了应对混乱童年带来的不确定性，好男人们形成了一套信念体系：只要他们能把每件事都做好，生活中的一切都会顺利。有时，为了应对被遗弃的经历，这些男人还会形成一种信念体系，认为他们的童年是理想的、没有问题的（这与现实截然相反）。这些都是扭曲的信念，但这些幻想却帮助这些无助的小男孩应对了他们无法控制的混乱。

A second reason Nice Guys never accomplish their goal of having a smooth life is that they do the opposite of what works. By approaching adult situations with survival mechanisms that were formed when they were naive and powerless, they are insured of having very little success in creating anything that resembles stability in their lives.

好男人永远无法实现平稳生活目标的第二个原因是，他们做的与行之有效的事情截然相反。他们用在幼稚无力时形成的生存机制来应对成年人的处境，这注定了他们很难在生活中创造任何类似稳定的东西。

The dependence on these ineffective survival mechanisms keeps Nice Guys trapped in the memory of their fearful childhood experiences and perpetuates a vicious cycle. The more frightened they are, the more they use their childhood survival mechanisms. The more they use these ineffective mechanisms, the less successful they are at negotiating the complexities, challenges and ambiguities of life. The less successful they are, the more fearful they become . . . you get the picture.

对这些无效生存机制的依赖，让“好人”们深陷童年恐惧经历的记忆，并陷入恶性循环。他们越害怕，就越会动用童年的生存机制。他们越是使用这些无效的机制，就越难以应对生活中的复杂性、挑战性和不确定性。他们越是难以应对，就越是恐惧……你懂的。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #17

挣脱束缚活动#17

Look over the following list of ways Nice Guys try to create a smooth, problem-free life. Write down an example of how you used each coping mechanism in childhood. Then, next to each, give an example of how you use this strategy to try to control your world in adulthood. Note how each of these behaviors keeps you feeling like a powerless victim. Share this information with a safe person.

看看下面列出的“好男人”们试图创造顺利无忧生活的方式。写下你在童年时期是如何运用每种应对机制的例子。然后在每个策略旁边，举例说明你成年后是如何运用这些策略试图掌控自己的世界的。注意这些行为是如何让你感觉自己像个无助的受害者的。请与一位值得信赖的人分享这些信息。

  • Doing it right.
    正确行事。
  • Playing it safe.
    稳扎稳打。
  • Anticipating and fixing.
    预测并解决问题。
  • Trying not to rock the boat.
    尽量不要惹是生非。
  • Being charming and helpful.
    魅力十足且乐于助人。
  • Never being a moment's problem.
    从不惹是生非。
  • Using covert contracts.
    使用隐性合同。
  • Controlling and manipulating.
    控制和操纵。
  • Caretaking and pleasing.
    照顾和取悦。
  • Withholding information.
    隐瞒信息。
  • Repressing feelings.
    压抑感情。
  • Making sure other people don't have feelings.
    确保其他人没有感情。
  • Avoiding problems and difficult situations.
    避免问题和困难情况。

*****************************


Overcoming The Wimp Factor — Reclaiming Personal Power

克服懦弱因素——重拾个人力量

I define personal power as a state of mind in which a person is confident he can handle whatever may come. This kind of power not only successfully deals with problems, challenges and adversity, it actually welcomes them, meets them head on, and is thankful for them. Personal power isn't the absence of fear. Even the most powerful people have fear. Personal power is the result of feeling fear, but not giving in to the fear.

我将个人力量定义为一种心态，在这种心态下，一个人自信能够应对任何可能发生的情况。这种力量不仅能成功地应对问题、挑战和逆境，它还能真正地欢迎它们，迎难而上，并心怀感激。个人力量并非没有恐惧。即使是最强大的人也会感到恐惧。个人力量源于感受恐惧，但不屈服于恐惧。

There is a solution to the helplessness and vulnerability Nice Guys feel. Recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome allows Nice Guys to embrace the personal power that is their birthright. Reclaiming personal power includes:

好男人的无助感和脆弱感是有解决办法的。摆脱“好男人综合症”能让好男人拥抱与生俱来的个人力量。重拾个人力量包括：

  • Surrendering.
    屈服。
  • Dwelling in reality.
    沉浸于现实。
  • Expressing feelings.
    表达感受。
  • Facing fears.
    面对恐惧。
  • Developing integrity.
    培养正直。
  • Setting boundaries.
    设定界限。


Surrendering Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

投降帮助好人重拾个人权力

Ironically, the most important aspect of reclaiming personal power and getting what one wants in love and life is surrender. Surrender doesn't mean giving up, it means letting go of what one can't change and changing what one can.

讽刺的是，在爱情和生活中，重拾个人力量、获得自己想要的一切，最重要的一点就是臣服。臣服并不意味着放弃，而是意味着放下自己无法改变的，去改变自己可以改变的。

Letting go doesn't mean not caring or not trying. Letting go means letting be. It is like opening up a tightly clenched fist and releasing the tension stored inside. At first the fingers will want to return to their former clenched position. The hand almost has to be retrained to open up and relax. So it is with learning how to surrender and let go.

放手并不意味着不在乎或不去尝试。放手意味着顺其自然。这就像张开紧握的拳头，释放内心的紧张。起初，手指会想要回到之前紧握的位置。手几乎需要重新训练才能打开和放松。学习如何臣服和放手也是如此。

Surrender allows recovering Nice Guys to let go and respond to life's complex beauty, rather than trying to control it. Surrender allows these men to see life as a laboratory for learning, growth, and creativity. Surrender allows recovering Nice Guys to see each life experience as a "gift" from the universe to stimulate growth, healing and learning. Instead of asking, "Why is this happening to me?" the recovering Nice Guy can respond to life's challenges by pondering, "What do I need to learn from this situation?"

臣服让正在康复中的“好男人”能够放下过去，去感受生活复杂而美丽的一面，而不是试图控制它。臣服让这些男人将生活视为学习、成长和创造的实验室。臣服让正在康复中的“好男人”将每一次人生经历都视为宇宙赐予的“礼物”，以促进成长、疗愈和学习。与其问“为什么会发生在我身上？”，正在康复中的“好男人”可以通过思考“我需要从这种情况中学到什么？”来应对生活中的挑战。

Gil exemplifies the process of letting go. Gil had reached a crisis point in his relationship with his girlfriend Barb. Gil had originally begun couples counseling with Barb to "fix" her problem. He claimed that she was depressed, angry all the time, and had no interest in sex. He reported that he constantly walked on eggshells, trying to make sure he never did anything to upset her.

吉尔就是“放手”的典范。吉尔和女友芭芭拉的关系一度陷入危机。吉尔最初开始为芭芭拉做夫妻咨询，是为了“解决”她的问题。他声称芭芭拉情绪低落，总是生气，对性生活毫无兴趣。他说，他总是小心翼翼，努力确保自己不会做任何让她难过的事情。

Gil and Barb were both in their early fifties and had been living together for eight years. They had discussed marriage, but both felt apprehension due to the unsettled nature of their relationship. After a couple months of couples counseling, Gil began to entertain the idea that all the problems in the relationship might not be about Barb. He began looking at his own caretaking and controlling behavior.

吉尔和芭芭拉都五十出头，已经同居八年了。他们曾讨论过结婚，但由于关系不稳定，两人都感到担忧。经过几个月的夫妻咨询，吉尔开始意识到，这段关系中的所有问题可能并非都与芭芭拉有关。他开始反思自己的照顾和控制行为。

He also became aware that he had few outside interests and no male friends. After a couple more months, he joined a No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's group.

他也意识到自己几乎没有什么业余爱好，也没有男性朋友。几个月后，他加入了“不再是好人！”（No More Mr. Nice Guy!）男子团体。

Even as Gil began to look at his own problems and ineffective life patterns, he kept seeking the "key" for making Barb better. It was a slow process, but Gil began to see that he could not do anything to change Barb and that he was going to have to focus on himself. As he began to let go and detach from Barb he felt tremendous anxiety. He had a deep fear that he was going to "get in trouble." He also believed that Barb couldn't handle her problems without his help.

即使吉尔开始审视自己的问题和无效的生活方式，他也一直在寻找让芭芭拉变得更好的“钥匙”。这是一个缓慢的过程，但吉尔开始意识到，他无法改变芭芭拉，他必须专注于自己。当他开始放手，不再依赖芭芭拉时，他感到无比焦虑。他深深地害怕自己会“惹上麻烦”。他还相信，如果没有他的帮助，芭芭拉无法解决自己的问题。

With the support of the group, Gil surrendered. He came to realize that he would be OK regardless of whether he and Barb made it as a couple. Much to his surprise, their relationship began to improve. As he let go of trying to solve her problems and detached from her moods, Gil found that he had fewer frustrations and resentments. He even began to see Barb as a "gift" to help him work through his issues with his angry father.

在小组的支持下，吉尔放下了一切。他意识到，无论他和芭芭拉能否成为情侣，他都会安然无恙。令他惊讶的是，他们的关系开始改善。当他不再试图解决芭芭拉的问题，不再受她情绪的影响时，吉尔发现自己的沮丧和怨恨减少了。他甚至开始把芭芭拉视为一份“礼物”，帮助他解决与愤怒的父亲之间的问题。

A year later he announced to his men's group that he and Barb had set a date to get married. He reported that they were getting along better than he would have ever imagined. He shared that the turning point seemed to be when he made the decision that he didn't care whether they made it together or not. That decision represented a conscious letting go of trying to control something that was clearly not in his control. Ironically, he shared that the process of letting go allowed him to receive what he really wanted.

一年后，他向他的男士小组宣布，他和芭芭拉已经定下了结婚日期。他说，他们相处得比他想象的还要好。他说，人生的转折点似乎就在他做出决定，不再在乎他们是否在一起的时候。这个决定代表着他有意识地放下了试图控制那些显然无法掌控的事情。讽刺的是，他分享说，放下的过程让他得到了真正想要的东西。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #18

挣脱束缚活动#18

Think about one "gift" from the universe which you initially resisted but can now be seen as a positive stimulus for growth or discovery.

想一想宇宙给予你的一份“礼物”，你最初会抗拒它，但现在它可以被视为成长或发现的积极刺激。

Are there any similar gifts in your life right now to which you need to surrender?

您现在的生活中是否存在类似的需要您臣服的礼物？

Share this information with a safe person.

与安全的人分享此信息。

*****************************


Dwelling In Reality Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

活在现实中帮助好人重拾个人权力

Nice Guys try to control their world by creating belief systems about people and situations that are not based in reality. They then act as if these beliefs are accurate. This is why their behavior often seems illogical to outside observers.

好人试图通过构建一些不基于现实的关于人和事的信念体系来控制他们的世界。然后，他们假装这些信念是正确的。这就是为什么他们的行为在外人看来常常不合逻辑。

Les, an unassuming man in his late thirties, had a brief affair with a co-worker. During his initial therapy session I asked Les why he thought he had an affair. "I don't know," he replied, "I guess I just wanted some attention."

莱斯，一位谦逊的三十多岁男士，曾与一位同事有过一段短暂的恋情。在他最初的心理咨询过程中，我问莱斯他为什么认为自己有外遇。“我不知道，”他回答说，“我猜我只是想引起注意。”

I continued by asking him how he expressed his anger toward his wife. With a puzzled look, he responded, "I never get angry at Sarah."

我继续问他如何向妻子表达愤怒。他一脸疑惑地回答：“我从不生莎拉的气。”

"You mean you two have been married for 10 years and she's never done anything to piss you off?" I asked in mock surprise.

“你是说你们结婚十年了，她从来没有做过让你生气的事？”我假装惊讶地问道。

To listen to Les talk about his wife, it was evident he had her on a pedestal. It was as equally clear that he was not dwelling in reality when it came to his marriage. When I asked specific questions about his wife, Les revealed how Sarah had gained 60 pounds since they married, refused to cook, was depressed, no longer wanted to have sex with him, treated him with contempt, and would rage at him without provocation. In spite of all these things, Les maintained that his wife was the woman of his dreams and that he loved her dearly.

听莱斯谈论他的妻子，显然他把她奉若神明。同样明显的是，他在婚姻问题上也并不现实。当我问起他妻子的具体情况时，莱斯透露，莎拉婚后胖了60磅，拒绝做饭，情绪低落，不再想和他发生性关系，轻蔑地对待他，还会无缘无故地对他发脾气。尽管如此，莱斯仍然坚称他的妻子是他梦寐以求的女人，他深爱着她。

Throughout the next few months of therapy, I consistently held up a mirror of reality to Les in regard to his wife and his relationship with her. This was a slow and difficult process. Les needed to see Sarah in a certain way because of his fear of being alone. To dwell in reality might mean he would have to do something frightening or difficult.

在接下来几个月的治疗中，我不断地向莱斯展现现实，让他看到他妻子以及他与妻子的关系。这是一个缓慢而艰难的过程。莱斯需要以某种方式看待莎拉，因为他害怕孤独。沉溺于现实，可能意味着他不得不做一些令人恐惧或困难的事情。

As Les began to face his fears of abandonment he also began to see his wife more accurately. This change allowed him to start asking for what he wanted, set boundaries, and express his feelings of resentment and anger. It soon became apparent that Sarah had no desire to look at her role in the relationship or make any kind of changes. Though it was painful and frightening, accepting things as they really were allowed Les to make the decision to move out and file for divorce.

当莱斯开始直面被抛弃的恐惧时，他也开始更准确地看待自己的妻子。这种改变让他开始主动提出自己的需求，设定界限，并表达自己的怨恨和愤怒。很快，他发现莎拉根本不想审视自己在这段关系中的角色，也不想做出任何改变。尽管这让他感到痛苦和恐惧，但接受现实让莱斯最终做出了搬出去并提出离婚的决定。

Dwelling in reality allowed Les to look at why he had created the kind of system he had with Sarah. It put him in a position to make difficult but realistic decisions. It allowed him to access the inner power he needed to make significant changes in his life. It also opened the door for him to find someone who was available to help him create the kind of relationship he wanted.

沉浸于现实让莱斯得以反思自己为何与莎拉建立了这样的关系。这让他能够做出艰难却现实的决定。这让他能够获得改变人生所需的内在力量。这也让他有机会找到一个愿意帮助他建立理想关系的人。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #19

挣脱束缚活动#19

Pick one area in your life in which you routinely feel frustrated or out of control. Step back from the situation. Is the difficulty you are having with the situation the result of you trying to project the reality you want to believe onto it? If you had to accept the reality of this situation, how might you change your response to it?

选出你生活中经常感到沮丧或失控的一个方面。暂时放下它。你现在遇到的困难，是不是因为你试图将自己想要相信的现实投射到它身上？如果你必须接受这个现实，你会如何改变自己的应对方式？

*****************************


Expressing Feelings Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

表达感受帮助好男人重拾个人权力

Nice Guys are terrified of two kinds of feelings — their own and everyone else's. Any kind of intensity causes Nice Guys to feel out of control. As children, feeling things intensely invited either negative attention or no attention at all. Therefore, it came to feel safer to clamp a lid down tightly on any emotion that might attract too much negative attention or might cause them to feel abandoned.

好男人害怕两种情绪——他们自己的和其他人。任何强烈的情绪都会让他们感到失控。孩童时期，强烈的情绪要么招致负面关注，要么根本不受关注。因此，他们觉得更安全的做法是，紧紧抑制任何可能引起过多负面关注或让他们感到被抛弃的情绪。

I remember early in our marriage when Elizabeth would express her frustration over my inability to share what I was feeling. Like most Nice Guys, I had come to see feelings as a dangerous thing. After 30 plus years of conditioning, I had no clue what Elizabeth wanted from me.

我记得我们刚结婚的时候，伊丽莎白会因为我无法倾诉自己的感受而感到沮丧。像大多数“好男人”一样，我渐渐把感情看作危险的东西。经过30多年的磨练，我完全不知道伊丽莎白想从我这里得到什么。

Even as I began to become aware of my feelings, I often kept them to myself. It is almost comical how infrequently it crosses a Nice Guy's mind to tell his partner what he is feeling. On one occasion Elizabeth confronted me when I shared a feeling with her that I had been harboring for some time. "Why didn't you tell me about that when you first felt it?" she questioned.

即使我开始意识到自己的感受，我也常常将它们藏在心里。一个好男人竟然很少会想到要告诉他的伴侣自己的感受，这简直滑稽可笑。有一次，当我向伊丽莎白倾诉我内心深处的某种感受时，她质问我：“你第一次感受到这种感觉时，为什么不告诉我？”

"I'm doing better," I replied in typical Nice Guy fashion. "It only took me two weeks to get around to telling you."

“我感觉好多了，”我以典型的好男人风格回答道。“我只花了两周时间就来告诉你了。”

I frequently hear Nice Guys rationalize the withholding of their feelings by claiming they don't want to hurt anyone. The truth is they are covering their own butts. What they are really saying is that they don't want to do anything that might recreate their childhood experiences. They're really not trying to protect anyone from harm, they're just trying to keep their world smooth and under control.

我经常听到好男人为了掩饰自己的感情而辩解，声称他们不想伤害任何人。事实上，他们只是在掩饰自己的感情。他们真正的意思是，他们不想做任何可能重蹈童年覆辙的事情。他们并非真的想保护任何人免受伤害，只是想让自己的世界保持平稳、可控。

I frequently tell Nice Guys, "Your feelings are just feelings, they won't kill you." Regardless of whether a Nice Guy is feeling anxious, helpless, shameful, lonely, rageful, or sad, his feelings aren't life-threatening.

我经常对好男人说：“你的感觉只是感觉，它们不会杀死你。”无论好男人感到焦虑、无助、羞愧、孤独、愤怒还是悲伤，他的感觉都不会危及生命。

The goal of teaching Nice Guys to embrace their feelings is not to make them soft and "touchy-feely." Men who are in touch with their feelings are powerful, assertive, and energized. Contrary to what many Nice Guys believe, they don't have to become more like women in order to have their feelings. This is why I support men in learning about their feelings from other men.

教导好男人拥抱自己的情感，目的并非让他们变得软弱和“感情用事”。能够体察自己情感的男人强大、自信、充满活力。与许多好男人的想法相反，他们不必为了拥有自己的情感而变得更像女人。这就是为什么我支持男人向其他男人学习了解自己的情感。

While there is no formula, or "right" way to get reconnected with repressed feelings, support groups can teach, model, and support this slow, but important process. In a sense, a therapy group can become like a family. In this environment, recovering Nice Guys can ask for the kind of help in dealing with feelings that was never available to them as children. Since feelings are often messy, a group environment can represent a supportive place to momentarily feel out of control. Here, recovering Nice Guys find out they won't fall overboard and drown if they rock the boat. They also learn that they won't shrivel up and die if someone else around them has a feeling.

虽然没有公式或“正确”的方法来帮助人们重新连接被压抑的情绪，但支持小组可以教导、示范和支持这个缓慢但重要的过程。从某种意义上说，治疗小组可以成为一个家庭。在这样的环境中，正在康复的“好人”可以寻求童年时期从未得到过的帮助来处理情绪。由于情绪通常很混乱，小组环境可以成为一个支持性的场所，让他们暂时感到失控。在这里，正在康复的“好人”会发现，即使他们打破常规，也不会落水溺亡。他们也明白，即使周围的人有这种感觉，他们也不会因此而萎靡不振。

Feelings are an integral part of human existence. By learning the language of feelings, recovering Nice Guys can begin to let go of a lifetime of unnecessary baggage. As they do, they experience a newfound energy, optimism, intimacy, and zest for life.

情感是人类生存不可或缺的一部分。通过学习情感的语言，正在康复中的“好男人”可以开始放下一生中不必要的包袱。与此同时，他们会体验到全新的活力、乐观、亲密感以及对生活的热情。

This reality hit home with me a few years ago in a very unexpected way. Elizabeth came to me one day and revealed that she had backed into a parked car. She felt like a bad little child and waited for me to scold her. Even before I had a chance to respond, she began to put up a wall and withdraw as a way of protecting herself.

几年前，我突然意识到了这一点。有一天，伊丽莎白来找我，说她倒车撞到了一辆停着的车。她觉得自己像个坏孩子，等着我训斥她。甚至在我还没来得及回应之前，她就开始筑起高墙，试图躲避我，以此来保护自己。

I got angry — not about the car, but about the manner in which she was pushing me away. I expressed my feelings clearly and directly. Without shaming or attacking I said "Stop." With an intensity of emotion that surprised both of us, I let her know that I wasn't pushing her away and I wasn't going to accept her pushing me away. I told her that I did have feelings about the car, but I had even stronger feelings about how she was acting. I said, "Just let me have my feelings about the car, and then we'll work it out."

我生气了——不是因为车，而是因为她推开我的方式。我清晰直接地表达了我的感受。我没有羞辱她，也没有攻击她，而是说：“别再说了。”我以一种令我们俩都感到惊讶的强烈情感，让她知道我没有推开她，也不会接受她推开我。我告诉她，我对车确实有感觉，但我对她的行为有更强烈的感受。我说：“让我表达我对车的感受，然后我们再解决。”

Later, Elizabeth revealed to me (and several of her friends) how much safer she felt when I had my feelings. She was able to hear that I was upset about the car but that I didn't think she was bad and I wasn't going to abandon her. The fact that I had such intensity about not letting her push me away actually made her feel secure and loved. As a result, she felt safe to stay connected to me and hear my feelings about the car. The whole incident brought us closer and has since provided a reference point for the healing power of expressing emotions in powerful and direct ways.

后来，伊丽莎白向我（以及她的几个朋友）透露，当我表达自己的感受时，她感到更加安全。她能听出我对那辆车感到难过，但我并不认为她不好，也不会抛弃她。我如此坚决地不让她把我推开，这实际上让她感到了安全和被爱。因此，她感到安心，可以与我保持联系，倾听我对那辆车的感受。整个事件让我们更加亲近，也让我们感受到了以有力而直接的方式表达情绪的治愈力量。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #20

挣脱束缚活动 #20

Some guidelines about expressing feelings.

一些关于表达感受的指导方针。

  • Don't focus on the other person, "You are making me mad."
    不要把注意力集中在别人身上，“你让我生气了。”

Instead, take responsibility for what you are feeling: "I am feeling angry."

相反，要对自己的感受负责：“我感到很生气。”

  • Don't use feeling words to describe what you are thinking, as in "I feel like Joe was trying to take
    不要用感觉词来描述你的想法，比如“我觉得乔试图 advantage of me."
    “对我有利。”

Instead, pay attention to what you are experiencing in your body: "I'm feeling helpless and frightened."

相反，要注意你身体的感受：“我感到无助和害怕。”

  • In general, try to begin feeling statements with "I", rather than "you."
    一般来说，尝试用“我”而不是“你”来开始表达感受。

Try to avoid the crutch of saying "I feel like ." As in "I feel like you are being mean to me."

尽量避免说“我觉得”。例如“我觉得你对我很刻薄”。

*****************************


Facing Fears Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

直面恐惧帮助好人重拾个人力量

Fear is a normal part of human experience. Everyone experiences fear, even those people who seem to be fearless. Healthy fear is a warning sign that danger may be approaching. This is different from the fear Nice Guys experience on a daily basis.

恐惧是人类体验的正常组成部分。每个人都会经历恐惧，即使是那些看似无所畏惧的人。健康的恐惧是危险即将来临的警示信号。这与“好人”们日常经历的恐惧截然不同。

For Nice Guys, fear is recorded at the cellular level. It is a memory of every seemingly life-threatening experience they ever had. It was born of a time of absolute dependency and helplessness. It originated in not having their needs met in a timely, judicious manner. It was fostered by fearful systems that discouraged risk and rewarded conservatism. It was heightened by the reality that life is messy and chaotic and any kind of change promises a journey into the unknown. I call this kind of fear, Memory Fear.

对“好人”来说，恐惧记录在细胞层面。它是他们经历过的每一次看似危及生命的经历的记忆。它源于他们完全依赖和无助的时期。它源于他们的需求未能得到及时、明智的满足。它被那些不鼓励冒险、奖励保守主义的令人恐惧的制度所滋生。生活混乱无序，任何改变都预示着通往未知的旅程，这一现实加剧了这种恐惧。我把这种恐惧称为“记忆恐惧”。

Because of the memory fear created in childhood, Nice Guys still approach the world as if it is dangerous and overpowering. To cope with these realities, Nice Guys typically hunker down and play it safe.

由于童年时期记忆中的恐惧，好男人仍然视世界为危险且难以掌控。为了应对这些现实，好男人通常会选择隐居，谨慎行事。

As a consequence of playing it safe, Nice Guys experience a lot of needless suffering.

由于谨慎行事，好人遭受了很多不必要的痛苦。

Suffering because they avoid new situations.

由于逃避新情况而遭受痛苦。

Suffering because they stay with the familiar.

因为他们呆在熟悉的地方，所以很痛苦。

Suffering because they procrastinate, avoid, and fail to finish what they start. Suffering because they make a bad situation worse by doing more of what has never worked in the past.

他们痛苦，是因为他们拖延、逃避，最终无法完成他们开始的事情。他们痛苦，是因为他们做了更多过去从未成功过的事情，让本来就糟糕的情况变得更糟。

Suffering because they expend so much energy trying to control the uncontrollable.

他们之所以痛苦，是因为他们耗费了太多精力试图控制无法控制的事情。

Nolan is a good example of the paralyzing effect of memory fear. Nolan came to see me on the recommendation of a friend. He had been separated from his wife for a year but was having a difficult time making a final decision about getting divorced.

诺兰就是记忆恐惧麻痹效应的典型例子。诺兰在朋友的推荐下来见我。他和妻子分居一年了，但一直难以最终决定是否离婚。

Nolan frequently told me he was "confused." This confusion was mixed with a strong dose of guilt.

诺兰经常告诉我他感到“困惑”。这种困惑还夹杂着强烈的愧疚感。

Nolan constantly weighed all the issues. What if he left his wife and later realized that it was a mistake?

诺兰不断权衡各种问题。如果他离开妻子，后来才意识到这是一个错误，该怎么办？

What if he messed up his kids' lives? What if his children never wanted to talk to him again? What if his friends thought he was bad? What if God sent him to hell? As long as Nolan stayed "confused" about what he should do, he remained paralyzed.

如果他把孩子们的生活搞砸了怎么办？如果他的孩子们再也不想跟他说话怎么办？如果他的朋友们觉得他不好怎么办？如果上帝把他打入地狱怎么办？只要诺兰对自己该做什么感到“困惑”，他就无法行动。

When I told Nolan that I didn't think he was confused but that he was afraid, he was initially defensive.

当我告诉诺兰，我认为他不是感到困惑，而是感到害怕时，他最初是处于防御状态。

He didn't like seeing himself as being fearful. As we explored the memory fear from his childhood, he came to realize that any mistakes he made as a child seemed to have everlasting consequences. He believed the same would be true in his present situation.

他不喜欢看到自己感到恐惧。当我们探究他童年时期的记忆恐惧时，他开始意识到，童年时犯下的任何错误似乎都会带来永久的后果。他相信，在目前的处境中，情况也是如此。

Behind Nolan's fear of making a decision was the childhood fear that he wouldn't be able to handle whatever happened. Together we brainstormed all the possible consequences of divorcing his wife.

诺兰害怕做决定的背后，是童年时期的恐惧，害怕自己无法应对任何可能发生的情况。我们一起思考了与他妻子离婚可能带来的所有后果。

Behind each potential consequence was the unconscious belief that he wouldn't be able to handle it.

每一个潜在后果的背后都潜意识地相信他无法处理它。

I sent Nolan home with his list of fears along with a more accurate statement about each: No matter what happened, he would handle it. The following week, Nolan proudly announced that he had contacted an attorney. Even though he felt tremendous fear and anxiety, he found courage in repeating his new found mantra: "I can handle it."

我把诺兰列好的恐惧清单和对每件事更准确的描述送回家：无论发生什么，他都会处理好。接下来的一周，诺兰自豪地宣布他已经联系了律师。尽管他感到无比的恐惧和焦虑，但他还是鼓起勇气，重复着他新发现的口头禅：“我能处理好。”

Facing present day fears is the only way to overcome memory fear. Every time the Nice Guy confronts a fear, he unconsciously creates a belief that he can handle whatever it is he is afraid of. This challenges his memory fear. Challenging this memory fear makes the things outside of him seem less threatening.

直面当下的恐惧是克服记忆恐惧的唯一方法。每当“好人”面对恐惧时，他都会无意识地建立起一种信念，相信自己能够应对任何令他恐惧的事情。这挑战了他的记忆恐惧。挑战这种记忆恐惧，会让外界的威胁感减弱。

As these things seem less frightening, he feels more confident in confronting them. The more this confidence grows, the less threatening life seems.

当这些事情不再那么可怕时，他更有信心去面对它们。这种信心越强，生活就越没有威胁。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #21

挣脱束缚活动 #21

List one fear that has been controlling your life. Once you decide to confront the fear, begin repeating to yourself, "I can handle it. No matter what happens, I will handle it." Keep repeating this mantra until you take action and stop feeling fear.

列出一个一直控制着你生活的恐惧。一旦你决定直面恐惧，就开始对自己重复：“我能应付。无论发生什么，我都能应对。” 不断重复这句咒语，直到你采取行动，不再感到恐惧。

*****************************


Developing Integrity Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

培养正直品格有助于好人重拾个人权力

Most Nice Guys pride themselves on being honest and trustworthy. In reality, Nice Guys are fundamentally dishonest. They have the ability to tell a lie or withhold the truth and still believe the illusion that they are basically honest people. Since dishonesty is a fear-based behavior, telling lies and withholding the truth robs Nice Guys of their personal power.

大多数好人都以诚实守信为荣。但实际上，好人从根本上来说并不诚实。他们有能力撒谎或隐瞒真相，却仍然相信自己本质上是诚实的人。由于不诚实是一种基于恐惧的行为，因此撒谎和隐瞒真相会剥夺好人的个人权力。

I define lying as anything less than the truth. This may seem evident to most people, but it is important to define "lying," and "telling the truth," because Nice Guys are adept at creating definitions that justify their behavior. It is not unusual to hear them make statements like "I'm pretty honest" or "I'm honest most of the time" without the slightest awareness of their contradiction of terms. In almost a childlike manner, Nice Guys will often offer the following defense: "I didn't lie, I just didn't tell everything."

我把撒谎定义为任何不实之词。这对大多数人来说似乎显而易见，但定义“撒谎”和“说实话”至关重要，因为好男人擅长为自己的行为创造定义。我们常常听到他们说“我很诚实”或“我大多数时候都很诚实”，却丝毫没有意识到自己自相矛盾。好男人常常会以近乎孩子般的方式为自己辩护：“我没有撒谎，我只是没有把所有事情都说出来。”

Joel was the owner of a successful construction company. On occasion, he would leave work a little early and catch an afternoon movie before heading home. Because he feared his wife's disapproval, he would refrain from telling her how he spent those afternoons. He would always have some cover story ready in case she tried to call him while he was out. The irony of the situation is that there was absolutely no reason for Joel to lie to his wife. In spite of all the effort Joel put into hiding the truth of his whereabouts, it never crossed his mind that he was lying to himself and to his wife. The bottom line was that Joel's lying perpetuated a fear-based relationship with his wife and robbed him of his personal power.

乔尔是一家成功的建筑公司的老板。有时，他会早点下班，看完下午的电影再回家。因为害怕妻子不高兴，他总是隐瞒自己下午是如何度过的。他总是准备好一些掩饰的故事，以防妻子趁他外出时打电话给他。讽刺的是，乔尔根本没有理由对妻子撒谎。尽管乔尔竭尽全力隐瞒自己的真实行踪，但他从未想过自己是在欺骗自己，也在欺骗妻子。归根结底，乔尔的谎言加剧了他与妻子之间基于恐惧的关系，并剥夺了他的个人权力。

When Nice Guys are learning to tell the truth I encourage them to pay attention to the things they least want others to know, what they least want to reveal. These are the things they are most likely to hold back — and the things they most need to tell. Sometimes they have to practice telling a certain truth several times until all of these pieces of information get told.

当好男人学习说真话时，我鼓励他们关注那些他们最不想让别人知道、最不想透露的事情。这些是他们最有可能隐瞒的事情，也是他们最需要说出来的事情。有时，他们必须反复练习说出某个真相，直到把所有这些信息都说出来。

Sometimes after telling the truth, Nice Guys will report that it was a "mistake" because someone reacted with anger. Telling the truth is not a magic formula for having a smooth life. But living a life of integrity is actually easier than living one built around deceit and distortion.

有时，好人说了真话之后，会因为对方愤怒的反应而说这是个“错误”。说真话并非让生活顺利的灵丹妙药。但诚实的生活实际上比充满欺骗和扭曲的生活更容易。

Developing integrity is an essential part of recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome.

培养正直的品格是摆脱“好人综合症”的一个重要部分。

My definition of integrity is "deciding what feels right and doing it."

我对正直的定义是“决定什么是正确的，并去做”。

The alternative is using the "committee approach." This method of decision-making and acting is based on trying to guess what everyone else would think is right. Following this committee approach is the quickest path to confusion, fear, powerlessness, and dishonesty.

另一种选择是采用“委员会模式”。这种决策和行动方式建立在试图猜测其他人认为什么是正确的基础之上。这种委员会模式最容易导致混乱、恐惧、无力和不诚实。

When applying the definition above, there are two ways to be out of integrity, but only one way to be in it. When a Nice Guy never even bothers to ask himself, "What do I think is right?" or uses the committee method, he will always be out of integrity. If he asks himself what he believes is right but doesn't do it, he is also out of integrity. Only by asking himself what he believes is right and then doing it does he become a man of integrity.

运用上述定义，不正直有两种方式，但正直只有一种方式。如果一个好人从来不问自己“我认为什么是对的？”，或者不采用委员会的方法，他就永远不正直。如果他问自己什么是对的，但却不去做，他同样不正直。只有问自己什么是对的，然后去做，他才能成为一个正直的人。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #22

挣脱束缚活动 #22

Choose one area in which you have been out of integrity. Identify your fear that keeps you from telling the truth or doing the right thing. Reveal this situation to a safe person. Then go and tell the truth or do what you have to do to make the situation right. Tell yourself you can handle it. Since telling the truth may create a crisis for you or others, have faith that everyone involved will survive this crisis.

选择一个你曾经失信的领域。找出阻碍你说出真相或做正确事情的恐惧。把情况告诉一个安全的人。然后去说真话，或者做你必须做的事情来纠正这种情况。告诉自己你能处理好。既然说真话可能会给你或他人带来危机，那就相信所有相关人员都能安然度过这场危机。

*****************************


Setting Boundaries Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Personal Power

设定界限有助于好人重拾个人权力

Boundaries are essential for survival. Learning to set boundaries allows Nice Guys to stop feeling like helpless victims and reclaim their personal power. Boundary setting is one of the most fundamental skills I teach to recovering Nice Guys.

界限对于生存至关重要。学会设定界限可以让好男人不再感到无助，并重拾个人力量。设定界限是我教给好男人恢复自我的最基本技能之一。

I demonstrate the concept of boundaries by laying a shoestring on the ground. I tell the Nice Guy that I am going to cross his boundary and push him backwards. I instruct him to stop me when he begins to feel uncomfortable. It is not unusual for a Nice Guy to stand well back from the line, allowing me to violate his space several steps before he even begins to respond. Once I start pushing, it's not uncommon for a Nice Guy to let me push him back several steps before he does anything to stop me. Sometimes a Nice Guy will let me push him all the way to the wall.

我通过在地上放一根鞋带来演示界限的概念。我告诉好男人，我要越过他的界限，然后把他往后推。我指示他，当他开始感到不舒服时，就阻止我。好男人通常会站在离界线很远的地方，让我在他开始反应之前侵犯他的空间几步之遥。一旦我开始推他，好男人通常会让我把他往后推几步，然后才出手阻止我。有时，好男人甚至会让我把他一直推到墙边。

I use this exercise as a graphic demonstration of the need for boundaries in all areas of life. Nice Guys are usually more comfortable back-pedaling, giving in, and keeping the peace. They believe if they take one more step backward, the other person will quit pushing, and then everything will be smooth.

我用这个练习来生动地说明在生活的各个方面都需要设定界限。好男人通常更乐于后退、妥协，并保持和平。他们相信，只要自己再退一步，对方就会停止逼迫，然后一切都会顺利。

It is not unusual for recovering Nice Guys to go a little overboard when they first learn about boundary setting. They have a tendency to swing from one extreme to another. They become Kamikaze boundary setters. They try to set boundaries with a sledge hammer or machete. They usually learn in time that they only have to use as much resistance as necessary to get the job done.

正在康复中的“好男人”在刚开始学习设定界限时，有些过火是很正常的。他们容易从一个极端走向另一个极端。他们会像神风特攻队一样设定界限。他们试图用大锤或砍刀来设定界限。但通常他们最终会明白，只要能达到目的，就只需要施加必要的阻力。

In time, they also learn that boundary setting isn't about getting other people to be different, but about getting themselves to be different. If someone is crossing their boundary, it isn't the other person's problem, it is theirs.

随着时间的推移，他们也会明白，设定界限并非为了让别人变得不同，而是为了让自己变得不同。如果有人越界了，那不是别人的问题，而是他们自己的问题。

Because of memory fear, Nice Guys often unconsciously reinforce the very behaviors they find intolerable. Due to their childhood conditioning, they teach the people around them that they will accept having their boundaries violated. As recovering Nice Guys begin to take responsibility for how they let people treat them, their own behavior begins to change. As they stop reinforcing things they aren't willing to tolerate, the people around them are given the opportunity to behave differently. This gives relationships a chance to survive and grow.

由于对记忆的恐惧，好男人常常会无意识地强化那些他们无法忍受的行为。由于童年时期的条件反射，他们告诉周围的人，他们可以接受自己的界限被侵犯。随着正在康复中的好男人开始为他人对待他们的方式承担责任，他们自身的行为也开始改变。当他们不再强化那些他们不愿容忍的行为时，周围的人就有机会做出不同的行为。这为人际关系的生存和发展提供了机会。

Jake, an enlisted man in his mid-twenties, is a good example of how tolerating intolerable behavior can kill a relationship, and how setting boundaries can give a relationship a chance to survive.

杰克是一名二十五六岁的士兵，他是一个很好的例子，说明容忍无法容忍的行为会毁掉一段感情，而设定界限可以让一段感情有机会存活下来。

Just prior to his marriage to his wife Kisha, Kisha had an affair with an old boyfriend. Because Jake didn't want to lose her, he forgave her and promised to never bring up her infidelity. This established a pattern of Kisha pretty much doing whatever she wanted while Jake withheld his feelings and walked on eggshells. He would always measure his words in order to avoid saying anything "wrong" that might upset her.

就在与妻子基莎结婚前，基莎曾与前男友有过一段婚外情。杰克不想失去她，于是原谅了她，并承诺绝不再提起她的不忠。这让他养成了基莎为所欲为的习惯，而杰克则隐瞒着自己的感情，小心翼翼。他总是谨言慎行，避免说出任何可能让她难过的“错话”。

On one occasion, while they were out drinking with some friends, Kisha got drunk. Whenever she had too much to drink she would become belligerent and promiscuous. On this occasion she made several demeaning remarks to Jake and spent most of the evening slow dancing with other men in the bar.

有一次，他们和几个朋友出去喝酒，基莎喝醉了。每当她喝多了，就会变得好斗、放荡。这次她对杰克说了好几句贬低的话，晚上大部分时间都在酒吧里和其他男人慢舞。

After holding his tongue as long as he could, Jake finally told Kisha that she was drunk and that it was time to go home. She swore at him and kept on doing what she was doing. Jake retaliated by calling her a "bitch" and drove home.

杰克尽量忍住不说，最后终于告诉基莎，她喝醉了，该回家了。基莎破口大骂，继续做着她该做的事情。杰克回骂了她一个“婊子”，然后开车回家了。

One of her friends brought Kisha home the next morning. For the rest of the day she gave Jake the silent treatment. He tried to hold out, but after a few hours of misery, he apologized for calling her a bitch.

第二天早上，基莎的一个朋友把她带回了家。那天剩下的时间里，她一直对杰克冷言冷语。杰克努力忍耐，但痛苦了几个小时后，他终于为自己骂她“婊子”而道歉了。

Later that week, he somewhat reluctantly talked about the episode in his No More Mr. Nice Guy! group.

那周晚些时候，他在“不再是好人！”小组中有些不情愿地谈论了这件事。

The group members lovingly confronted him. They pointed out how his willingness to tolerate his wife's intolerable behavior gave her a license to act in any way she pleased. They told Jake that the problem was not Kisha, it was him. Until Jake changed, his wife would have no incentive to change. By not setting boundaries he was robbing his marriage of the opportunity to become what it could be.

小组成员们充满爱意地与他对质。他们指出，他容忍妻子令人无法忍受的行为，等于让她为所欲为。他们告诉杰克，问题不在于基莎，而在于他。除非杰克改变，否则他的妻子不会有改变的动力。他不设定界限，就剥夺了婚姻发展的机会。

The next day, Jake confronted his wife. He acknowledged his role in their situation. He told her that he was no longer going to tolerate intolerable behavior. He told her his boundaries. He would no longer tolerate Kisha dancing or flirting with other men. He would not tolerate her demeaning him in front of their friends. He told her that if she wanted to stay married to him, she had to go to treatment for her drinking problem.

第二天，杰克与妻子对质。他承认了自己在他们处境中扮演的角色。他告诉她，他再也不会容忍那些无法容忍的行为。他告诉她，他的底线。他不会再容忍基莎和其他男人跳舞或调情。他不会再容忍她在朋友面前贬低他。他告诉她，如果她想继续和他结婚，就必须去接受酗酒治疗。

Kisha responded by telling Jake that no one was going to tell her what to do. She packed a bag and moved out that night to a friend's house. Even though Jake was miserable the next few days, he resisted the temptation to call her and beg her to come back. Instead, he called some guys in the group.

基莎的回应是告诉杰克，没人会告诉她该做什么。她收拾行李，当晚就搬到了一个朋友家。尽管接下来的几天杰克很痛苦，但他还是忍住了打电话求她回来的冲动。他给团里的几个人打了电话。

Three nights later, Kisha called and said she wanted to talk. She came over and told Jake that even though she initially wanted to tell him to go to hell, she knew he was right. For the first time in their marriage, she said she felt respect for him. She said she wanted to save their marriage and was willing to do whatever it took to make it work. The following week Kisha entered treatment.

三天后的一个晚上，基莎打来电话说她想谈谈。她过来告诉杰克，虽然她一开始想让他滚蛋，但她知道他是对的。她说，在他们的婚姻中，这是她第一次对他感到尊重。她说，她想挽救他们的婚姻，并愿意尽一切努力让它继续下去。接下来的一周，基莎开始接受治疗。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #23

挣脱束缚活动 #23

Before you can start setting boundaries, you have to become aware of how much you back up from your line to avoid conflict or to keep the peace. For the next week, observe yourself. Do you say "yes" when you would rather say "no"? Do you agree to do something to avoid conflict? Do you avoid doing something because someone might get upset at you? Do you tolerate an intolerable situation, hoping that it will just go away? Write these observations down and share them with a safe person.

在开始设定界限之前，你必须意识到自己为了避免冲突或维持和平而放弃了多少底线。在接下来的一周里，观察一下自己。当你想说“不”的时候，你会说“是”吗？你会为了避免冲突而同意做某事吗？你会因为别人可能会生气而逃避做某事吗？你会容忍无法忍受的情况，希望它自己就此消失吗？写下这些观察结果，并与一个安全的人分享。

*****************************


Take A Walk On The Wild Side

野外漫步

There is no "key" to a smooth life. Being "good" or doing it "right" doesn't insulate Nice Guys from the chaotic, ever-changing realities of life. All the Nice Guy paradigm does is create wimpy men who allow bullies to kick sand in their face or shame them for loading the dishwasher "wrong."

顺利的生活没有“秘诀”。做好事或做“对”事并不能让好男人免受混乱、瞬息万变的现实的困扰。好男人模式只会创造出懦弱的男人，任由恶霸们把沙子踢到脸上，或因为“错误地”把洗碗机放进去而羞辱他们。

As recovering Nice Guys begin to surrender, dwell in reality, express their feelings, face their fears, develop integrity, and set boundaries, they access a power that allows them to welcome and embrace the challenges and "gifts" of life. Life isn't a merry-go-round, it's a roller coaster. As they reclaim personal power, recovering Nice Guys can experience the world in all of its serendipitous beauty. Life won't always be smooth, it may not always be pretty, but it will be an adventure — one not to be missed.

当正在康复的“好男人”开始臣服于现实，沉浸于现实，表达感受，直面恐惧，培养正直，设定界限时，他们便会获得一种力量，让他们能够迎接并拥抱生活中的挑战和“礼物”。生活并非旋转木马，而是过山车。当他们重拾个人力量时，正在康复的“好男人”便能体验到这个世界所有偶然的美好。生活不会总是一帆风顺，也未必总是美好，但它将是一场冒险——一场不容错过的冒险。

# ↩

Chapter 6

# ↩

第六章

Reclaim Your Masculinity

重拾男子气概


Contrary to the prevailing sentiments of the last few decades, it is OK to be a guy.

与过去几十年的主流观点相反，现在做个男人是可以的。

Men born after World War II had the misfortune of growing up during the only era of recent western history in which it was not always a good thing to be male. This was primarily the result of two significant family and social changes in the post war era: 1) boys were disconnected from their fathers and other healthy male role models, and 2) boys were forced to seek approval from women and accept a female definition of what it meant to be male.

二战后出生的男性不幸地成长于近代西方历史上唯一一个男性并非总是被看作好事的时代。这主要源于战后时期两大重大的家庭和社会变革：1）男孩与父亲及其他健康的男性榜样的疏离；2）男孩被迫寻求女性的认可，并接受女性对男性的定义。

As a result of these two dynamics, many boys and men came to believe that they had to hide or eliminate any negative male traits (like those of their fathers or other "bad" men) and become what they believed women wanted them to be. For many men, this life strategy seemed essential if they wanted to be loved, get their needs met, and have a smooth life.

这两种动力的结果是，许多男孩和男人开始相信，他们必须隐藏或消除任何负面的男性特质（例如他们父亲或其他“坏”男人的特质），并成为他们认为女人希望他们成为的样子。对许多男人来说，如果他们想要被爱、满足需求并过上顺利的生活，这种生活策略似乎至关重要。

Due to the continuing social change of the last half of the 20th century, this belief system is no longer limited to just men of the baby boom generation. I frequently observe men in their thirties, twenties, and teens, with strong Nice Guy traits. It seems that each successive generation of men are becoming more and more passive.

由于20世纪后半叶持续的社会变革，这种信仰体系已不再仅限于婴儿潮一代的男性。我经常观察到三十几岁、二十几岁和十几岁的男性，他们身上都带有强烈的“好男人”特质。似乎每一代男性都变得越来越被动。

This social conditioning effects Nice Guys in many ways:

这种社会条件对好男人的影响表现在很多方面：

  • Nice Guys tend to be disconnected from other men.
    好男人往往与其他男人疏远。
  • Nice Guys tend to be disconnected from their own masculinity.
    好男人往往与自己的男子气概脱节。
  • Nice Guys tend to be monogamous to their mothers.
    好男人往往对他们的母亲一夫一妻制。
  • Nice Guys tend to be dependent on the approval of women.
    好男人往往依赖于女性的认可。



Nice Guys Tend To Be Disconnected From Other Men.

好男人往往与其他男人疏远。

I frequently hear Nice Guys make comments such as:

我经常听到好人发表这样的评论：

  • "I'm just not comfortable with other men. I don't know what to talk about."
    “我和其他男人在一起感觉不自在。我不知道该聊些什么。”
  • "Most men are jerks."
    “大多数男人都是混蛋。”
  • "I used to have male friends, but my wife made it such a hassle to do things with them that I just gave up."
    “我以前有一些男性朋友，但我老婆让我跟他们相处很麻烦，所以我就放弃了。”
  • "I tend to be a loner."
    “我倾向于独来独往。”

Many Nice Guys have difficulty connecting with men because of the limited positive male contact they experienced in childhood. Because these men did not have a positive bond with their father, they never learned the basic skills necessary to build meaningful relationships with men.

许多“好男人”难以与男性建立联系，因为他们童年时期缺乏与男性的积极接触。由于这些男人与父亲缺乏积极的联系，他们从未学会与男性建立有意义的关系所需的基本技能。

Another common trait among Nice Guys is the belief that they are different from other men. This distorted thinking usually began in childhood when they tried to be different from their "bad" or unavailable father. In adulthood, Nice Guys often create a similar dynamic with men in general. Nice Guys may convince themselves they are different from (better than) other men because they believe:

好男人的另一个共同特征是，他们认为自己与其他男人不同。这种扭曲的思维通常始于童年时期，当时他们试图与“坏”或无法沟通的父亲区别开来。成年后，好男人通常会与一般男人产生类似的互动。好男人可能会说服自己，自己与其他男人不同（或比其他男人更好），因为他们相信：

  • They aren't controlling.
    他们不会控制别人。
  • They aren't angry and rageful.
    他们不会生气或暴怒。
  • They aren't violent.
    他们不会使用暴力。
  • They are attentive to a woman's needs.
    他们关注女人的需求。
  • They are good lovers.
    他们是好的爱人。
  • They are good fathers.
    他们是好父亲。

As long as Nice Guys are disconnected from men or believe they are different from other men, they cut themselves off from the many positive benefits of male companionship and the power of a masculine community.

只要好男人与男人脱节，或者相信自己与其他男人不同，他们就会切断自己与男性陪伴带来的诸多积极好处以及男性群体的力量之间的联系。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #24

挣脱束缚活动 #24

Look over the list above. Note the ways you have consciously or unconsciously tried to be different from your father and/or other men. How does the belief that you are different keep you disconnected from other men?

看看上面的清单。想想你有意或无意地试图与你父亲和/或其他男人不同的方式。这种“你与众不同”的信念是如何阻碍你与其他男人建立联系的？

*****************************


Nice Guys Tend To Be Disconnected From Their Masculinity

好男人往往缺乏男子气概

I define masculinity as that part of a man that equips him to survive as an individual, clan, and species . Without this masculine energy we would have all become extinct eons ago. Masculinity empowers a man to create and produce. It also empowers him provide for and protect those who are important to him. These aspects of masculinity include strength, discipline, courage, passion, persistence, and integrity.

我将男子气概定义为男人身上赋予他作为个体、氏族和物种生存所需的部分。如果没有这种男子气概，我们人类早在很久以前就灭绝了。男子气概赋予男人创造和生产的能力，也赋予他养育和保护那些对他重要的人的能力。男子气概的这些方面包括力量、自律、勇气、激情、坚持和正直。

Masculine energy also represents the potential for aggressiveness, destructiveness, and brutality. These characteristics frighten Nice Guys — and most women — therefore Nice Guys work especially hard to repress these traits.

男性能量也代表着潜在的攻击性、破坏性和残暴性。这些特质令好男人——以及大多数女人——感到恐惧，因此，好男人会格外努力地压抑这些特质。

Most Nice Guys believe that by repressing the darker side of their masculine energy they will win the approval of women. This seems logical considering the anti-male climate that has permeated our culture since the 1960s. Ironically, these same men frequently complain that women seemed to be attracted to "jerks" rather than Nice Guys like them. Many women have shared with me that due to the absence of any discernible life energy in Nice Guys, there is little to be attracted to. They also reveal that their tendency to be attracted to "jerks" is because these men have more of a masculine edge to them.

大多数“好男人”认为，压抑自身阴暗的阳刚之气就能赢得女性的认可。考虑到自20世纪60年代以来弥漫在我们文化中的反男性氛围，这似乎合乎逻辑。讽刺的是，这些男人却经常抱怨，女人似乎更容易被“混蛋”吸引，而不是像他们一样的“好男人”。许多女性告诉我，由于“好男人”身上缺乏任何可察觉的生命力，她们几乎不会受到任何吸引。她们还透露，她们更容易被“混蛋”吸引，是因为这些男人身上更有阳刚之气。

As Nice Guys try to avoid the dark side of their masculinity, they also repress many other aspects of this male energy force. As a result, they often lose their sexual assertiveness, competitiveness, creativity, ego, thirst for experience, boisterousness, exhibitionism, and power. Go watch little boys on the playground and you will see these qualities. I am convinced that these are good things worth keeping.

当“好男人”试图逃避自身男子气概的阴暗面时，他们也压抑了这种男性能量的许多其他方面。结果，他们常常丧失了性自信、竞争力、创造力、自我意识、对经验的渴望、喧闹、爱表现以及力量。去看看操场上的小男孩，你就会发现这些品质。我坚信，这些都是值得保留的美好品质。

One of the most visible consequences of the repression of masculine energy in Nice Guys is their lack of leadership in their families. Out of fear of upsetting their partner or appearing too much like their controlling, authoritarian, or abusive fathers, Nice Guys frequently fail to be the leader their family needs. Consequently, the job of leading the family often falls on their wives. Most of the women I talk to don't want this job, but end up taking it by default.

好男人压抑男性能量最明显的后果之一，就是他们在家庭中缺乏领导力。由于害怕惹恼伴侣，或害怕显得太像控制欲强、专制或虐待成性的父亲，好男人常常无法成为家庭所需的领导者。因此，领导家庭的重担往往落在了妻子身上。我采访过的大多数女性并不想要这份工作，但最终却不得不默认。


Nice Guys Tend To Be Monogamous To Their Mothers

好男人往往对他们的母亲保持一夫一妻制

Becoming and remaining monogamous to their mothers is a common pattern for Nice Guys. This unconscious bond is the result of a normal childhood developmental phenomenon gone amuck. Let me explain.

对母亲保持一夫一妻制是“好男人”的常见模式。这种无意识的联系是儿童时期正常发育现象失控的结果。让我来解释一下。

All little boys naturally fall in love with their mother and desire to have her all to themselves. Healthy mothers and fathers help their sons successfully move through this normal developmental stage. As they do, the young boy individuates from his mother, bonds with men, and becomes available for an intimate relationship with another woman in adulthood.

所有小男孩都会自然地爱上自己的母亲，渴望完全属于自己。健康的父母会帮助儿子顺利度过这个正常的发育阶段。在这个阶段，小男孩逐渐脱离母亲，与男性建立联系，并在成年后准备好与另一个女人建立亲密关系。

Each parent plays a significant role in facilitating this healthy transition. First, the mother must know how to give enough to meet the child's needs without creating dependency. She must also know how to get her own needs met so she is not tempted to use her son to fill the void. Second, the father must be present and have a healthy bond with his son. This connection helps the little boy move from the cozy lap of his mother to the challenging world of men.

每位父母在促进这一健康过渡的过程中都扮演着重要的角色。首先，母亲必须懂得如何给予孩子足够的关爱，同时又不至于让孩子产生依赖性。她也必须懂得如何满足自己的需求，这样才不会想要用儿子来填补内心的空虚。其次，父亲必须陪伴在儿子身边，与他建立健康的亲子关系。这种联系能够帮助小男孩从母亲温暖的怀抱中走向充满挑战的男性世界。

As stated above, most Nice Guys do not report having had close a relationship with their father in childhood. As a result, many Nice Guys were forced into an unhealthy bond with their mother. This bond might have formed if they had to please an angry, critical, or controlling mother. More often than not, the bond was the result of being forced to take care of a needy, dependent, or smothering mother. Without a supportive father, these boys had to negotiate an impossible situation on their own.

如上所述，大多数“好男人”在童年时期与父亲的关系并不亲密。因此，许多“好男人”被迫与母亲建立一种不健康的关系。这种关系的形成可能是因为他们不得不取悦一个脾气暴躁、爱批评或控制欲强的母亲。更多时候，这种关系是被迫照顾一个需要照顾、依赖他人或令人窒息的母亲的结果。没有父亲的支持，这些男孩不得不独自应对这种难以应对的困境。

Both childhood situations — trying to please an angry or controlling mother, or becoming mother's little partner — created a dynamic in which Nice Guys unconsciously became monogamous to their mothers and did not individuate in a healthy way.

这两种童年情况——试图取悦愤怒或控制欲强的母亲，或成为母亲的小伴侣——都产生了一种动态，在这种动态中，好男人无意识地对母亲保持专一的态度，并没有以健康的方式实现个体化。

When a Nice Guy has been conditioned to be monogamous to his mother in childhood, his adult partner will know at some level that he is not really available. The partner may not consciously connect this to his bond with his mother, but she knows something is missing.

如果一个好男人在童年时期就被训练成只对母亲忠诚，那么他的成年伴侣在某种程度上会意识到他并非真正地需要他。他可能不会有意识地将这归咎于他与母亲的联系，但母亲知道他缺少了一些东西。

Anita, a woman in her late fifties, was married to a man who was monogamous to his mother. I met Anita when she called and made an appointment for individual counseling. She believed her husband was having an affair and she wanted some advice. As we began our session she sat down on the couch and smiled nervously.

安妮塔，一位年近五十的女性，嫁给了一位对其母亲一夫一妻制的丈夫。我遇到安妮塔时，她打电话预约了一次个人咨询。她认为丈夫有外遇，想寻求一些建议。咨询开始时，她坐在沙发上，紧张地笑了笑。

"I feel so foolish coming here, but I just don't know who to talk to. I feel crazy, because I think my husband is having an affair with his secretary. He denies it but I know something is going on, there's just too much evidence."

“我觉得自己来这里太傻了，但我不知道该跟谁说。我感觉自己疯了，因为我觉得我丈夫和他的秘书有染。他否认了，但我知道肯定有什么事，证据实在太多了。”

Anita's smile disappeared and was replaced with a look of grief. She took a tissue and dabbed the corner of her eye.

安妮塔的笑容消失了，取而代之的是悲伤的表情。她拿起纸巾，擦了擦眼角。

"Dutton, that's my husband, has been through so much lately. He's under a lot of pressure at work, things have been tight financially, and his mother died last year. He was really close to her and I think it was really hard on him."

“达顿，也就是我的丈夫，最近经历了很多。他工作压力很大，经济拮据，去年他母亲去世了。他和母亲关系非常亲密，我觉得这对他来说真的很难。”

Anita told of her suspicions of her husband's infidelity but then came back to the subject of his mother again.

安妮塔谈到了她对丈夫不忠的怀疑，但随后又谈起了他母亲的话题。

"If I didn't know better, I'd say his infatuation with his secretary began right after his mother died. It's like he needed something to fill a hole in his life. I always liked his mother. She was a nice lady, but I always had the feeling that Dutton was more connected to her than he was to me. Is that crazy?" she asked quizzically. "To be jealous of your mother in law?"

“要不是我了解得更清楚，我估计他对秘书的迷恋是从他母亲去世后开始的。就好像他需要点什么来填补人生的空虚。我一直很喜欢他母亲。她人很好，但我总觉得达顿跟她比跟我更亲近。这太疯狂了吧？”她疑惑地问道。“嫉妒你的婆婆？”

I encouraged Anita to tell me more about Dutton's family.

我鼓励安妮塔告诉我更多有关达顿家庭的事情。

"Other than his father," She continued, "He believes it was great. That's because of his mother of course.

“除了他的父亲，”她继续说道，“他认为这很棒。当然，这是因为他的母亲。”

She was a real saint. His father was extremely harsh with the kids. Their mom was the one they turned to for nurturing. She was really good at listening and being there for them."

她真是个圣人。他父亲对孩子们极其严厉。孩子们都向母亲寻求关爱。她非常善于倾听，并且陪伴着他们。

Anita seemed relieved to be able to talk about something besides her suspicions of her husband.

安妮塔似乎松了一口气，因为她可以谈论一些除了对丈夫的怀疑之外的事情。

"Before she died, Dutton paid for them to get carpet in their house and bought them two nice reclining chairs because he knew his father never would. He used to drive her places because he knew his father wouldn't. He treated his mother real special — I think to make up for what she had to go through living with his dad. One time I was angry at him and I accused him of treating his mother better than he treated me. He blew up." Anita made an explosion gesture with her arms. "He told me to never say that again. He didn't talk to me for two weeks after that. I learned not to bring up that subject."

“她去世前，达顿帮他们铺了地毯，还买了两把舒适的躺椅，因为他知道他父亲永远不会这么做。他以前还开车送她去很多地方，因为他知道他父亲不会这么做。他对他母亲特别好——我想是为了弥补她和他父亲一起生活时所经历的痛苦。有一次我生他的气，指责他对他母亲比对我好。他勃然大怒。”安妮塔摆出一个爆炸的手势。“他告诉我以后别再说这种话了。之后两周他都没跟我说话。我学会了以后再也不提这个话题了。”

Anita paused for moment. "Do you think there could be any connection between his mom dying and him having an affair? They loved each other so much. Maybe his secretary fills that void. Does that sound crazy to you?"

安妮塔顿了顿。“你觉得他妈妈去世和他有外遇之间会有什么联系吗？他们彼此深爱着对方。也许他的秘书填补了他们之间的空白。你觉得这听起来很疯狂吗？”


Nice Guys Tend To Seek The Approval Of Women

好男人倾向于寻求女性的认可

Due to their family and social conditioning, Nice Guys tend to seek the approval women. Even as they are trying to become what they believe women want them to be and doing what they believe women want them to do, Nice Guys tend to experience tremendous frustration in gaining the approval they so intensely desire.

由于家庭和社会环境的影响，好男人倾向于寻求女性的认可。即使他们努力成为自己认为女性希望他们成为的样子，做自己认为女性希望他们做的事情，好男人也往往会在获得他们渴望的认可的过程中经历巨大的挫败感。

This frustration is due to the reality that in general, women view men who try to please them as weak and hold these men in contempt. Most women do not want a man who tries to please them — they want a man who knows how to please himself. Women consistently share with me that they don't want a passive, pleasing wimp. They want a man — someone with his balls still intact.

这种挫败感源于一个现实：一般来说，女人认为那些试图取悦她们的男人软弱无能，并且鄙视他们。大多数女人不想要一个试图取悦她们的男人——她们想要一个懂得取悦自己的男人。女人总是跟我说，她们不想要一个被动、讨好别人的懦夫。她们想要一个男人——一个仍然有精气神的男人。


Getting Your Testicles Back

恢复睾丸

Avoiding relationships with men and seeking the approval of women prevents Nice Guys from getting what they really want in love and life. In order to reverse the effects of the Nice Guy Syndrome Nice Guys have to reclaim their masculinity. The process involves coming to believe that it really is a good thing to be a man and embracing all of their masculine traits. Reclaiming one's masculinity involves:

回避与男性交往并寻求女性的认可，会阻碍“好男人”在爱情和生活中获得他们真正想要的东西。为了扭转“好男人综合症”的影响，好男人必须重拾男子气概。这个过程包括：相信身为男人是一件好事，并拥抱自己身上所有的男性特质。重拾男子气概包括：

  • Connecting with other men.
    与其他男人建立联系。
  • Getting strong.
    变得强壮。
  • Finding healthy male role models.
    寻找健康的男性榜样。
  • Reexamining one's relationship with one's father.
    重新审视与父亲的关系。


Connecting With Men Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Masculinity

与男性交往有助于好男人重拾男子气概

Connecting with men is essential for reclaiming masculinity. Building relationships with men requires a conscious effort. This process begins with a commitment to develop male friendships. In order to do this, recovering Nice Guys must be willing to make the time, take risks, and be vulnerable. For most Nice Guys, time seems to be a big factor that keeps them disconnected from men. It takes time to talk with a neighbor, call up a friend, or go to a ballgame. Since many Nice Guys are enmeshed with their wives, families, or work, this means taking time away from these things.

与男性建立联系对于重拾男子气概至关重要。与男性建立关系需要有意识的努力。这个过程始于致力于发展男性友谊。为了做到这一点，正在恢复的“好男人”必须愿意抽出时间，承担风险，并展现脆弱的一面。对大多数“好男人”来说，时间似乎是让他们与男性疏远的一个重要因素。与邻居聊天、给朋友打电话或去看球赛都需要时间。由于许多“好男人”都忙于妻子、家庭或工作，这意味着他们不得不抽出时间远离这些事情。

Connecting with men involves doing guy things with guys. There is no right way to do this, but it can include joining a team, going to sporting events, joining a prayer or discussion group, having a poker night, doing volunteer work, going fishing, going for a run, or just hanging out.

与男人交往意味着和男人做一些男人该做的事情。没有绝对正确的方法，但可以加入团队、参加体育赛事、加入祈祷或讨论小组、参加扑克之夜、做志愿者工作、钓鱼、跑步，或者只是一起出去玩。

Alan is an example of what can happen when a recovering Nice Guy makes the decision to connect with men. Alan had a difficult time doing things for himself, especially with other men. When he made a conscious effort to begin addressing this issue he had to first take a look at what kept him disconnected from men and what he could do to start changing the pattern.

艾伦就是一个例子，展现了正在康复中的“好男人”决定与男性交往时会发生什么。艾伦曾经很难独自处理事情，尤其是与其他男性交往。当他有意识地开始解决这个问题时，他必须首先审视是什么让他与男性疏远，以及他可以做些什么来改变这种模式。

One of the first things Alan did was to join a men's therapy group. Even then, it took more than a year before he began doing things with the men outside of the group. As he did, these men were able to give him feedback about his defense mechanisms that kept him isolated. These men also supported him in changing the ways he related to his wife.

艾伦做的第一件事就是加入一个男性治疗小组。即便如此，他还是花了一年多的时间才开始和小组外的男性们互动。在互动过程中，这些男性能够就他那些让他感到孤立的防御机制提供反馈。这些男性也支持他改变与妻子的相处方式。

Alan also joined a health club where he started playing volleyball and racquetball with other men. Later, he took the lead in starting up a softball team. At first it was difficult to take time out just for himself, especially when it meant being away from his family.

艾伦还加入了一个健身俱乐部，在那里他开始和其他男人一起打排球和壁球。后来，他带头组建了一支垒球队。起初，他很难抽出时间给自己，尤其是当这意味着要远离家人的时候。

It took a few years, but Alan now has a couple of close male friends as well as several other guys he sees on a regular basis. He even takes a yearly road trip with friends across the country for a weekend of golf. He looks at these trips with the guys as one of the highlights of his year.

几年过去了，艾伦现在结识了几个亲密的男性朋友，还有几个经常见面的男士。他甚至每年都会和朋友们一起驾车穿越全国，周末去打高尔夫。他把这些和朋友们的旅行视为一年中的亮点之一。

Both Alan and his wife Marie believe Alan's conscious decision to connect with men saved their marriage. Alan had made his wife his emotional center. His life revolved around trying to please her and make her happy. Due to his ineffective covert contracts, Alan never believed Marie gave as much to him as he gave to her. As a result, he was often resentful and passive-aggressive. When Alan began to get his emotional and social needs met with men, it took a lot of pressure off his wife.

艾伦和妻子玛丽都认为，艾伦有意识地与男性交往挽救了他们的婚姻。艾伦把妻子当成了自己的情感中心。他的生活总是围绕着取悦她、让她快乐。由于那些无效的秘密合同，艾伦从未相信玛丽给予他的和他给予她的一样多。结果，他常常心怀怨恨，消极被动。当艾伦开始通过男性来满足自己的情感和社交需求时，妻子的压力减轻了很多。

As Alan reclaimed his masculine energy, he also began to look more attractive to Marie. Even though it was initially difficult to tell her that he was going to spend time with his friends, she respected him when he did. This newfound respect rekindled the feelings she first felt toward Alan early in their relationship.

随着艾伦重拾阳刚之气，他对玛丽也开始更具吸引力。虽然一开始很难告诉她他要和朋友们一起玩，但当他这样做时，玛丽还是很尊重他。这份新发现的尊重重新点燃了她对艾伦在两人交往初期的那种感觉。

As Alan found out, there are numerous benefits from developing male relationships. Perhaps one of the most significant benefits for Nice Guys is that it improves their relationships with women. I consistently tell Nice Guys, "The best thing you can do for your relationship with your girlfriend or wife is to have male friends." As they get many of their emotional needs met with men, recovering Nice Guys become less dependent, needy, manipulative and resentful in their relationships with women.

正如艾伦所发现的，发展男性关系有很多好处。或许对“好男人”来说，最重要的好处之一就是它能改善他们与女性的关系。我经常告诉“好男人”：“你能为与女友或妻子的关系做的最好的事情就是拥有男性朋友。” 由于他们的许多情感需求都能从男性身上得到满足，正在康复的“好男人”在与女性的关系中会变得不那么依赖、不那么需要、不那么操纵，也不会那么怨恨。

Developing male relationships makes recovering Nice Guys less susceptible to seeking women's approval or allowing themselves to be defined by the opposite sex. If the Nice Guy's girlfriend or wife is angry at him or thinks he is a jerk, he can take comfort in knowing his buddies think he is OK. He is therefore less likely to resort to peacekeeping or fixing to try and keep his partner happy.

发展男性关系可以让正在康复的“好男人”不那么容易寻求女性的认可，也不容易被异性定义。如果“好男人”的女朋友或妻子生他的气，或者觉得他是个混蛋，他可以安心地知道，他的朋友们都觉得他还好。因此，他不太可能为了取悦伴侣而诉诸“维和”或“修复关系”。

Friendships with men have the potential for tremendous depth and intimacy because there is no sexual agenda. A Nice Guy will frequently avoid doing anything that might upset his partner and cause her to not want to have sex with him. With men, recovering Nice Guys don't feel like they have to please, placate, lie, caretake or sacrifice like they believe they have to with women. Not having a sexual agenda removes the fear and dysfunctional dances so common for Nice Guys in their relationships with the opposite sex.

与男性的友谊能够发展出深厚而亲密的关系，因为这种友谊没有性方面的考虑。好男人通常会避免做任何可能让伴侣不高兴、让她不愿与他发生性关系的事情。与男性相处时，正在康复中的好男人不会像对待女人那样，觉得必须取悦、安抚、撒谎、照顾或牺牲。没有性方面的考虑，消除了好男人在与异性交往中常见的恐惧和不正常的舞蹈。


Breaking The Monogamous Bond To Mom

打破与母亲的一夫一妻制

Developing male relationships helps undo a Nice Guy's monogamous bond with his mother. Little boys get pulled into unhealthy relationships with their mothers only when their fathers allow it. The solution to reversing this dynamic is creating healthy relationships with men.

发展男性关系有助于瓦解“好男人”与母亲之间的一夫一妻制关系。只有在父亲允许的情况下，小男孩才会与母亲建立不健康的关系。扭转这种局面的办法是与男性建立健康的关系。

When my daughter Jamie was 18, she had a boyfriend who had been conditioned to be monogamous to his mother. The boy's father frequently traveled for his job, was emotionally unavailable, rigid, and demanding. The boy's mother smothered her son and made him her emotional partner.

我女儿杰米18岁时，交了一个男朋友，他被灌输了一种对母亲忠贞不渝的观念。男孩的父亲经常出差，感情上冷漠，刻板又苛刻。男孩的母亲对儿子百依百顺，把他变成了自己的情感伴侣。

On several occasions, Jamie felt as if she were competing with her boyfriend's mother for his attention and affection. Unfortunately, since the mother had first dibs on him, she usually won. It felt strange to Jamie to be jealous of and in competition with her boyfriend's mother. Nevertheless, she passed the situation off as just a case of her boyfriend and his mother having a very "close" relationship.

杰米有好几次都觉得自己在和男友的母亲争夺他的注意力和爱。不幸的是，由于男友的母亲拥有优先权，所以通常都是她赢。杰米觉得嫉妒男友的母亲并与之竞争很奇怪。尽管如此，她还是把这种情况当成了男友和他母亲关系非常“亲密”的一个例子。

One Friday night, Jamie and I went out for dinner. While we ate, she shared her frustration of having to compete with her boyfriend's mother, especially now that he had joined the Marines and was away at boot camp. I empathized with my daughter and shared the facts of life with her.

一个星期五的晚上，我和杰米出去吃饭。吃饭的时候，她跟我诉说了自己不得不和男朋友的妈妈竞争的沮丧，尤其是现在男朋友已经加入了海军陆战队，正在参加新兵训练营。我感同身受，跟她分享了生活中的种种。

"Your boyfriend is a classic Nice Guy," I told her. "He has been conditioned to be monogamous to his mother. Unfortunately, that means that he will never really be able to bond completely with you.

“你男朋友是个典型的好男人，”我告诉她。“他已经习惯了只对他妈妈忠诚。可惜的是，这意味着他永远无法和你真正建立起完整的感情。”

Something will always get in the way. You may be tempted to focus on that thing, as if it is the problem.

总会有事情阻碍你。你可能会忍不住把注意力集中在那件事上，仿佛它就是问题所在。

But the real problem is his relationship with his mother."

但真正的问题是他与母亲的关系。”
