Jamie wasn't thrilled with what I told her, but for an 18-year-old she was pretty intuitive and knew what I was saying was true. She even shared a few examples of the ways she had already seen this happen.

杰米对我告诉她的事情不太高兴，但对于一个18岁的孩子来说，她的直觉相当敏锐，知道我说的是真的。她甚至还分享了一些她亲眼见过的例子。

"Is there any hope?" Jamie asked. "Can he ever break free from his mother and become available for me?"

“还有希望吗？”杰米问。“他能摆脱他妈妈，回到我身边吗？”

"Yes," I said, "there is one hope. He has to learn to connect with men in ways that he couldn't with his father. I think it is a good thing," I told her, "that he is in the Marines and connecting with men. You can support that too. If you two continue dating or even marry, encourage his relationships with men. They are the one hope you have of him breaking his monogamous bond with his mother."

“是的，”我说，“还有一线希望。他必须学会用他和父亲无法做到的方式与男人沟通。我觉得，”我告诉她，“他在海军陆战队服役，并且和男人保持联系，这是一件好事。你也可以支持他这样做。如果你们继续约会，甚至结婚，鼓励他和男人交往。他们是你让他打破与母亲一夫一妻制的唯一希望。”

A month or so later, Jamie flew down to San Diego with her boyfriend's parents to attend his graduation from boot camp. As usual, his mother acted possessive and territorial. Amazingly, Jamie noticed a difference in her boyfriend. On several occasions, he set boundaries with his mother and refused to let her hook an emotional hose up to him. Jamie could tell that this was primarily the result of her boyfriend having bonded with several guys in boot camp and from embracing his own masculinity.

大约一个月后，杰米和男友的父母一起飞往圣地亚哥参加他的新兵训练营毕业典礼。像往常一样，他的母亲表现出强烈的占有欲和领地意识。令人惊讶的是，杰米注意到了男友的变化。有几次，他与母亲划清界限，不让她对他施加情感压力。杰米看得出来，这主要是因为她的男友在新兵训练营里和几个男生交往过，而且他拥抱了自己的男子气概。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #25

挣脱束缚活动 #25

List three men whom you would like to get to know better. Next to each man's name list a possible activity you could do together. Next to this, write down a date and make a commitment to contact him by this day.

列出三个你想进一步了解的男士。在每位男士的名字旁边，列出一项你们可以一起做的活动。在旁边写下一个日期，并承诺在此日期之前联系他。

*****************************


Getting Strong Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Masculinity

变得强大有助于好男人重拾男子气概

Masculinity denotes strength and power. Because of their conditioning, Nice Guys tend to fear these traits. As a result, they often become emotionally and physically soft. Some even take pride in this softness. I've met many Nice Guys who work out or practice martial arts, but who are still afraid of their strength.

男子气概象征着力量和威力。由于环境的制约，好男人往往惧怕这些特质。结果，他们往往在情感和身体上变得软弱。有些人甚至以这种软弱为荣。我见过很多好男人，他们虽然锻炼身体或练习武术，但仍然惧怕自己的力量。

Embracing one's masculinity mean's embracing one's body, power, and spaciousness. In order to do this, recovering Nice Guys have to stop putting junk into their bodies and train them to respond to the physical demands of being male. This involves eating healthy foods, eliminating drugs and alcohol, working out, drinking lots of water, playing, relaxing, and getting enough rest. Whether the Nice Guy stays fit by running, swimming, weight training, martial arts, playing basketball, volleyball, or tennis, this physical strength translates into self-confidence and power in every other aspect of his life.

拥抱男子气概意味着拥抱自己的身体、力量和宽广的胸怀。为了做到这一点，正在康复中的“好男人”必须停止摄入垃圾食品，并训练身体适应男性的生理需求。这包括健康饮食、戒除毒品和酒精、锻炼身体、多喝水、玩耍、放松身心、获得充足的休息。无论“好男人”是通过跑步、游泳、力量训练、武术、打篮球、打排球还是打网球来保持健康，这种体能都会转化为他生活中其他方面自信和力量。

Travis, an attorney in his early fifties is a good example. Travis came to see me to deal with his marital difficulties. During the first session of counseling, two things became immediately evident: First, Travis was a Nice Guy, and second, he had a drug and alcohol problem. I told him I would work with him only if he got a drug and alcohol assessment, quit drinking, and started attending Alcoholics Anonymous.

五十多岁的律师特拉维斯就是一个很好的例子。他来找我解决他的婚姻问题。在第一次咨询中，我立刻意识到了两件事：首先，特拉维斯是个好人；其次，他有毒品和酒精问题。我告诉他，只有他接受毒品和酒精评估、戒酒并开始参加匿名戒酒会，我才会帮他。

Travis complied with all my boundaries and asked if he could join one of the No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups.

特拉维斯遵守了我的所有界限，并询问他是否可以加入“不再是好人！”团体之一。

Over the next several months, Travis's relationship with his wife was up and down like a yo-yo. In addition to marital problems, it also became apparent that Travis had a number of other lifestyle problems. His diet consisted primarily of fast food. He was a chain smoker and he drank several cups of coffee a day. He worked long hours and got absolutely no exercise.

接下来的几个月里，特拉维斯和妻子的关系就像溜溜球一样起伏不定。除了婚姻问题，特拉维斯的其他一些生活方式问题也逐渐显现。他的饮食主要以快餐为主。他烟瘾很大，每天要喝好几杯咖啡。他工作时间很长，而且完全不运动。

Over the next several months, Travis began to address these issues one at a time. He started taking time away from work to attend AA meetings and spend time with other recovering men. He decided to have a surgery he had been putting off for years. Since he wouldn't be able to smoke for a few days, he decided it was a good time to quit for good. After his surgery, he began going for walks during his lunch hour.

在接下来的几个月里，特拉维斯开始逐一解决这些问题。他开始抽出时间参加匿名戒酒会，并与其他正在康复的男士们相处。他决定去做一项拖延多年的手术。由于接下来几天都不能吸烟，他决定是时候彻底戒烟了。手术后，他开始利用午餐时间散步。

He started drinking more water and cut back on his coffee and soft drink consumption. He even took a week off from work and went fishing with some friends in Alaska.

他开始多喝水，减少了咖啡和软饮料的摄入量。他甚至请了一周假，和朋友们去阿拉斯加钓鱼。

About 10 months after joining the Nice Guy group, he shared that he was filing for divorce. With his lifestyle changes and the support of the group he had come to realize that his combative relationship with his wife was his last bad habit that needed to go. While relaying his decision to the group, he revealed that his wife blamed the group for killing their marriage. Travis smiled and then wiped a tear from the corner of his eye. "Thanks to this group, I feel strong. I never could have made these changes without your help. This group didn't kill my marriage, it saved my life."

加入“好男人”小组大约10个月后，他透露自己正在申请离婚。随着生活方式的改变和小组的支持，他逐渐意识到，与妻子的争吵是他最后一个需要改掉的坏习惯。在向小组成员传达他的决定时，他透露妻子指责小组毁掉了他们的婚姻。特拉维斯笑了笑，擦去了眼角的泪水。“多亏了这个小组，我感觉自己坚强起来了。如果没有你们的帮助，我永远不可能做出这些改变。这个小组没有毁掉我的婚姻，而是拯救了我的人生。”



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #26

挣脱束缚活动 #26

Identify three ways in which you neglect your body. Write down three ways in which you can start taking better care of yourself.

找出你忽视自己身体的三个方面。写下三个你可以开始更好地照顾自己的方法。

Seeking Out Healthy Role Models Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Masculinity

寻找健康的榜样有助于好男人重拾男子气概

I encourage recovering Nice Guys to visualize what they think a healthy male would look like and think of healthy masculine traits they would like to develop. With that picture in mind, they can go out and look for men who have these kinds of qualities. These men may be in their church, their company, their softball team, even characters on TV or the movies. By observing how these men live their lives and interact with the world, the Nice Guy can begin assimilating a healthier model of manhood.

我鼓励正在康复中的“好男人”们想象一下他们心目中健康男性的形象，并思考他们想要培养的健康男性特质。带着这样的想象，他们可以去寻找拥有这些特质的男人。这些男人可能在他们的教堂、公司、垒球队，甚至是电视或电影里的人物。通过观察这些男人的生活方式和与世界的互动，好男人可以开始吸收更健康的男子气概。

Like many recovering Nice Guys, I have done this work by committee. I developed a friendship with one man who was good at doing guy things. I formed a relationship with another guy who was a hard worker. I created a relationship with a man who was comfortable revealing himself and sharing his feelings. I made another friend who was good at taking risks and challenging himself. Each of these men in their own way helped me see what it looks like to be male and have been role models for reclaiming my own masculinity.

像许多正在恢复“好男人”状态的男人一样，我通过“委员会”来完成这项工作。我和一个擅长做男人该做的事的男人建立了友谊。我和另一个努力工作的男人建立了关系。我和一个乐于展现自我、分享感受的男人建立了关系。我还结识了另一个善于冒险和挑战自我的朋友。这些男人都以各自的方式帮助我了解了作为男人的真正意义，并成为我重拾男子气概的榜样。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #27

挣脱束缚活动 #27

Visualize what you think a healthy male would look like. What personality traits would he posses?

想象一下，一个健康的男性应该是什么样子。他会拥有哪些性格特征？

Write these down. Do you know anyone who has a number of these traits? How could you use this person as a healthy role model?

把这些写下来。你认识的人中有人具备这些特质吗？你可以如何将这个人作为健康的榜样？

Reexamining Their Relationship With Their Father Helps Nice Guys Reclaim Their Masculinity

重新审视与父亲的关系有助于好男人重拾男子气概

As I've mentioned before, most Nice Guys do not report having had a close relationship with their father in childhood. Either their fathers were passive, unavailable, absent, or defined in some negative way. Reclaiming their masculinity requires that Nice Guys examine their relationships with their fathers and take a look at them through adult eyes.

正如我之前提到的，大多数“好男人”在童年时期与父亲的关系并不亲密。他们的父亲要么被动，要么疏远，要么缺席，要么被赋予了某种负面的定义。要重拾男子气概，他们需要审视与父亲的关系，并用成年人的眼光看待他们。

Matthew, a computer programmer in his mid-forties is a good example of this process. On one occasion in a No More Mr. Nice Guy! group, Matthew stated that he had no intention of attending his father's funeral if he should die. Months later, after exploring his relationship with his father in group, he decided to call and confront his dad when he didn't get invited to a family function.

四十五六岁的电脑程序员马修就是一个典型的例子。有一次，在“不再是好人！”的群里，马修说，如果父亲去世，他根本不打算参加他的葬礼。几个月后，在群里探讨了他和父亲的关系后，他决定打电话质问父亲，因为他没有被邀请参加一个家庭聚会。

Matt's mother had always portrayed his dad as a villain while representing herself as a victim. While talking with his father, Matthew came to the realization that even though his dad had problems, he wasn't bad like his mother had made him out to be. From this encounter, Matthew also realized that he had created a similar scenario with his wife in which he identified her as the villain and himself as the victim. Not only did that phone call to his father begin to change his relationship with his dad, but also with his wife.

马特的母亲一直把他的父亲描绘成恶棍，而自己则扮演受害者的角色。在与父亲交谈时，马修意识到，尽管父亲有问题，但他并不像母亲描述的那样坏。从这次经历中，马修也意识到，他和妻子之间也存在着类似的关系，他把妻子视为恶棍，而自己则是受害者。那通打给父亲的电话不仅改变了他与父亲的关系，也改变了他与妻子的关系。

For Nice Guys, reexamining their relationship with their fathers means seeing their dads through their own eyes as they really are. It means taking them out of the gutter or off the pedestal. This may require that Nice Guys hold them accountable by expressing their feelings to them — including rage and anger.

对于好男人来说，重新审视与父亲的关系意味着要用自己的眼睛看清父亲的真面目。这意味着要把父亲从低谷或高位中拉出来。这可能需要好男人通过向父亲表达自己的感受——包括愤怒和愤慨——来追究他们的责任。

This is essential, even if these men are dead. Sometimes this takes place in their fathers' presence, sometimes not. It's not so important that the father is available to do this work. What is essential is that recovering Nice Guys embrace the male heritage they and their fathers share.

即使这些男人已经去世，这一点也至关重要。有时，他们的父亲会在场，有时则不会。父亲是否有空做这件事并不重要。重要的是，正在康复的“好男人”们要拥抱他们和父亲共同拥有的男性传统。

The goal is to find a way to view fathers more accurately. Recovering Nice Guys can begin to accept these men for who they were and are — wounded human beings. This shift is essential if Nice Guys are going to view themselves more accurately, accept themselves for who they are, and reclaim their masculinity.

我们的目标是找到一种更准确地看待父亲的方式。正在康复的“好男人”可以开始接受这些男人的过去和现在——他们都是受伤的人。如果“好男人”想要更准确地看待自己，接受自己的本来面目，并重拾男子气概，这种转变至关重要。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #28

挣脱束缚活动 #28

Embracing masculinity involves coming to see Dad more accurately. To facilitate this process, create a list. On the left side, list a number of your father's characteristics. Write the opposite characteristic on the right side. Indicate where on the spectrum between the two that you see yourself.

拥抱男子气概意味着更准确地看待父亲。为了促进这一过程，请创建一个列表。在左侧列出你父亲的一些特征。在右侧写下相反的特征。指出你认为自己处于两者之间的哪个位置。

When recovering Nice Guys do this exercise they are often surprised at what they discover about their fathers and themselves.

当正在康复中的好男人做这个练习时，他们常常会对关于他们的父亲和他们自己的发现感到惊讶。

They often see how they have made their fathers into a caricature — a distortion of who they really are.

他们常常看到自己如何将父亲描绘成漫画人物——扭曲了他们的真实面目。

They may realize that if the man they have become is based on a reaction to how they saw their fathers, they too have become caricatures. Remember, the opposite of crazy is still crazy.

他们或许会意识到，如果自己之所以成为这样的人，是源于他们对父亲的认知，那么他们也成了漫画人物。记住，疯狂的反义词依然是疯狂。

They realize that if their lives are a reaction to Dad, then Dad is still in control.

他们意识到，如果他们的生活是对爸爸的反应，那么爸爸仍然掌控着一切。

They discover that they can be different from Dad without being the opposite.

他们发现，他们可以与爸爸有所不同，但不必完全相反。

They often come to realize that they have more traits in common with their fathers than they had previously realized or wanted to accept.

他们常常会意识到，他们与父亲拥有比他们之前意识到的或愿意接受的更多的共同特征。

*****************************


Passing The Benefits of Masculinity On To The Next Generation

将男子气概的好处传递给下一代

Part 1: Snakes And Snails
第一部分：蛇和蜗牛

As I work to raise my sons I realize that they are growing up in a world very much like the one that created my generation of Nice Guys. Boys are disconnected from men and are dependent on gaining the approval of women.

在努力抚养儿子的过程中，我意识到他们成长的世界，与我那一代“好男人”的世界非常相似。男孩与男性的联系被切断，依赖于获得女性的认可。

This was brought home to me a couple of years back when our family moved to a new home, the summer before my son Steve entered the fourth grade. When I attended his PTA open house I was jolted with a dose of reality. In kindergarten through fifth grade, there was only one male classroom teacher in the whole school. That's about a 20-to-1 ratio. As the teachers were introduced a grade at a time and then stood together on the gym floor, I received a visual picture of the environment in which little boys spend the most impressionable years of their lives.

几年前，我儿子史蒂夫升入四年级前的那个夏天，我们家搬进了新家，这让我深刻地体会到了这一点。参加他家的家长教师协会开放日时，我被现实深深震撼。从幼儿园到五年级，全校只有一名男教师。男女教师比例大约是20比1。当老师们一个年级一个年级地被介绍，然后一起站在体育馆的地板上时，我仿佛看到了小男孩们在人生中最易受影响的时期所处的环境。

As a recovering Nice Guy, I have a unique chance to pass a new model of masculinity not only to my sons and daughter, but to a whole generation of boys and girls. The more time I spend working with Nice Guys, the more I realize that this process represents a powerful tool for giving the next generation a saner model of what it means to be men and women.

作为一个正在康复的“好男人”，我拥有一个独一无二的机会，可以向我的儿女，乃至整整一代的男孩女孩，传递一种新的男子气概。我与“好男人”们合作的时间越长，就越意识到，这个过程是一个强大的工具，能够让下一代人对男人和女人的价值观有一个更理智的理解。

Unfortunately, our culture provides few rituals in which adult males help boys leave the comfort of a nursery ruled by women (home, preschool, school) and enter the world of adult manhood. Robert Bly discusses the importance of these rituals in his book Iron John.

遗憾的是，我们的文化中很少有仪式，让成年男性帮助男孩们离开女性统治的舒适托儿所（家庭、幼儿园、学校），步入成年男子的世界。罗伯特·布莱在他的著作《铁人约翰》中探讨了这些仪式的重要性。

In "primitive" societies, Bly writes, the boys are pretty much raised by the women until early adolescence. When it is time for the boys to leave the sphere of female influence and move into the men's world, the men of the tribe stage a raid. They put on their war paint, enter the village, and steal the boys away. The women, on cue, weep, protest, and do their best to hang onto the boys. After the men have taken the boys outside the village for their period of initiation, the women get together and ask, "How did I do? Was I believable?" In these cultures, the men and women work together to facilitate this process of transition and initiation.

布莱写道，在“原始”社会中，男孩们基本上是由女性抚养长大，直到青春期早期。当男孩们即将脱离女性的影响范围，进入男性的世界时，部落里的男人们会发动突袭。他们涂上战漆，进入村庄，把男孩们偷走。而女人则按部就班地哭泣、抗议，竭尽全力地留住男孩们。当男人们把男孩们带到村外进行成人礼后，女人们会聚在一起问：“我做得怎么样？我的表现让人信服吗？”在这些文化中，男人和女人会共同努力，促成这个转变和成人礼的过程。

These days, boys try to make this transition from a world ruled by women, but they can't do it on their own. I have a theory that the phase that adolescent boys pass through where they dress sloppily, look scraggly, act aggressively, hole up in their room, slouch, play loud music, swear, and spit a lot, are all unconsciously aimed at making themselves so repulsive that even their mothers can't stand them. This helps them break the symbiotic bonds with their moms. Nevertheless, these young men still need help from adult males in pulling away from their mothers without feeling guilt and shame and without overly self-destructive behaviors.

如今，男孩们试图从女性统治的世界转型，但他们无法独自完成。我有一个理论：青春期男孩会经历衣着邋遢、衣冠不整、行为攻击性、躲在房间里、懒散地坐着、大声播放音乐、骂脏话、随地吐痰的阶段，而这些行为都是在无意识地让自己变得令人厌恶，甚至连母亲都无法忍受。这有助于他们打破与母亲的共生关系。然而，这些年轻人仍然需要成年男性的帮助，才能摆脱母亲的束缚，而不会感到内疚和羞愧，也不会做出过度的自我毁灭行为。

I believe recovering Nice Guys can help boys find a saner model of what it means to be male in our culture. This is because there are certain things that boys can only learn from men. As Nice Guys embrace their masculinity, they can teach their sons what it means to be male. This includes how to handle their aggression, how to handle their libido, how to relate to women, how to bond with a man, and perhaps most importantly, how to embrace their own masculinity. Men teach these lessons both by example and by interaction with young boys.

我相信，让“好男人”回归正轨，可以帮助男孩们找到一个更理智的榜样，理解我们文化中男性的意义。这是因为有些东西只有男孩才能从男人身上学到。当“好男人”拥抱自己的男子气概时，他们可以教导儿子如何成为男人。这包括如何应对自己的攻击性，如何控制自己的性欲，如何与女性相处，如何与男性建立联系，或许最重要的是，如何拥抱自己的男子气概。男人通过身教和与男孩的互动来传授这些经验。

As a recovering Nice Guy, I also benefit from being with my adolescent sons and their friends. When I'm around my boys, I get to see unbridled maleness in action. Not only do I get to teach my sons how to handle their testosterone-related behaviors such as aggression and sexuality, they also show me how to embrace mine.

作为一个正在康复的“好男人”，与青春期的儿子们以及他们的朋友们相处也让我受益匪浅。当我和孩子们在一起时，我能看到他们肆无忌惮的男性气质。我不仅教会了儿子们如何应对与睾酮相关的行为，例如攻击性和性欲，他们也教会了我如何接纳自己的行为。

This reciprocal process requires time and interaction. Fathers need to take their sons hunting and fishing, work on cars with them, take them to work, coach their teams, take them to ball games, work out with them, take them on business trips, and let them tag along with them when they go out with the guys. All of these activities help boys move successfully into the male world.

这个互惠过程需要时间和互动。父亲需要带儿子去打猎、钓鱼、一起修车、带他们去上班、指导球队、带他们去看球赛、一起锻炼、带他们出差，以及在和男孩们一起出去玩的时候让他们陪伴自己。所有这些活动都有助于男孩成功地融入男性世界。

This process is not just limited to a man's biological sons. Nice Guys can get involved with young relatives, scouts, sports teams, school activities, or big brothers.

这个过程并不仅限于男人的亲生儿子。好男人可以与年轻的亲戚、童子军、运动队、学校活动或老大哥交往。

Trey, a single man in his late thirties, illustrates the power of this male influence. One night in his men's group, Trey talked about his nephew who was being raised by Trey's sister, a single mother. Trey had strong feelings about what was happening to his nephew because the boy was going through some of the same rebellion and acting out with alcohol that Trey had experienced at the same age. The group encouraged Trey to reach out to his nephew and be a positive male influence.

特雷是一位年近三十的单身男士，他展现了男性影响力的力量。一天晚上，在男士小组里，特雷谈到了他的侄子，他是由特雷的姐姐（一位单亲妈妈）抚养长大的。特雷对侄子的情况感同身受，因为他现在也经历了一些和他同龄时一样的叛逆和酗酒行为。小组成员鼓励特雷去帮助侄子，成为积极的男性影响力。

The next week in group, Trey was beaming. He told how he had taken his nephew to the hardware store and how the two of them had put together a workbench. His nephew was thrilled with the male contact. Trey came away with a feeling that he had done something positive to help change the direction of a struggling young boy.

接下来的一周，在小组活动中，特雷笑容满面。他讲述了自己带侄子去五金店，以及他们两人如何一起组装工作台的故事。侄子对这位男士的帮助感到非常兴奋。特雷离开时感觉自己做了一件积极的事情，帮助一个正在挣扎的小男孩改变了方向。

挣脱束缚活动 #29

How can you provide a healthy male support system for the boys and young men you know? List three boys along with an activity you can participate in with them.

如何为你认识的男孩和年轻男性提供健康的男性支持系统？列举三个男孩，并列出一项你可以和他们一起参加的活动。

Passing The Benefits On

传递利益

Part 2: Sugar And Spice

第二部分：糖和香料

Little girls can benefit from this reclaimed masculine energy as well. The men in one of my No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups showed me the benefits of male energy on a young girl in a powerful way. One of the members, LeMar, had a 12-year-old daughter with bone cancer. She had to have a leg removed and undergo chemotherapy and radiation. As a result, she had spent countless days and nights in a hospital bed. One Friday evening, while LeMar was sitting at the hospital with his daughter, the members of his men's group showed up unexpectedly to take him out to dinner.

小女孩也能从这种重新焕发的男性能量中受益。我的“不再是好人！”小组里的男人们向我展示了男性能量对年轻女孩的强大益处。其中一位成员勒马尔，他12岁的女儿患了骨癌。她不得不切除一条腿，并接受化疗和放疗。因此，她在医院的病床上度过了无数个日日夜夜。一个星期五的晚上，勒马尔正陪着女儿在医院里，他的男士小组成员突然出现，带他出去吃饭。

In addition to providing masculine support for LeMar during a difficult time, the men also produced an unexpected dividend. Energized by their presence, LeMar's daughter sat up and received hugs from each guy. That night she needed less medication and slept better than she had in weeks. The next day, all she could talk about was "her guys" who had come to visit her the night before.

除了在勒马尔困难时期给予她男性般的支持外，这些男人还带来了意想不到的收获。勒马尔的女儿在他们的陪伴下充满活力，坐了起来，接受了每个男人的拥抱。那天晚上，她需要的药物更少了，睡得也比几周前好多了。第二天，她谈论的全是前一天晚上来看望她的“她的男人们”。

Recovering Nice Guys can show their daughters what a real man looks like. Girls benefit by seeing their father set boundaries, ask for what they want in clear and direct ways, work hard, create, produce, have male friends, and make their own needs a priority. As with little boys, girls can learn what it means to be male both by watching their fathers and by interacting with them. This modeling will have a positive influence on their choice of future partners.

正在恢复“好男人”形象的父母可以向女儿们展示真正的男人是什么样子。女孩们会从看到父亲设定界限、以清晰直接的方式表达自己的需求、努力工作、创造、生产、结交男性朋友以及将自身需求放在首位中受益。和小男孩一样，女孩们也可以通过观察父亲以及与父亲互动来学习身为男性的意义。这种榜样作用将对她们未来伴侣的选择产生积极的影响。


A No-Lose Situation

一个不会输的局面

As Nice Guys reclaim their masculine energy, everyone wins. Not only does the recovering Nice Guy get to experience deeper bonds with men, but his relationships with women grow too. Perhaps most significantly, a whole new generation of boys and girls reap the benefits of seeing what a healthy male really looks like.

随着“好男人”重拾阳刚之气，人人皆赢。康复中的“好男人”不仅能与男性建立更深厚的联系，与女性的关系也随之发展。或许最重要的是，新一代的男孩女孩们将受益于了解健康男性的真实面貌。

Chapter 7

# ↩

第七章

Get The Love You Want: Success Strategies For Intimate Relationships

得到你想要的爱：亲密关系的成功策略


"I'm a victim of her dysfunction."

“我是她功能障碍的受害者。”

Karl, a successful businessman in his mid-thirties, began his first counseling session with the preceding analysis of his relationship with his wife Danita. Though over six-foot-two and professionally dressed in a dark suit and tie, Karl looked like a little boy sitting on the sofa in my office. Karl's frustration and helplessness regarding his most intimate relationship was unmistakable.

卡尔是一位三十五六岁的成功商人，他的第一次咨询始于对他与妻子丹妮塔关系的分析。尽管卡尔身高超过六英尺二英寸，身着深色西装打着领带，但坐在我办公室的沙发上，他看起来就像个小男孩。卡尔对他最亲密的关系的沮丧和无助显而易见。

As Karl continued to talk about his marriage, it became apparent that he was intimidated by his wife Danita. He claimed she was "angry all the time." When talking about her he used adjectives like "relentless" and "steamroller." Because of his fear of her anger, he lied to her and avoided interacting with her.

随着卡尔继续谈论他的婚姻，他明显被妻子丹妮塔吓到了。他声称她“总是生气”。谈到她时，他用了“无情的”和“压路机”之类的形容词。因为害怕她发怒，他撒了谎，避免与她互动。

"In many ways," Karl revealed, "Danita is just like my mother. There was just no pleasing Mom. I learned to just avoid her and tune her out when she was bitching. I got really good at lying and hiding what I didn't want her to know about. I guess I'm still pretty good at that today."

“在很多方面，”卡尔透露，“丹妮塔就像我妈妈一样。我根本无法取悦妈妈。我学会了躲着她，在她抱怨的时候不理她。我非常擅长撒谎，也擅长隐瞒我不想让她知道的事情。我想我现在仍然很擅长这个。”

As Karl brought the discussion back to the present, he revealed, "Every other area of my life is great. If it wasn't for Danita, my life would be perfect. I just don't think she knows how to be happy."

当卡尔把话题拉回到现实时，他透露道：“我生活的其他方面都很棒。如果没有丹妮塔，我的生活会很完美。我只是觉得她不知道如何获得快乐。”


Intimate Strangers

亲密的陌生人

In general, Nice Guys end up in my office for one of two reasons. Sometimes some hidden behavior —an affair, surfing for pornography on the Internet, smoking pot — has blown up in their face and created a crisis with their wife or girlfriend. More often, their call to a therapist is motivated by some problem or dissatisfaction in their most intimate relationship; their partner doesn't want to have sex as often as they do, she is depressed, angry, unavailable, or unfaithful (or all of the above).

一般来说，好男人来我的诊所通常有两个原因。有时，一些隐藏的行为——婚外情、上网浏览色情内容、吸食大麻——会让他们暴露无遗，并导致他们与妻子或女友之间出现危机。更常见的情况是，他们寻求心理咨询的动机是他们最亲密的关系中存在一些问题或不满；他们的伴侣不像他们那样频繁地发生性关系，她感到沮丧、愤怒、无法沟通或不忠（或者以上所有情况都有）。

These men usually believe there is a simple answer to their problem. Some of them are sure everything will be OK if they can just stop doing that one thing that keeps making their partner so angry. The rest of them are convinced that if they can get their partner to change, then life will be great.

这些男人通常相信他们的问题有一个简单的答案。有些人确信，只要他们能停止做那件让伴侣一直生气的事情，一切都会好起来。而其他人则坚信，只要他们能让伴侣改变，生活就会变得美好。

A note: The majority of the men with whom I work are heterosexual. Though I have observed similar relationship issues with gay men, many of the examples I use in this chapter reflect male/female relationships. More often than not, I will use the pronoun "she" or "her" when referring to the Nice Guy's partner.

注：我接触的男性大多是异性恋。虽然我也观察到男同性恋也存在类似的关系问题，但本章中我引用的许多例子都反映的是男女关系。在提及“好男人”的伴侣时，我通常会使用代词“她”或“她”。

Intimate relationships are often an area of great frustration and bewilderment for Nice Guys. Most Nice Guys profess a great desire for intimacy and happiness with their significant other. Nevertheless, intimacy represents an enigmatic riddle for the majority of these men.

亲密关系常常让“好男人”感到沮丧和困惑。大多数“好男人”都声称自己渴望与伴侣拥有亲密的、幸福的感情。然而，对于大多数“好男人”来说，亲密关系却如同一个谜。

Here is what I have concluded after several years of observing countless Nice Guys:

这是我多年来观察无数好男人后得出的结论：

Even though Nice Guys often profess a deep desire to be intimately connected with another individual, their internalized toxic shame and childhood survival mechanisms make such connections difficult and problematic.

尽管“好男人”常常表达出与他人建立亲密关系的强烈愿望，但他们内心的有害羞耻感和童年生存机制使得这种关系变得困难且成问题。


Why Nice Guys Struggle To Get The Love They Want

为什么好男人很难得到他们想要的爱情

There are a number of reasons why Nice Guys have difficulty getting the love they want. These include:

好男人难以获得他们想要的爱情有很多原因。其中包括：

  • Their toxic shame.
    他们的有毒羞耻感。
  • The dysfunctional relationships they co-create.
    他们共同创造的功能失调的关系。
  • Their patterns of enmeshment and avoidance.
    他们的纠缠和回避模式。
  • The familiar childhood relationship dynamics they recreate.
    他们重建的熟悉的童年关系动态。
  • Their unconscious need to remain monogamous to their mother.
    他们潜意识里需要对母亲保持一夫一妻制。
  • They are "bad enders".
    他们是“坏人”。



Toxic Shame Prevents Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

有毒的羞耻感会阻止好男人得到他们想要的爱

Intimacy implies vulnerability. I define intimacy as "knowing the self, being known by another, and knowing another." Intimacy requires two people who are willing to courageously look inward and make themselves totally visible to another. Internalized toxic shame makes this kind of exposure feel life-threatening for Nice Guys.

亲密意味着脆弱。我将亲密定义为“了解自己，被他人了解，并了解他人”。亲密需要两个人都愿意勇敢地审视内心，并完全展现自我。内化的毒性羞耻感会让这种暴露让“好男人”感到生命受到威胁。

Intimacy, by its nature, would require the Nice Guy to look into the abyss of his most inner self and allow others to peer into these same places. It would require the Nice Guy to let someone get close enough to see into all the nooks and crannies of his soul. This terrifies Nice Guys, because being known means being found out. All Nice Guys have worked their entire lives to become what they believe others want them to be while trying to hide their perceived flaws. The demands of intimacy represent everything Nice Guys fear most.

亲密关系的本质在于，它要求好男人洞察自己内心深处的深渊，并允许他人窥视这些深渊。这要求好男人允许他人足够接近，看透他灵魂的每一个角落。这让好男人感到恐惧，因为被了解就意味着被发现。所有好男人都毕生致力于成为他们认为别人希望他们成为的样子，同时努力隐藏自己被认为的缺点。亲密关系的要求正是好男人最害怕的一切。


Co-Creating Dysfunctional Relationships Prevents Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

共同创造不正常的关系会阻止好男人获得他们想要的爱

The Nice Guy's ongoing attempt to hide his perceived badness makes intimacy a challenge. The moment Nice Guys enter a relationship they begin a balancing act. In relationships, a life-and-death struggle is played out to balance their fear of vulnerability with their fear of isolation. Vulnerability means someone may get too close to them and see how bad they are. Nice Guys are convinced that when others make this discovery, these people will hurt them, shame them, or leave them.

好男人总是试图隐藏自己明显的坏毛病，这让亲密关系变得棘手。好男人一旦进入一段感情，就开始寻找平衡。在感情中，他们会在对脆弱的恐惧和对孤立的恐惧之间进行一场生死搏斗。脆弱意味着有人可能会过于接近他们，发现他们的坏毛病。好男人坚信，当别人发现这一点时，他们会伤害他们、羞辱他们，甚至离开他们。

The alternative doesn't seem any better. Isolating themselves from others recreates the abandonment experiences that were so terrifying in childhood.

另一种选择似乎也好不到哪里去。将自己与他人隔离，会让他们重温童年时被遗弃的恐怖经历。

In order to balance his fear of vulnerability and fear of abandonment, a Nice Guy needs help. He finds it in people who are equally wounded and also have difficulty with intimacy. Together they co-create relationships that simultaneously frustrate all parties while protecting them from their fear of being found out.

为了平衡对脆弱和被抛弃的恐惧，好男人需要帮助。他从那些同样受过伤、同样难以建立亲密关系的人那里找到了帮助。他们共同创造了一种关系，这种关系既让双方都感到沮丧，又保护他们免受被发现的恐惧。

Even though it may look like many of the problems Nice Guys experience in relationships are caused by the baggage their partner brings with them, this is not the case. It is the relationship the Nice Guy and his partner co-create that is the problem.

尽管看起来好男人在感情中遇到的很多问题似乎都是由伴侣带来的包袱造成的，但事实并非如此。问题在于好男人和伴侣共同创造的关系。

It is true that Nice Guys often pick partners who appear to be projects, and indeed, they do at times pick some pretty messed up people. The fact that these partners may have challenges — they are single moms, they have financial problems, they are angry, addictive, depressed, overweight, non-sexual, or unable to be faithful — is precisely the reason Nice Guys invite these people into their lives. As long as attention is focused on the flaws of the partner, it is diverted away from the internalized toxic shame of the Nice Guy. This balancing act ensures that the Nice Guy's closest relationship will most likely be his least intimate.

诚然，好男人经常会选择那些看起来像项目一样的伴侣，而且他们有时确实会选择一些相当混乱的人。这些伴侣可能面临挑战——他们是单亲妈妈，有经济问题，易怒、上瘾、抑郁、超重、没有性生活或无法忠诚——这正是好男人邀请这些人进入自己生活的原因。只要注意力集中在伴侣的缺点上，就能转移人们对好男人内化有害羞耻感的注意力。这种平衡行为确保了好男人最亲密的关系很可能是他最不亲密的关系。


Patterns Of Enmeshment And Avoidance Prevent Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

纠缠和回避模式阻碍了好男人获得他们想要的爱

This intimacy balancing act gets played out in two distinct ways for Nice Guys. The first is through becoming overly involved in an intimate relationship at the expense of one's self and other outside interests. The second is through being emotionally unavailable to a primary partner while playing the Nice Guy role outside of the relationship. I call the first type of Nice Guy an enmesher and the second type an avoider .

这种亲密关系的平衡对“好男人”来说有两种截然不同的表现方式。第一种是过度投入亲密关系，牺牲了自我和其他外部利益。第二种是，在感情之外扮演“好男人”的角色，却对主要伴侣缺乏情感上的投入。我把第一种“好男人”称为“纠缠者”，第二种“好男人”称为“逃避者”。

The enmeshing Nice Guy makes his partner his emotional center. His world revolves around her. She is more important than his work, his buddies, his hobbies. He will do whatever it takes to make her happy.

缠人如缠的“好男人”会把伴侣当成自己的情感中心。他的世界都围着她转。她比他的工作、朋友、爱好都重要。他会不惜一切代价让她开心。

He will give her gifts, try to fix her problems, and arrange his schedule to be with her. He will gladly sacrifice his wants and needs to win her love. He will even tolerate her bad moods, rage attacks, addictions, and emotional or sexual unavailability — all because he "loves her so much."

他会送她礼物，尽力帮她解决问题，并安排好自己的时间陪伴她。为了赢得她的爱，他甘愿牺牲自己的欲望和需求。他甚至会容忍她的坏心情、暴怒、成瘾，以及情感或性方面的冷漠——这一切都是因为他“太爱她了”。

I sometimes refer to enmeshing Nice Guys as table dogs . They are like little dogs who hover beneath the table just in case a scrap happens to fall their way. Enmeshing Nice Guys do this same hovering routine around their partner just in case she happens to drop him a scrap of sexual interest, a scrap of her time, a scrap of a good mood, or a scrap of her attention. Even though they are settling for the leftovers that fall from the table, enmeshing Nice Guys think they are getting something really good.

我有时把那些纠缠不休的好男人比作“餐桌狗”。他们就像小狗一样，在桌子底下徘徊，就为了不让一点残羹剩饭掉到自己身上。纠缠不休的好男人也会在伴侣身边徘徊，就为了不让伴侣给他一点性趣、一点时间、一点好心情，或者一点注意力。即使他们只是在讨好桌上掉下来的残羹剩饭，纠缠不休的好男人也觉得自己得到了真正的好处。

On the surface it may appear that the enmeshing Nice Guy desires, and is available for an intimate relationship, but this is an illusion. The Nice Guy's pursuing and enmeshing behavior is an attempt to hook up an emotional hose to his partner. This hose is used to suck the life out of her and fill an empty place inside of him. The Nice Guy's partner unconsciously picks up on this agenda and works like hell to make sure the Nice Guy can't get close enough to hook up the hose. Consequently, the Nice Guy's partner is often seen as the one preventing the closeness the Nice Guy desires.

表面上，好男人似乎渴望并愿意建立亲密关系，但这不过是幻觉。好男人的追求和纠缠行为，其实是想在伴侣身上挂上一根情感软管。这根软管会吸干伴侣的生命，填补他内心的空虚。好男人的伴侣会无意识地察觉到这种意图，并拼命确保好男人无法靠近到足以连接这根软管的程度。因此，好男人的伴侣常常被视为阻碍好男人获得他渴望的亲密关系的人。

The avoider can be a little tougher to get a handle on. The avoiding Nice Guy seems to put his job, hobby, parents, and everything else before his primary relationship. He may not seem like a Nice Guy to his partner at all because he is often nice to everyone else but her. He may volunteer to work on other people's cars. He may spend weekends fixing his mother's roof. He may work two or three jobs.

回避型人格可能更难驾驭。回避型好男人似乎把工作、爱好、父母以及其他一切都置于他的主要关系之上。在伴侣眼中，他可能根本不像个好男人，因为他经常对除了她之外的所有人都很好。他可能会主动去修理别人的车。他可能会利用周末去修理他母亲的屋顶。他可能身兼两三职。

He may coach his children's sports teams. Even though he may not follow his partner around and cater to her every whim, he still operates from a covert contract that since he is a Nice Guy, his partner should be available to him, even if he isn't available to her.

他或许会执教孩子们的运动队。即便他不会像伴侣那样事事跟随，满足她的每一个心血来潮，但他仍然秉持着一种隐秘的契约：既然他是个好男人，即使伴侣无法陪伴他，他也应该对他言听计从。

Both patterns, enmeshing and avoiding, inhibit any real kind of intimacy from occurring. They may help the Nice Guy feel safe, but they won't help him feel loved.

纠缠和回避这两种模式都会阻碍任何真正的亲密关系的发生。它们或许能让好男人感到安全，但却无法让他感受到爱。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #30

挣脱束缚活动 #30

Are you an enmesher or an avoider in your present relationship? How would your partner see you? Does the pattern ever change? What roles have you played in past relationships?

在目前的感情关系中，你是纠缠型还是逃避型？你的伴侣会怎么看待你？这种模式会改变吗？在过去的感情关系中，你扮演了什么角色？

*****************************


Recreating Familiar Childhood Relationship Patterns Prevents Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

重现熟悉的童年关系模式会阻止好男人获得他们想要的爱

It is human nature to be attracted to what is familiar. Because of this reality, Nice Guys create adult relationships that mirror the dynamics of their dysfunctional childhood relationships. For example: If listening to his mother's problems as a child gave a Nice Guy a sense of connection, he may grow up believing such behavior equals intimacy. In order to feel valuable and connected in his adult relationships, he will have to pick a partner who has her fair share of problems.

被熟悉的事物吸引是人类的天性。正因如此，好男人成年后建立的关系往往与他童年时期不和谐的关系如出一辙。例如：如果小时候倾听母亲诉说烦恼能给好男人带来一种归属感，那么长大后他可能会认为这种行为等同于亲密。为了在成年后的关系中感受到价值感和归属感，他必须选择一个和母亲有着相同烦恼的伴侣。

If he was trained to caretake and fix needy or dependent family members, the Nice Guy may find a way to do the same in his adult relationships.

如果他接受过照顾和解决有需要或依赖的家庭成员的训练，那么好男人可能会在成年人的关系中找到做同样事情的方法。

If he believed he could only get his own needs met after he had met the needs of other more important people, the Nice Guy may sacrifice himself for the sake of his partner.

如果好男人认为只有在满足了其他更重要的人的需求之后才能满足自己的需求，那么他可能会为了伴侣而牺牲自己。

If he was abandoned in childhood, he may choose partners who are unavailable or unfaithful.

如果他在童年时期被抛弃，他可能会选择那些不值得信任或不忠诚的伴侣。

If he grew up with an angry, demeaning, or controlling parent, he may choose a partner with similar traits.

如果他在一个愤怒、贬低或控制欲强的父母身边长大，他可能会选择具有类似特征的伴侣。

Occasionally, the person the Nice Guy chooses to help him recreate his childhood relationship patterns isn't the way he unconsciously needs her to be when the relationship begins. If this is the case, he will often help her become what he needs. He may project upon her one or more traits of his parents. He may act as if she is a certain way, even when she isn't. His unconscious dysfunctional needs may literally force his partner to respond in an equally dysfunctional way.

偶尔，好男人选择帮助他重塑童年关系模式的人，并非他在这段关系开始时潜意识里想要的样子。如果是这样，他通常会帮助她成为他需要的样子。他可能会将自己父母的一个或多个特质投射到她身上。他可能会表现得好像她是某种样子，即使她并非如此。他潜意识里的功能失调的需求，可能会迫使他的伴侣以同样功能失调的方式回应。

For example. As a child, I never knew what kind of mood my father would be in when I came into the house. More often than not, it wasn't good. I learned to come home prepared for the worst. I later recreated the same pattern in my marriage. I projected my father's unpredictable moods onto my wife and would frequently arrive home prepared for her to be angry. Even if she was in a good mood, my defensiveness often triggered some kind of confrontation between us. Thus Elizabeth came to look like my angry father and I perpetuated a familiar, though dysfunctional relationship dynamic.

比如，小时候，我从来不知道我回家时父亲会是什么心情。很多时候，父亲的心情都不好。我学会了回家做好最坏的打算。后来，我在婚姻中也重蹈覆辙。我把父亲反复无常的情绪投射到妻子身上，经常回家时做好她生气的准备。即使她心情好，我的防御性也常常会引发我们之间的某种冲突。于是，伊丽莎白变成了我愤怒的父亲的样子，而我则延续着一种熟悉却不正常的人际关系。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #31

挣脱束缚活动 #31

We tend to be attracted to people who have some of the worst traits of both of our parents. Instead of blaming your partner for your unconscious choice, identify the ways in which she helps you recreate familiar relationship patterns from your childhood. Share this with your partner.

我们往往会被那些身上同时拥有父母某些糟糕特质的人所吸引。与其责怪你的伴侣让你无意识地做出选择，不如想想她是如何帮助你重现童年时期熟悉的恋爱模式的。和你的伴侣分享这一点。

*****************************


The Unconscious Need To Remain Monogamous To Mom Prevents Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

潜意识里对妈妈保持一夫一妻制的需求，使得好男人得不到他们想要的爱

The tendency of Nice Guys to be monogamous to their mothers seriously inhibits having any kind of a genuinely intimate relationship with a partner in adulthood. Nice Guys are creative in finding ways to maintain this childhood bond. What all of these behaviors have in common is that they all effectively insure that the Nice Guy will not be able to bond in any significant way with any woman except his mother.

好男人倾向于对母亲保持专一的倾向，这严重阻碍了他们成年后与伴侣建立任何真正亲密的关系。好男人善于想方设法维持这种童年时期的纽带。所有这些行为的共同点在于，它们都有效地确保了好男人无法与母亲以外的任何女性建立任何重要的联系。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #32

挣脱束缚活动 #32

The following are a few of the ways Nice Guys unconsciously maintain a monogamous bond to their mothers. Look over the list. Note any of the behavior patterns that may serve to keep you monogamous to your mother. Share this information with a safe person.

以下是“好男人”无意识地与母亲保持一夫一妻制关系的几种方式。仔细阅读清单。注意任何可能有助于你与母亲保持一夫一妻制的行为模式。请将这些信息分享给一个安全的人。

  • Over-involvement with work or hobbies.
    过度投入工作或爱好。
  • Creating relationships with people who need fixing.
    与需要修复的人建立关系。
  • Addictions to drugs or alcohol.
    吸毒或酗酒。
  • Sexual addictions to pornography, masturbation, fantasy, chat lines, or hookers.
    对色情、手淫、幻想、聊天热线或妓女的性成瘾。
  • Affairs.
    婚外情。
  • Sexual dysfunction — lack of desire, inability to get or maintain an erection, or premature ejaculation.
    性功能障碍——缺乏欲望、无法勃起或无法维持勃起、早泄。
  • Forming relationships with women who are angry, sick, depressive, compulsive, addicted, unfaithful, or otherwise unavailable.
    与愤怒、生病、抑郁、强迫、上瘾、不忠或其他无法得到的女人建立关系。
  • Avoiding intercourse or taking vows of celibacy.
    避免性交或发誓独身。

*****************************


Being Bad Enders Prevents Nice Guys From Getting The Love They Want

坏结局会阻止好人得到他们想要的爱

Finally, Nice Guys have difficulty getting the love they want because they spend too much time trying to make bad relationships work. Basically, Nice Guys suffer from the age-old problem of looking for love in all the wrong places. If a Nice Guy spends all of his time stuck in a bad relationship, it pretty much guarantees he won't find one that might work better.

最后，好男人很难得到他们想要的爱情，因为他们花了太多时间试图维持糟糕的感情。基本上，好男人都犯了一个老毛病，那就是总是在错误的地方寻找爱情。如果一个好男人把所有时间都花在糟糕的感情上，那么他几乎肯定找不到更好的。

When healthy individuals recognize that they have created a relationship that is not a good fit, or that a partner they have chosen lacks the basic qualities they desire, they move on. Not Nice Guys. Due to their conditioning, Nice Guys just keep trying harder to get a non-workable situation to work or get someone to be something they are not. This tendency frustrates everybody involved.

当健康的人意识到自己建立的感情并不合适，或者他们选择的伴侣缺乏他们渴望的基本品质时，他们就会离开。好男人则不然。由于他们自身的条件反射，好男人只会更加努力地让一个不可行的局面变得顺利，或者让对方变成他们自己不喜欢的样子。这种倾向会让所有相关人员感到沮丧。

Even when Nice Guys do try to end a relationship, they are not very good at it. They frequently do it too late and in indirect, blaming, or deceitful ways. They typically have to do it several times before it sticks. I often joke that, on average, it takes Nice Guys about nine attempts to end a relationship.

即使“好男人”真的试图结束一段感情，他们也不太擅长。他们经常行动太迟，而且方式迂回曲折、指责或欺骗。他们通常要反复尝试几次才能成功。我经常开玩笑说，平均而言，“好男人”要尝试九次才能结束一段感情。

(Unfortunately, this isn't far from the truth).

（不幸的是，这与事实相差不远）。


Strategies For Building Successful Relationships

建立成功关系的策略

There are no perfect relationships. There are no perfect partners. Relationships by their very nature are chaotic, eventful, and challenging. The second part of this chapter is not a plan for finding a perfect partner or creating the perfect relationship. It is simply a strategy for doing what works. By adapting the points below and changing the way in which they live their lives, recovering Nice Guys will change the way they have relationships. Nice Guys can:

没有完美的关系，也没有完美的伴侣。关系的本质是混乱、多变且充满挑战的。本章的第二部分并非旨在帮助人们找到完美的伴侣或建立完美的关系，而只是一种行之有效的策略。通过调整以下几点并改变生活方式，恢复“好男人”的生活方式将改变他们的人际关系。“好男人”可以：

  • Approve of themselves.
    认可自己。
  • Put themselves first.
    把自己放在第一位。
  • Reveal themselves to safe people.
    向安全的人展现自己。
  • Eliminate covert contracts.
    消除隐性合同。
  • Take responsibility for their own needs.
    对自己的需求负责。
  • Surrender.
    屈服。
  • Dwell in reality.
    活在现实中。
  • Express their feelings.
    表达自己的感受。
  • Develop integrity.
    培养正直。
  • Set boundaries.
    设定界限。
  • Embrace their masculinity.
    拥抱自己的男子气概。

Previous chapters have included illustrations of what can happen to relationships when Nice Guys begin to make these life changes. Let's examine a little more closely how applying a couple of these life strategies can help recovering Nice Guys get the love they want.

前几章已经举例说明了当“好男人”开始做出这些生活改变时，他们的感情会发生什么变化。让我们更深入地探讨一下，如何运用这些生活策略来帮助“好男人”重获他们想要的爱情。



*****************************

A Warning

警告

If you are in a relationship, the program of recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome presented in this book will seriously affect you and your partner. One of two things will happen:

如果你正在恋爱中，本书中介绍的“好人综合症”康复方案将会对你和你的伴侣产生重大影响。以下两种情况之一将会发生：

1) Your present relationship will begin to grow and evolve in exciting and unpredictable ways.

1）你们现在的关系将开始以令人兴奋和难以预测的方式成长和发展。

2) Your present relationship will be sent to a long overdue grave.

2) 你现在的感情将会被送进早就该埋葬的坟墓。

*****************************


Learning To Approve Of Themselves Helps Nice Guys Get The Love They Want

学会认可自己能帮助好男人获得他们想要的爱

The essence of recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome is the conscious decision to live one's life just as one desires. I frequently encourage recovering Nice Guys to be just who they are, without reservation . I support them in deciding what is right for them and being that with all of their energy for the whole world to see. The people who like them just as they are will hang around. The people who don't, won't.

摆脱“好人综合症”的本质在于自觉地选择按照自己想要的方式生活。我经常鼓励正在康复中的“好人”们做回自己，毫无保留。我支持他们决定什么是适合自己的，并全力以赴地展现给全世界。喜欢他们本来面目的人会继续留在他们身边。不喜欢他们的人，就别想再见了。

This is the only way to have a healthy relationship. No one really wants to believe that they have to be false or hide who they really are to get someone to love them or stay with them. Yet, this is a common dynamic in the intimate relationships Nice Guys create.

这是拥有健康关系的唯一途径。没有人真的愿意相信，为了得到爱或陪伴，自己必须虚伪或隐藏真我。然而，这却是“好男人”创造的亲密关系中常见的现象。

George is a good example of what can happen when a recovering Nice Guy decides to start pleasing himself and stop pleasing his partner. Throughout his relationship with his wife Susan, George's primary goal was to make her happy. Over a period of five years George gave up hunting and fishing (two of his passions), quit hanging out with his friends, turned the control of his finances over to Susan, and supported her in quitting her job because she was unhappy at work. These changes occurred gradually.

乔治是一个很好的例子，展现了当一个正在康复的“好男人”决定开始取悦自己，不再取悦伴侣时会发生什么。在与妻子苏珊交往期间，乔治的首要目标就是让她快乐。在五年的时间里，乔治放弃了打猎和钓鱼（他的两项爱好），不再和朋友出去玩，将财务管理权移交给苏珊，并支持她因为工作不开心而辞职。这些改变是循序渐进的。

All were an attempt on George's part to please Susan. Nevertheless, Susan was rarely happy. By the time George joined a No More Mr. Nice Guy! group, he felt helpless and resentful and was ready to leave his wife. George saw Susan as the cause of the frustration he was feeling. George spent the first few weeks in our group complaining about his wife.

乔治所做的一切都是为了取悦苏珊。然而，苏珊却很少感到快乐。乔治加入“不再是好人！”小组后，感到无助和怨恨，并准备离开妻子。乔治认为苏珊是他沮丧的根源。乔治在我们小组的最初几周里一直在抱怨他的妻子。

Eventually, the group members began to confront George on his victim role and challenged him to do something different instead of blaming Susan. It took a few more months, but George began to change. The most significant change was a conscious decision to quit trying to make Susan happy. He realized that his attempts to please her weren't working and were causing him to feel resentful.

最终，小组成员开始质问乔治，让他改正受害者的角色，而不是责怪苏珊。几个月过去了，乔治开始改变。最重要的改变是他有意识地决定不再试图取悦苏珊。他意识到自己取悦苏珊的尝试并没有奏效，反而让他感到怨恨。

George began by setting aside one weekend a month to go hunting or fishing. When Susan tried several different methods to manipulate him or guilt him out of his decision, he held fast. Next, instead of handing his paycheck over to Susan to control, he began giving himself an allowance to spend how he wanted. This too drew resistance from his wife. Perhaps the most frightening step was when George set up a budget and told Susan that if she wanted more control over the income, she would have to go back to working full time.

乔治一开始每个月都会留出一个周末去打猎或钓鱼。苏珊想尽各种办法操纵他或让他内疚，让他改变决定，但他始终坚持。接下来，他不再把工资交给苏珊管，而是开始给自己零花钱，想怎么花就怎么花。这也引起了妻子的反对。或许最让他害怕的是，乔治制定了预算，并告诉苏珊，如果她想更好地掌控收入，就必须回去全职工作。

Ironically, two things began to happen. George felt less like a victim and actually started having more positive feelings toward Susan. Second, Susan began taking charge of her own life and became less dependent on George. After about a year in group, George shared how much more content he was and how much his marriage had improved. He gave credit to the group members who supported him in finding the courage to start pleasing himself and stop trying to make Susan happy.

讽刺的是，两件事开始发生。乔治不再觉得自己是受害者，反而开始对苏珊产生更积极的感觉。其次，苏珊开始掌控自己的生活，不再依赖乔治。加入小组大约一年后，乔治分享了他感到更加满足，婚姻也得到了显著改善。他感谢小组成员的支持，让他找到了勇气，开始取悦自己，不再试图取悦苏珊。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #33

挣脱束缚活动 #33

List some of the ways you try to please your partner. What changes would you make if you did not have to worry about making her happy?

列举一些你试图取悦伴侣的方法。如果你不必担心如何让她开心，你会做出哪些改变？

*****************************


Setting Boundaries Helps Nice Guys Get The Love They Want

设定界限帮助好男人获得他们想要的爱

The subject of boundaries was presented in Chapter Five. Nowhere is the issue of boundary setting more important for Nice Guys than in their most intimate relationships. By setting healthy boundaries with their partners, Nice Guys create situations in which both they and their partner can feel safe to be vulnerable and experience true intimacy.

界限的主题已在第五章中讨论过。对于好男人来说，设定界限在他们最亲密的关系中尤为重要。通过与伴侣设定健康的界限，好男人能够创造一种环境，让他们和伴侣都能感到安全，能够展现脆弱，体验真正的亲密。

I show Nice Guys, often with their partners watching, how to step up to their line and set boundaries. On more than one occasion, I have had the partner of a Nice Guy applaud during the demonstration. The Nice Guy will turn, slack-jawed, and say, "You mean you want me to stand up to you, dear?"

我经常在好男人的伴侣面前，向他们展示如何坚守自己的底线，设定界限。好男人的伴侣不止一次在演示过程中鼓掌。好男人会转过身，目瞪口呆地说：“亲爱的，你是说你想让我反抗你吗？”

"Of course I do," she will respond, "I don't want to be married to someone I can walk all over."

“当然了，”她会回答，“我可不想嫁给一个我可以随意欺负的人。”

Then I warn him. "Your wife is telling you the truth. She doesn't feel safe knowing she can push you around. She wants to know that you will stand up to her. That is how she will feel secure in the relationship. But, here's the catch. She has to test to see if she can trust you. The first time you set a boundary with her she may react intensely. She will push against it. She will tell you that you are wrong for setting that boundary. She will do her best to find out if your boundary is for real."

然后我警告他：“你妻子说的是实话。她知道自己可以对你指手画脚，心里不踏实。她想知道你会不会反抗她。这样她才能在这段关系中感到安全。但是，问题就在这里。她需要测试一下，看看她是否能信任你。你第一次和她设定界限时，她可能会反应强烈。她会反抗。她会告诉你，你设定界限是错误的。她会竭尽全力去检验你的界限是否真实。”

When a recovering Nice Guy sets boundaries with his partner, it makes her feel secure. In general, when women feel secure, they feel loved. She will also come to know that if her partner will stand up to her, he is also likely to stand up for her. Setting boundaries also creates respect. When a Nice Guy fails to set boundaries it communicates to his partner that he doesn't really honor himself, so why should she?

当一个正在康复中的好男人与伴侣设定界限时，这会让她感到安全。一般来说，当女性感到安全时，她们会感受到爱。她也会明白，如果她的伴侣敢于反抗她，他也可能会支持她。设定界限也能赢得尊重。当一个好男人不设定界限时，这等于在向他的伴侣传递一个信息：他根本不尊重自己，所以她又何必呢？

To help Nice Guys decide if they need to set a boundary with a particular behavior, I have them apply the Second Date Rule. Using the second date rule, Nice Guys ask themselves, "If this behavior had occurred on the second date, would there have been a third?" This question helps them see if they have been putting up with something that they shouldn't.

为了帮助好男人判断是否需要为某种行为设定界限，我让他们运用“第二次约会规则”。运用“第二次约会规则”，好男人会问自己：“如果这种行为在第二次约会时发生，还会有第三次吗？”这个问题能帮助他们了解自己是否一直在忍受不该忍受的事情。

When trying to decide how to deal with a behavior they have deemed unacceptable, I encourage Nice Guys to apply the Healthy Male Rule. Following this rule of thumb, they simply ask themselves, "How would a healthy male handle this situation?" For some reason, just asking this question connects them with their intuitive wisdom and helps them access the power they need to respond appropriately.

当好男人在思考如何应对他们认为不可接受的行为时，我鼓励他们运用“健康男性法则”。按照这条经验法则，他们只需问自己：“一个健康的男人会如何处理这种情况？” 不知何故，仅仅问这个问题就能让他们与直觉智慧联系起来，并帮助他们获得做出适当回应所需的力量。

Once the Nice Guy knows he can set a boundary any time he needs to, he can let people move toward him, get close, have feelings, be sexual, and so on. He can let these things happen because he is confident that at any point, if he begins to feel uncomfortable, he can say "stop," "no," or "slow down," or can remove himself. He can do whatever he needs to do to take care of himself.

一旦好男人知道他可以在任何需要的时候设定界限，他就可以允许别人靠近他、亲近他、产生感情、发生性关系等等。他之所以允许这些事情发生，是因为他相信，如果在任何时候，他开始感到不舒服，他可以说“停”、“不”或“慢下来”，或者干脆离开。他可以做任何他需要做的事情来照顾自己。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #34

挣脱束缚活动 #34

Are there any areas in your personal relationships in which you avoid setting appropriate boundaries? Do you:

在你的人际关系中，你是否在某些方面逃避设定适当的界限？你是否：

  • Tolerate intolerable behavior.
    容忍无法容忍的行为。
  • Avoid dealing with a situation because it might cause conflict.
    避免处理可能导致冲突的情况。
  • Not ask for what you want.
    不要要求你想要的东西。
  • Sacrifice yourself to keep the peace.
    牺牲自己来维持和平。

If you applied the Second Date rule or the Healthy Male rule to these situations, how might you change your behavior?

如果您将“第二次约会”规则或“健康男性”规则应用于这些情况，您会如何改变自己的行为？

Additional Strategies For Happy, Healthy Relationships

建立幸福健康关系的其他策略

In addition to the program of recovery presented in previous chapters of No More Mr. Nice Guy!, there are a few additional strategies that will help Nice Guys get the love they want. These include:

除了《不再是好人！》前几章介绍的恢复计划外，还有一些额外的策略可以帮助好男人获得他们想要的爱。这些包括：

  • Focusing on their relationship, not their partner.
    关注他们的关系，而不是他们的伴侣。
  • Not reinforcing undesirable behaviors.
    不强化不良行为。
  • Doing something different.
    做一些不同的事情。



Focusing On Their Relationship, Not Their Partner Helps Nice Guys Get The Love They Want

专注于他们的关系，而不是他们的伴侣，帮助好男人得到他们想要的爱

Wounded people are attracted to wounded people. When Nice Guys enter a relationship, they frequently choose partners who look more dysfunctional than they do. This creates a dangerous illusion that one of them is sicker than the other. This is a distortion, because healthy people are not attracted to unhealthy people — and vice versa. I frequently tell couples that if you have one obviously wounded person in a relationship, you always have two. No exception.

受伤的人会被受伤的人吸引。好男人在开始一段感情时，往往会选择看起来比自己更不正常的伴侣。这会造成一种危险的错觉，让人觉得他们中有一个比另一个更病态。这是一种扭曲，因为健康的人不会被不健康的人吸引——反之亦然。我经常告诉情侣们，如果一段感情中有一个明显受伤的人，那么你们之间总会有两个。没有例外。

When my wife Elizabeth and I first got together, we created a system in which she was identified as the broken one while I was designated the healthy one. These scripts worked well for both of us until she started going to counseling. One day, she came home from a therapy session and announced that she had discovered that I was just as messed up as she was. Because I couldn't entertain the idea that I was "messed up," I responded, "No, you are really finding out that you are just as healthy as I am."

我和妻子伊丽莎白刚在一起的时候，我们创建了一个系统，她被认定为“残缺的一方”，而我则被认定为“健康的一方”。这些“剧本”对我们俩都很有效，直到她开始接受心理咨询。有一天，她做完心理咨询回家后，告诉我说，她发现我和她一样糟糕。因为我无法接受“糟糕”的想法，所以我回答说：“不，你实际上是在发现，你和我一样健康。”

The relationship system we had created together allowed both of us to play familiar, yet dysfunctional roles. Unfortunately, it also prevented any kind of real intimacy until Elizabeth began to challenge the status quo. I've listened to countless Nice Guys who have formed relationships like the one Elizabeth and I initially created. These men have the belief that they are victims to their "sick" partner's dysfunction. This illusion keeps everyone involved stuck in repetitive, ineffective patterns.

我们共同构建的关系体系让我们彼此扮演着熟悉却又功能失调的角色。不幸的是，它也阻碍了任何真正的亲密关系，直到伊丽莎白开始挑战现状。我听过无数“好男人”建立起像我和伊丽莎白最初建立的那种关系。这些男人相信自己是“病态”伴侣功能失调的受害者。这种错觉让每个人都陷入了重复、无效的模式。

By focusing on their relationship instead of their partner, recovering Nice Guys are able to use their partner to get in touch with their childhood experiences of abandonment, neglect, abuse, and smothering. They can use this information to better understand why they have created the kind of relationship system they have. This process enables them to make changes that allow them to get what they want in their intimate relationships.

通过专注于他们的关系而不是伴侣，正在康复中的“好男人”能够利用伴侣来触及他们童年时期被遗弃、忽视、虐待和窒息的经历。他们可以利用这些信息更好地理解自己为何会创建这样的关系体系。这个过程使他们能够做出改变，从而在亲密关系中得到自己想要的东西。

Instead of saying "if she would just. . .", the recovering Nice Guy has to ask,

正在康复中的好男人不应该说“如果她愿意……”，而应该问，

"Why did I need to co-create this relationship?"

“我为什么需要共同建立这种关系？”

"How does this relationship let me play familiar roles?"

“这段感情怎么能让我演熟悉的角色呢？”

"How does this relationship let me meet unconscious needs?"

“这种关系如何让我满足潜意识的需求？”

"Why did I invite this person into my life?"

“我为什么要邀请这个人进入我的生活？”

When the recovering Nice Guy begins asking these kinds of questions, he can begin to see his significant other as a partner in healing. This not only shifts how he views his partner, but also allows him to address childhood issues that prevent him from having a truly intimate relationship.

当正在康复中的好男人开始问这些问题时，他就能开始把自己的另一半视为疗愈的伙伴。这不仅改变了他看待伴侣的方式，也让他得以正视阻碍他建立真正亲密关系的童年问题。

In the beginning of this chapter, we met Karl — whose wife Danita seemed as hard to please as his cold, critical mother. Karl never knew when his mom might get angry, criticize, or shame him. In adulthood, Karl co-created a similar dynamic with Danita. When she got angry, Karl would use all of his childhood survival mechanisms, like avoidance and withdrawal, to try to cope. Karl would accuse Danita of "being angry all the time" and would walk on eggshells to avoid upsetting her. Karl would tell himself, "I don't deserve this." He would then retreat and create escape scenarios in his head.

在本章的开头，我们认识了卡尔——他的妻子丹妮塔似乎和他冷漠挑剔的母亲一样难以取悦。卡尔从来都不知道母亲什么时候会生气、批评他或羞辱他。成年后的卡尔与丹妮塔共同创造了类似的互动关系。当她生气时，卡尔会用尽他童年时期的所有生存机制，比如回避和退缩，试图应对。卡尔会指责丹妮塔“总是生气”，并小心翼翼地避免惹她生气。卡尔会告诉自己：“我不该遭受这样的对待。”然后他会退缩，并在脑海中构思各种逃避的场景。

The relationship began to shift when Karl came to see Danita as a "gift" whom he invited into his life to help him clean out his old issues around his fear of angry and critical people. As Karl made this shift several things began to happen. He began to have grief for what he went through as a child. He began standing up to Danita rather than avoiding and withdrawing. As he came to see Danita's anger as a result of her own childhood wounding, Danita began to look less and less angry to him. As his view of his wife began to shift, Karl began to feel more loving toward Danita and their relationship began to show marked improvement.

当卡尔把丹妮塔视为一份“礼物”时，他们的关系开始发生转变。他邀请丹妮塔进入他的生活，帮助他清除过去那些害怕愤怒和挑剔之人的毛病。随着卡尔的转变，一些事情开始发生。他开始为自己童年的经历感到悲伤。他开始勇敢地面对丹妮塔，而不是回避和退缩。当他意识到丹妮塔的愤怒源于她自己童年的创伤时，丹妮塔在他眼中也开始变得越来越不愤怒。随着卡尔对妻子的看法开始转变，他对丹妮塔的爱也开始增多，他们的关系也开始显著改善。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #35

挣脱束缚活动 #35

The next time you find yourself feeling frustrated, resentful, or rageful at your partner, ask yourself these questions:

下次当你发现自己对伴侣感到沮丧、怨恨或愤怒时，问自己这些问题：

"Why have I invited this person into my life?"

“我为什么要邀请这个人进入我的生活？”

"What do I need to learn from this situation?"

“我需要从这种情况学到什么？”

"How would my view of this situation change if I saw it as a gift? "

“如果我把它看作一份礼物，我对这种情况的看法会发生怎样的变化？”

*****************************


Not Reinforcing Undesirable Behaviors Helps Nice Guys Get The Love They Want

不强化不良行为有助于好男人获得他们想要的爱

A couple of years ago we bought a Weimaraner puppy. We decided that if we were going to have a big dog in the house, we should take him to obedience school. One of the first lessons we learned was that we were the ones who needed obedience training. Most dogs that behave badly, we found out, have been conditioned to do so by ignorant or inconsistent owners.

几年前，我们买了一只魏玛猎犬幼犬。我们决定，如果家里要养一只大狗，就应该带它去服从学校。我们学到的第一课就是，我们才是需要服从训练的人。我们发现，大多数行为不端的狗狗，都是被无知或反复无常的主人训练出来的。

In many ways, humans aren't much different from pets. People often behave the way they have been trained to behave. For example, if a person gives his dog a treat when he pisses on the carpet, the dog will keep pissing on the carpet. The same is true for humans. If the Nice Guy reinforces his partner's undesirable behaviors, she will keep behaving in undesirable ways.

在很多方面，人类与宠物并无太大区别。人们通常会按照被训练的方式行事。例如，如果一个人在狗狗往地毯上撒尿时给它零食，狗狗就会继续往地毯上撒尿。人类也是如此。如果“好人”强化伴侣的不良行为，伴侣就会继续做出不良行为。

Here is the irony for Nice Guys: Nice Guys like the idea of a smooth and problem-free relationship.

对于好男人来说讽刺的是：好男人喜欢顺利、无问题的关系。

Typically, if their partner is unhappy, depressed, angry, or having a problem, they will jump right in and try and fix it or make it better. They believe that by doing so, they will make the problem go away and everything will quickly get back to normal. Unfortunately, this is like giving a dog a treat for pissing on the carpet. Every time a Nice Guy responds to or pays attention to a behavior he would like to eliminate, he is actually reinforcing that very behavior. This reinforcement increases the likelihood that that behavior will occur again. For example, Joe's wife frequently came home from work in a silent rage over some problem she experienced with a co-worker. It bothered Joe when his wife was in this mood. In an attempt to relieve his anxiety, he would ask his wife what was wrong. After a little coaxing, she would spend the next couple of hours venting to Joe about how mistreated she was at work. Joe would listen and offer helpful suggestions, hoping that by doing so, she would get over her mood.

通常情况下，如果伴侣不开心、沮丧、愤怒或遇到问题，他们会立即介入，试图解决问题或改善现状。他们相信这样做就能解决问题，一切很快恢复正常。然而，这就像给狗狗零食，让它在地毯上撒尿一样。每当好男人回应或关注他想要消除的行为时，他实际上是在强化这种行为。这种强化增加了这种行为再次发生的可能性。例如，乔的妻子经常因为与同事之间的一些问题而默默地怒气冲冲地下班回家。妻子的这种情绪让乔很困扰。为了缓解焦虑，他会问妻子出了什么问题。经过一番劝说后，妻子会在接下来的几个小时里向乔倾诉她在工作中受到的不公平对待。乔会倾听并提出有用的建议，希望通过这样做，妻子能摆脱这种情绪。

In his attempt for short-term anxiety relief, Joe had actually helped create a long-term problem. Every time he asked his wife what was wrong, listened for hours, and offered advice, he was actually reinforcing a behavior pattern he found undesirable. In dog obedience school we learned that if you want an undesirable behavior to go away, you stop paying attention to it. The same is true in relationships.

乔试图短期缓解焦虑，实际上却制造了一个长期问题。每次他问妻子出了什么问题，一连几个小时倾听，并给出建议，他实际上是在强化一种他认为不好的行为模式。在狗狗服从学校，我们学到，如果你想要消除不良行为，就必须停止关注它。在人际关系中也是如此。

Like many Nice Guys, Joe felt like a victim to his wife's behavior. He was oblivious to the fact that he was responsible for perpetuating a behavior he found undesirable. When the men in his No More Mr. Nice Guy! group pointed this fact out to him, he decided to try something different.

和许多“好男人”一样，乔觉得自己是妻子行为的受害者。他完全没有意识到，自己要为这种让他感到不快的行为负责。当他的“不再是好男人！”小组成员指出这一点时，他决定尝试一些不同的方法。

The next time his wife came home in a silent, withdrawn mood, he didn't say anything. He ate dinner in silence and then went out to the garage. Even though he felt intense anxiety, he resisted his impulses to try to "fix" his wife. As he lay awake in bed that night, the deafening silence kept him awake for hours.

妻子再次回家时，他沉默寡言，情绪低落，他什么也没说。他默默地吃完晚饭，然后去了车库。尽管他感到极度焦虑，但他还是克制住了想要“修复”妻子的冲动。那天晚上，他躺在床上，震耳欲聋的寂静让他好几个小时都无法入睡。

The next morning, the silence continued. Joe was afraid this behavior might go on forever. In an attempt to relieve his anxiety, he tried making a little small talk. His wife responded with one-word answers and left for work.

第二天早上，沉默仍在继续。乔担心这种行为会一直持续下去。为了缓解焦虑，他试着和妻子闲聊了几句。妻子只用一个词回答了他，然后就去上班了。

That evening, it seemed as if a miracle had occurred. Joe's wife came home from work in a good mood and asked Joe if he wanted to go for a walk. While walking she told him how she had resolved the previous day's problem. Joe revealed to his wife how uncomfortable it had made him to not try to fix her problem the previous evening. She responded by telling Joe that she didn't want him to try to fix her problems and that she liked it better that he had given her some space to work it out on her own.

那天晚上，仿佛奇迹发生了。乔的妻子心情愉快地下班回家，问乔想不想出去走走。她一边走，一边告诉他自己是如何解决前一天问题的。乔向妻子透露，前一天晚上没有主动解决她的问题让他感到非常不舒服。妻子回应乔说，她不想让他主动解决她的问题，她更喜欢他给她一些空间，让她自己解决问题。


Doing Something Different When Beginning A New Relationship Helps Nice Guys Get The Love They Want

开始一段新恋情时，做一些不同的事情能帮助好男人获得他们想要的爱

For Nice Guys who see a relationship come to an end, or for the ones who are presently single, I encourage them to take a different approach when beginning new relationships. Relationships are messy and there is no way to eliminate the bumps and potholes, but we don't have to make them any more difficult than they already are. This is one area where I strongly encourage Nice Guys to do something different. That is, enter relationships with a healthy agenda, rather than an unconscious, dysfunctional one.

对于那些目睹一段感情走到尽头的“好男人”，或者目前单身的“好男人”，我鼓励他们在开始新恋情时换个角度。感情本来就很复杂，没有办法消除其中的坎坷和伤痕，但我们不必让它们变得比现在更难。在这方面，我强烈建议“好男人”们做些改变。也就是说，要带着健康的心态开始一段感情，而不是带着无意识的、不正常的心态。

Doing something different means choosing a different kind of partner. A fixer-upper may be a fun challenge when it comes to restoring a car, but it's a terrible way to choose a partner. Nice Guys have a tendency, due to their own insecurities, to pick partners who seem like they need a little polishing.

尝试不同的事意味着选择不同类型的伴侣。修理汽车或许是个有趣的挑战，但选择伴侣却很糟糕。好男人由于自身的不安全感，往往会选择那些看起来需要一点打磨的伴侣。

Because they don't know why a healthy or independent person would want them, they settle for a diamond in the rough. They tend to pick partners who have had troubled childhoods, are sexual abuse survivors, have had a string of bad relationships, are depressed, are having money problems, are overweight, or are struggling single moms. Then they go to work operating from a covert contract —fixing, caretaking, and pleasing — all with the hope that she will turn out to be a polished gem.

因为他们不知道一个健康独立的人为什么会想要他们，所以他们选择璞玉。他们往往会选择那些童年坎坷、遭受过性虐待、经历过一系列糟糕关系、患有抑郁症、经济困难、超重，或是生活拮据的单亲妈妈作为伴侣。然后，他们开始通过秘密合同进行工作——修复、照顾、取悦——所有这些都是希望她最终能成为一颗精致的宝石。

Unfortunately, this strategy rarely works.

不幸的是，这种策略很少奏效。

When recovering Nice Guys create relationships with people who don't need fixing, they improve their odds of finding the love they want. This doesn't mean searching for the perfect partner, just one who is already taking responsibility for her own life. Over time, the members of my No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups have come up with a number of traits to consciously look for when creating new relationships.

当正在康复的“好男人”与不需要修复的人建立关系时，他们找到理想爱情的几率会更高。这并不意味着要寻找完美的伴侣，而是一个已经对自己的生活负责的人。随着时间的推移，我的“不再是好男人！”小组的成员们总结出了一些在建立新关系时需要有意识地寻找的特质。

These traits include (in no particular order):

这些特征包括（无特定顺序）：

  • Passion.
    激情。
  • Integrity.
    正直。
  • Happiness.
    快乐。
  • Intelligence.
    智慧。
  • Sexual assertiveness.
    性自信。
  • Financial responsibility.
    财务责任。
  • Commitment to personal growth.
    致力于个人成长。

Nice Guys who are already in a relationship may find it unsettling if their current partner doesn't fare well by this list (especially if it is the Nice Guy doing the evaluating). That doesn't mean they need to jump out of the relationship and go looking for greener pastures. Instead, I encourage these men to first begin addressing their own behaviors and look at why they needed to create the kind of relationship they have with their present partner.

已经有伴侣的“好男人”可能会因为现任伴侣在这份名单上表现不佳而感到不安（尤其是当“好男人”在评估他们时）。但这并不意味着他们需要放弃这段关系，去寻找更美好的未来。相反，我鼓励这些男人首先反思自己的行为，并思考为什么他们需要与现任伴侣建立这样的关系。

Finding a new partner won't be the solution if the Nice Guy still needs the same kind of relationship. I have found that when recovering Nice Guys begin dealing with their own dysfunctional patterns, their relationships also begin changing. At times these changes cause them to reevaluate their desire to get out. Sometimes they confirm it is time for a change.

如果“好男人”仍然需要维持原有的关系，那么寻找新的伴侣并非解决问题的办法。我发现，当“好男人”开始处理自身不正常的人际关系模式时，他们的关系也会开始发生变化。有时，这些变化会让他们重新评估是否真的想摆脱这种关系。有时，他们会确认是时候做出改变了。

Nice Guys have a strong tendency to try to do everything "right." This list isn't meant as a magic formula. There are no perfect people and no perfect relationships. But by consciously looking for the traits listed above in a prospective partner, Nice Guys can save themselves a lot of grief and improve their chances of actually finding what they are looking for.

Embrace The Challenge

迎接挑战

Recovering Nice Guys can have fulfilling, intimate relationships. Life is a challenge and so are relationships. As they implement the recovery strategies presented in this book, recovering Nice Guys put themselves in the position to embrace these challenges and get the love they want.

正在康复中的“好男人”也能拥有充实而亲密的感情。生活充满挑战，感情亦是如此。通过运用本书中提出的康复策略，正在康复中的“好男人”能够更好地应对这些挑战，最终获得他们渴望的爱情。

Coming Out Of The Closet Helps Nice Guys Get The Sex They Want

出柜帮助好男人获得他们想要的性

Internalized shame and fear are the greatest barriers to a satisfying sex life. A man can read all the books he wants on "How To Pick Up Women" or watch all the instructional videos on improving sexual technique. None of these things will help him get good sex as long as he has shame and fear about being sexual or being a sexual being. Getting good sex is dependent on recovering Nice Guys bringing their shame and fear out of the closet and into the open were they can be looked at and released. This step cannot be skipped!

内化的羞耻感和恐惧是获得令人满意的性生活的最大障碍。男人可以读所有他想读的“如何泡妞”书籍，也可以看所有关于提高性技巧的教学视频。但只要他对性行为或作为性存在感到羞耻和恐惧，这些都无法帮助他获得美好的性生活。获得美好的性生活取决于恢复“好男人”的形象，将他们的羞耻感和恐惧从隐秘处释放出来，让它们被审视和释放。这一步不可跳过！

Cleaning out sexual shame requires accepting, non-judgmental people. A Nice Guy cannot do this work on his own. To release sexual shame and fear, the recovering Nice Guy must expose every aspect of his sexual self to safe, supportive people. This revealing allows the Nice Guy to release his shame and fear and free up the emotional energy it took to keep them hidden and repressed. These safe people can also give the Nice Guy supportive messages that he is not bad for being a sexual being.

清除性羞耻感需要包容、不带评判的人。好男人无法独自完成这项工作。为了释放性羞耻感和恐惧，正在康复中的好男人必须向安全、支持他的人敞开心扉，展现他性方面的方方面面。这种坦诚能让好男人释放他的羞耻感和恐惧感，释放那些隐藏和压抑这些情绪的能量。这些安全的人也会给好男人一些支持性的信息，让他知道，作为一个性存在，他并不坏。

Breaking Free: Pop Quiz

Most Nice Guys initially deny having any shame and fear about sex. Take the following quiz to see if you are in denial about your own sexual shame and fear.

1. Think back to your first sexual experience. Was it:

1. 回想一下你的第一次性经历。是这样的吗？

A. A joyous experience which you could share with family and friends?

B. Hidden, rushed, guilt-ridden, or in a less than ideal situation?

C. Painful, abusive, or frightening?


A. 一段你可以与家人朋友分享的快乐经历？B. 这段经历是隐秘的、匆忙的、充满内疚的，还是处境不太理想？C. 这段经历是痛苦的、虐待性的还是令人恐惧的？

2. When it comes to masturbation:

2. 谈到手淫：

B. Would there be a crisis if your partner "caught" you doing it?

C. Do you do it compulsively or in secret?


A. 你和你的伴侣会开诚布公地、自在地谈论这个话题吗？B. 如果你的伴侣“发现”你这样做，会引起什么危机？C. 你是强迫性地这样做，还是偷偷地这样做？

3. When it comes to your sexual experiences, thoughts, or impulses:

3. 当谈到你的性经历、想法或冲动时：

A. You are comfortable revealing everything about yourself to your partner.

B. You have secrets that you have never shared with anyone.

C. Some aspect of your sexuality has caused a crisis in an intimate relationship.

D. At some time in your life you have tried to eliminate or limit some problematic sexual behavior.


A. 你乐于向伴侣透露自己的一切。B. 你有一些从未与任何人分享的秘密。C. 你的某些性行为导致了亲密关系危机。D. 在你的人生中，你曾试图消除或限制某些有问题的性行为。

If you answered anything but "A" on any of the questions, you have sexual shame and fear. Read on.

如果你对任何一个问题的回答不是“A”，那么你就是性羞耻和恐惧。继续阅读。

Lyle, introduced earlier in the chapter, is a good example of how recovering Nice Guys can bring their sexual shame and fear out of the closet. Lyle was a good Christian, husband, and father who struggled with compulsive sexual behavior. Everything came crashing down for Lyle when his wife found a phone bill and called some of the strange numbers. She was bewildered and devastated. Never in her wildest dreams (or nightmares) did she think that Lyle might be involved in anything like pornography or phone sex. Little did she know she had just discovered the tip of the iceberg. 本章前面介绍的莱尔是一个很好的例子，他展现了正在康复的“好男人”如何将自己的性羞耻和恐惧公之于众。莱尔是一位虔诚的基督徒、丈夫和父亲，他一直在与强迫性性行为作斗争。当他的妻子发现一张电话账单并拨打了几个陌生号码时，莱尔的一切都崩溃了。她感到困惑和崩溃。她做梦也没想到（或者噩梦也没想到），莱尔竟然会参与色情或电话性爱之类的事情。她当时并不知道，她发现的只是冰山一角。面对证据，莱尔起初佯装惊讶，否认自己知道证据的来源。最终，他崩溃了，把一切都说了出来。好吧，几乎是全部。又过了几个星期，经历了几次情绪上的对峙，以及我打来的一个电话，一切才水落石出。

每当他透露一些秘密的想法或行为时，他都会感到一种轻松的感觉，就像肩上的重担被卸了下来一样。

In my No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups I encourage recovering Nice Guys to bring their sexual shame out of the closet. I support them in talking explicitly about their sexuality. Taking Matters Into Their Own Hands Helps Nice Guys Get The Sex They Want

I regularly tell Nice Guys, "No one was put into this world to meet your needs but you." This is especially true with sex. When recovering Nice Guys decide to take responsibility for their own needs and take matters into their own hands, they put themselves in a position to get the quantity and quality of sex they want. Let me explain.

我经常跟好男人说：“除了你自己，没有人是为了满足你的需求而来到这个世界的。” 这句话在性方面尤其适用。当正在康复中的好男人决定为自己的需求负责，并掌控一切时，他们就能够获得他们想要的性生活的数量和质量。让我来解释一下。

All significant behavior patterns are the sum of many, much smaller behavior patterns. The most effective way to change a behavior is to change its smallest elements. For example, if a Nice Guys is not getting as much sex as he wants or isn't getting the kind of sex he wants, the only way to change this behavior pattern is to change its smallest components. Rather than going out and trying to have more sex, it is more effective to change the little things that create the overall pattern of not getting much sex. Change the little things, and the big picture changes as a result.

所有重要的行为模式都是由许多更小的行为模式组成的。改变一种行为最有效的方法是改变其最小的元素。例如，如果一个“好男人”没有得到他想要的性生活次数，或者没有得到他想要的那种性生活，那么改变这种行为模式的唯一方法就是改变其最小的组成部分。与其出去尝试更多性生活，不如改变那些导致性生活不规律的小细节更有效。改变小细节，整体格局就会随之改变。

Before Nice Guys can have exciting, passionate, and fulfilling sexual experiences with other people, they must learn how to have the same with themselves. By taking matters into their own hands — by practicing healthy masturbation — recovering Nice Guys can change the most basic dynamics that shape the bigger picture of how they do sex.

在“好男人”能够与他人拥有激动人心、激情四射、令人满足的性体验之前，他们必须学会如何与自己拥有同样的体验。通过掌握主动权——练习健康的自慰——康复中的“好男人”可以改变塑造他们性行为方式的最基本动力。

Consider the logic:

考虑一下逻辑：

  • Until a Nice Guy can be sexual with himself without shame, he won't be able to be sexual with another person without shame.
    除非好男人能够毫无羞耻地与自己发生性关系，否则他将无法毫无羞耻地与他人发生性关系。
  • Until a Nice Guy is comfortable giving pleasure to himself, he won't be able to receive pleasure from someone else.
    除非好男人能够舒适地为自己带来快乐，否则他将无法从他人那里获得快乐。
  • Until a Nice Guy can take responsibility for his own arousal and pleasure when he is by himself, he won't be able to take responsibility for his own arousal and pleasure when is with someone else.
    除非好男人能够为自己独处时的性唤起和快乐负责，否则他将无法为自己与他人在一起时的性唤起和快乐负责。
  • Until a Nice Guy can be sexual with himself without using pornography or fantasy to distract himself, he won't be able to have sex with someone else without needing similar things to distract him.
    除非好男人能够不使用色情或幻想来分散自己的注意力，否则他将无法与他人发生性关系，而无需类似的东西来分散他的注意力。

It's not just about orgasms. It does not require outside stimulation from pornography and doesn't use trances or fantasy to stay distracted from shame and fear. It is about learning to pay attention to what feels good. Most of all, it is about accepting sole responsibility for one's sexual pleasure and expression.

好男人可以通过练习我所说的健康自慰来改变这些状态。健康自慰是一个释放性能量的过程。它没有目标或目的。它不仅仅是性高潮。它不需要色情作品的外部刺激，也不用通过恍惚状态或幻想来转移对羞耻和恐惧的注意力。它是关于学习关注那些感觉良好的事物。最重要的是，它关于承担个人性快感和性表达的全部责任。

Many Nice Guys are initially uncomfortable with the discussion of healthy masturbation. The concept seems like an oxymoron. In general, Nice Guys have tremendous internalized shame around masturbation. They also frequently surround themselves with people who reinforce this shame (partner, religion, etc.). Many Nice Guys also struggle with compulsive masturbation. They fear that attempting any kind of self-gratification might open up Pandora's Box.

许多好男人最初对健康自慰的讨论感到不适。这个概念似乎自相矛盾。一般来说，好男人对自慰有着强烈的内化羞耻感。他们身边也经常有强化这种羞耻感的人（伴侣、宗教信仰等等）。许多好男人也难以控制强迫性自慰。他们担心尝试任何形式的自我满足都可能打开潘多拉魔盒。

I have found that when recovering Nice Guys work on learning how to pleasure themselves without using fantasy or pornography there is no way for their behavior to become compulsive. I have also found that when they share the experience with other non-judgmental men, their shame diminishes rapidly.

我发现，当“好男人”们努力学习如何在不借助幻想或色情内容的情况下取悦自己时，他们的行为就不会变成强迫性的。我还发现，当他们与其他不带偏见的男人分享这种经历时，他们的羞耻感会迅速消退。

A Note About Pornography

关于色情内容的说明

I am not opposed to pornography legally or morally, but I think it is bad for men for several reasons:

我并不从法律或道德上反对色情，但我认为它对男性有害，原因如下：

  • Pornography creates unrealistic expectations of what people should like and what sex should be like.
    色情制品让人们对性生活抱有不切实际的期望。
  • Pornography addicts men to bodies and body parts.
    色情制品使男性对身体和身体部位上瘾。
  • Pornography can easily become a substitute for a real sexual relationship.
    色情制品很容易成为真实性关系的替代品。
  • Pornography creates a trance in which men can be sexual while staying distracted from their shame and fear.
    色情制品使男性在恍惚状态中可以进行性行为，同时又能摆脱羞耻和恐惧。
  • Pornography compounds shame because it is usually hidden and used in secret.
    色情制品加剧了羞耻感，因为它通常被隐藏并秘密使用。

I tell Nice Guys, if you are going to use pornography, do it openly. Doing so tends to break the trance and takes the buzz out of it.

我告诉“好男人”，如果你要看色情片，就公开地看。这样很容易打破恍惚状态，让人失去兴奋感。


A Note About Fantasy

关于幻想的注释

Fantasy is a form of dissociation — the process of separating one's body from one's mind. When a person fantasizes while being sexual he is purposefully and actively leaving his body. While some sex therapists advocate fantasy as a way of improving a sex life, it is actually the best way I know to kill it. Fantasizing during sex makes about as much sense as thinking about a Big Mac while eating a gourmet meal. About the only thing fantasy accomplishes is to distract a person from his shame and fear or cover up the fact that he is having bad sex.

幻想是一种分离的形式——将一个人的身体与精神分离的过程。当一个人在性爱中幻想时，他实际上是在有意识地、主动地离开自己的身体。虽然一些性治疗师提倡幻想是改善性生活的一种方式，但实际上，这是我知道的消除幻想的最佳方法。性爱中幻想的意义，就好比一边吃着美食一边想着巨无霸一样。幻想唯一的作用，就是转移一个人的羞耻感和恐惧感，或者掩盖他性生活不愉快的事实。

Healthy masturbation helps the recovering Nice Guy change the core dynamics that prevent him from getting good sex. Healthy masturbation:

健康的自慰可以帮助正在康复的“好男人”改变阻碍他获得良好性爱的核心动力。健康的自慰：

  • Helps remove the shame and fear of being sexual.
    帮助消除性方面的羞耻感和恐惧感。
  • Puts the Nice Guy in charge of his own sexual needs.
    让好男人掌控自己的性需求。
  • Removes dependency on unavailable partners or pornography.
    摆脱对无法获得的伴侣或色情内容的依赖。
  • Helps the Nice Guy learn to please the person that matters most — himself.
    帮助好男人学会取悦最重要的人——他自己。
  • Gives the Nice Guy permission to have as much good sex as he wants.
    允许好男人随心所欲地享受美妙的性爱。
  • Puts the Nice Guy in charge of his own pleasure.
    让好男人掌控自己的快乐。

Changing these dynamics through healthy masturbation enhances and intensifies the experience of making love with another person. Terrance provides a good example.

通过健康的自慰来改变这些动态，可以增强和强化与他人做爱的体验。特伦斯就是一个很好的例子。

Terrance originally came to therapy looking for a quick fix to his "problem" so his fiancée would not break up with him. In the first several sessions, I focused on the subject of him making his needs a priority. As with most Nice Guys, this initially made him uncomfortable (to put it mildly). Terrance was terrified that if he wasn't a great lover and didn't keep his girlfriend happy, she would leave him like his ex-wife did.

I began by encouraging Terrance to do a few non-sexual things just for himself. I reassured him regularly that this would make him more attractive to his fiancée, not less. I encouraged him to find a time when he would be undisturbed in which he could focus on his own pleasure and arousal. I suggested that he do this without having a goal of climaxing and without using fantasy or pornography. I encouraged him to pay attention to what felt good to him and to observe the ways he unconsciously tried to distract himself from his shame and fear.

Breaking Free Activity #38

挣脱束缚活动#38

Set aside a time to practice healthy masturbation. Choose a comfortable place where you will be undisturbed. Practice by looking at yourself and touching yourself without using pornography or fantasy. Pay attention to how it feels to experience your sexuality without any goals or agendas (such as having an orgasm). Also observe any tendency to distract yourself from what you are experiencing (going into fantasy, becoming goal-oriented, having distracting thoughts, loss of physical sensation). Just observe these experiences and use them as information about your shame and fear.

留出时间练习健康的自慰。选择一个舒适且不受干扰的地方。练习时，不要借助色情或性幻想，而是观察自己、触摸自己。留意在没有任何目标或计划（例如达到性高潮）的情况下体验性生活的感觉。同时，观察是否有转移注意力的倾向（例如陷入幻想、以目标为导向、产生分散注意力的想法、失去身体知觉）。观察这些经历，并将它们作为你羞耻感和恐惧感的来源。
