Saying "No" To Bad Sex Helps Nice Guys Get The Sex They Want

对不良性行为说“不”有助于好男人获得他们想要的性

When it comes to sex, Nice Guys are consummate bottom feeders. They settle for scraps and come back begging for more. Nice Guys settle for distorted images of bodies in pornography. They settle for the faceless sex of 900 numbers and chat rooms. They settle for trying to persuade unavailable people to begrudgingly be sexual with them. They settle for quick, compulsive masturbation. They settle for passionless, mechanical lovemaking. They settle for trances and fantasy. Nice Guys do a lot of settling.

说到性，好男人简直就是彻头彻尾的底层食客。他们只求得残羹冷炙，然后回来乞求更多。好男人满足于色情作品中扭曲的肉体形象。他们满足于900号码和聊天室里不露面的性爱。他们满足于试图说服那些无法得到满足的人勉强与他们发生性关系。他们满足于快速、强迫性的自慰。他们满足于毫无激情、机械的性爱。他们满足于恍惚和幻想。好男人总是在妥协。

As long as a Nice Guy is willing to settle for bad sex, he limits his opportunities to experience good sex.

只要好男人愿意接受糟糕的性爱，他就限制了自己体验美好性爱的机会。

I regularly tell Nice Guys, "You have to be willing to let go of what you've got to get what you want."

我经常告诉好男人：“你必须愿意放弃你所拥有的，才能得到你想要的。”

Good sex can occur only when a recovering Nice Guy decides to stop settling for bad sex!

只有当一个正在康复的好男人决定不再接受糟糕的性爱时，美好的性爱才会发生！

So what does good sex look like? If we base our answer on what we see in movies or pornography, we will only keep perpetuating a formula for bad sex. Here is how I define "good sex."

Good sex consists of two people taking full responsibility for meeting their own needs. It has no goal. It is free of agendas and expectations. Rather than being a performance, it is an unfolding of sexual energy.

美好的性爱是两个人全心全意地满足自身需求。它没有目标，不受任何安排和期望的影响。它并非一种表演，而是性能量的展现。

It is about two people revealing themselves in the most intimate and vulnerable of ways. Good sex occurs when two people focus on their own pleasure, passion, and arousal, and stay connected to those same things in their partner. All of these dynamics allow good sex to unfold in unpredictable, spontaneous, and memorable ways.

When recovering Nice Guys decide they will no longer settle for anything less than good sex, they begin to take responsibility for doing something different.

当康复中的“好男人”决定不再满足于不美好的性生活时，他们开始承担起责任，做一些不同的事情。

  • They let go of the concept of being a great lover.
    他们放弃了成为伟大情人的想法。
  • They practice being clear and direct.
    他们练习清晰直接地表达自己。
  • They choose available partners.
    他们选择可用的伴侣。
  • They don't settle for scraps.
    他们不满足于残羹剩饭。
  • They decide that bad sex is not better than no sex!
    他们认为糟糕的性爱并不比没有性爱好！

Aaron is a good example of what can happen when a recovering Nice Guy decides to say "no" to bad sex. For the first few weeks in the No More Mr. Nice Guy! group, Aaron vented his frustrations and shared how helpless he felt to get Hannah to want to have sex with him. It was obvious that Aaron believed his wife held the key to his sexual happiness and that he was angry over her willful refusal to use that key. As a result, he felt "rejected" and "worthless."

亚伦就是一个典型的例子，他展现了正在康复中的“好男人”决定对糟糕的性生活说“不”会发生什么。在“不再是好男人！”小组的最初几周里，亚伦倾诉了他的沮丧，并分享了他如何无力说服汉娜和他发生性关系。显然，亚伦认为妻子掌握着他性福的钥匙，而他对妻子故意拒绝使用这把钥匙感到愤怒。结果，他感到“被拒绝”和“一文不值”。

After a few weeks, I suggested that Aaron go on a sexual moratorium in which he refrained from having sex with Hannah for a period of six months. During this time I suggested that he focus on doing things he had given up when he and Hannah got married. I also encouraged him to tell Hannah whatever he was feeling. I shared with him that a sexual moratorium would make it easier for him to do these things because he wouldn't be so concerned about maintaining the possibility of her availability. If they weren't having sex, he wouldn't have to worry about doing something that might make her angry and cause her to withhold sex.

起初，亚伦很困惑，不知道这个计划怎么能让汉娜想和他发生更多的性关系。

I told him the goal was not to get her to have more sex, but for him to reclaim his key and stop feeling like a victim.

我告诉他，目的不是让她发生更多性关系，而是让他重新获得钥匙，不再感觉自己是个受害者。

Even though he was initially hesitant, he acknowledged that he wasn't having much sex anyway. With the support of the men in the group, Aaron decided to go home that night and tell his wife what he planned to do.

He went out with some guy friends he hadn't seen in a couple of years. He began to share his feelings with his wife.

Breaking Free Activity #39

挣脱束缚活动 #39

Consider going on a sexual moratorium. Consciously refrain from sex for a predetermined period of time. No matter what your sexual situation is, it can be a powerful learning experience. Most guys initially resist the idea, but once they make the decision to do it, they find it to be a very positive experience. A sexual moratorium can have many benefits:

  • Helps break dysfunction cycles.
    帮助打破功能障碍循环。
  • Eliminates pursuing and distancing.
    消除追求和疏远。
  • Releases resentment.
    释放怨恨。
  • Allows the Nice Guy to see that he can live without sex.
    让好男人看到他没有性生活也可以。
  • Helps the Nice Guy realize that no one else but him holds the key to his sexual experience.
    帮助好男人意识到除了他自己，没有人掌握着他性经验的关键。
  • Helps the Nice Guy see how he settles for bad sex.
    帮助好男人看到他是如何接受糟糕的性生活的。
  • Eliminates fear that the Nice Guy's partner can withhold sex or approval.
    消除对好男人的伴侣可能拒绝性生活或不同意的恐惧。
  • Helps the Nice Guy pay attention to the meaning of sexual impulses. Whenever the Nice Guy feels the impulse to be sexual, he can automatically ask himself, "Why am I feeling sexual?"
    帮助好男人关注性冲动的含义。每当好男人感到性冲动时，他都会自动问自己：“我为什么会有性冲动？”
  • Helps break addictive patterns by eliminating compulsive masturbation, pornography, and other addictive behaviors.
    通过消除强迫性手淫、色情和其他成瘾行为，帮助打破成瘾模式。
  • Helps the Nice Guy begin to address feelings he has been avoiding with sex.
    帮助好男人开始解决他一直通过性来回避的感受。

Before beginning a sexual moratorium, discuss it with your partner. It helps to set a specific time. I suggest three to six months. It can be done.

在开始性生活暂停之前，请与伴侣讨论。设定一个具体的时间会很有帮助。我建议三到六个月。这是可以做到的。

Decide on the parameters of the moratorium. Once you have begun, pay attention to slips and sabotaging behaviors, from both you and your partner. Remember, it is a learning experience. You don't have to do it perfectly.

确定暂停期的参数。一旦开始，要注意你和伴侣的失误和破坏行为。记住，这是一个学习的过程。你不必做到完美。

Following The Example Of The Bull Moose Helps Nice Guys Get The Sex They Want

效仿公驼鹿可以帮助好男人获得他们想要的性

In nature, the alpha male and the bull moose don't sit around trying to figure out what will make the girls like them. They are just themselves: fierce, strong, competitive, and sexually proud. Because they are what they are and do what they do, prospective mates are attracted.

在自然界中，雄性驼鹿和雄性大驼鹿不会闲着没事琢磨怎么才能让雌性喜欢自己。它们就是它们自己：凶猛、强壮、好胜、性取向自在。正因为它们的本性，正因为它们的行为，未来的伴侣才会被吸引。

As in nature, the greatest aphrodisiac is self-confidence. As recovering Nice Guys become comfortable just being themselves, they begin to look more attractive. Self-respect, courage, and integrity look good on a man. As recovering Nice Guys chart their own path and put themselves first, people respond.

正如自然界中一样，最强大的催情剂就是自信。当正在康复中的好男人能够安于做自己时，他们就会变得更有魅力。自尊、勇气和正直对一个男人来说是件好事。当正在康复中的好男人开始规划自己的人生道路，把自己放在第一位时，人们就会做出回应。

I've listened to recovering Nice Guys tell of "selfishly" putting their needs first and then being surprised when a seemingly unavailable partner expresses a desire to be sexual. One client, who hadn't had sex with his wife in 14 months, shared in a Nice Guy group that he was tired of listening to his wife complain about her work problems. That night, for the first time in 15 years of marriage, he told his wife that he was too tired to listen. Even though she was initially angry, later that night she asked him if he wanted to make love.

我听过一些正在康复中的“好男人”讲述他们“自私地”把自己的需求放在第一位，然后当一个看似遥不可及的伴侣表达出性欲时，他们却大吃一惊。一位客户已经14个月没和妻子发生性关系了，他在“好男人”群里分享道，他厌倦了听妻子抱怨工作上的问题。那天晚上，结婚15年来，他第一次告诉妻子，他太累了，听不下去了。尽管妻子一开始很生气，但那天晚上晚些时候，她还是问他是否想做爱。


A Force Of Nature

自然之力

The very thing that makes sex so exciting is exactly what makes it so terrifying. Sex is powerful, chaotic, and wild. It crackles with cosmic energy. It draws us like a moth to a flame. As recovering Nice Guys release their sexual shame and fear, take responsibility for their own pleasure, refuse to settle for bad sex, and practice being just who they are, they put themselves in the position to embrace this cosmic force without fear or reservation. This is when the sex really gets good.

性爱如此刺激，恰恰也正是它令人恐惧的原因。性爱强大、混乱、狂野。它迸发出宇宙能量，如同飞蛾扑火般吸引着我们。当“好男人”们逐渐释放性方面的羞耻感和恐惧，为自己的愉悦负责，拒绝接受糟糕的性爱，并练习做真实的自己时，他们就能毫无畏惧、毫无保留地拥抱这股宇宙力量。这时，性爱才会真正变得美好。

第九章

Get The Life You Want: Discover Your Passion And Purpose In Life, Work, And Career

过上你想要的生活：发现你在生活、工作和事业中的激情和目标


If there were no limits on your life:

如果你的生活没有限制：

  • Where would you live?
    你会住在哪里？
  • What would you be doing in your leisure time?
    你的闲暇时间会做什么？
  • What kind of work would you be engaged in?
    你会从事什么样的工作？
  • What would your home and surroundings look like?
    你的家和周围环境是什么样子的？

As you look at the reality of your life, ask yourself two questions: First, are you creating the life you want? Second, if not, why not?

当你审视现实生活时，问自己两个问题：第一，你正在创造你想要的生活吗？第二，如果没有，为什么？

In general, the Nice Guys with whom I have worked have been intelligent, industrious, and competent individuals. While most are at least moderately successful, the majority have not lived up to their full abilities or potential. Nor have they created the kind of life they really desire.

总的来说，我共事过的“好男人”们都聪明、勤奋、能干。虽然大多数人至少取得了一定的成功，但大多数人并没有充分发挥自己的能力或潜力，也没有创造自己真正渴望的生活。

Since Nice Guys spend so much time seeking approval, hiding their flaws, playing it safe, and doing the opposite of what works, it makes sense that they would typically fall short of being all they can be. This is perhaps the greatest tragedy wrought by the Nice Guy Syndrome — countless intelligent and talented men wasting their lives and wallowing in the mire of mediocrity.

由于“好男人”花费大量时间寻求认可，隐藏自身缺点，追求安全，并采取与实际效果相反的做法，他们通常无法充分发挥自身潜能也就不足为奇了。这或许是“好男人综合症”造成的最大悲剧——无数才华横溢的男性浪费生命，沉溺于平庸的泥潭。


Nice Guys On The Job

工作中的好人

Most Nice Guys initially come to counseling to deal with the way their life paradigm is affecting their intimate relationships. These relationship problems often overshadow the reality that they are equally dissatisfied with their job, career, or life direction in general. The dynamics that keep Nice Guys stuck in dysfunctional, unsatisfying relationships are often the same dynamics that keep them stuck in dysfunctional and unsatisfying vocations.

大多数“好男人”最初寻求心理咨询是为了解决他们的生活方式对亲密关系的影响。这些关系问题常常掩盖了他们对工作、事业或人生方向同样不满的现实。导致“好男人”深陷不正常、不满意的感情关系的因素，往往也导致他们深陷不正常、不满意的职业。

There are numerous reasons why Nice Guys tend to be less than they can be in life, work, and career.

有很多原因导致好男人在生活、工作和事业上表现不佳。

These include:

这些包括：

  • Fear
    恐惧
  • Trying to do it right
    试图把事情做好
  • Trying to do everything themselves
    试图自己做所有事情
  • Self-sabotage
    自我破坏
  • A distorted self-image
    扭曲的自我形象
  • Deprivation thinking
    剥夺思维
  • Staying stuck in familiar but dysfunctional systems
    停留在熟悉但功能失调的系统中



Fear Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

恐惧使好人无法过上他们想要的生活

If I were to identify one common factor at the core of every problem experienced by Nice Guys, it would be fear. Pretty much everything Nice Guys do or don't do is governed by fear. Their thoughts are funneled through fear-encrusted neurons in their brains. Their interactions are dictated by the politics of fear.

如果要我找出好男人所经历的所有问题的核心因素，那就是恐惧。好男人所做的或不做的几乎一切都受恐惧支配。他们的思维通过大脑中充满恐惧的神经元进行传导。他们的互动受恐惧政治的支配。

  • It is fear that prevents a Nice Guy from demanding the raise he has been promised.
    正是恐惧阻止了好人要求他被承诺的加薪。
  • It is fear that keeps a Nice Guy from going back to school to get the education or training he needs to pursue a truly fulfilling career.
    正是恐惧阻止了好人重返学校接受他需要的教育或培训，以追求真正令人满意的职业。
  • It is fear that prevents a Nice Guy from quitting a job he despises.
    正是恐惧阻止了好人辞去他鄙视的工作。
  • It is fear that gets in the way of a Nice Guy starting the business of his dreams.
    正是恐惧阻碍了好人开始他梦想的事业。
  • It is fear that prevents a Nice Guy from living where he really wants to live and doing what he really wants to do.
    正是恐惧阻止了好人生活在他真正想住的地方，做他真正想做的事情。

Nice Guys are afraid of making a mistake, afraid of doing it wrong, afraid of failure, afraid of losing it all. Right alongside these fears of disaster is the paradoxical fear of success . Nice Guys are typically afraid that if they are truly successful:

好男人害怕犯错，害怕做错事，害怕失败，害怕失去一切。除了对灾难的恐惧之外，还有对成功的矛盾恐惧。好男人通常担心，如果他们真的成功了：

  • They will be found out to be frauds.
    他们会被发现是骗子。
  • They won't be able to live up to people's expectations.
    他们将无法达到人们的期望。
  • They will be criticized.
    他们将受到批评。
  • They won't be able to handle the increased expectations.
    他们将无法应对不断增长的期望。
  • They will lose control over their lives.
    他们将失去对生活的控制。
  • They will do something to mess up everything.
    他们将做一些把一切都搞砸的事情。

Rather than facing these fears — real or imagined — Nice Guys typically settle for operating at a fraction of their full potential.

好人通常不会面对这些恐惧——无论是真实的还是想象的——而是只发挥出他们全部潜力的一小部分。


Trying To Do It Right Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

努力做好事会阻止好人得到他们想要的生活

The essence of all life is evolution and change. In order for this process to occur naturally and completely in an individual, a person has to be willing to let go of control. Letting go allows the beautiful, serendipitous chaos of creation to resonate through one's self. The result is a dynamic, fulfilling life.

所有生命的本质在于进化和变化。为了让这个过程在个体身上自然而完整地发生，一个人必须愿意放弃控制。放下控制，才能让造物之美、偶然的混沌与自身产生共鸣。最终，你将拥有充满活力、充实的人生。

Nice Guys are obsessed with trying to keep their lives smooth and uneventful. They do this by trying to "do it right" and following the "rules." Unfortunately, this life strategy is the most effective way to put a lid on any creative life energy. This lid kills their passion and prevents Nice Guys from living up to their full potential.

好男人执着于让自己的生活平淡无奇。他们通过“把事情做好”和遵守“规则”来做到这一点。不幸的是，这种生活策略最有效地扼杀了任何创造性的生命能量。这种“盖子”扼杀了他们的激情，阻碍了好男人充分发挥他们的潜力。

  • Trying to do it right robs Nice Guys of their creativity and productivity.
    试图把事情做好会剥夺好男人的创造力和生产力。
  • Striving for perfection keeps Nice Guys focused on their imperfections.
    追求完美会让好男人专注于自己的不完美之处。
  • Seeking external validation and approval keeps Nice Guys stuck in mediocrity.
    寻求外界的认可和赞同会让好男人陷入平庸。
  • Attempting to hide flaws and mistakes prevents Nice Guys from taking risks or trying something new.
    试图隐藏缺点和错误会阻止好男人冒险或尝试新事物。
  • Following the rules make Nice Guys rigid, cautious, and fearful.
    循规蹈矩会让好男人变得僵化、谨慎和恐惧。

It is because of these self-imposed limits that many Nice Guys are dissatisfied, bored, or unhappy with their life and vocation.

正是由于这些自我强加的限制，许多好男人对自己的生活和职业感到不满意、无聊或不快乐。


Trying To Do Everything Themselves Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

试图自己做所有事情会阻止好人获得他们想要的生活

As children, Nice Guys did not get their needs met in timely, judicious ways. Some were neglected, some were used, some were abused, some were abandoned. All grew up believing that it was a bad or dangerous thing for them to have needs. All grew up convinced that if they were going to have anything in life, it would be up to them.

小时候，“好人”的需求得不到及时、合理的满足。有的被忽视，有的被利用，有的被虐待，有的被抛弃。他们从小就坚信，有需求是一件坏事或危险的事。他们从小就坚信，人生的一切，都取决于自己。

Consequently, Nice Guys are terrible receivers. They are terrified of asking for help. They are completely miserable when others try to give to them. They have difficulty delegating to others.

因此，好人是糟糕的接受者。他们害怕寻求帮助。当别人试图给予他们帮助时，他们会感到非常痛苦。他们很难将任务委托给别人。

Because they believe they have to do it all themselves, Nice Guys rarely live up to their full potential. Nobody can be good at everything or succeed all on their own. Nice Guys believe they should be able to. They might be jacks-of-all-trades, but they are typically masters of none. This childhood conditioning ensures that they will never be all they can be in any area of life.

因为相信所有事情都必须自己做，好男人很少能充分发挥自己的潜力。没有人能够独自精通所有事情或取得成功。好男人相信自己应该能够做到。他们或许样样精通，但通常什么都不是精通的。这种童年的习性注定了他们永远无法在生活的任何领域达到最佳状态。


Self-Sabotage Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

自我破坏会阻止好人获得他们想要的生活

Because of their fear of success, Nice Guys are masters of self-sabotage. They undermine their success by:

因为害怕成功，好男人是自我破坏的高手。他们会通过以下方式破坏自己的成功：

  • Wasting time.
    浪费时间。
  • Making excuses.
    找借口。
  • Not finishing projects.
    没有完成项目。
  • Caretaking other people.
    照顾其他人。
  • Having too many projects going at once.
    同时进行太多项目。
  • Getting caught up in chaotic relationships.
    陷入混乱的关系。
  • Procrastinating.
    拖延。
  • Not setting boundaries.
    没有设定界限。

Nice Guys are typically good at looking just good enough. But to be really great — to really rise to the top — invites too much unwanted attention and scrutiny. The bright lights of success threaten to illuminate their self-perceived cracks and flaws.

好男人通常都善于让自己看起来足够好。但要真正优秀——真正爬到顶峰——就会招来太多不必要的关注和审视。成功的光芒反而会照亮他们自认为的缺陷和缺点。

Consequently, Nice Guys find many creative ways to make sure they are never too successful. If they don't start something, they won't fail. If they don't finish something, they won't be criticized. If they have too much going on at once, they won't have to do any one thing well. If they have enough good excuses, people won't expect too much of them.

因此，好男人会想出许多有创意的方法来确保自己不会太成功。如果他们不开始做某事，就不会失败。如果他们没有完成某事，就不会受到批评。如果他们同时要做太多事情，他们就不必把任何一件事做好。如果他们有足够好的借口，人们就不会对他们抱有太高的期望。


A Distorted Self Image Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

扭曲的自我形象会阻碍好人过上他们想要的生活

Because their needs were not met in a timely, judicious fashion in childhood, Nice Guys developed a distorted view of themselves. With a naive, immature logic they came to the conclusion that if their needs were not important, neither were they. This is the basis of their toxic shame. At their core, all Nice Guys believe they are not important or good enough.

由于童年时期的需求未能得到及时、合理的满足，好男人形成了扭曲的自我认知。他们凭借幼稚、不成熟的逻辑得出结论：如果自己的需求不重要，那么他们自己也不重要。这便是他们“有毒羞耻感”的根源。从本质上讲，所有好男人都认为自己不重要或不够好。

If a Nice Guy was called on to take care of a critical, needy, or dependent parent, he received a double dose of toxic shame. A child believes he should be able to please a critical parent, fix the problems of a depressed parent, and meet the needs of a smothering parent. Unfortunately, he can't.

As a result of their inability to fix, please, or take care of one or more parents, many Nice Guys developed a deep-seated sense of inadequacy. They believed they should be able to do the job. Nevertheless, they never could seem to do it right or good enough — mom was still depressed, dad was still critical.

由于无法弥补、取悦或照顾父母一方或多方，许多“好男人”养成了根深蒂固的无能感。他们认为自己应该能够胜任这份工作。然而，他们似乎永远都做得不够好——妈妈仍然很沮丧，爸爸仍然爱批评。

This internalized sense of inadequacy and defectiveness is carried into adulthood. Some Nice Guys compensate by trying to do everything right. They hope that by doing so, no one will ever find out how inadequate they are. Other Nice Guys just give up before they try.

这种内化的不足感和缺陷感会延续到成年。有些好男人会努力把每件事都做好，以此来弥补自己的不足。他们希望这样做，就没有人会发现自己的不足之处。而另一些好男人则在尝试之前就放弃了。

This feeling of inadequacy prevents Nice Guys from making themselves visible, taking chances, or trying something new. It keeps them in the same old rut, never seeing how talented and intelligent they really are. Everyone around them can see these things, but their distorted childhood lenses won't let them accurately see their true potential and ability.

这种自卑感会阻碍“好男人”展现自我，让他们不敢冒险，不敢尝试新事物。这让他们墨守成规，永远无法发现自己真正的才华和智慧。他们身边的人都能看到这些，但他们扭曲的童年视角却让他们无法准确看到自己的真正潜力和能力。

The result of this distorted self-image is an emotional and cognitive glass ceiling . This invisible lid prevents Nice Guys from being all they can be. If they do try to rise above it, they bump their heads and tumble down to more familiar territory.

这种扭曲的自我形象会导致情感和认知上的“玻璃天花板”。这层无形的“盖子”阻碍了“好男人”充分发挥潜能。如果他们真的试图突破它，就会碰壁，跌落到更熟悉的领域。


Deprivation Thinking Prevents Nice Guys From Getting the Life They Want

剥夺思维阻碍好人获得他们想要的生活

Not having their needs adequately met in childhood created a belief for Nice Guys that there wasn't enough of what they needed to go around. This deprivation experience became the lenses through which they viewed the world.

童年时期得不到充分满足的需求，让“好男人”们产生了一种想法：他们的生活所需远远不够。这种匮乏的经历，成为了他们看待世界的视角。

This paradigm of scarcity and deprivation makes Nice Guys manipulative and controlling. It causes them to believe they better hang on to what they've got and not take too many chances. It leads them to resent other people who seem to have what they lack.

这种匮乏和剥夺的模式让好男人变得控制欲强、好操纵。这让他们相信最好保住自己拥有的东西，不要冒太多风险。这让他们怨恨那些似乎拥有他们所缺乏的东西的人。

Because of their deprivation thinking, Nice Guys think small. They don't believe they deserve to have good things. They find all kinds of ways to make sure their view of the world is never challenged. They settle for scraps and think it is all they deserve. They create all kinds of rationalizations to explain why they will never have what they really desire. Because of their self-fulfilling beliefs, Nice Guys rarely live up to their potential or get what they really want in life.

由于缺乏“剥夺性思维”，好男人的眼界很狭隘。他们不相信自己配得上好东西。他们想方设法确保自己的世界观不受挑战。他们勉强维持现状，认为这就是他们应得的。他们编造各种理由来解释为什么自己永远无法得到真正想要的东西。由于他们自我实现的信念，好男人很少能充分发挥自己的潜力，也很少能得到他们真正想要的生活。

困在功能失调但熟悉的系统中，会阻碍好人获得他们想要的生活

As stated in previous chapters, two major factors prevent Nice Guys from getting what they want in love. The first is that they tend to recreate familiar, yet dissatisfying relationships. They find partners who will help them create the same dysfunctional kinds of relationships they experienced as children.

正如前几章所述，有两个主要因素阻碍了“好男人”在爱情中得到他们想要的东西。首先，他们倾向于重建熟悉却不令人满意的关系。他们会寻找伴侣，而伴侣会帮助他们建立起儿时经历过的那种不正常的人际关系。

These men then frequently see themselves as being victims to the dysfunction of their partners. Nice Guys have a difficult time seeing that they were attracted to these people for a reason.

这些男人常常会把自己视为伴侣功能障碍的受害者。好男人很难意识到自己被这些人吸引是有原因的。

Second, Nice Guys rarely experience the kind of relationships they want because they are bad enders. When a healthy person would pack up and move on, Nice Guys just keep doing more of the same, hoping that something will miraculously change.

其次，好男人很少能拥有他们想要的那种感情，因为他们不擅长处理分手。当一个健康的人会收拾行李离开时，好男人只会继续做同样的事情，希望事情会奇迹般地改变。

Nice Guys aren't much different in their jobs. They are attracted to careers and work situations that allow them to recreate the dysfunctional roles, relationships, and rules of their childhood. They often see themselves as helpless victims to these situations. Rarely do they see why they need these systems to be the way they are and that they have the choice to leave.

好男人在工作中也没什么不同。他们被那些能让他们重现童年时期不正常的角色、关系和规则的职业和工作环境所吸引。他们常常把自己视为这些环境无助的受害者。他们很少意识到为什么他们需要这些体系，以及他们可以选择离开。

Unconsciously recreating familiar family patterns in their jobs and careers keeps Nice Guys stuck and dissatisfied. While they are perpetuating the dysfunction of their childhood, they rarely do what they really want or rise to the top of their chosen vocation.

在工作和事业中无意识地重现熟悉的家庭模式，让“好男人”陷入困境，感到不满。他们延续着童年时期的种种缺陷，却很少能做自己真正想做的事情，也很少能在自己选择的职业中取得成就。


Realizing Your Passion and Potential

发挥你的热情和潜力

I frequently tell the men in my No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups that my goal is for every one of them to leave the group a millionaire. This statement really has very little to do with money or material wealth — it is about discovering passion and living up to potential.

我经常跟“不再是好人！”群里的男士们说，我的目标是让他们每个人离开群时都成为百万富翁。这句话其实和金钱或物质财富关系不大——它关乎发现激情，并发挥潜能。

As stated above, the Nice Guys I counsel are generally intelligent, talented men. As these men work on recovering from the Nice Guy Syndrome, they begin to accept themselves just as they are. This acceptance of the self allows them to embrace their passions and face their fears.

如上所述，我辅导的“好男人”通常都聪明且才华横溢。随着这些男人努力摆脱“好男人综合症”，他们开始接受真实的自己。这种接纳让他们能够拥抱激情，直面恐惧。

The formation of a more accurate view of the self and the world allows the abundance of the universe to begin flowing freely into their lives. Sometimes this takes the form of money. Sometimes it takes the form of love. Sometimes it takes the form of sex. Sometimes it takes the form of the bright lights of fame. Sometimes it includes all of the above.

形成更准确的自我观和世界观，让宇宙的丰盈开始自由地流入他们的生活。有时，这丰盈以金钱的形式出现。有时，这丰盈以爱的形式出现。有时，这丰盈以性的形式出现。有时，这丰盈以名利的形式出现。有时，这丰盈包含以上所有。

The remainder of this chapter presents a strategy to help recovering Nice Guys become all they can be. The following pages present a plan that has already helped countless Nice Guys discover their passion and live up to their potential. It can do the same for you.

本章的剩余部分将介绍一种策略，帮助正在康复中的“好男人”充分发挥他们的潜能。接下来的几页将介绍一个已经帮助无数“好男人”发现激情、发挥潜能的计划。它也能帮到你。


Facing Fears Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

直面恐惧让好人过上他们想要的生活

Charlie could have been the poster child for passionless, underachieving Nice Guys. When I first met Charlie he was stuck in a job he hated and living a life characterized by mediocrity and fear. Charlie had completed his engineering degree a couple of years before, yet he was still working at the same job he had held before starting college. Charlie's employers had promised him a big promotion upon graduation. When they failed to keep their promise, Charlie just stifled his resentment and kept on doing the same old thing he had always done.

查理本可以成为那种缺乏激情、成绩平平的“好人”的典型代表。我第一次见到查理时，他正困在一份自己讨厌的工作里，过着平庸和恐惧的生活。查理几年前就拿到了工程学位，却仍然在做着上大学前的工作。查理的雇主承诺他毕业后会升职。但当他们未能兑现承诺时，查理只是压抑着怨恨，继续做着他一直以来的老一套。

Charlie's single passion was flying. In spite of warnings of disaster from his mother, he had begun taking flying lessons after he finished college. Though Charlie dreamed of earning his pilot's license, he never seemed to be able to complete the necessary requirements to achieve his goal.

查理唯一的爱好就是飞行。尽管母亲警告他飞行可能会带来灾难，但他大学毕业后还是开始学习飞行。查理梦想着考取飞行员执照，但他似乎始终无法达到实现目标所需的条件。

A woman at his work introduced Charlie to my website. When he read the description of a Nice Guy he was mortified. He couldn't figure out how someone could know him so well. It took him six months before he worked up the nerve to send me an email. It took him another two months to send me a second. From the first time Charlie looked at my website he knew he needed to join a men's group, but the idea of being that vulnerable terrified him.

他同事的一位女士把查理介绍到我的网站上。当他看到“好男人”的描述时，他惊呆了。他无法理解，怎么会有人这么了解他。他花了六个月才鼓起勇气给我发了一封电子邮件。又过了两个月，他才给我发了第二封。查理第一次浏览我的网站时，就知道自己需要加入一个男性团体，但想到自己会变得如此脆弱，就让他感到恐惧。

It was at that point that Charlie made a decision that changed his life. Charlie decided that if something frightened him that much, he needed to face his fear and do it. Little did he know then, but that decision was just the beginning of a journey that would lead Charlie toward the rediscovery of his passion and purpose in life.

就在那时，查理做出了一个改变他人生的决定。查理决定，如果某件事让他如此恐惧，他就必须直面恐惧，勇敢地去做。当时他并不知道，这个决定只是一段旅程的开始，它将引领查理重新发现他的激情和人生目标。

Over the next year and a half Charlie lived by one credo: If he was afraid of something, he confronted that fear. Charlie's progress was slow but steady. Basically, he crawled until he could walk. He took baby steps until he could run. Once he got going, there was no stopping him.

在接下来的一年半里，查理一直秉持着一个信条：如果他害怕什么，就勇敢面对。查理的进步缓慢而稳健。基本上，他先是爬到能走，然后一步步蹒跚而行，直到能跑。一旦他迈出第一步，就势不可挡。

Over the period of about eighteen months Charlie took several steps toward rediscovering his passion and purpose in life.

在大约十八个月的时间里，查理采取了几步措施来重新发现他的生活激情和目标。

He became more and more active in his No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's group, revealing himself and confronting fellow group members.

他在“不再是好人！”男子团体中变得越来越活跃，暴露了自己的身份并与团体成员对峙。

He began looking at the neglect he experienced and the distorted fear-based messages he received in his family.

他开始反思自己所遭受的忽视以及家人给他传递的扭曲的、基于恐惧的信息。

He asked his father to come to counseling with him where he confronted him on his unavailability and lack of concern for his well being in childhood.

他请父亲陪他一起去接受心理咨询，并直言父亲在童年时期缺乏关心和照顾。

He quit blaming his lack of money for flying lessons on his girlfriend.

他不再将没钱上飞行课归咎于女友。

He changed flying schools when his current school was unable to provide him with the type of instruction and equipment he needed.

当他现在的飞行学校无法为他提供他所需要的指导和设备时，他就转学了。

He began interviewing for jobs that took advantage of his engineering degree.

他开始面试能发挥其工程学位优势的工作。

He began confronting his feelings of inadequacy, family messages about playing it safe, and distorted beliefs about his qualifications as an engineer.

他开始正视自己的不足感、家人劝他谨慎行事的话语以及对他作为工程师资格的扭曲看法。

He confronted his girlfriend (whom he was initially terrified of) about her lack of participation in household responsibilities.

他质问女友（他最初很害怕她）为何不承担家务。

He took his solo flight and got his pilot's license.

他独自飞行并获得了飞行员执照。

He allowed his men's group to take him out for his birthday to a restaurant where he faced his fear of being the center of attention.

在他的生日那天，他允许他的同伴带他去一家餐馆，在那里他克服了成为关注焦点的恐惧。

He applied for and got a job with an engineering firm that expressed a belief that he was capable, talented, and had something to offer their company.

他申请并得到了一家工程公司的工作，该公司相信他有能力、有才华，可以为公司做出贡献。

When Charlie told the group about getting the job I realized that I had witnessed a metamorphosis of epic proportions. Charlie had gone from being an introverted, frightened, and passive Nice Guy, to an evolving man with passion and purpose.

当查理告诉大家他得到这份工作时，我意识到自己见证了一场史诗般的蜕变。查理从一个内向、胆怯、被动的好人，变成了一个充满激情、目标明确的成熟男人。

I asked Charlie to send me an email with his formula for success. Here is what he wrote:

我请查理给我发一封邮件，附上他的成功秘诀。他是这样写的：

Bob,

Here is roughly how I arrived at the new job.

1) Very first before anything else could happen I had to stop being a victim.

2) Began by setting boundaries. At first they were small ones and they grew with time.

3) From the boundaries being set and respected I started believing in myself.

4) Honesty came along somewhere during this time.

5) Believing that I am an adult, I have an education, and I am qualified to take on the role of an Industrial Engineer.

6) I always knew that my previous employer was dysfunctional and that it was comfortable for a reason.

When I finally realized and accepted that I did not need that system to survive, I could finally move on.

Charlie

鲍勃，我大致是这样开始新工作的。1）首先，我必须停止当受害者，之后才能开始其他的事情。2）从设定界限开始。起初，界限很小，随着时间的推移逐渐扩大。3）从设定并尊重界限开始，我开始相信自己。4）在这段时间里，我开始变得诚实。5）我相信自己已经成年，受过教育，并且有资格担任工业工程师。6）我一直都知道，我之前的雇主运作不正常，而且他们给人感觉舒服是有原因的。当我最终意识到并接受我不需要那个系统来生存时，我终于可以继续前进了。查理

Breaking Free Activity #40

挣脱束缚活动#40

Look over the list below. Choose one of the items and name a tangible fear from your life. Write down how you will confront that specific issue. Then, take a small step toward facing that fear.

看看下面的清单。选择其中一项，并说出你生活中一个切实存在的恐惧。写下你将如何面对这个具体问题。然后，迈出一小步，去面对它。

Ask someone to encourage and support you. Don't try to do it alone.

寻求他人的鼓励和支持。不要试图独自一人。

Remember, no matter what happens, you will handle it.

记住，无论发生什么事，你都会处理好的。

Ask for a raise or promotion

要求加薪或晋升

Quit an unsatisfying job

辞去一份不满意的工作

Start your own business

创办自己的企业

Go back to school

回学校

Confront a conflict situation

面对冲突局势

Promote an idea or something you have created

推广你的想法或创造的东西

Pursue a lifelong goal

追求终身目标

Spend more time with a hobby or interest

花更多时间培养爱好或兴趣

*****************************


Charting Their Own Path Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

规划自己的道路可以让好人过上他们想要的生活

Most folks — Nice Guys included — do not consciously take responsibility for creating the kind of life they want. Most people just accept where they are and act as if they have little power in shaping an exciting, productive, and fulfilling life.

大多数人——包括“好人”——并没有自觉承担起创造自己想要的生活的责任。大多数人只是接受现状，表现得好像自己无力塑造一个令人兴奋、富有成效、充实的生活。

When I talk with Nice Guys about taking charge of their lives, most have a difficult time wrapping their brain around the concept. It just doesn't fit their paradigm that they can make choices and act to make these choices a reality.

当我和“好男人”们谈论如何掌控自己的人生时，大多数人都难以理解这个概念。这根本不符合他们那种“自己可以做出选择，并采取行动去实现这些选择”的思维模式。

I encourage Nice Guys to visualize creating a life where they do what they love and get paid for it. Most of them have difficulty with this concept. They act as if I am asking them to believe a fairy tale. Occasionally they will dismiss the idea with the excuse, "Not everybody can be lucky like you (referring to me) and have a job they really love and get paid well for it too." For a while, I accepted this logic until it dawned on me that the life I was living had nothing to do with luck.

我鼓励“好男人”们想象自己能做自己喜欢的事，还能获得丰厚的报酬。他们中的大多数人都难以接受这个概念。他们表现得好像我在让他们相信一个童话故事。有时他们会用“不是每个人都能像你（指我）一样幸运，拥有一份真正热爱的工作，还能获得丰厚的报酬”这样的借口来驳斥这个想法。有一段时间，我接受了这种逻辑，直到我逐渐意识到，我的生活与运气无关。

Earning a Ph.D. involved a conscious decision, persistence, and hard work — not luck.

获得博士学位需要有意识的决定、坚持和努力，而不是运气。

Building a counseling practice involved facing fears, quitting a secure, well-paying job, making sacrifices, working a second job to pay bills, learning by trial and error, and a living through a period of poverty — not luck.

建立咨询实践需要面对恐惧、辞去稳定、高薪的工作、做出牺牲、做第二份工作来支付账单、通过反复试验学习、度过一段贫困时期——而不是靠运气。

Developing my skills as a therapist involved a commitment to personal growth, constant evolution, and a financial investment in my own therapeutic process — not luck.

培养我作为治疗师的技能需要致力于个人成长、不断进步以及对我自己的治疗过程的财务投资——而不是运气。

Writing a book, building a website, and getting published required persistence and the confrontation of numerous fears — not luck.

写书、建网站并出版需要坚持不懈并克服无数恐惧——而不是运气。

I'm not anything special. I'm an ordinary guy with ordinary talents. I have many of the same fears as the Nice Guys with whom I work. I don't have any special talent or skill that the majority of my clients don't have.

我没什么特别的。我只是个普通人，拥有普通的才能。我和那些共事的“好人”们有很多共同的恐惧。我没有任何大多数客户所不具备的特殊才能或技能。

What's the difference?

有什么区别？

  • A conscious decision to face fears.
    自觉地决定面对恐惧。
  • A conscious decision to not settle for mediocrity.
    自觉地决定不满足于平庸。
  • A conscious decision to make my own rules.
    自觉地决定制定自己的规则。

Think about the people you respect or look up to. Most probably started with nothing but still found ways to create interesting, productive, and passionate lives. These people charted their own paths and made their own rules. What makes them different? Most are just ordinary people who took charge of their lives.

想想那些你尊敬或敬仰的人。他们大多白手起家，却依然找到了创造精彩纷呈、卓有成效、充满激情人生的方法。这些人规划了自己的道路，制定了自己的规则。是什么让他们与众不同？他们大多只是掌控自己人生的普通人。

Here's the good news — if they can do it, so can you. One of my favorite affirmations is, What one man can do, another man can do. Think about it — if others have taken charge and created lives worth emulating, so can you. The only thing stopping you from having the kind of life you really want is you . It is time to start charting your own path, making your own rules, and making your dreams a reality.

好消息是——如果他们能做到，你也能。我最喜欢的箴言之一是：一个人能做到的，另一个人也能做到。想想看——如果其他人都能掌控局面，创造出值得效仿的人生，你也能。唯一阻止你拥有真正想要的生活的，就是你自己。现在是时候开始规划你自己的道路，制定你自己的规则，并将你的梦想变成现实了。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #41

挣脱束缚活动#41

What do you really want in life? What prevents you from making it happen? Write down three things you want to make happen in your life. Then write a personal affirmation that will take you where you want to go and post it on a sheet of paper where you can see it. Share your dreams and your affirmation with a safe person.

你真正想要什么？是什么阻碍了你实现它？写下你人生中想要实现的三件事。然后写下一句能带你到达目的地的个人誓言，并把它贴在你能看到的地方。与一个值得信赖的人分享你的梦想和誓言。

*****************************


Letting Go Of Trying To Do It Right Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

放弃努力做好事可以让好人过上他们想要的生活

This book began as a few chapters I planned to write to give to the men in my first No More Mr. Nice Guy! group. Initially, when there was no agenda or goal, my writing was a spontaneous recording of my growing insight into the Nice Guy Syndrome. Before long, clients and family members began suggesting that I write a book. It seemed like a logical extension of what I was already doing so the idea made perfect sense.

这本书最初只是我计划写的几章，送给我第一个“不再是好人！”小组的男士们。起初，我没有任何计划或目标，只是即兴地记录我对“好人综合症”的理解。不久之后，客户和家人开始建议我写一本书。这似乎是我目前工作的一个合理延伸，所以这个想法完全合情合理。

It was at that time that something began to change. Rather than being just a few insights and illustrations written for the benefit of a handful of clients, my effort became directed at producing something that would be deserving of publication and widespread distribution. I began hearing people suggest things like "bestseller," "Oprah," and "get rich."

就在那时，事情开始发生变化。我不再只是为少数客户撰写一些见解和插图，而是专注于创作一些值得出版和广泛传播的作品。我开始听到人们推荐“畅销书”、“奥普拉”和“致富”之类的词。

What was once an effortless labor of love began to falter under the weight of expectation. In order to live up to the lofty standards people were suggesting, my book had better be good. Not just good —perfect!

曾经毫不费力、充满爱意的创作，如今在重压之下却显得力不从心。为了达到人们所期望的崇高标准，我的书必须写得好。不仅仅是好，而是完美！

With that agenda I labored for six years to complete No More Mr. Nice Guy! The most common question I was asked by friends and family during this time was "When are you going to finish your book?"

抱着这样的目标，我努力了六年，终于完成了《不再是好人》！这段时间，朋友和家人最常问我的问题就是：“你什么时候才能写完这本书？”

Over the years the manuscript went through at least three major revisions as well as extensive editing. Numerous factors contributed to the length of time it took to finish the book, but the number one reason was that I thought it had to be perfect. I thought the book had to be perfect to be published. I thought it had to be perfect for anyone to buy it. It thought it had to be perfect for it to help anyone.

多年来，手稿至少经历了三次重大修订和大量的编辑。许多因素导致了这本书耗时如此之长，但最重要的原因是我认为它必须完美无缺。我认为这本书必须完美无缺才能出版。我认为它必须完美无缺才能让任何人购买。我认为它必须完美无缺才能帮助任何人。

Unfortunately, this gross misperception had a number of detrimental consequences: I believed I had to write everything I knew about the Nice Guy Syndrome! The original manuscript of this book was probably four times its present length. I believed I had to be an eloquent writer. I believed the text had to be flawless.

不幸的是，这种严重的误解带来了一系列有害的后果：我以为我必须把我所知道的关于“好人综合症”的一切都写出来！这本书的原稿大概是现在的四倍长。我以为我必须是一位雄辩的作家。我以为文字必须完美无瑕。

I went to therapy to find out why I couldn't finish my book. My children became disillusioned, predicting that I never would finish. My wife half-seriously threatened to leave me if I didn't finish.

我去接受心理治疗，想知道为什么我没能完成这本书。我的孩子们感到失望，预测我永远也写不完。我的妻子半开玩笑地威胁我，如果我写不完，她就离开我。

Finally, after years of frustration, I had a breakthrough. A very wise person suggested that I give myself permission to never publish the book. I felt an immediate sense of relief.

终于，在经历了多年的挫折之后，我终于取得了突破。一位非常明智的人建议我允许自己永远不出版这本书。我顿时感到如释重负。

I realized that I had gotten away from my original goal — to write a few insights that would help a few men live better lives. Once I let go of the burden of having to get published, be a best-selling author, and appear on Oprah, everything changed. I went back to my original agenda. From then on when I wrote, I only asked myself one thing: "Will this help my clients find answers to their problems?" I also kept reminding myself that my clients would never get a chance to benefit from my insight if I never finished the book.

我意识到自己已经偏离了最初的目标——写一些洞见，帮助一些人过上更好的生活。一旦我放下了出版书籍、成为畅销书作家以及上奥普拉脱口秀的重担，一切都改变了。我回归了最初的目标。从那时起，每次写作，我只问自己一件事：“这能帮助我的客户找到问题的答案吗？”我也不断提醒自己，如果我没写完这本书，我的客户就永远没有机会从我的洞见中受益。

Once I gave up the belief that No More Mr. Nice Guy! had to be perfect, things began to fall into place. I completed the book. Clients reported that it was changing their lives. Therapists began to request copies for their clients. Radio talk show hosts and newspaper and magazine writers began contacting me for interviews. I hired an agent. Publishers began pursuing me.

自从我不再相信《不再是好人！》必须完美无缺后，一切开始步入正轨。我完成了这本书。客户们说这本书改变了他们的生活。治疗师们开始为他们的客户索要这本书。电台脱口秀主持人、报刊杂志撰稿人开始联系我进行采访。我聘请了一位经纪人。出版商也开始联系我。

Trying to do it right only sucked the life out of No More Mr. Nice Guy! Letting go and letting it just be "good enough" set me free to embrace my passion and create something of lasting value. This same principle applies to every area of the recovering Nice Guy's life.

试图把事情做好只会让我不再是“好人”！放手，让它“足够好”让我自由地拥抱激情，创造一些具有持久价值的东西。同样的原则也适用于正在康复中的“好人”生活的各个方面。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #42

挣脱束缚活动#42

How does your perfectionism or need to do it right get in the way of realizing your passion and potential? Pick one thing that you have always wanted to do: Write a book, turn your hobby into a business, move, go back to school, fully embrace a talent.

你的完美主义或对完美主义的执着，是如何阻碍你实现激情和潜力的？选一件你一直想做的事情：写一本书，把你的爱好变成事业，搬家，重返校园，或者充分发挥你的才能。

Now, ask yourself the question: If you knew ahead of time that this endeavor would be a success, would you hesitate to do it? Would this knowledge set you free from the belief that you have to do it perfectly? Would this knowledge motivate you to get started or complete what you have already begun? What risks would you be willing to take if you knew ahead of time that there was no way for you to fail?

现在，问问自己：如果你事先知道这件事会成功，你会犹豫吗？这种认知能让你摆脱必须完美完成的信念吗？这种认知能激励你开始做这件事，或者完成你已经开始的事情吗？如果你事先知道你不可能失败，你愿意承担什么样的风险？

What are you waiting for? Let go of the need to do it perfectly and just do it!

还在等什么？别再追求完美，直接行动吧！

*****************************


Learning To Ask For Help Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

学会寻求帮助可以让好人过上他们想要的生活

A major reason Nice Guys frequently fail to live up to their potential is that they believe they have to do everything themselves. Phil is a good example.

好男人常常无法充分发挥自身潜力的一个主要原因是，他们认为自己必须事事亲力亲为。菲尔就是一个很好的例子。

Phil's goal in life was to be rich. He seemed to have a lot of things going for him: He was good looking, intelligent, outgoing, and funny. Yet Phil always seemed to fall way short of achieving his lofty goals and dreams. A number of things got in the way — taking shortcuts, procrastination, and insecurities about whether he deserved to get what he really wanted.

菲尔的人生目标是致富。他似乎拥有很多优点：英俊、聪明、外向、风趣。然而，菲尔似乎总是无法实现他的远大目标和梦想。很多事情阻碍了他——走捷径、拖延，以及对自己是否值得得到真正想要的东西的不安全感。

Perhaps Phil's greatest roadblock was his difficulty asking people for help. Phil had a number of faulty core beliefs about people helping him. He didn't believe he deserved to get what he wanted. He didn't believe his needs were important to other people. He believed the surest way not to get his needs met was to ask in clear and direct ways.

菲尔最大的障碍或许在于他难以向人求助。菲尔对别人的帮助抱有诸多错误的核心观念。他不相信自己应该得到自己想要的东西。他不相信自己的需求对别人来说很重要。他认为，用清晰直接的方式提出请求，最肯定无法满足他的需求。

One day in his No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's group, Phil was lamenting about the lack of sex in his relationship with his wife. I asked Phil if he asked his wife to have sex with him. He said "no." I asked him if he believed his wife wanted to have sex with him. To this he also replied in the negative.

有一天，菲尔在“不再是好人！”的男士小组里，抱怨着自己和妻子之间缺乏性生活。我问菲尔，他是否要求妻子和他发生性关系。他说“没有”。我问他，是否相信妻子想和他发生性关系。他也回答说“没有”。

I told Phil that I thought his lack of sex was symptomatic of a bigger problem in his life — him not thinking his needs were important and not believing that other people wanted to help him meet his needs. I suggested that changing his beliefs about his sexual needs might be the place to begin changing core beliefs that prevented him from having other things he wanted in life.

我告诉菲尔，我认为他缺乏性生活是他生活中一个更大问题的征兆——他不认为自己的需求重要，也不相信别人会帮助他满足这些需求。我建议，改变他对性需求的看法，或许能让他从根本上改变那些阻碍他获得其他生活所需的核心信念。

The next week, Phil was grinning from ear to ear. "My wife and I had sex," he beamed. The group shared in his enthusiasm. They wanted to know how it happened. "I asked," was Phil's simple reply.

接下来的一周，菲尔笑得合不拢嘴。“我和妻子做爱了，”他笑容满面地说。大家也分享了他的热情。他们想知道是怎么发生的。“我问的，”菲尔简单地回答道。

I questioned Phil how his wife felt about having sex with him. "She was fine about it," he replied. "She said she likes having sex with me, but that I hadn't asked in a long time so she didn't think I was interested."

我问菲尔，他妻子对和他做爱有什么感觉。“她觉得没事，”他回答。“她说她喜欢和我做爱，但我很久没主动开口了，所以她觉得我没兴趣。”

A week later, Phil told the group he was dreading asking his father-in-law to borrow money to get the old single-pane windows replaced on his house. Some of the group members began asking questions about the cost. Some shared that they had done that kind of project before. I suggested that Phil ask the group to help him. It was like pulling teeth, but Phil asked the men if they would help him replace his windows. The members of the group responded unanimously that they would be glad to. About a month later the men got together at Phil's house and had the equivalent of an old fashion barn-raising.

一周后，菲尔告诉大家，他很害怕向岳父借钱更换房子的旧单层玻璃窗。一些小组成员开始询问费用。有些人说他们以前做过类似的项目。我建议菲尔请大家帮忙。这就像拔牙一样费劲，但菲尔还是问大家是否愿意帮他更换窗户。大家一致表示很乐意。大约一个月后，大家聚集在菲尔家，举行了一场相当于老式谷仓搭建的聚会。

These two experiences had a tremendous impact on Phil. He began to see that his needs were important, that people wanted to help him meet his needs, and that the surest way of getting people to help was to ask.

这两次经历对菲尔的影响巨大。他开始意识到自己的需求很重要，人们都愿意帮助他，而获得帮助最可靠的方法就是主动请求。

Phil began to build on this new paradigm. Within a few weeks he shared a plan with the group to start his own business. A friend of the family had offered to help him get started in his own landscaping business. This prospect especially excited him because the seasonal work would allow him to teach snowboarding during the winter — a life long dream.

菲尔开始在这个新模式的基础上继续发展。几周后，他向大家分享了自己创业的计划。一位家庭朋友主动提出帮助他创办自己的园林绿化公司。这个前景让他特别兴奋，因为这份季节性工作可以让他在冬天教滑雪——这可是他毕生的梦想。

An old friend offered to be his financial backer. His wife volunteered to look for a job that paid health insurance benefits. Men in the group offered to help him write a business plan and set up his bookkeeping.

一位老朋友主动提出为他提供经济支持。他的妻子主动提出帮他找一份有医疗保险福利的工作。小组里的男人们主动提出帮他写一份商业计划书，并帮他做账。

As long as Phil tried to do everything himself, he struggled to get what he wanted. Once he started asking for help and letting people be there for him, his life began to turn around. He is now headed in the direction of creating the kind of life and vocation he has always dreamed of.

菲尔总是想自己做所有事，却总是难以得到自己想要的东西。一旦他开始寻求帮助，并接受别人的帮助，他的生活就开始发生转变。现在，他正朝着创造自己一直梦想的生活和职业的方向前进。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #43

挣脱束缚活动#43

Do you believe your needs are important? Do you believe other people want to help you meet your needs?

你认为自己的需求很重要吗？你相信别人会愿意帮助你满足你的需求吗？

On a sheet of paper, make a list of helpers you have in your life right now. These can be friends and family members. They can be professionals such as doctors, lawyers, therapists, and CPAs.

在一张纸上，列出你目前生活中的帮助者。这些人可以是朋友、家人，也可以是专业人士，比如医生、律师、治疗师和注册会计师。

After making the list, answer the following questions:

列出清单后，回答以下问题：

  • What kind of helpers do you still need?
    您还需要什么样的帮助者？
  • How can you use these helpers more effectively?
    您如何才能更有效地利用这些帮助者？
  • How do you prevent these people from helping you?
    您如何阻止这些人帮助您？

Start looking for opportunities to ask these people for help. Build networks. Before asking for help, repeat the affirmation: This person wants to help me get my needs met.

开始寻找机会向这些人寻求帮助。建立人脉网络。在寻求帮助之前，重复以下肯定：这个人想帮助我满足我的需求。

*****************************


Identifying Self-Sabotaging Behaviors Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

识别自我破坏行为可以让好人过上他们想要的生活

As mentioned earlier in the chapter, Nice Guys find numerous creative ways to sabotage their success in life. They waste time, they procrastinate, they start things but don't finish, they spend too much time fixing other people's problems, they distract themselves with trivial pursuits, they create chaos, they make excuses.

正如本章前面提到的，好男人总能想出各种花招来破坏自己人生的成功。他们浪费时间，拖延，做事有始无终，花太多时间解决别人的问题，用琐碎的事情分散自己的注意力，制造混乱，找借口。

Sal is a good example of this. Sal was raised by a passive father and a schizophrenic mother. Neither parent was available to pay attention to him or meet his needs. At a young age he had to take responsibility for the welfare of his younger brother. Sal had virtually no options as a child. When he felt frightened or overwhelmed, he would just hunker down and trudge ahead with dogged determination.

萨尔就是一个很好的例子。萨尔的父亲消极被动，母亲患有精神分裂症。父母双方都无法关心他，满足他的需求。他很小的时候就不得不承担起照顾弟弟的责任。萨尔小时候几乎没有选择。每当他感到害怕或不知所措时，他就会蜷缩起来，带着顽强的决心艰难前行。

As an adult, Sal ran a body shop for his uncle. His uncle was a cheap, shortsighted, marginally involved business owner. It was Sal's job to create a profitable business with the limited resources and dissatisfied employees his uncle provided. (Sal actually operated on the assumption that this feat could be successfully accomplished!)

成年后，萨尔为叔叔经营一家车身修理厂。叔叔是个吝啬、目光短浅、几乎不参与经营的企业主。萨尔的工作就是利用叔叔有限的资源和不满意的员工，打造一家盈利的企业。（萨尔实际上就是假设这项壮举能够成功实现！）

Every week when Sal came to men's group, his first order of business was to pop a couple of Tylenol to quiet the stress headache created by trying to negotiate an impossible situation at work. On one occasion I asked Sal if he wanted to explore options for his work situation. "What's the use?" he replied. "There's nothing that can be done."

萨尔每周来男士小组，第一件事就是吃几片泰诺，缓解工作中难以应付的局面带来的压力性头痛。有一次，我问萨尔想不想探索一下解决工作困境的方案。“有什么用？”他回答说，“什么办法也做不了。”

For about fifteen minutes, group members asked questions and proposed options. Sal looked like a man undergoing a root canal with no anesthetic.

大约十五分钟的时间里，小组成员们互相提问，并提出了各种方案。萨尔看起来就像一个正在接受没有麻醉的根管治疗的人。

"Could you talk with your uncle and let him know how difficult your job is with the resources he gives you?"

“你能和你叔叔谈谈，让他知道，凭借他给你的资源，你的工作有多困难吗？”

"I've tried that. He doesn't care."

“我试过了。他不在乎。”

"Could you offer profit sharing to motivate your employees?"

“你能提供利润分享来激励你的员工吗？”

"My uncle is too cheap. He would never go for it."

“我叔叔太小气了。他绝对不会买这个的。”

"Could you hire an assistant to help with the work load?"

“你能雇一个助手来帮忙分担工作量吗？”

"We tried that once and it didn't work out."

“我们尝试过一次，但没有成功。”

"Could you get out of management and go back to painting cars?"

“你能辞去管理职务，回去继续画汽车吗？”

"I would make more money but it is too toxic."

“我想赚更多的钱，但这太有害了。”

"Could you get out of the auto body business and do something else?"

“你能退出汽车车身行业，做点别的吗？”

"Like what? I've got a mortgage, a wife, and two kids. How am I supposed to start over now?"

“比如什么？我有房贷，有妻子，还有两个孩子。我现在该怎么重新开始呢？”

"What is your passion, your dream job?"

“你的热情所在是什么？你的梦想职业是什么？”

This time, Sal paused for a moment before answering. "I've always wanted to teach martial arts. There is no way it could ever happen though. I'd have to work evenings and weekends. My wife just wouldn't go for that and I'd be away from the kids too much."

这次，萨尔停顿了一下才回答。“我一直想教武术。但这根本不可能。我得在晚上和周末工作。我妻子不会同意，而且我会离孩子太远。”

With each question asked and each option proposed, Sal grew noticeably tenser. His eyes reflected a terror as if he was being interrogated by Gestapo agents with cattle prods and ice picks. When it became apparent that exploring possible options only aggravated his fear and caused him to shut down further, the group members mercifully backed off. Later, Sal referred to the experience as "being reamed by the group."

随着每一个问题被提出，每一个选项被提出，萨尔的紧张感明显增强。他的眼神中流露出恐惧，仿佛被盖世太保特工用电击棒和冰锥审问。当意识到探索各种可能的选择只会加剧他的恐惧，让他更加封闭自己时，小组成员仁慈地让步了。后来，萨尔把这段经历称为“被小组成员痛骂”。

In most situations, Nice Guys aren't victims to others, they victimize themselves. By his attitudes and actions, Sal all but guaranteed that he would never experience any kind of success or satisfaction in his job. It was much more familiar and comfortable to stay stuck in a stressful, no-win situation.

在大多数情况下，好人不会成为别人的受害者，而是会成为自己的受害者。萨尔的态度和行为几乎注定了他永远不会在工作中获得任何成功或满足感。陷在充满压力、毫无胜算的境地，对他来说更熟悉、更舒服。

Every Nice Guy with whom I have worked has at some point had to make a conscious decision to stop sabotaging himself. This is a crucial aspect in recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome. In order to start getting what they want in life, work, and career, recovering Nice Guys have to make the conscious decision to get out of their own way.

我共事过的每一位好男人，都曾在某个时刻不得不做出一个清醒的决定，不再自我破坏。这是从“好男人综合症”中康复的关键。为了在生活、工作和事业中得到自己想要的东西，正在康复中的好男人必须做出清醒的决定，不再给自己设限。

One way of doing this is by changing the way they think about change. This begins with Nice Guys becoming aware of why they unconsciously create so many barriers that keep them feeling stuck. A mortgage, a wife, a lack of a degree, debt, children — are all just excuses. Making significant life changes doesn't require chucking all these things. It means seeing them for what they really are —excuses — and taking small steps in the direction one wants to be going.

做到这一点的方法之一是改变他们对改变的看法。首先，好男人要意识到为什么他们会无意识地设置那么多障碍，让自己感到停滞不前。房贷、妻子、没有学位、债务、孩子——这些都只是借口。做出重大的人生改变并不需要抛弃所有这些。这意味着要看清它们的真面目——借口——并朝着自己想要的方向迈出一小步。

For example, Sal could begin teaching martial arts one evening per week. He could begin working at paying down personal debt to enable him to change jobs in the future. He could refocus time spent on trivial, unsatisfying activities.

例如，萨尔可以开始每周抽出一个晚上教武术。他可以开始努力偿还个人债务，以便将来换工作。他可以把时间花在那些琐碎、不尽兴的事情上。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #44

挣脱束缚活动 #44

Identify how you sabotage yourself. Once you have identified your patterns, determine what you have to do differently to get what you really want. Review each item below and identify specific behaviors that will help you stop sabotaging yourself and achieve your goals.

找出你是如何自我破坏的。一旦你确定了自己的行为模式，就需要思考如何改变才能实现你真正想要的。回顾以下每一项，找出一些具体的行为，帮助你停止自我破坏，实现目标。

Focus

重点

Do it now

立即行动

Accept "good enough" rather than "perfect"

接受“足够好”而不是“完美”

Finish what you start

完成你开始的事情

Don't start new projects until the old ones are completely finished Don't make excuses

旧项目未完成前不要开始新项目，不要找借口

Detach from other people's problems

远离他人的问题

Share your strategy with a safe person. Check in with them on a regular basis to monitor how you are doing (failing to do this part would be an effective way to sabotage yourself).

与一位值得信赖的人分享你的策略。定期与他们沟通，了解你的进展（不这样做很容易自取灭亡）。

*****************************


Developing A More Accurate View Of The World Allows Nice Guys To Get the Life They Want

培养更准确的世界观可以让好人过上他们想要的生活

Ever wonder why other people seem to have so much more than you — more money, a better job, a nice car, a prettier wife? Do you envy these people? Do you resent them for having what you don't? Do you wonder when it will be your turn?

你是否想过，为什么别人似乎比你拥有更多——更多的钱、更好的工作、更漂亮的车、更漂亮的妻子？你羡慕这些人吗？你是否怨恨他们拥有你所没有的东西？你是否想过，什么时候轮到你？

Due to their early life experiences, Nice Guys tend to be ruled by deprivation thinking. They believe there is only so much to go around, and if someone else already has a lot, there is less for them.

由于早年的生活经历，“好男人”往往受剥夺性思维的支配。他们认为，人人都有有限的资源，如果别人已经拥有很多，自己能得到的就更少了。

Nice Guys have a difficult time comprehending that we live in an abundant, ever-expanding universe.

好人很难理解我们生活在一个丰富且不断扩张的宇宙中。

They tend to see the goodies as being in short supply. They hang on tightly to what they've got, fearing there won't be more when it is gone. They believe they have to control and manipulate to ensure that what little is out there won't go away. They play it safe, not trusting that their needs will always be abundantly met.

他们往往觉得好东西总是供不应求。他们紧紧抓住自己拥有的东西不放，唯恐失去后就再也得不到了。他们认为自己必须控制和操纵，才能确保那点儿微不足道的东西不会消失。他们行事谨慎，不相信自己的需求总能得到充分满足。

This paradigm of scarcity can be illustrated by a Nice Guy named Russell. As a successful salesman, Russell earned a comfortable six-figure income. He religiously put forty percent of his take home pay into savings and investments. He kept a minimum balance of $30,000 in his checking account. In spite of his ability to create financial wealth, Russell was controlled by his deprivation thinking. Russell was so afraid of financial ruin that he would not allow his wife to buy a $9.00 video for his children at Costco if it wasn't in the budget.

这种稀缺性可以用一个名叫罗素的“好人”来阐释。作为一名成功的销售员，罗素的收入高达六位数，令人欣慰。他总是把40%的净收入存入储蓄和投资账户。他的支票账户至少有3万美元的余额。尽管罗素拥有创造财富的能力，但他却被剥夺思维所控制。罗素非常害怕经济崩溃，以至于如果预算不够，他甚至不允许妻子在好市多（Costco）为孩子们买一台9美元的录像机。

Russell's deprivation thinking in regard to money was a reflection of his view of the world in general.

罗素对于金钱的剥夺思想反映了他对整个世界的看法。

His father was miserly and rigid. He seemed to single Russell out for critical treatment, while heaping praise and favor on his two brothers. Later, before he died, his father cut Russell out of his will and gave Russell's share to the church. It is no wonder that Russell viewed the world through lenses clouded by deprivation.

他的父亲吝啬又刻板。他似乎总是挑出拉塞尔来批评，而对他的两个兄弟却大加赞赏和宠爱。后来，他的父亲临终前，把拉塞尔从遗嘱中剔除，把拉塞尔的那部分遗产捐给了教会。难怪拉塞尔的眼光总是被贫困蒙蔽。

When we come to see the world as a place of abundance we come to realize that there is plenty for everyone. Everything we need is flowing by us — all we have to do is get out of the way of our own small thinking and let it come.

当我们将世界视为一个富足之地时，我们就会意识到，每个人都拥有充足的资源。我们所需的一切都在身边流淌——我们只需摆脱狭隘的思维，让它们自然而然地到来。

Look around at the wealth — the cars people drive, the houses they live in, the trips they take. You can't argue with the sheer material abundance that can be created in our world. If other people are living full, abundant lives, why not you? Remember, what one man can do another man can do.

看看周围的财富——人们开的车，住的房子，旅行的次数。你无法否认我们这个世界所能创造的物质财富之丰富。如果其他人都过着充实富足的生活，你为什么不呢？记住，一个人能做到的事情，另一个人也能做到。

If one man can make a million dollars, why can't you?

如果一个人可以赚一百万美元，你为什么不能？

If one man can start the business of his dreams, why can't you?

如果一个人可以开创他梦想的事业，你为什么不能？

If one man can drive a Mercedes, why can't you?

如果一个人可以驾驶奔驰，为什么你不能？

If one man can quit a crummy job and find a better one, why can't you?

如果一个人可以辞掉一份糟糕的工作并找到一份更好的工作，你为什么不能呢？

If one man can be a snowboarding instructor, why can't you?

如果一个人可以成为滑雪教练，为什么你不能？

Unfortunately, the world can't give us something that we're not ready to receive. Since deprivation thinking keeps a person holding tightly to what he already has, there is no receptivity for receiving more.

不幸的是，世界无法给予我们尚未准备好接受的东西。由于剥夺思维会让人紧紧抓住已有的东西不放，因此无法接受更多。

As Phil found out, when we ask for what we want, and expect to receive it, it will come one way or another.

正如菲尔所发现的，当我们要求我们想要的东西并期望得到它时，它就会以某种方式实现。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #45

挣脱束缚活动#45

Set this ebook down for a few moments and close your eyes. Take a couple of deep breaths and exhale slowly. Clear your mind.

把这本电子书放下，闭上眼睛。深呼吸几次，然后慢慢呼气。清空你的思绪。

Once you are relaxed, picture yourself living in an abundant world. In this abundant world, there are no restraints or limitations. Good things flow past you continuously. Imagine every abundant thing you have ever desired — car, home, friends, love, joy, wealth, success, peace of mind, challenge. Visualize yourself living your life surrounded by this abundance.

放松下来后，想象自己生活在一个富足的世界。在这个富足的世界里，没有任何束缚或限制。美好的事物源源不断地从你身边流过。想象一下你渴望的一切——汽车、房子、朋友、爱情、喜悦、财富、成功、内心的平静、挑战。想象你生活在这富足的包围之中。

Repeat this visualization several times a day until it begins to feel real to you. Open your arms, your heart, and your mind. Get out of the way, and let it happen.

每天重复几次这样的观想，直到它开始变得真实。张开你的双臂，敞开你的心扉，敞开你的思想。放下阻碍，让它自然发生。

*****************************


Get the Life You Want: The No More Mr. Nice Guy! Strategy For Success

过你想要的生活：告别“好好先生”！成功策略

Nice Guys believe there is a set of rules that govern all behavior. They are convinced that if they can figure out these rules and successfully abide by them, they will have a smooth, happy life. They also believe that there are dire consequences for failing to discern and obey these rules.

好男人相信有一套规则支配着一切行为。他们坚信，只要能理解并遵守这些规则，就能拥有顺利幸福的生活。他们也相信，不了解并遵守这些规则会带来可怕的后果。

Discovering passion and purpose requires figuring out what works and what doesn't. Mature, successful people establish their own rules. These rules are measured by only one standard: Do they work?

发现激情和目标需要弄清楚什么有效，什么无效。成熟成功的人会制定自己的规则。衡量这些规则的唯一标准是：它们有效吗？

Over the years, the men in my No More Mr. Nice Guy! groups have discovered a number of rules that work for them. These rules have helped them discover their passion and live up to their potential. These rules have helped them create the kind of life and vocation they really desire.

多年来，我的“不再是好人！”小组里的男士们发现了一些对他们有用的规则。这些规则帮助他们发现了自己的热情所在，并发挥了自身的潜力。这些规则帮助他们创造了自己真正渴望的生活和职业。



*****************************

Breaking Free Activity #46

挣脱束缚活动#46

Read over the list of rules below. Try a few of them on for size. Add to the list your own personal rules. Write these rules on note cards and put them where you can see them every day.

阅读下面的规则列表。尝试其中几条，看看效果如何。然后添加你自己的个人规则。把这些规则写在便签上，放在你每天都能看到的地方。

1. If it frightens you, do it.

1. 如果某件事令你害怕，那就去做吧。

2. Don't settle. Every time you settle, you get exactly what you settled for.

2. 不要妥协。每次妥协，你得到的都和你妥协的一样。

3. Put yourself first.

3. 把自己放在第一位。

4. No matter what happens, you will handle it.

4.无论发生什么事，你都会处理好。

5. Whatever you do, do it 100%.

5.无论做什么，都要全力以赴。

6. If you do what you have always done, you will get what you have always got.

6. 如果你做你一直在做的事情，你就会得到你一直得到的东西。

7. You are the only person on this planet responsible for your needs, wants, and happiness.

7. 你是这个星球上唯一对自己的需求、愿望和幸福负责的人。

8. Ask for what you want.

8. 提出你想要的东西。

9. If what you are doing isn't working, try something different.

9. 如果你正在做的事情没有效果，请尝试其他方法。

10. Be clear and direct.

10. 清晰直接。

11. Learn to say "no."

11.学会说“不”。

12. Don't make excuses.

12.不要找借口。

13. If you are an adult, you are old enough to make your own rules.

13. 如果你已经成年，你就已经足够大，可以制定自己的规则了。

14. Let people help you.

14.让别人帮助你。

15. Be honest with yourself.

15. 对自己诚实。

16. Do not let anyone treat you badly. No one. Ever.

16. 不要让任何人亏待你。永远不要。

17. Remove yourself from a bad situation instead of waiting for the situation to change.

17. 让自己摆脱困境，而不是等待情况改变。

18. Don't tolerate the intolerable — ever.

18.永远不要容忍无法容忍的事情。

19. Stop blaming. Victims never succeed.

19. 不要责怪别人。受害者永远不会成功。

20. Live with integrity. Decide what feels right to you, then do it.

20. 诚实生活。决定什么对你来说是正确的，然后去做。

21. Accept the consequences of your actions.

21. 承担你行为的后果。

22. Be good to yourself.

22. 善待自己。

23. Think "abundance."

23.思考“富足”。

24. Face difficult situations and conflict head on.

24. 直面困难局面和冲突。

25. Don't do anything in secret.

25.不要偷偷做任何事。

26. Do it now.

26.现在就做。

27. Be willing to let go of what you have so you can get what you want.

27. 愿意放弃你所拥有的，这样你才能得到你想要的。

28. Have fun. If you are not having fun, something is wrong.

28. 玩得开心。如果你不开心，那一定是哪里出了问题。

29. Give yourself room to fail. There are no mistakes, only learning experiences.

29. 给自己失败的空间。没有错误，只有学习的经验。

30. Control is an illusion. Let go; let life happen.

30. 控制只是幻象。放手吧，让生活自然发生。

*****************************


It is time to start getting what you want. Breaking free from the Nice Guy Syndrome will allow you to discover your true passion and potential. By taking responsibility for creating the kind of life you really want, you can become all that you were meant to be.

是时候开始追求你想要的一切了。摆脱“好人综合症”能让你发现自己真正的激情和潜力。肩负起责任，创造你真正想要的生活，你就能成为你命中注定成为的样子。

# ↩

Epilogue

# ↩

结语


It took me six years to write this book. During this time I have worked with countless Nice Guys and their partners. I have averaged three No More Mr. Nice Guy! men's groups per week. In group time alone, that's over 1,800 hours of working with Nice Guys. During this time I have observed many exciting and profound things.

我花了六年时间写这本书。在此期间，我与无数“好男人”及其伴侣共事。我平均每周参加三次“不再是好男人！”男士小组活动。仅小组活动时间，我就花了超过1800个小时与“好男人”们共事。在此期间，我观察到了许多激动人心且意义深远的事情。

I have watched countless men go from being helpless, passive, controlling, and resentful victims to becoming empowered, integrated males.

我目睹了无数男人从无助、被动、控制欲强、怨恨的受害者转变为强大、健全的男人。

I have seen numerous relationships dramatically improve and I've seen just as many die an overdue death.

我见过无数的关系显著改善，也见过同样多的关系走向消亡。

I've listened to unsolicited testimonials and read letters of gratitude from both men and women about the changes they have experienced in their lives.

我听过主动提供的证言，读过男人和女人写的感谢信，讲述了他们生活中经历的变化。

I have received responses from men and women all over the world who have seen themselves or someone they love in the description of the Nice Guy on my website.

我收到了来自世界各地的男男女女的回复，他们在我的网站上对“好男人”的描述中看到了自己或他们所爱的人。

Based on observing all of these things, here is my greatest discovery: The tools and insights presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! work!

基于对所有这些事情的观察，我最大的发现是：《不再是好人！》中提出的工具和见解有效！

Having finished reading this book, I encourage you to start again at the beginning. Take the time to do the Breaking Free exercises. If you have not already done so, find a safe person or group to assist you on your journey of recovery from the Nice Guy Syndrome. If you are in a relationship, ask your partner to read the book. Share with him or her the insights you are discovering about yourself.

读完这本书后，我鼓励你从头开始。花点时间做“挣脱束缚”练习。如果你还没有这样做，请找一个可靠的人或团体来帮助你走出“好人综合症”。如果你正在恋爱中，请让你的伴侣读一读这本书。和他或她分享你对自己的发现。

Working the program of recovery presented in No More Mr. Nice Guy! is one of the greatest gifts you can give yourself and your loved ones. As you learn to approve of yourself you will discover within you an unimaginable ability to love and accept love and to live life to its fullest. This kind of expansiveness is initially frightening, but it is the essence of who you are and what you are meant to be.

实践《不再是好人！》中提出的康复计划，是你能给予自己和所爱之人最棒的礼物之一。当你学会认可自己时，你会发现自己内心蕴藏着一种难以想象的能力，去爱、去接受爱，去尽情享受生活。这种豁达最初或许令人畏惧，但它正是你是谁以及你注定成为什么样的人的精髓所在。

With this discovery of your true self comes unlimited freedom. Freedom to be just who you are.

发现真我，你就会获得无限的自由。做真实的自己，自由自在。

Freedom to stop seeking approval. Freedom to start getting what you want.

不再寻求认可的自由。开始获得你想要的一切的自由。

格洛弗博士在他的网站 www.NoMoreMrNiceGuy.com 上提供了许多帮助人们恢复“好男人”形象的资源。其中包括：

    印刷材料和录音。
  • Free online support groups.
    免费在线支持小组。
  • Therapy groups for men.
    男性治疗小组。
  • Long distance counseling by phone and email.
    通过电话和电子邮件进行远程咨询。

    研讨会、讲习班和课程。
  • Calendar of current events.
    时事日历。

    问答专栏。

Take advantage of the resources offered on NoMoreMrNiceGuy.com and start getting what you want in love and life.

利用 NoMoreMrNiceGuy.com 提供的资源，开始获得您在爱情和生活中想要的东西。


About The Author

关于作者

Dr. Robert Glover is the world’s leading authority on the Nice Guy Syndrome , which grew out of his own search for answers to his frustrations in love and life. Since recognizing and labeling the Nice Guy Syndrome over ten years ago, Dr. Glover has spent thousands of hours working with hundreds of Nice Guys and their partners. In addition to working with these men in individual and couples therapy, Dr. Glover leads No More Mr. Nice Guy! therapy groups and conducts seminars around the country on

罗伯特·格洛弗博士是“好人综合症”研究领域的世界权威，这一综合症源于他本人对爱情和生活中各种挫折的探寻。自十多年前发现并命名“好人综合症”以来，格洛弗博士已花费数千小时与数百名“好人”及其伴侣合作。除了为这些男性提供个人和夫妻治疗外，格洛弗博士还在全国各地领导“不再是好人！”治疗小组，并举办研讨会。

Breaking Free From The Nice Guy Syndrome . His website, www.NoMoreMrNiceGuy.com, presents resources to assist men in their recovery. Dr. Glover is a frequent guest on radio talk shows and has been featured in numerous publications, including Esquire Magazine .

摆脱“好人综合症”。他的网站 www.NoMoreMrNiceGuy.com 提供各种资源，帮助男性康复。格洛弗医生经常做客电台脱口秀节目，并曾被《时尚先生》等众多出版物报道。

Dr. Glover, a Certified Marriage and Family Therapist, with a doctorate in Marriage and Family Therapy, is married to Elizabeth Oreskovich, also a psychotherapist. Together they co-direct the Center For Healing And Recovery. They have four children and make their home in Tacoma, Washington.

格洛弗博士是一位获得认证的婚姻与家庭治疗师，拥有婚姻与家庭治疗博士学位。他的妻子伊丽莎白·奥雷斯科维奇也是一位心理治疗师。他们共同领导着“疗愈与康复中心”。他们育有四个孩子，现居华盛顿州塔科马。
